- **rule:** `AuraDatabaseRestore`'s terminal success phase is **`Submitted`**, not `Completed`, and the one-shot guard must list it. `markSubmitted` deliberately refuses to write `Completed` — Aura restores asynchronously and the v2beta1 database endpoint returns only an `id` with no status (id 87 / the `DatabaseSummary` landmine), so completion is genuinely unobservable and claiming it would assert something the operator cannot know. When you add or change a one-shot Aura CR, the guard and the writer must be read **together**: a guard that tests a phase nothing writes is dead code that looks correct.
- **why:** The guard listed only `"Completed"` and `"Error"`. Because nothing ever wrote `"Completed"`, it never fired: every subsequent reconcile — an operator restart, a cache resync, any watch event on the CR — re-submitted the **same restore**, silently overwriting a database that had already been restored. Data loss, from a two-word mismatch between a guard and its writer, in a controller that had **no test coverage at all**. Found while auditing the user-facing docs for id 90, because the API reference documented a `Completed` phase the code cannot produce — the doc drift and the bug had the same root.
- **pinned-by:** `TestAuraDatabaseRestore_IsOneShot` (`internal/controller/aura_native_controllers_test.go`) reconciles three times and asserts `RestoreDatabase` was called **exactly once**, and that the terminal phase is `Submitted`. Verified to fail (3 calls) against the pre-fix guard. `TestAuraDatabaseRestore_InFlightAttemptIsNotReplayed` pins the ambiguous state (no replay, no requeue, `RestoreOutcomeUnknown`, message names the console) and `TestAuraDatabaseRestore_TransientFailureStillRetries` pins that the marker does not wedge a legitimate retry.
- **crash safety (added 2026-07-31): a phase-based one-shot guard also needs an AMBIGUOUS state.** Fixing the guard stopped the every-event replay but left a narrower hole: if the process stopped between Aura accepting the restore and the status write, the persisted phase was `Restoring` — which the guard permits — so the next reconcile re-sent a destructive restore. Aura offers no idempotency key and no way to read a restore's state, so the outcome genuinely cannot be recovered. `Submitting` is now persisted BEFORE the API call (its write error is propagated — an unrecorded attempt is the thing being prevented) and finding it on a fresh reconcile is terminal with reason `RestoreOutcomeUnknown`, telling the user to check the console. A transient failure REPORTED by Aura clears the marker so retries still work; the residual risk is a 5xx, which says the server erred rather than that nothing was applied.
- **enforcement:** unit test. The other one-shot reconcilers were checked and are safe **by a different mechanism, which is worth knowing before you "make them consistent"**: `aurarestore` does guard on phase but uses the same `auraRestore*` constants its writers use (so the two cannot drift apart), while `aurasnapshot` and `auradatabasebackup` have **no phase guard at all** — they are idempotent through their recorded external ID (`status.snapshotId` / `status.backupId`), which is the more robust pattern. **PROSE-ONLY — at risk**: nothing mechanically ties a guard's phase set to the phases its controller writes, so a raw string guard like the one that caused this is not detectable by grep.

### id 94 — A destructive, non-idempotent operation must be guarded by an UNCACHED read, never the informer cache
- **scope:** `internal/controller/neo4jrestore_alldatabases.go` (`issueGuardAllowsRecreate` and its call site before `RecreateDatabaseWithSeedURI` / `CreateDatabaseWithSeedURIOptions`); the `APIReader` field on `Neo4jRestoreReconciler` (`internal/controller/neo4jrestore_controller.go`) and its wiring from `mgr.GetAPIReader()` in `cmd/main.go`; the `APIReader` field and `reader` on `Neo4jShardedDatabaseReconciler` (`internal/controller/neo4jshardeddatabase_controller.go`), through which each pass reads its sharded database, so `destructiveRestorePending` (the rule 64 guard of the drop-and-recreate), `shardedSeedConsumable` and the phase steps decide from the last pass's write (id 136).
- **rule:** Before issuing an operation that is destructive and cannot be safely repeated, re-check the "have I already done this?" flag against the **API server**, not the manager's client. `mgr.GetClient()` reads through the informer **cache**, which lags writes — so "persist the flag before returning, and the next pass will see it" is necessary but **NOT sufficient**. The next reconcile can legitimately read a pre-write copy and do the work twice. Fail CLOSED on a read error (defer rather than risk a second attempt) and fail OPEN when no `APIReader` is wired, since the cached check has already run and this can only ever be an additional guard.
- **why:** Reproduced 2026-08-02 on a local Kind cluster, and it **permanently bricks a database**. The all-databases restore issued `dbms.recreateDatabase` for `neo4j` **twice in the same second** — two `RestoreStarted` events at `01:11:02` carrying consecutive resourceVersions (237709, 237710). Two concurrent seeds then raced on the same staging directory and Neo4j parked the database offline for good: `currentStatus=offline`, `requestedStatus=online`, `statusMessage="Unable to obtain lock on file: /data/databases/neo4j/temp-copy/neo4j/database_lock"`. It never recovers, so **no timeout rescues it** — an earlier change had raised the spec's budget from 12 to 20 minutes on the theory that the restore was merely slow, which would only have delayed the same failure by eight minutes while looking like a fix. That budget was reverted once the real cause was known. The code already carried the comment "issue the create/recreate exactly ONCE … re-issuing would wipe a partially-seeded database", so the INTENT was right and only the mechanism was wrong: a cached read cannot enforce once-only.
  Two smaller contributors worth knowing: `markDatabaseResult` swallows its status-write error (`_ = retry.RetryOnConflict(...)`), so a failed flip is silent; and the per-database loop drives one database per pass, so a single stuck database blocks every later one — `customers` and `inventory` sat at `Pending` for the whole budget behind a bricked `neo4j`.
- **pinned-by:** `TestIssueGuardRefusesASecondRecreate` (`internal/controller/neo4jrestore_alldatabases_test.go`) covers all five arms: server-says-Running refuses even when the caller's cached copy says Pending, first-pass allows, no-entry-yet allows, read-failure refuses, and unwired-APIReader fails open. `TestShardedReconcile_AStaleCacheDoesNotAnnounceReadyTwice` (`internal/controller/neo4jshardeddatabase_create_once_test.go`) pins the sharded database's read: its cached client serves the copy from before the Ready write, and the pass must not act on it (it does with the read moved back to the cache).
- **enforcement:** unit test + the uncached read itself. **PROSE-ONLY — at risk** for other destructive paths: nothing mechanically detects a once-only guard that reads through the cache. When adding one, ask what happens if the very next reconcile does not see your write — and note that this class is invisible to unit tests using a fake client, because the fake is strongly consistent.

### id 92 — A poll loop that discards its error is an undiagnosable stall; and a Gomega failure description is evaluated EAGERLY
- **scope:** the `StatusRunning` branch of `internal/controller/neo4jrestore_alldatabases.go` (`seedingProgressMessage`, `noteDatabaseProgress`); the `Eventually(...).Should(Equal("Completed"), …)` assertions in `test/integration/all_databases_restore_test.go`, `standard_database_pvc_restore_test.go`, `standard_database_minio_restore_test.go`.
- **rule:** Two independent traps, found together because each hid the other.
  1. **A requeue-driven poll must record WHY it is still waiting — and a status message alone cannot distinguish SLOW from STUCK.** The message holds only the latest state, so one sample of "0/1 allocations online" reads the same whether it just got there or has sat there since the first poll. Announce the progress as an EVENT as well, saying **how long** the database has shown it (`EventReasonRestoreSeedProgress`: "0/3 allocations online, unchanged for 40m"), which is exactly the missing signal. It is the restore's summary, once on the first poll and every 10 minutes after, from an event source of its own — not on every poll, which spent the restore's event budget, and not one per database change, which outran it on a large restore (id 166). (It was an event per database repeated unchanged, read through the apiserver's aggregated count and first/last timestamps.) The all-databases restore's poll read `online, total, _, stErr := DatabaseOnlineState(...)` and used `stErr` only to gate the success branch — so a database that never came online, and a `SHOW DATABASE` that failed on every single poll, were indistinguishable: both left `status.phase: Running` with an empty message, no event, and no log line (the branch logs nothing by design), for the entire test budget. Record the distinction — poll failed / no allocations yet / N-of-M online — and persist it only when the message CHANGES so a steady state still costs no API writes.
  2. **Gomega's optional failure description is an ordinary function argument, so its values are read when the assertion is BUILT, not when it fails.** `Should(Equal("Completed"), "…message=%q results=%+v", restore.Status.Message, restore.Status.DatabaseResults)` reports the CR as it was immediately after `Create` — i.e. always empty — no matter what the object later contained. Pass a lone `func() string` instead: Gomega calls that only on failure (`buildDescription` in `gomega/internal/assertion.go`).
- **why:** The 2026-07-31 extended run failed this spec with `phase=Running message="" results=[]` after 12 minutes. Trap 2 meant `message=""`/`results=[]` were guaranteed regardless of reality, and trap 1 meant the operator had recorded nothing either — so the run was **unfalsifiable after the fact**, and an investigation spent several steps reasoning about an empty `databaseResults` that was only an artefact of the test's own printf. Reproducing locally settled what the logs could not: all three databases (`neo4j`, `inventory`, `customers`) seed and come online in ~95 s with the same 30 s requeue, and CI issued only the first — `neo4j` never came online there. Neither change makes that spec pass; they make the next occurrence say which of the two it is.
- **pinned-by:** `TestSeedingProgressMessageDistinguishesStallFromProgress` (`internal/controller/neo4jrestore_alldatabases_test.go`) asserts the three states are mutually distinguishable and that a failing poll is reported as failing. Trap 2 is now **test-pinned** by `TestNoEagerGomegaStatusDescriptions` (`internal/controller/eager_gomega_description_guard_test.go`), which scans every `test/integration/*_test.go` for an `Eventually`/`Consistently` description that passes `.Status.*` as a printf argument. It lives in `internal/controller` on purpose — the integration package's own tests need a Kind cluster, so a guard there would not run in the blocking unit lane.
  **Why it is mechanical and not prose:** the first fix corrected three sites and wrote this rule; a later scan found **three more**, one of them in the very spec the investigation was about. Prose did not hold the line. Two lessons are baked into the guard itself: it balances parentheses from the `.Should(` rather than the start of the line (counting from the line start lets the `)` that closes `Eventually(...)` zero the depth immediately, so the guard silently inspects one line and never fires — it did exactly that until a deliberately reintroduced bug failed to trip it), and it fails loudly if it finds no specs to scan at all.
- **enforcement:** unit test (trap 1) + convention (trap 2). Note the related asymmetry left in place deliberately: the single-database cluster Cypher path guards against `dbms.recreateDatabase` being asynchronous with `cypherRestoreOnlineAcceptable` (a prior offline observation or a grace window), and the all-databases path has **no such guard** — so it can in principle accept pre-recreate allocations as "online". Untouched here because changing acceptance semantics needs its own evidence; recorded so the next reader knows it is a known difference, not an oversight.

### id 93 — A 100m CPU limit starves Neo4j AND anything exec'd beside it; pod-Ready also does not prove Bolt works
- **scope:** `applyCIOptimizations` / `applyCIOptimizationsStandalone` / `getCIAppropriateResourceRequirements` and `waitForBoltReady` in `test/integration/integration_suite_test.go`; `all_databases_restore_standalone_test.go`; `buildStandaloneHealthScript()` in `internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go` and `buildHealthScript()` in `internal/resources/health_script.go`.
- **rule:** Two facts, and it matters which one is load-bearing — an earlier version of this entry got that wrong.
  1. **THE CAUSE: an integration pod must get the 500m CPU limit, not the 100m base.** `getCIAppropriateResourceRequirements()` sets a CPU **limit of 100m**. `applyCIOptimizations` raises it to 500m — but it takes a `*Neo4jEnterpriseCluster`, so no standalone spec could reach it and standalones ran at a tenth of a core. Neo4j then needs ~3 minutes to reach `Started.`, and a **second JVM in the same cgroup — which is exactly what `kubectl exec … cypher-shell` is** — cannot finish booting within `podExecTimeout`: it prints NOTHING and is SIGKILLed at 60 s, every time, indefinitely. Use `applyCIOptimizationsStandalone` for standalone specs. Raising the *limit* costs no reservation; it only permits the burst cluster specs already permit.
  2. **Secondary, still true: pod-Ready does not prove Bolt works.** `/conf/health.sh` checks the Neo4j process plus **7474** — on a cluster server, also the HTTP cluster status endpoints for a bounded while after a start (id 181) — and never touches **7687**. So readiness cannot be read as "usable over Bolt". `waitForBoltReady` gates on an actual `RETURN 1`.
- **why:** The standalone all-databases spec failed **four consecutive extended runs** while 51 specs passed — including cluster specs using the *identical* in-pod `cypher-shell` technique, which pass only because `applyCIOptimizations` gives them 5× the CPU. The diagnosis took three wrong turns worth recording: (a) "the retry budget is too short" (id 80) — widening it to 6 minutes just produced six identical failures instead of two, but that is what made the pattern legible; (b) "the exec stream is wedged" — ruled out because the readiness probe is *itself* an exec and kept passing; (c) "Bolt is cold behind an HTTP-only gate" — ruled out by the container log, which shows `Bolt enabled on 0.0.0.0:7687` at 00:19:18 and `Started.` at 00:20:08, i.e. Bolt was serving for five minutes while `cypher-shell` still produced nothing. The signature that finally identified it: **`signal: killed` with EMPTY output, repeated at exactly the exec deadline**, means the process never got far enough to write a byte — a starved JVM, not a network or protocol problem.
- **pinned-by:** `applyCIOptimizationsStandalone` (`test/integration/integration_suite_test.go`), called by `all_databases_restore_standalone_test.go`; `waitForBoltReady` for fact 2. **PROSE-ONLY — at risk**: nothing stops a new standalone spec from using the base helper and inheriting the 100m limit. Manual check: a standalone integration spec that exec's into its pod must call `applyCIOptimizationsStandalone`.
- **enforcement:** convention + the shared helpers. **The product-side question stays OPEN:** adding a Bolt check to health.sh would make readiness mean what callers assume, but it changes when pods join Services and how rolling upgrades gate — it needs its own design, not a line slipped in with a test fix.

### id 90 — `multi_database` is fixed at instance creation, lives only on v2beta1, and is UNKNOWABLE for v1-created instances
- **scope:** `internal/aura/instance_v2beta1.go` (`CreateInstanceV2`, `GetInstanceV2`, `InstanceTypeV2`); `internal/controller/aura_multidatabase.go`; `AuraInstanceSpec.MultiDatabase` / `.OrganizationID` and `AuraInstanceObservation.MultiDatabase` / `.DefaultDatabaseID` in `api/v1beta1/aurainstance_types.go`; the create branch in `internal/controller/aurainstance_controller.go` (`observeOrCreate`) and the refusal path in `internal/controller/auradatabase_controller.go`; `aura.ReasonMultiDBOnly` / `IsMultiDatabaseOnly` in `internal/aura/errors.go`.
- **rule:** An Aura instance can host databases beyond its own built-in one only if it was created **multi-database**, and that is decided at creation and never again — Aura publishes no conversion API. Four consequences, all load-bearing:
  1. **Creating one requires v2beta1.** v1's create body has no such field, so an instance created through v1 — i.e. every instance this operator made before `spec.multiDatabase` existed — can never host an `AuraDatabase`. `spec.multiDatabase: true` switches the CREATE call to `POST /v2beta1/organizations/{org}/projects/{project}/instances`; everything afterwards (observe, resize, pause/resume, upgrade, delete) stays on v1, which does see v2beta1-created instances. The tier must be translated (id 87 item 11) — never pass a v1 tier name — and the AuraDS tiers have no v2beta1 equivalent at all.
  2. **The tier is NOT the control, but it IS a gate.** Necessary and not sufficient, in both directions: a business-critical instance created through v1 is still single-database and its database create is refused just like a free one — *and* only `business-critical` and `virtual-dedicated-cloud` accept `multi_database` at all (`free` and `professional` are refused with HTTP 400 / `multi-database-tier-not-supported`, verified live on both). So documentation saying "multi-database tiers" without "created with the flag" is wrong, and CEL must restrict the flag to those two tiers. Confirmed on `virtual-dedicated-cloud` by an intentionally-invalid-region probe that got past the tier check and failed on the project's tier entitlement instead (`db-tier-invalid`).
  3. **The verdict is THREE-VALUED — yes / no / unknown — and must never be collapsed to two.** `multi_database` appears only on the v2beta1 instance **detail** (not v1's GET, not even the v2beta1 LIST), and that endpoint 500s for v1-created instances (id 87 item 10). So `status.atProvider.multiDatabase` unset means UNKNOWN, and an unknown verdict must still attempt the create. Recording "false" on a probe failure would wrongly block every AuraDatabase against the majority of instances. Because the underlying fact is immutable, the probe is **one-shot**, guarded by the `neo4j.com/multi-database-probed` annotation — re-probing would burn an API call every reconcile forever on precisely the instances where it cannot work.
  4. **BOTH API refusals are terminal and must be reclassified as such.** The create-side one (`multi-database-tier-not-supported`, 400) is terminal because `type` is immutable; the database-side one is next. The instance reconciler routes both through `failTerminal` (no requeue) rather than its default requeue-forever `fail`.
  5. **The database-side refusal is a 409 and must be reclassified as TERMINAL.** `{"message":"Only multi database Instances can add databases","reason":"multi-db-only"}` arrives with HTTP 409 — the same status Aura uses for "another operation is in flight". `IsConflict` therefore excludes `ReasonMultiDBOnly` explicitly, and the AuraDatabase controller translates it into a refusal (`Ready=False`, reason `InstanceNotMultiDatabase`) with **no requeue**.
  6. **Adoption-by-name must not bind a `multiDatabase` CR to an unconfirmed instance.** The name-match loop in `observeOrCreate` runs BEFORE the create branch (it is the idempotency guard), and the v1 list cannot report `multi_database`. Unguarded, a `multiDatabase: true` CR whose name matches a pre-existing single-database instance — which is every instance an earlier operator version made — silently adopts it and never creates the right one, reporting Ready against an instance that can never host an `AuraDatabase`. `verifyAdoptableAsMultiDatabase` confirms via v2beta1 first and adopts only on a definite yes. A definite no and an UNCONFIRMED answer are treated the same (do not adopt) because they cannot be distinguished — the v2beta1 instance GET answers 500 both for a v1-created instance and for an outage — but neither is terminal: the reconciler requeues with reason `AdoptionBlocked` so it never binds the wrong instance, never creates a duplicate paid one, and self-heals. `spec.instanceId` remains the deliberate escape hatch (it bypasses name matching entirely).
  7. **`AuraInstance.spec.organizationId` must participate in the DATABASE coords too.** It is required to create a multi-database instance, so an instance can legitimately carry the org while using inline credentials and no provider config. `resolveAuraDBCoords` originally consulted only the resource's own `organizationId` and the provider default, so every `AuraDatabase`/backup/restore against such an instance failed `TargetUnresolved` — or, with a provider default that differed, silently targeted the WRONG organization. Precedence is: the resource's own override → the instance's → the provider default.
  Also: fields the v2beta1 create silently drops (id 87 item 12) are **refused** in combination with `multiDatabase`, in CEL and again in `multiDatabaseCreateRequest`, rather than quietly ignored.
- **why:** Before this, the three `AuraDatabase*` CRDs could not work against any operator-created instance, and the way they failed told the user nothing: a bare 409 whose message ("Only multi database Instances can add databases") names no field, no fix, and no reason it will never succeed — retried every 30 seconds forever because `IsConflict` reads any 409 as "come back later" (the id 88 trap in a different guise). The three-valued rule is the subtle half: the obvious implementation reads the probe's failure as "not multi-database" and then refuses every AuraDatabase on every v1-created instance — turning a clear error for a few users into a silent wall for all of them. Discovered by walking the write paths live on 2026-07-31: create database → backup → restore → delete against a purpose-built multi-database Business Critical instance, after the same sequence had been refused on a free instance and on a v1-created enterprise-db one.
- **pinned-by:** `TestAuraInstance_MultiDatabaseCreatesViaV2beta1` pins that v1 create is NOT used, the tier translation, the org-scoped path, the annotation/status write, and that the next reconcile's wholesale `atProvider` rebuild does not erase the facts; `TestAuraInstance_ProbeFailureRecordsUnknownAndStopsAsking` pins one-shot probing, `unknown` (not false) on a 500, and that Ready survives; `TestAuraDatabase_RefusesKnownSingleDatabaseInstanceWithoutCallingTheAPI` and `TestAuraDatabase_TranslatesTheLiveMultiDBOnly409` pin both refusal routes, the `InstanceNotMultiDatabase` reason, `RequeueAfter == 0`, and that the message names `spec.multiDatabase` instead of echoing the API's; `TestAuraDatabase_UnknownVerdictStillAttemptsTheCreate` pins the three-valued rule from the other side (all in `internal/controller/aura_multidatabase_test.go`); `TestMultiDatabaseCreateRequest` pins acceptance of both capable tiers and the refusals for free/professional, the AuraDS tiers and silently-dropped fields; `TestSupportsMultiDatabaseMatchesWhatAuraAccepts` and `TestMultiDatabaseTierUnsupportedIsRecognised` pin the two-tier gate and its reason code; `TestAuraInstance_DoesNotAdoptANonMultiDatabaseInstance` pins landmine 6 for both the definite-no and unconfirmed cases (no adoption, no duplicate create, requeue, reason `AdoptionBlocked`) and `TestAuraInstance_AdoptsAConfirmedMultiDatabaseInstance` pins that a confirmed instance is STILL adoptable — without which a crash between create and the annotation write would leak a paid instance; `TestResolveAuraDBCoordsOrgPrecedence` pins landmine 7 including that the resource's own override still wins; `TestCreateInstanceV2RejectsASuccessWithNoID` pins that an empty create ID is an error, not an annotation; `TestMultiDatabaseOnlyIsTerminalNotAConflict` (`internal/aura/errors_v2beta1_shape_test.go`) pins the 409 reclassification while keeping `ongoing-database-operation` retryable; `TestInstanceTypeV2MapsTheVocabularies`, `TestCreateInstanceV2LiveContract` and `TestGetInstanceV2ReadsMultiDatabase` (`internal/aura/instance_v2beta1_test.go`) pin the client against verbatim live payloads.
- **enforcement:** unit test + CEL (`multiDatabase` immutable, tier restricted to `business-critical`/`enterprise-db`, incompatible-field combinations rejected) + runtime refusal in the reconcilers. **PROSE-ONLY — at risk** for the claim that a v2beta1-created instance stays fully manageable through v1: verified live for GET/DELETE and by v1 recognising the instance in its 409 operation guard, but resize and pause/resume on such an instance were not exercised to completion.

### id 98 — Dev mode's `-controllers` default must cover the whole dev registry, or CRs are accepted and silently ignored
- **scope:** `cmd/main.go` — `devControllerKeys` / `defaultDevControllers` and `devControllerRegistry`; `config/overlays/dev/kustomization.yaml` (which sets `--mode=dev`, so `make dev-up` and Tilt take this path).
- **rule:** Every key registered in `devControllerRegistry` MUST appear in `devControllerKeys`. Production mode (`setupProductionControllers`) loads all controllers unconditionally; dev mode loads only what the `-controllers` flag names. A registered-but-unlisted controller means dev mode **accepts the CR and then ignores it forever**. When a controller is deliberately excluded, the startup log now names it (`NOT loading controllers - their CRs will be accepted and then ignored`).
- **why:** The failure has no symptom to search for. All 12 `aura*` controllers were missing from the default for the whole of the Aura work: `kubectl apply` succeeded, and then `status` stayed **completely empty** — not a phase, not a condition — with no events and not one operator log line. It is indistinguishable from a hung reconcile, and it cost ~10 minutes of a release verification journey before the operator itself became the suspect. Helm/production installs were never affected, which is exactly why it survived: the CRDs worked for users and were inert for developers, so Aura could not be exercised locally at all.
- **pinned-by:** `TestDevControllerDefaultCoversRegistry` (`cmd/main_test.go`) asserts the default and the registry cover each other in both directions, and fails loudly if the registry is empty rather than passing vacuously. Verified to fire by removing `aurainvite` from the default.
- **enforcement:** test-pinned (`TestDevControllerDefaultCoversRegistry`, blocking `unit-tests` job).

### id 99 — Uninstalling a Managed plugin means editing `NEO4J_PLUGINS`; a Job cannot touch another pod's `/plugins`
- **scope:** `internal/controller/plugin_controller.go` — `RemoveFromNeo4jPluginList`, `MergeNeo4jPluginList`; `withoutPluginEntries` (`internal/controller/plugin_env_ownership.go`); the `plugins` EmptyDir volume in `internal/resources/cluster.go`.
- **rule:** Uninstall a Managed plugin by removing its name from the target StatefulSet's `NEO4J_PLUGINS` env var via `RemoveFromNeo4jPluginList` — the exact inverse of the install path's `MergeNeo4jPluginList`, and preserving every entry another controller owns (which names — those the plugin's record lists, `neo4j.com/plugin-entries.<plugin>`, and in Managed mode its own and its dependencies' — and what else the uninstall takes away in the same write: id 155). A plugin set to `spec.enabled: false` is taken off by the same write (id 160), and so is a Managed plugin whose name the target's image does not install (id 170). **Never** launch a Job to delete JAR files from `/plugins`: it is a per-pod `EmptyDir`, so a separate Job pod cannot address it, and the Neo4j Docker entrypoint repopulates it from `NEO4J_PLUGINS` on every container start. `RemoveFromNeo4jPluginList` returns its input unchanged when the plugin is absent, so callers must treat `result == input` as "skip the Update" and avoid a pointless rolling restart.
- **why:** Two bugs in one construction. The removal Job declared its **own** fresh `EmptyDir` named `plugins` and ran `rm -f /plugins/<name>*.jar` against it — deleting files in a directory it had just created, never touching Neo4j — so a Managed plugin was never actually uninstalled and came back on the next restart. Worse, the Job outlived the reconcile that created it: every later reconcile re-issued the same `Create` and failed `jobs.batch "<sts>-remove-plugin-<plugin>" already exists`, so the finalizer was **never released** and the CR stayed `Terminating` indefinitely, which also blocks namespace deletion. The integration suite missed it because those specs delete whole namespaces instead of deleting a `Neo4jPlugin` against a live deployment. Found by hand during the v1.14.0 verification journey.
- **pinned-by:** `TestRemoveFromNeo4jPluginList` and `TestRemoveFromNeo4jPluginListRoundTripsWithMerge` (`internal/controller/plugin_controller_unit_test.go`) pin removal, the untouched-foreign-plugin case, the unchanged-input signal, malformed-JSON erroring rather than wiping the list, and install/uninstall round-tripping. **No test asserts the absence of the Job — known gap:** the Job type is gone from the file, so there is nothing left to assert against.
- **enforcement:** unit test (list helper) + convention for the "no removal Job" half — **PROSE-ONLY — at risk** if someone reintroduces a Job-based uninstall.

### id 100 — Operator-managed conf keys must be excluded from EVERY user config map, not just `spec.config`
- **scope:** `internal/resources/cluster.go` — `operatorManagedConfKeys`, `buildNeo4jConfigForEnterprise`, `buildPropertyShardingConfig`; `internal/resources/memory_config.go` — `UserMemorySetting`, `GetMemoryConfigForCluster`.
- **rule:** A user-supplied conf key that the operator also derives must be dropped from **every** map a user can write it into — today `spec.config` and `spec.propertySharding.config` — using the single `operatorManagedConfKeys` set. Memory keys are then read back through `UserMemorySetting`, which consults both maps and is the only place that keeps `heap.initial_size` coherent with `heap.max_size`. Adding a new user-writable conf map means wiring it into **both** functions. Never let a user value reach the rendered conf by being appended after the operator's own block.
- **why:** `spec.config` had excluded the memory keys all along; `spec.propertySharding.config` merged everything verbatim, and its lines land **after** the operator's memory block where `DedupeNeo4jConf` keeps the last occurrence. A user `heap.max_size` therefore won while the derived `heap.initial_size` (60% of the memory **limit**) stayed — and the JVM refuses to start on `Initial heap size set to a larger value than the maximum heap size`. This shipped: `examples/property_sharding/development-property-sharding.yaml` set a 4G max against an 8Gi limit (derived initial 5G) and **CrashLoopBackOffed all three servers** when applied verbatim. `advanced-property-sharding.yaml` sat on the same path and survived only because its 12G happened to equal the derived value. The same hole let `server.bolt.tls_level` and `server.directories.certificates` be overridden through `propertySharding.config`, silently downgrading the operator-managed TLS posture.
- **pinned-by:** `TestHeapCoherenceAcrossUserConfigMaps` (precedence + clamping), `TestPropertyShardingConfigCannotOverrideOperatorMemoryKeys` (asserts the RENDERED conf — the struct-level view cannot see this bug), and `TestPropertyShardingConfigCannotOverrideTLSPosture` (the keys nothing reads back), all in `internal/resources/memory_config_test.go`. Each was verified to fire by reverting the corresponding half of the fix; the rendered-conf test reproduces the exact shipped failure (`initial_size="5G" max_size="4G"`).
- **enforcement:** test-pinned (three tests in the blocking `unit-tests` job).

### id 89 — An admin password beginning with `-` bricks the container; reject it, and never generate one
- **scope:** `internal/validation/admin_password_validator.go` (`ValidateAdminSecretPassword`); the call sites in `internal/validation/cluster_validator.go` (`resolveAdminSecretName`) and `internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go` (`standaloneAdminSecretName`); the `NEO4J_AUTH` construction in `internal/resources/cluster.go`; `randomPassword` in `test/integration/integration_suite_test.go`.
- **rule:** The admin password (key `password` in the Secret named by `spec.auth.adminSecret`, else the operator default) **must not begin with `-`**, and any generated password must anchor its first character. Both the cluster and standalone paths export `NEO4J_AUTH="<user>/<password>"`; the **upstream** Neo4j entrypoint then runs `neo4j-admin dbms set-initial-password <password>`, whose picocli parser reads a leading `-` as an **option**, so the positional parameter never binds. Validate inline (invariant 1) — the mis-parse is upstream, so the operator cannot fix it at runtime and rejecting the CR is the only way to make it comprehensible. A missing/unreadable Secret is deliberately NOT an error (it may be created after the CR, and the env var is projected `Optional:false`, so Kubernetes already blocks container creation with a clear message).
- **why:** The runtime symptom is maximally misleading. The container dies with `Missing required parameter: '<password>'` and **exit code 64** (picocli's usage-error code) — output that never mentions the password's shape — then CrashLoopBackOffs forever, and because the bad value is baked into the StatefulSet it never self-heals. It presents purely as "Neo4j won't start". `randomPassword` used `base64.RawURLEncoding`, whose alphabet includes `-`, so **1.58% of generated passwords** (measured over 200k samples; theoretical 1/64) tripped it — an intermittent CI failure that was **twice misdiagnosed as a Neo4j CalVer regression** (PR #314's 2026.04 cell, and again on the 2026-07-30 post-merge Extended run), and which motivated a CalVer anchor bump that had nothing to do with the actual cause. It was only identified once `.github/actions/collect-logs` was fixed to actually collect pod logs (id 84) — before that, the container output was never captured at all. Secondary effect worth knowing: a crash-looping JVM restarting for ~95 minutes on a 2-vCPU runner starves the node, which is a plausible aggravator for the unrelated-looking `boundedExec` exec-hang timeouts of id 80 seen in the same run.
- **pinned-by:** `TestValidateAdminSecretPassword_RejectsLeadingDash` and `TestValidateAdminSecretPassword_ToleratesAbsentSecretOrKey` (`internal/validation/admin_password_validator_test.go`) pin rejection of leading `-` (including after whitespace, and `--`), that the message names `set-initial-password` rather than restating the useless runtime error, that it never echoes the password, and that an absent Secret/key/value is tolerated; `TestResolveAdminSecretName` pins that validation inspects the SAME Secret the StatefulSet mounts. The generator anchor is **not** test-pinned — a 1-in-64 property cannot be asserted reliably in one run; it is enforced by construction (`"p" + …`) plus the comment at the call site.
- **enforcement:** runtime-enforced (inline validator rejects the CR) + unit test + convention for the generator — **PROSE-ONLY — at risk** for any FUTURE password source that bypasses `ValidateAdminSecretPassword` (e.g. a new CRD that projects its own `NEO4J_AUTH`). Not grep-guarded: the violating value is runtime data in a Secret, not a source pattern.

### id 88 — `aura.IsTransient` treats EVERY non-`APIError` as retryable; a deliberate refusal must be marked or it vanishes
- **scope:** `internal/aura/errors.go` (`IsTransient`); every `aura.IsTransient(err)` call site in `internal/controller/` (all the `aura*_controller.go` reconcilers plus `internal/controller/aura_fleet_provision.go`); `auraRetryableError`, `retryableAuraCall` and `isRetryableAuraCall` (`internal/controller/aura_common.go`) and their call sites: the list before each create and the create itself in `observeOrCreate` of `aurainstance_controller.go` (and `createMultiDatabaseInstance` in `aura_multidatabase.go`), `auracustomermanagedkey_controller.go` (with its adoption read), `auraipfilter_controller.go`, `auradatabase_controller.go` and `aurainvite_controller.go`, and the token rotation in `ensureToken` (`aura_fleet_provision.go`); the backup create in `auradatabasebackup_controller.go`.
- **rule:** `IsTransient` returns `true` for **any** error that is not an `*aura.APIError` — by design, on the assumption that such an error is a transport-level failure that never reached the API. Therefore an error the operator raises **itself** — a policy refusal, an unrecoverable-state decision, a "managementPolicies forbids this" — is ALSO reported as transient. When such an error can reach an `IsTransient` branch, it must carry a marker type that is checked FIRST (`fleetRefusalError` / `refusef` / `isAuraRefusal` in `internal/controller/aura_fleet_provision.go` is the reference implementation), or be routed to a terminal `fail(...)`/status write that never consults `IsTransient` (what the Aura CMK, instance, database, member and invite reconcilers do today). Never hand a bare `fmt.Errorf` decision of your own to an `IsTransient` branch.
  - **A step that returns one error from several calls classifies on a mark set at the Aura call, not on `IsTransient`.** The creates — `AuraInstance` (v1 and v2beta1), `AuraCustomerManagedKey`, `AuraIPFilter`, `AuraDatabase`, `AuraInvite` — each return one error from a list, the operator's own checks, the write of the external-ID annotation and the create. `retryableAuraCall` wraps the error where the Aura call failed, when `IsTransient` says to retry it; the caller retries quietly only what carries that mark (`isRetryableAuraCall`), after its refusal checks, and records everything else (`CreateFailed`). So a 429, a 5xx or a transport failure is retried as every other Aura call retries it, and an error the operator raises — a clone source not provisioned yet, a project without the requested configuration, a name taken by another key — still reaches status. The on-demand `AuraDatabaseBackup` checks its single call in place (`IsConflict` or `IsTransient`, as the snapshot create does). The fleet provisioner's token step classifies on the mark too: storing a minted token is a Kubernetes write, and its failure is reported. So does its deployment step (`ensureDeployment`, #566): `ListDeployments` and `CreateDeployment` are marked, and a failed write of the `neo4j.com/external-fleet-deployment-id` annotation after a create — a Kubernetes error the step's `!isAuraRefusal && aura.IsTransient` retried in silence — reaches `status.auraFleetManagement.message`. The next pass still adopts the deployment by name.
- **why:** The failure is silent and total: the reconcile returns `nil` with a requeue, so the operator retries forever, emits no event, and writes no status message — the user is told nothing at all while nothing progresses. This is not hypothetical. The fleet provisioner's "refusing to rotate an already-registered token" decision (id 86 landmine 2) was swallowed exactly this way; the operator would have looped indefinitely without ever surfacing the explanation. It was caught only because a test asserted the *status message* rather than just the absence of the rotate call. As of 2026-07-30 no other Aura reconciler is affected — each routes its own refusals to `fail(...)` — but the trap is latent for every new one, and the `IsTransient` fallback is correct and should NOT be changed to fix this. #525 (2026-10-24) found the two sides of it: the creates and the on-demand backup consulted nothing, so a 429 or a 5xx was recorded as `CreateFailed`/`BackupFailed` — phase `Error`, and a Warning on every retry, since an `APIError`'s text carries Aura's request ID; and the fleet token step did consult `IsTransient`, so a token Secret that could not be written (a Kubernetes error) went unreported while every pass minted, and rotated, again.
- **pinned-by:** `TestFleetProvision_RefusesToRotateARegisteredToken` (`internal/controller/aura_fleet_provision_test.go`) asserts the refusal reaches `status.auraFleetManagement.message` and not just that no rotate happened — asserting only the side effect's absence would pass even when the message is lost. `TestAuraCreate_ATransientFailureIsRetriedNotRecorded` (`internal/controller/aura_api_calls_test.go`: a 429, a 5xx, a transport failure and a 409 on each create and the backup; no Warning, no `Error`, a requeue) fails on the earlier controllers; `TestAuraCreate_AnErrorThatIsNotTransientStillReachesStatus` (same file: an `AuraInstance` whose clone source has no ID yet, a CMK create refused with a 400) fails when the instance or CMK caller asks `aura.IsTransient` instead of `isRetryableAuraCall`; `TestRetryableAuraCall` pins the mark; `TestFleetProvision_ARotationIsAnnouncedOnceItsTokenIsStored` (`internal/controller/aura_fleet_provision_test.go`) asserts the failed Secret write reaches `status.auraFleetManagement.message`, and `TestFleetProvision_AFailedDeploymentAnnotationWriteIsReported` (`internal/controller/aura_fleet_followups_test.go`) the failed annotation write — with a 503 of the list still retried quietly. **No guard covers the other reconcilers' own refusals — known gap:** their correctness rests on routing refusals to `fail(...)`, which nothing mechanically checks.
- **enforcement:** unit test (fleet path and the create paths) + convention — **PROSE-ONLY — at risk** for a new Aura call site. Deliberately **not** grep-guarded: catching "a self-raised error reaches an `IsTransient` branch" needs dataflow, not a pattern, and every candidate grep (`fmt.Errorf` near `IsTransient`) false-positives on the many legitimate wrapped API errors.

### id 87 — Where the live Aura API disagrees with its own published spec (verified 2026-07-30, extended 2026-07-31)
- **scope:** `internal/aura/fleet_v2beta1.go`, `internal/aura/ipfilter_v2beta1.go`, `internal/controller/aura_fleet_provision.go` (`parseFleetTime`); anything else built by reading `https://api.neo4j.io/v2beta1/spec.json`.
- **rule:** Where the published v2beta1 spec and the live API differ, the client follows the **observed behaviour**. Do not change these back to match the spec — each was corrected after being observed against a real account. Differences observed:
  1. **EVERY fleet-manager endpoint is `{"data":…}`-wrapped** — the single-deployment GET, `POST deployments`, and both `POST`/`PATCH .../token`. The spec declares all four **bare** (the GET as `allOf:[DetailedDeployment,{}]`, the writes with no envelope). Decoding bare yields an all-zero struct **with no error**, so the failure is silent: `CreateDeployment` returned an empty ID — so the external-ID annotation was written empty and every reconcile registered ANOTHER deployment — and the token calls returned an empty token. `POST deployments` also returns **HTTP 200, not the documented 201**. Verified by exercising the full lifecycle live on 2026-07-31.
  2. **`token.auto_rotate` (spec) is `token.auto_rotated` (live).**
  3. **`Server.mode_constraints` (spec) is `Server.mode_constraint` (live, singular).**
  4. **`IpFilter` returns an undocumented `brain_ip_addresses_enabled` boolean** absent from the spec entirely — and it is **WRITABLE**, not just returned (the API's empty-PATCH error lists it among the settable fields). Now modelled on `IPFilter` so a round-trip cannot clear it. Verified live 2026-08-01; this entry previously said "the operator drops it … but that is inference, not verified".
  5. **`IpFilter.updated_at` is declared `format: date-time` but returns RFC1123** (confirmed live 2026-08-01: `"Sat, 01 Aug 2026 10:22:01 GMT"`), while fleet token times return RFC3339 on the same API. Never assume one timestamp layout: `parseFleetTime` tries RFC3339(Nano) and RFC1123(Z) and treats Go's zero time as "never".
  6. **Live responses carry extra undocumented fields throughout** — `id`/`deployment_id`/`health`/`os_arch` on servers, `id`/`claimed_by` on tokens, `id`/`name`/`deployment_id` on both database shapes, `server_id`/`last_seen` on server databases.
  7. **Error body shapes differ BY API VERSION**: v1 returns `{"errors":[{message,reason}]}`, v2beta1 returns a bare `{message,reason}`. Both must be handled.
  8. **The OAuth token endpoints are interchangeable.** v2beta1 declares `tokenUrl: /v2beta1/oauth/token`, but a token from the v1 `/oauth/token` authenticates v2beta1 calls and vice versa. The operator authenticating once at the v1 URL is correct — this was previously flagged as an open risk and is now closed.
  9. **Two different database schemas on two similar endpoints** — see id 86 landmine 5. `.../deployments/{id}/databases` is `Database` (sizing); `.../servers/{sid}/databases` is `ServerDatabase` (role/writer/txn/lag/shards). Confirmed live: the deployment-level response genuinely has no shard or transaction data.
  10. **The v2beta1 instance GET returns HTTP 500 for a v1-created instance** — not 404 — with a body carrying an unrendered Go template and an internal address (redacted here; do not republish it): `invalid status code 404 [GET /aura-instances/{{.Instance_id}}]: <internal address redacted>/aura-instances/<id>`. The instance's *other* v2beta1 sub-resources (`…/databases`, `…/ip-filters`) resolve fine for the same instance; it is the instance GET alone that breaks. Anything reading that endpoint must treat every error as "unknown", never as "absent" or "false" — see id 90.
  11. **The v2beta1 tier vocabulary differs from v1's**, and the API itself is the authority: a bad `type` yields *"Input should be 'virtual-dedicated-cloud', 'business-critical', 'professional' or 'free'"*. v1 uses `free-db` / `professional-db` / `business-critical` / `enterprise-db`. Only `business-critical` is spelled the same in both. The v2beta1 instance create also takes **no `version`** and **no `tenant_id`/`project_id`** (the project is in the path; Aura picks the version), and requires only `name` + `type`.
  12. **v2beta1's handling of unknown request fields CHANGED.** On 2026-07-30, POSTing `totally_unknown_field` alongside a deliberately-bad `type` produced only the `type` error — unknown fields were silently dropped. **On 2026-09-30 the same probe returned `additional properties 'totally_unknown_field' not allowed`**: they are now rejected. The operator still refuses v1-only fields itself (the API's message does not name the CR field), and must not assume either behaviour is permanent.
  12a. **The v2beta1 WRITE contract can differ from the READ contract on the same resource** — not just in envelope but in FIELD NAMES and VALUE FORMAT. ip-filters writes `ip_range` (CIDR string) / reads `address`+`prefix_len`, and writes `entities` / reads `filtered_entities`, with the wrong name accepted at 200 and silently ignored (id 81). Never infer a request body from a response body on this API; probe it.
  13. **A THIRD error shape exists on v2beta1**: besides the bare `{message,reason}` of item 7, schema validation returns v1's `{"errors":[{field,message}],"message":…,"reason":"validation-error"}`, and *body* validation returns **plain text** (`- at '': missing property 'id'`). Also seen: capitalised keys (`{"Message":…,"Reason":…}`) — harmless only because Go's JSON decoding is case-insensitive. All four must round-trip through `newAPIError` with the status code preserved.
  14. **v1's instance `status` and v1's own operation guard can disagree.** While database-level v2beta1 operations were in flight, `GET /v1/instances/{id}` reported `status: running` while `POST /v1/instances/{id}/pause` refused with 409 *"currently undergoing an operation: creating"*. Do not treat v1 `status: running` as proof that a lifecycle action will be accepted. (Also: v1 rejects a bodyless POST without `Content-Type: application/json` — HTTP 415.)
  15. **Re-diffed and re-verified live on 2026-09-30** against a purpose-built multi-database Business Critical instance (created and deleted within ~15 minutes). The spec had changed on points the code relied on; the live API agreed with some and not others:
      - **Instance create now honours `storage` and `graph_analytics`** (`unavailable|serverless|plugin`), both previously refused by the operator on the multi-database path; **`vector_optimized` is refused on a multi-database instance** (`multi-database-capability-not-supported`). The operator now passes the first two and refuses the third with Aura's reason. **`spec.graphAnalytics`** (`unavailable|serverless|plugin`) replaces the deprecated `graphAnalyticsPlugin` boolean; because v1 has only `graph_analytics_plugin`, **`serverless` routes the CREATE through v2beta1 even without `multiDatabase`** (`wantsV2Create`), inheriting the org-ID requirement and the v1-only-field refusals, while the tier and `vectorOptimized` rules stay multi-database-only. `graphAnalytics` is immutable by CEL except for replacing the boolean with its equivalent. **Verified live 2026-09-30**: a plain `professional` instance created through v2beta1 with `graph_analytics: serverless` and no `multi_database` reports `serverless` / `multi_database: false` on v2beta1, and is managed normally through v1 — but **v1 reports `graph_analytics_plugin: false` for it**, because its boolean cannot express serverless. So NEVER converge or drift-check graph analytics from the v1 view; the operator applies it at creation only. Pinned by `TestServerlessGraphAnalyticsRoutesToV2Create`, `TestGraphAnalyticsResolutionAndV1Mapping` and the AuraInstance cases in `TestCELValidations`.
      - **Restore accepts BOTH `id` and `backup_id`** (the spec renamed it `backup_id`); the operator keeps `id`, the form verified on both dates.
      - **Database responses now carry `name`** (list, get, create). The operator does not yet match databases by name.
      - **Database create from `backup_id` or `source_database_id` (clone) returns `404 endpoint-not-found`** though the spec now documents both. Do not build on them until they answer.
      - **Database create returns HTTP 200**, not the documented 201.
      - **New surfaces, live but not modelled:** virtual graphs (need the `enable-graph-engine` org feature flag), activity feeds, import jobs; GDS sessions and agents as before. Fleet count fields are typed `string` in the spec but were integers live on 2026-07-30 — not re-checked (no Fleet deployment available).
- **why:** Five of the first nine cost real rework. Items 1–3 were implemented from the spec and produced empty telemetry and an empty token-expiry field — no error, no log, just zero values. Recording them stops a later spec-driven change from reverting the corrections. Item 8 closes a documented open question rather than leaving it as a permanent caveat.
- **pinned-by:** `TestFleetDeploymentsAndTokens` (`internal/aura/fleet_v2beta1_test.go`) serves live-shaped fixtures for 1–3, 6 and 9 and asserts the decoded values; `TestIPFilterLifecycle` (`internal/aura/ipfilter_v2beta1_test.go`) covers the ip-filter envelope. `TestGetInstanceV2ReadsMultiDatabase` and `TestCreateInstanceV2LiveContract` (`internal/aura/instance_v2beta1_test.go`) serve the verbatim live payloads for 10–12; `TestMultiDatabaseOnlyIsTerminalNotAConflict` and `TestNewAPIError_AllObservedShapes` (`internal/aura/errors_v2beta1_shape_test.go`) cover 13. Items 7, 8 and 14 are **PROSE-ONLY — at risk**; 4, 5, 10-13 are now covered by live-shaped fixtures: an undocumented field's absence, a timestamp layout and a cross-version auth equivalence cannot be pinned by a stub server that we ourselves write.
- **enforcement:** unit test (live-shaped fixtures) + **PROSE-ONLY — at risk**. Re-verify with a read-only sweep whenever the Aura spec is re-diffed; the sweep needs only an API client ID/secret and issues GETs exclusively.

### id 85 — Aura v1 is the lifecycle foundation; v1beta5 REMOVES fields we use, and the v1 CMK list is a summary
- **scope:** `internal/aura/client.go` (`DefaultBaseURL`), `internal/aura/types.go`, `internal/aura/cmk.go`, `internal/aura/instances.go`, `internal/aura/snapshots.go`; `internal/controller/auracustomermanagedkey_controller.go`, `internal/controller/aurainstance_controller.go`, `internal/controller/aurasnapshot_controller.go`.
- **rule:** The Aura instance lifecycle stays on **v1 (GA)**. Do NOT "upgrade" the base URL to `v1beta5`: it is **not a superset of v1** — it *removes* `secondaries_count`, `cdc_enrichment_mode` and snapshot `exportable` entirely (zero occurrences in the v1beta5 spec, schemas and examples alike), and drops `/graph-analytics/sessions*`. Moving would silently delete working CRD fields. v2beta1 is also not a candidate for lifecycle: it still has no pause/resume/snapshot/overwrite/upgrade/CMK (it *did* gain instance `memory`+`storage` scaling via PATCH, so "v2beta1 can't scale" is no longer true — the rest of the gap is what keeps us on v1). Three further v1 landmines: (1) **`GET /customer-managed-keys` returns a SUMMARY** (`id`, `name`, `tenant_id` only — `CustomerManagedKeySummary`); never compare `key_id`/`region`/`cloud_provider`/`instance_type` against a list entry, they are always empty there. Narrow on `name`, then confirm with `GET /{id}`. (2) `CreateInstanceRequest.Memory` must never serialize as `""` — a CEL rule requires `memory` for every tier except `free-db`, and the field carries `omitempty` so free-db omits it rather than sending an empty string. (3) `secondaries_count` and `cdc_enrichment_mode` are **absent from v1's PATCH `properties`** but real: they are named in the endpoint description and its "Update Secondary Count" / "Update CDC Enrichment Mode" examples. Same for snapshot `exportable`, which v1 shows only in examples and v2beta1 declares as required. Keep all three.
- **why:** The CMK summary mismatch was a live duplicate-registration bug: adoption compared four fields the list endpoint never returns, so it could never match, and a lost `neo4j.com/external-cmk-id` annotation would re-register a second key against the same KMS key. The v1beta5 removals invert the usual intuition that a newer beta is additive, so "just bump the spec version" is a regression waiting to happen. The undeclared-but-real PATCH fields look like operator invention on a naive schema diff and have already been mistaken for such once.
- **pinned-by:** `TestAuraCMK_AdoptByName` and `TestAuraCMK_SameNameDifferentKeyIsRefused` (`internal/controller/aura_controllers_test.go`) pin summary-shaped list output + detail-confirmed adoption + refusal to duplicate on a name collision; the `fakeCMKAPI.ListCustomerManagedKeys` fake returns `CustomerManagedKeySummary` deliberately. The v1beta5 removals are **PROSE-ONLY — at risk**: no test can guard a spec we do not call.
- **enforcement:** unit test (CMK adoption) + CEL (instance `memory`) + **PROSE-ONLY — at risk** for the version-choice rule. Recorded from the 2026-07-30 three-spec re-diff (v1 / v1beta5 / v2beta1). **Beware when re-running that diff with PyYAML:** the v1 `cdc_enrichment_mode` enum is written as bare `OFF`, which YAML 1.1 parses as boolean `false`, making the operator's correct `OFF;DIFF;FULL` enum look like drift.

  **The whole v1 lifecycle was verified live on 2026-08-01** (create → snapshot → restore → resize → pause → resume → tier upgrade → delete, on one throwaway professional-db). Results:
  - `CreateInstanceResponse` matches the live create body field-for-field, one-time credentials included.
  - The **undeclared PATCH fields are real** — landmine 3 upgraded from inference to verified: `secondaries_count` on a professional-db is rejected with `database-incapable-of-action` ("only available on Business Critical and Virtual Dedicated Cloud"), i.e. a SEMANTIC refusal, not "unknown field".
  - **`storage` is DERIVED from `memory`, not independently settable.** A memory resize auto-scales storage (2GB → 4GB alongside 1GB → 2GB), and a mismatched pair is a hard 400 `invalid-memory-size` naming both values. A CR whose `spec.storage` disagrees with its tier will therefore retry that permanent error forever — set memory and leave storage unset unless it matches.
  - **`connection_url` is NULL while an instance is resuming.** `syncStatus`'s `if observed.ConnectionURL != ""` guard is load-bearing: without it a pause/resume cycle would blank the published connection URL.
  - Every lifecycle POST is bodyless and returns **202 with the FULL instance object** (pause/resume/upgrade/snapshot-restore). `RestoreSnapshot` discards it (`nil` out) — a free status signal thrown away, same pattern as the v2beta1 database restore.
  - Pausing an already-paused instance is 409 `instance-action-unavailable`; the tier upgrade flips `type` to `business-critical` and makes `metrics_integration_url` appear.
  - **Snapshots:** `Snapshot` matches live exactly (`instance_id`, `snapshot_id`, `profile`, `status`, `timestamp`, `exportable`), `?date=` is accepted, and the profile constants are right (`Scheduled` for automatic, `AdHoc` for on-demand). `exportable` flips false → true on completion — it means "exportable NOW", the same as v2beta1 database backups. **The CREATE response carries ONLY `snapshot_id`** (202), so status/profile/exportable must not be copied from it; a scheduled snapshot runs immediately after instance creation, so an early `CreateSnapshot` legitimately returns 409 `snapshot-not-allowed`.

### id 84 — Composite-action steps must never self-gate on `failure()`
- **scope:** `.github/actions/collect-logs/action.yml` (all steps); the `Collect cluster logs` step in `.github/workflows/integration.yml` and `.github/workflows/integration-tests.yml`. Applies to any future composite action under `.github/actions/`.
- **rule:** A step *inside* a composite action MUST NOT use `failure()` / `success()` to detect that the **calling job** failed — inside a composite action those functions evaluate against that action's **own** step statuses, not the job's. Gate at the **caller** (`if: failure()` on the `uses:` step) and keep the composite's own steps `if: always()`. Do not "restore" the `if: failure()` that used to sit on the collect-logs steps.
- **why:** Both collect-logs steps carried `if: failure()`. On a red run nothing *inside* the action had failed yet, so `failure()` was false and **every step was skipped silently** — no `cluster-debug.log`, no uploaded artifact, and no way to see why a pod died. Verified on run 30304634581 (PR #314): the action's log group opened and closed in 0.2 ms *after* a 5-spec failure, and `integration-test-logs-2026.04-enterprise` was absent from the run's artifacts — leaving a crash-looping Neo4j (`rbac-shared-cluster-server-0/1`, 12 restarts) undiagnosable from CI alone. The failure mode is invisible: the action still *appears* in the job log, so the gap only shows up when someone goes looking for the logs.
- **pinned-by:** no automated guard — GitHub workflow/action YAML is not covered by the `unit-tests` job. Manual check (anchored so it matches real step conditions, not the prose/comments that *document* this rule): `grep -rnE '^[[:space:]]*if:[[:space:]]*failure\(\)' .github/actions/` must return **nothing** (0 today). The caller-side gate lives on the `uses: ./.github/actions/collect-logs` steps in both integration workflows.
- **enforcement:** convention + code review — **PROSE-ONLY — at risk**. Deliberately not added to `scripts/check-invariants.sh` (that guard is reserved for the 5 constitution invariants; this is CI-harness wiring). Fixed alongside the 2026.04 → 2026.06 CalVer anchor bump.

### id 95 — Never return a requeue Result together with a non-nil error
- **scope:** every reconciler in `internal/controller/` (all non-test `.go` files).
- **rule:** A `return` MUST NOT combine `ctrl.Result{RequeueAfter: …}` or `{Requeue: true}` with a non-nil error. Choose `ctrl.Result{}, err` for a transient failure (controller-runtime's error backoff retries it and the error metrics count it), or `ctrl.Result{RequeueAfter: d}, nil` for an expected condition that is already surfaced in status, an event or a log (a spec the user must fix; a server rejection that will repeat). `fail()` helpers route by `isPermanentServerRejection` (`Neo.ClientError.*` → fixed interval, `nil`; anything else → the error). The `Neo4jRole` `fail()` also gives the fixed interval to a nil error — a refusal the controller made itself, a built-in role the target does not have — and to a learn-mode status write that will fail the same way again (`lastingStatusWriteFailure`: `errNeo4jRoleCRDOutdated`, an invalid or too-large status). A `fail()` called with a nil error must never return `{}, nil`: that is "done", and nothing requeues the object.
- **why:** controller-runtime (v0.25.1) IGNORES the Result whenever the error is non-nil and logs a warning saying so, so every such return meant something other than it said: 99 had accumulated across 15 files, each one a dead delay plus a warning line per occurrence. They were NOT the cause of the Neo4jRole reconcile storm seen with a dropped database (#408) — that rate came from cluster status changes re-enqueuing roles through the dependent watch, which bypasses backoff; #408 fixed it by not attempting the doomed grant. The conversion to `{}, err` is behaviour-neutral for exactly this reason; only the validation returns in the cluster controller and the permanent-rejection branch of the `fail()` helpers changed behaviour, to the fixed interval their authors wrote. The role's missing built-in went back as "done" with a nil error and was not retried until another object's change woke it; its outdated-CRD failure went to error backoff, which grows past a quarter of an hour, so a role recovered that long after its CRDs were applied (#526, read from the code).
- **pinned-by:** `TestNoRequeueWithError` (`internal/controller/requeue_with_error_guard_test.go`) parses the package and fails on any such return; `TestRoleFail_LastingFailuresRequeueOnTheFixedInterval` and `TestSetLearnState_APruningAPIServerIsALastingFailure` (`internal/controller/neo4jrole_requeue_test.go`), which fail with the nil and lasting-write branches removed.
- **enforcement:** unit test (AST guard).

### id 97 — Composite and alias DDL is pinned to a Cypher language; never left to the server default
- **scope:** `internal/neo4j/composite.go` (constituent CREATE/ALTER/DROP, local and remote), `internal/neo4j/aliases.go` (Neo4jDatabaseAlias DDL), and any future statement containing a graph reference `<composite>.<name>`.
- **rule:** Constituent and alias statements go through `Cypher5()` with the parts quoted separately (`` `comp`.`name` ``). The one Cypher-25-only form, OIDC credential forwarding, goes through `Cypher25()` with the qualified name quoted WHOLE (`` `comp.name` ``). Never emit either form unpinned.
- **why:** the two languages read graph references differently. Cypher 5: `` `comp`.`name` `` is the constituent, `` `comp.name` `` a plain alias whose name contains a dot. Cypher 25: `` `comp`.`name` `` is refused outright (42NAA, "separate name parts should not be quoted individually") and `` `comp.name` `` is the constituent. Since v1.17.0 (#416) a new CalVer deployment defaults to Cypher 25, so the unpinned DDL made every composite on a fresh CalVer deployment `Failed` — caught by the extended suite on the release commit, 2026-09-30; the v1.16.0 journey had run composites only on 5.26, and the OIDC path (`CYPHER 25` + separately quoted parts) had never worked. `CYPHER 5` is accepted by 5.26.0, 5.26.28 and 2026.08.1. The dotted-alias ordering trap (42N87) exists in BOTH languages.
- **pinned-by:** `TestRemoteAliasStatementPinsCypher25OnlyForOIDC`, `TestCompositeAndAliasDDLIsPinnedToCypher5` (`internal/neo4j/composite_remote_test.go`); the extended spec `composite_database_deployed_test.go` on the CalVer anchor.
- **enforcement:** unit test + extended integration suite.

### id 101 — A Neo4jDatabase's (and a Neo4jCompositeDatabase's) default language is validated against the target server's version, and the clause is emitted only on CalVer
- **scope:** `internal/neo4j/create_database.go` (`DefaultLanguageForImage`, `buildCreateDatabaseQuery`), `internal/neo4j/client.go` (`CreateDatabaseWithLanguage` and the other three create methods, which all render through that one builder); `internal/validation/database_validator.go` (`validateCypherLanguage`); `internal/controller/neo4jdatabase_controller.go` (`resolveDatabaseCreateInputs`, `ensureDatabase`); and for `Neo4jCompositeDatabase.spec.defaultCypherLanguage`, `internal/validation/compositedatabase_validator.go` (`validateCypherLanguage`) and `internal/controller/neo4jcompositedatabase_controller.go` (`compositeCypherLanguage`), which feed `CreateCompositeDatabase` / `AlterCompositeDatabaseLanguage` in `internal/neo4j/composite.go`.
- **rule:** `spec.defaultCypherLanguage` is gated on the TARGET deployment's image tag (cluster or standalone), by `neo4j.ParseVersion`/`SupportsCypherLanguageVersion` — never on the operator's own notion of version. On the 5.26 LTS `"25"` is a validation ERROR naming `spec.defaultCypherLanguage` and the LTS, and `"5"` is accepted with a warning but the `DEFAULT LANGUAGE CYPHER` clause is OMITTED from the statement; on CalVer both values are emitted, on EVERY create path (plain, topology, seedURI, seedURI + topology). The controller computes the language once (`resolveDatabaseCreateInputs`) and hands the same value to whichever create method it picks. An image tag that cannot be parsed is not gated (the request passes through). A `Neo4jCompositeDatabase` follows exactly the same rule: `"25"` on the LTS is an error, `"5"` is accepted with a warning and the clause is left out of BOTH `CREATE COMPOSITE DATABASE` and `ALTER DATABASE ... SET DEFAULT LANGUAGE CYPHER` (`compositeCypherLanguage` calls `DefaultLanguageForImage`) — the two validators must not drift apart again. Do not add a fifth create method that renders its own `CREATE DATABASE` text: go through `buildCreateDatabaseQuery`.
- **why:** the field was gated nowhere. On 5.26 `"25"` passed validation and failed at `CREATE DATABASE` with a raw syntax error in `status.message`, while the composite validator refused the identical value up front — the two disagreed. They disagreed the other way too: the composite validator also refused `"5"` on the LTS, which `Neo4jDatabase` accepts, so a manifest that was valid on CalVer and harmless on the LTS could not be applied there. Worse, the plain create path (neither `topology` nor `seedURI`) called `CreateDatabase`, which took no Cypher version, so on CalVer the field was silently DROPPED for exactly the simplest spec. Four near-identical functions each rendering their own statement is how one of them lost the clause. Found alongside, and fixed with it: `topology` on a standalone target was forwarded to `CREATE DATABASE` although the docs and the validator warning say it is ignored, and `primaries: 0` there was a validation error for a field that does nothing — both now match the documented behaviour (ignored, warning only; negative counts are still refused).
- **pinned-by:** `TestDefaultLanguageForImage` and `TestBuildCreateDatabaseQuery_DefaultLanguage` (`internal/neo4j/create_database_test.go`) — the full LTS/CalVer × plain/topology/seed/seed+topology matrix of generated Cypher; `TestDatabaseValidator_CypherLanguageIsGatedOnTargetVersion` (`internal/validation/database_validator_test.go`) for cluster and standalone targets; `TestResolveDatabaseCreateInputs` (`internal/controller/neo4jdatabase_create_inputs_test.go`); for composites `TestCompositeDatabaseValidator_CypherLanguageIsCalVerOnly` (`internal/validation/compositedatabase_validator_test.go`) and `TestCompositeCypherLanguage` (`internal/controller/neo4jcompositedatabase_language_test.go`).
- **enforcement:** unit test + validator.

### id 102 — A schema field that no code reads must warn when set
- **scope:** every CRD field whose Go doc comment says "Reserved" / "accepted but not acted on". The warning text and the "is it set" test live in `internal/validation/noeffect_warnings.go`; they reach the user through each kind's existing channel — `PluginValidator.Validate` and `DatabaseValidator.Validate` (their `Warnings`), `ClusterValidator.ValidateCreateWithWarnings`/`ValidateWithWarnings` (`NoEffectWarnings`), and the `NoEffectWarnings` methods / functions for `Neo4jEnterpriseStandalone`, `Neo4jBackup`, `Neo4jRestore` and `AuraInstance`, which the reconcilers turn into events. Today: `Neo4jPlugin` `spec.resources`, `spec.source.registry` (+ `.tls`), `spec.security.securityPolicy`; cluster and standalone `spec.tls.certificateSecret`; standalone-only `spec.tls.strictPeerValidation: false` (only an explicit `false` — the CRD defaults the field to `true`, so `true` cannot be told from "not set"); cluster `spec.topology.placement.nodeSelector` and `.requiredDuringScheduling`; cluster and standalone `spec.upgradeStrategy.maxUnavailableDuringUpgrade` other than `1` (the CRD default — the operator restarts one server at a time, as Neo4j's rolling upgrade requires, and the #488 gate waits for each one's databases); `Neo4jDatabase` `spec.initialData.{source (other than cypher), configMapRef, secretRef, storage}`; `CloudIdentity.serviceAccount` and `AutoCreateSpec.enabled: false` under backup and restore storage; `storage.path` on a `pvc` — a `Neo4jBackup`'s `spec.storage.path` and a `Neo4jRestore`'s `spec.source.storage.path` and `spec.source.pitr.baseBackup.storage.path` (`pvcRestorePathNoEffectWarnings`): the backup Job writes, and the restore Job and the seed proxy read, the PVC's root, the path is joined in only for a bucket (backup-restore.md Rule 44); `AuraInstance` `spec.connectionSecretFormat: custom`; standalone-only `spec.upgradeStrategy.upgradeTimeout`, `healthCheckTimeout` and `stabilizationTimeout` other than their CRD defaults (`standaloneUpgradeTimeoutWarnings`: a duration equal to the default, `1800s` for `30m`, is the default; the standalone's `postUpgradeHealthCheck` is not read either and has no warning, because `false` says what happens and `true` is the default). The same channel carries a change that reaches nothing although the field is read: `spec.storage.className` (cluster and standalone) while it names a StorageClass the existing data volumes do not use — `StorageClassChangeWarning` (`internal/validation/storage_validator.go`), from the PVCs the storage pass lists (`recordStorageClassChange`, `internal/controller/storage_class_change.go`, called from `reconcileStorageExpansion` and `reconcileStandaloneStorageExpansion`), recorded with `recordScoped` apart from the validator's set; an empty className (the cluster's default class) and a deployment without volumes say nothing, and `kubectl neo4j validate --connect` gives the same warning before the apply (`storageClassFindings`, `cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate.go`), from the volumes the storage pass finds (`ClusterDataVolumeClasses`, `StandaloneDataVolumeClasses`, which the reconcilers call too; pinned by `TestValidate_Connected_ClassNameAgainstTheVolumesThatExist`, #566); cluster and standalone `spec.service.route.tls.secretName` and `spec.mcp.http.service.route.tls.secretName` (`routeNoEffectWarnings`: no Route the operator writes names a certificate, and the OpenShift Route API has no field that takes a Secret the way an Ingress does — #536).
- **rule:** A field the schema accepts but no controller, builder or validator reads MUST raise a Warning event (reason `ValidationWarning`) when the user sets it, worded `spec.<path> is accepted but has no effect today: <what to do instead>`. The warning is advisory: it is never added to an error list and never blocks a reconcile. Do not remove such a field from the CRD to be rid of it (manifests that set it are already deployed); do not warn about a field that IS read, or about a value that describes exactly what happens (`initialData.source: cypher`, `autoCreate.enabled: true`). When a controller starts reading the field, delete its warning, its "Reserved" comment and its api_reference note in the same change. **Cadence:** every advisory warning event — `ValidationWarning` on every kind, the cluster's `TopologyWarning`, and the other warnings that stay true while the object is otherwise fine (id 145 lists them) — goes through the reconciler's `advisoryWarnings.record` (`internal/controller/advisory_warnings.go`; `recordScoped` where two checks share a reason): it is announced when it starts to apply and again every `advisoryWarningRefresh` (30 minutes, inside the API server's one-hour event TTL) while it still does, never on every reconcile pass. A warning that stops applying is forgotten, so one that comes back is announced at once.
- **why:** a silently ignored field is worse than a rejected one — the user believes a node selector, a registry TLS policy or a plugin memory limit is in force and nothing is. Several fields were documented as "Reserved" in the API reference but were invisible at the moment of use: `kubectl apply` accepted them and nothing in the events said they did nothing. The validator is the right place because it is where every other advisory already comes from (invariant 1: no admission webhooks, so the CRD schema cannot warn). The standalone, backup, restore and Aura reconcilers had no warning channel at all; they now emit the same event from the same text. Found alongside: `SecurityValidator` (since deleted) was wired into no aggregator, so its older `tls.certificateSecret`-with-TLS-disabled rule never ran; that case is now covered by the warning above (see id 103 for why the validator was deleted rather than wired in). Every reconcile pass used to emit every warning again: 35 identical events in three minutes on one standalone (v1.21.0 journey), each spending the object's event budget — client-go's spam filter allows 25 per object, then one every five minutes — so the flood could crowd out the events that report real changes. #532 found three more: a standalone's `upgradeStrategy` timeouts bound steps of a cluster's rolling upgrade that a standalone's upgrade does not have, and nothing on its path read them; and a `spec.storage.className` edit reached no volume — a PVC keeps the class it was created with, and the StatefulSet's claim template cannot change (a storage expansion recreates the StatefulSet with the new class, which only volumes created afterwards would get) — while nothing said so.
- **pinned-by:** `TestStandaloneValidator_NoEffectWarnings_UpgradeTimeouts` (`internal/validation/standalone_upgrade_strategy_test.go`: each timeout warns, the CRD defaults read from the CRD YAML do not, a cluster never does), `TestStorageClassChangeWarning` (`internal/validation/storage_class_change_test.go`), `TestReconcileStorageExpansion_WarnsWhileTheClassNamesNoVolumesClass` (`internal/controller/storage_class_change_test.go`: cluster and standalone, once over three passes, kept apart from the validator's set, forgotten when the class matches again); each fails with its warning removed. `TestClusterValidator_NoEffectWarnings`, `TestStandaloneValidator_NoEffectWarnings`, `TestStandaloneValidator_NoEffectWarnings_StrictPeerValidation`, `TestPluginValidator_NoEffectWarnings`, `TestDatabaseValidator_NoEffectWarnings`, `TestCloudStorageNoEffectWarnings`, `TestBackupValidator_NoEffectWarnings`, `TestRestoreNoEffectWarnings`, `TestRestoreNoEffectWarnings_StoragePathOnAPVCSource`, `TestAuraInstanceNoEffectWarnings` (`internal/validation/noeffect_warnings_test.go`) — each asserts the warning is present AND the CR is still accepted; the emission wiring is pinned by `TestRecordValidationWarnings_KeepsEachReason`, `TestStandaloneReconcile_WarnsOnTLSCertificateSecret`, `TestBackupReconcile_WarnsOnNoEffectCloudIdentity`, `TestRestoreReconcile_WarnsOnNoEffectCloudIdentity` and `TestAuraInstanceReconcile_WarnsOnCustomConnectionSecretFormat` (`internal/controller/noeffect_warnings_test.go`); the cadence by `TestAdvisoryWarnings` and `TestStandaloneReconcile_WarnsOnceNotEveryPass` (`internal/controller/advisory_warnings_test.go`) — emitting on every pass fails the latter.
- **enforcement:** unit test. There is no machine check that a NEW reserved field gains a warning — that part is convention + code review (add the field's "Reserved" comment and its warning together).

### id 103 — A validator nothing constructs is dead code; do not encode Neo4j setting names from memory
- **scope:** `internal/validation/` (every `*Validator` type); the aggregators (`ClusterValidator`, `StandaloneValidator`, `DatabaseValidator`, …) and the reconcilers that construct the per-kind validators; the auth provider value space in `auth_validator.go` (`documentedAuthProviders`, `legacyAuthProviders`, `AuthProviderWarnings`).
- **rule:** Every `*Validator` struct declared in `internal/validation` must be constructed by a reconciler, a command, or an aggregator that is itself reachable from one. A new validator is not done until it is wired in. Rules that name Neo4j settings or their value spaces must be checked against the version's configuration-settings reference (the `check-neo4j-docs` skill; for 5.26 the `5.x` branch of `neo4j/docs-operations`, `configuration/configuration-settings.adoc`) before they are encoded. Wiring an old, never-run validator into an aggregator is a behaviour change for every deployed CR, not a tidy-up: audit each rule against the docs first, and ship anything that could reject a previously accepted CR as a warning.
- **why:** `SecurityValidator` was never constructed anywhere. Its 690-line test file passed and its rules read as live, so nobody noticed — and when audited against the docs, most of its rules were wrong. It "deprecated" `dbms.security.auth_cache_max_capacity` / `auth_cache_ttl` / `authorization_cache_max_capacity`, but the first two still exist on 5.26 with no deprecation and the replacements it named (`dbms.security.authentication_cache.*`) do not; it required `dbms.security.jwt.secret`, `dbms.security.saml.metadata_url` and `dbms.security.oidc.issuer` / `.client_id`, none of which are Neo4j settings (OIDC is `dbms.security.oidc.<provider>.*`); it validated `dbms.security.ssl.policy.default.*`, `dbms.security.procedures.default_allowed` as a boolean and `dbms.security.logs.password_obfuscation`, none of which exist on 5.26 (SSL policy is `dbms.ssl.policy.*`, which `ConfigValidator` forbids outright); and it restricted the LDAP mechanism to `simple`/`DIGEST-MD5`/`GSSAPI` when the reference says any JNDI SASL mechanism is valid. Wiring it in would have rejected CRs that start fine on a real server. Its remaining rules were duplicates: `TLSValidator` already checks the TLS mode and `issuerRef`, `AuthValidator` the typed LDAP and OIDC blocks, and the `tls.certificateSecret` rule is covered by the no-effect warning (id 102). Neo4j itself refuses a malformed value of a real setting at startup (`server.config.strict_validation`). These claims were re-checked on 2026-10-02 against `configuration-settings.adoc` on both the `5.x` and `dev` (CalVer) branches and the "Single sign-on integration" page (`/5/` and `/current/`); the two lines agree on every one of them.
