  The same drift was live in a validator that IS wired: `AuthValidator` accepted `oidc`, `jwt`, `kerberos`, `saml` and `custom` for `authenticationProviders` / `authorizationProviders` and refused `plugin-<name>`. Both settings document the same values on both lines: `native`, `ldap`, `plugin-<name>` (configuration settings) and `oidc-<name>` (SSO page). Refusing `plugin-<name>` blocked the Kerberos Add-On setup `docs/user_guide/security.md` describes (`[plugin-Neo4j-Kerberos, native]`). Following this rule, `plugin-<name>` is now accepted, the five old names still validate and warn (`AuthProviderWarnings`, through each Kind's `NoEffectWarnings` channel), and anything else is still refused.
- **pinned-by:** `TestEveryValidatorIsWiredIn` (`internal/validation/validators_wired_test.go`) — parses the package, then walks `internal/` and `cmd/` for constructions, and fails naming any validator type no reconciler can reach. For the provider values: `TestAuthProviders_MatchTheManual`, `TestAuthProviders_SecurityGuideKerberosSetupValidates` and `TestAuthProviderWarnings_ReachBothKinds` (`internal/validation/auth_validator_test.go`).
- **enforcement:** unit test. The documentation check (settings names and value spaces) is convention + code review.

### id 104 — A quantity the user typed never goes through `resource.MustParse`
- **scope:** every place a spec field becomes a `resource.Quantity`: `internal/controller/user_quantity.go` (`parseUserQuantity`, `invalidQuantityError`), `ensureBackupPVC` / `ensureTempStagingPVC` (`neo4jbackup_controller.go`), `ensureRestoreTempStagingPVC` (`neo4jrestore_controller.go`), the storage-expansion checks (`storage_expansion.go`, `standalone_storage_expansion.go`), and the StatefulSet builders via `resources.StorageRequest` (`internal/resources/cluster.go`, `createStatefulSet` in the standalone controller); validation in `StorageValidator` and `StandaloneValidator.validateStorage`, which both call `storageSizeError` (`internal/validation/storage_validator.go`).
- **rule:** `resource.MustParse` is for constant literals only (`"100m"`, `"2Gi"`). A value from a spec uses `resource.ParseQuantity`. Where the caller can return an error, use `parseUserQuantity(path, value)` and let a reconciler that sees `isInvalidQuantity(err)` report phase `Invalid` (backup) or `Failed` with the field path and value in the message, without returning the error for a backoff retry. Where a builder cannot return an error, use `resources.StorageRequest`, which turns a malformed size into a zero request that the apiserver refuses, rather than a panic. A guard elsewhere (an inline validator, a CRD `Pattern`) is not a reason to skip this: the controller must not depend on it.
- **why:** a panic in a reconciler takes the whole manager down and restart-loops every other CR. `Neo4jBackup.spec.storage.pvc.size` reached `MustParse` with no check at all until the validator learned to refuse it; `spec.options.tempStorage.size` was guarded only by a CRD pattern. The same shape was live on the data volume: `StorageValidator`'s format regex admits capital `5K`, which is not a Kubernetes quantity (decimal kilo is lowercase `k`), and `Neo4jEnterpriseStandalone.spec.storage.size` was checked only for being non-empty — so `size: "fifty"` or `"10 Gi"` on a standalone, or `5K` on a cluster, passed validation and crashed the manager in the StatefulSet builder. Both validators now call one check, `storageSizeError`: a size is valid when Kubernetes parses it and it is greater than zero, so the two Kinds accept the same sizes (`1.5Gi` and `100.5Gi` on both, `0` and `5K` on neither). The cluster's old regex had also refused decimals and lowercase `k`, which the apiserver accepts, and admitted `0`, which no store fits in.
- **pinned-by:** `TestStandaloneAndClusterAgreeOnStorageSize` (`internal/validation/storage_validator_test.go`) — the same sizes through both validators' real entry points; `TestEnsureBackupPVC_MalformedSizeIsAnErrorNotAPanic`, `TestEnsureTempStagingPVC_MalformedSizeIsAnErrorNotAPanic`, `TestEnsureRestoreTempStagingPVC_MalformedSizeIsAnErrorNotAPanic`, `TestHandleOneTimeBackup_MalformedPVCSizeReportsInvalid`, `TestStorageExpansionChecks_MalformedSizeIsAnErrorNotAPanic`, `TestStandaloneStatefulSet_MalformedStorageSizeDoesNotPanic`, `TestParseUserQuantity` (`internal/controller/neo4jbackup_user_quantity_test.go`); `TestBuildServerStatefulSet_MalformedStorageSizeDoesNotPanic` (`internal/resources/storage_request_test.go`); `TestStandaloneValidator_StorageSizeMustBeAQuantity` and `TestStorageValidator_isValidStorageSize` (`internal/validation/`).
- **enforcement:** unit test. There is no machine check that a NEW `MustParse` of user input is not added — that part is convention + code review.

### id 105 — A formed cluster short of a server is Degraded, not Forming; dependents keep working against it
- **scope:** `internal/controller/cluster_availability.go` (`assessServerShortfall`, `serverIsMember`, `verdictNoneShort`, `clusterHasFormed`, `clusterAcceptsWork`, `statefulSetRolloutInFlight`, `serversUnavailableSince`); `reconcileServerShortfall`, `verifyNeo4jClusterFormation` (`formationCheck`), `isUpgradeRequired` and the diagnostics gate in `internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`; the dependent gates `ResolvedTarget.IsReady` (`cluster_resolver.go`), `Neo4jBackupReconciler.isClusterReady`, `Neo4jDatabaseReconciler.isClusterReady`, `clusterSupportsPropertySharding`, `getTargetDeployment` (`plugin_controller.go`) and the split-brain detector's phase check; `--server-unavailable-grace` (`cmd/main.go`, Helm `serverUnavailableGrace`); the messages, `outageDetail` (`internal/controller/cluster_availability.go`) and `notReadyServerPods`.
- **rule:** Once a cluster has formed (phase `Ready`/`Degraded`, or condition `ClusterFormed=True` — never set back to False), a server shortfall outside a template rollout is assessed from `SHOW SERVERS`, not reported as Forming. With a majority of the expected servers members (`Enabled` or `Cordoned`, id 224) and `Available`, the cluster stays `Ready` with condition `Degraded=True` (reason `ServersUnavailable`) for the grace period, measured from that condition's `lastTransitionTime`, then turns phase `Degraded` (Ready condition False, reason `ClusterDegraded`, one `ClusterDegraded` Warning). Phase `Forming` is kept for: never formed; a template rollout or an unobserved StatefulSet generation (#262); a split-brain; Neo4j unreachable (but see below); fewer members than expected (a scale-up); and the loss of a majority (with one `ClusterQuorumLost` Warning). Dependents that act THROUGH the cluster — users, roles, bindings, auth rules, aliases, composites, replicas, databases, sharded databases, backups — use `clusterAcceptsWork` (phase `Ready` or `Degraded`, or the Ready condition True — the phase matters on its own because the backup controller passes a standalone through `standaloneAsCluster`, which copies the phase but not the conditions). Anything that ROLLS the servers — starting a rolling upgrade, installing a plugin — requires phase `Ready` AND no `Degraded=True`, except a write that replaces only servers already down because of what it changes (a plugin's, id 152; a held upgrade's new target, id 162). Diagnostics (ServersHealthy, DatabasesHealthy, `server_health`) are collected whenever the cluster has formed. Keep the Degraded condition's reason identical between the grace period and the Degraded phase, or the grace clock resets. `ClusterDegraded` and `ClusterQuorumLost` are deduplicated in memory (`firstAnnouncement`, cleared when every server is back): the informer cache can hand the next reconcile the old phase. A server that is down has a NULL address in `SHOW SERVERS`; `withKnownAddresses` fills it from the per-cluster address book (then `status.diagnostics`) so the condition names the pod and the `server_health` series keeps its labels, and `RecordServerHealth` withdraws every series a cluster no longer reports. **Unreachable is not a lost majority:** when a formed cluster cannot be queried at all (no `SHOW SERVERS`) while Kubernetes still reports a majority of its server pods Ready, and the current connection-failure streak is younger than the grace period, the verdict is `Unmeasured` and nothing is written: phase and conditions stay as they were. Past the grace, or without a Ready majority, it is `Forming` as before, so a cluster that stays unreachable (a wrong admin password) still pauses its dependents. **What the messages say:** the status message and the Degraded condition of a formed cluster short of a server (within the grace, `Degraded`, or a lost majority) say what Neo4j reports — `2 of 3 servers available; unavailable: <pod> (<health>)` — and, while Neo4j still lists every server available (a pod terminating, a readiness probe lagging a restart), which server pods Kubernetes does not report ready: `Neo4j still lists 3 of 3 servers available; server pods not ready: <pod> (terminating)` (`outageDetail`). The formation check's words ("Cluster formation in progress: 2/3 servers visible") and the rollout check's ("Waiting for every server pod to be updated and ready: …") describe a cluster that is `Forming`, and only its message carries them. **Short of nothing:** when every expected server is an available member and each of their pods is ready, nothing is short, and nothing is written (`NoneShort`, id 225). **Dropped servers in kept pods:** a formed cluster whose kept pods run servers Neo4j has dropped — started on the volumes of servers a scale-down removed — is `Forming` with a message that names them and the way out, announced once (`DroppedServersReturned`, id 229); a raise is held so this does not happen (id 229).
- **why:** #444. Any one server restart (pod delete, OOM kill, node drain) used to drop the cluster to `Forming` with Ready `Unknown`: every dependent paused until the server came back, backups stopped exactly when they matter, ServersHealthy and `server_health` froze at their last healthy values because diagnostics ran only while `Ready`, ArgoCD showed `Progressing`, `kubectl neo4j explain Forming` described first formation, and a server that never came back left the cluster `Forming` forever. `PhaseDegraded` existed in the vocabulary, the explain text and the ArgoCD mapping but nothing set it. A formed server keeps its identity across restarts and stays `Enabled` in `SHOW SERVERS` while it is `Unavailable`, which is what tells a lost server from a joining one. The grace period (default 5m, matching `ConnectivityDegraded`) keeps a routine restart from flapping the phase; the readiness-probe lag after a restart (`initialDelaySeconds` 45, up to 2 minutes more) is treated as part of the same shortfall, not as a rollout. Majority rule: a cluster with half or fewer of its servers cannot commit to `system`, so its dependents must pause. The v1.22.0 re-walk (#553) found the messages still in formation terms: a `Ready` cluster with a server down carried the formation check's "Cluster formation in progress" in its Degraded condition, and during a pod's termination window its message read "Waiting for every server pod to be updated" although nothing was being updated.
- **pinned-by:** `TestAssessServerShortfall`, `TestWithKnownAddresses`, `TestReconcileServerShortfall_StaleCacheDoesNotAnnounceTwice`, `TestClusterAcceptsWork`, `TestClusterHasFormed`, `TestStatefulSetRolloutInFlight`, `TestReconcileServerShortfall_GraceDegradedQuorumLost`, `TestReconcileServerShortfall_QuorumLostFromReadyRecordsFormation`, `TestReconcileServerShortfall_FirstFormationStaysForming`, `TestReconcileServerShortfall_RolloutStaysFormingWithoutFormationEvent`, `TestUpdateClusterStatusWritesExtraConditionsSilently`, `TestIsUpgradeRequired_NotWhileAServerIsDown`, `TestPluginWaitsForEveryServer`, `TestBackupGateAcceptsAReadyStandalone` (`internal/controller/cluster_availability_test.go`); `TestFailedUpgrade_AServerShortIsRecordedThenDegraded` (`internal/controller/upgrade_failed_shortfall_test.go`: a `Failed` upgrade's retry no longer keeps the normal pass, and so this assessment, from running while a server is down; id 149); `TestPhaseToConditionStatus_Table` (`internal/controller/conditions_unit_test.go`); `TestClusterMetrics_RecordServerHealth_WithdrawsSeriesNoLongerReported` (`internal/metrics/metrics_test.go`). Walked live on Kind (2026.08.1, 3 servers) 2026-10-03: two pod restarts never left `Ready`; a held-down server turned `Degraded` at the 2m grace; two down gave `Forming` + one `ClusterQuorumLost`; recovery went `Degraded` → `Ready`. The walk found the duplicate `ClusterDegraded`, the `<nil>` address, the stale `server_health` series and the missed `ClusterQuorumLost` that the rule above now covers. **Unmeasured (v1.20.0 journey, 2026-10-09):** killing one server's container (`kill 1`) wrote `phase: Forming` once on a formed cluster, twice in two tries: the client Service still routed the Bolt check to the dying container, so there was no server list, and an unreachable cluster was always `Forming`. With the fix, on Kind (2026.08.1, 3 servers): two container kills, 260 one-second samples, never `Forming`; a wrong admin password kept the cluster `Ready` for the 5-minute grace (88 `Unmeasured` passes, nothing written), then `Forming` with `ConnectivityDegraded`, and back to `Ready` 16s after the password was restored, same pods. Pinned by `TestReconcileServerShortfall_UnreachableWithReadyMajorityWritesNothing` and the unreachable rows of `TestAssessServerShortfall`. **Messages:** `TestReconcileServerShortfall_OutageMessagesSayWhatTheServersReport` and `TestOutageDetail` (`internal/controller/cluster_events_once_test.go`).
- **enforcement:** unit test.

### id 106 — A split backup archive is one artifact: gate the option on the target's version, and move or delete its parts together
- **scope:** `Neo4jBackup.spec.options.splitArchivePartSize` (`api/v1beta1/neo4jbackup_types.go`); `splitArchivePartSizeError` / `MinSplitArchivePartSize` (`internal/validation/backup_validator.go`); `buildBackupCommand` + `splitArchivePartBytes` and `buildRetentionScript` (`internal/controller/neo4jbackup_controller.go`); `Version.SupportsSplitArchive` (`internal/neo4j/version.go`).
- **rule:** The option is a Kubernetes quantity, validated inline as `>= 1Gi` (or `0`, off) and passed to neo4j-admin as an exact byte count; it is refused in `buildBackupCommand` when the target image is older than 2026.09 (the backup Job runs the target's own image), with a message naming the field and the image, and refused when `additionalArgs` also carries `--split-archive-part-size`. A split artifact is `x.backup` (metadata) plus `x.backup.1…N`: anything that counts artifacts counts `*.backup`; anything that deletes or moves one acts on `x.backup` AND `x.backup.*`. Consumers need no change — restore (`--from-path`), cluster seeding (`seedURI`, cloud and the PVC seed proxy, which serves the whole volume) and replicas (`seedURI`/`pullURI`) all name the `.backup` file and Neo4j reads the parts.
- **why:** Neo4j 2026.09 added `--split-archive-part-size` for artifacts past the target storage's object-size limit. Passing it through `additionalArgs` already worked, unvalidated, but PVC retention selected `*.backup` and deleted only the metadata file, stranding every part on the volume forever. `1G` is 10^9 bytes, under Neo4j's 1GiB minimum, so a quantity check catches it before a Job fails. The split layout applies whenever the option is set, even to a 1MiB differential. Walked live on Kind (2026.09.0, 1.3M nodes, compress off) 2026-10-05: PVC and MinIO backups split into 1GiB + 674–706MiB parts behind a 24-byte metadata file; retention removed the older artifact with both parts; a standalone `neo4j-admin` restore, cluster restores via `seedURI` from MinIO and through the PVC seed proxy, and a `Neo4jReplicaDatabase` seeding from a split FULL then pulling a split-layout DIFF all returned every node.
- **pinned-by:** `TestSupportsSplitArchive` (`internal/neo4j/version_test.go`); `TestBackupValidator_SplitArchivePartSize`, `TestBackupValidator_SplitArchivePartSizeNotAlsoInAdditionalArgs` (`internal/validation/backup_split_archive_test.go`); `TestBuildBackupCommand_SplitArchivePartSize` (`internal/controller/backup_restore_field_findings_test.go`); `TestRetentionScript_MaxCountDeletesSplitParts`, `TestRetentionScript_MaxAgeDeletesSplitPartsAndKeepsNewest` (`internal/controller/neo4jbackup_retention_split_test.go`) — these execute the generated script against real files.
- **enforcement:** unit test. No integration spec: CI is anchored on 2026.08.1, which has no such option; the release journey carries the scenario.

### id 114 — A standalone carries a plugin's security and startup-only settings in neo4j.conf, never in its env
- **scope:** `writePluginToTarget`, `removeStandalonePluginConfEnv`, `pluginSecuritySettings` (`internal/controller/plugin_controller.go`); `pluginConfKey`, `specSecuritySettings`, `pluginConfSettings`, `unionPluginConfSettings` (`internal/controller/plugin_conf_settings.go`).
- **rule:** On a standalone the standalone controller folds every plugin's security and startup-only settings — automatic ones, `spec.security`, and those keys from the plugin's `spec.config` — into neo4j.conf, merged with the user's own `spec.config` (additive lists unioned). The plugin controller writes `NEO4J_PLUGINS` and the plugin's other settings (APOC's, …) as env vars there, but not those keys, and removes copies an earlier operator wrote: this plugin's tokens from additive lists, a scalar only where it still holds exactly the plugin's value. A cluster still takes them as env vars (its conf is built from env). The Neo4j image applies `NEO4J_*` env vars over neo4j.conf, so an env copy replaces the merged value.
- **why:** the v1.19.0 journey (2026-10-06) saw a standalone's own `dbms.security.procedures.unrestricted` (`gds.*,…`) cut to the plugin's `apoc.*` once a plugin was installed. Walked live on Kind 2026-10-07 (2026.08.1): reproduced on the previous operator (effective value `apoc.*`, conf `gds.*,custom.*`), then upgraded: the env copies were removed in one restart, the conf became `gds.*,custom.*,apoc.*` with `allowlist=apoc.*`, the effective settings matched, APOC answered, and the pod stayed put afterwards.
- **pinned-by:** `TestInstallPluginViaEnvironment_StandaloneKeepsConfKeysOutOfEnv`, `TestPluginConfSettings_IncludesSpecSecurity` (`internal/controller/plugin_install_mode_test.go`).
- **enforcement:** unit test.

### id 115 — A deleted cluster's metric series are withdrawn
- **scope:** `ClusterMetrics.Forget` (`internal/metrics/metrics.go`); the finalizer release in `handleDeletion` and the deletion branch's position in `Neo4jEnterpriseClusterReconciler.Reconcile` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`).
- **rule:** When the cluster's finalizer is released, `Forget` deletes every series labelled with its `cluster_name` + `namespace` (health, phase, replicas, reconcile, upgrade, backup and per-server families) and its server-health bookkeeping. Deletion is handled before `Reconcile` sets up its deferred reconcile recording, so the pass that forgot the cluster does not export it again.
- **why:** the v1.19.0 journey saw `cluster_healthy=1`, `cluster_replicas_total` and every `server_health` series of a deleted cluster exported until the operator restarted. Walked live on Kind 2026-10-07: 24 series while `Ready`, 0 after deletion (the first attempt left 12 reconcile series, recorded by the deletion pass's deferred metrics — hence the reordering).
- **pinned-by:** `TestClusterMetrics_ForgetWithdrawsTheClustersSeries` (`internal/metrics/metrics_test.go`).
- **enforcement:** unit test (Forget); the call site is walked live.

### id 116 — `kubectl neo4j validate --connect` resolves cross-references where `kubectl apply` would put the object, and as the operator resolves them
- **scope:** `connectedNamespace` and its call in `runValidate` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate.go`); `currentNamespace` (`status.go`); the `Neo4jBackup` entry of `validators` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate.go`) and `ResolveBackupShardedDatabase` (`internal/validation/backup_sharded_ref.go`), which the backup controller's `shardedPreflightStatic` (`internal/controller/neo4jbackup_sharded.go`) calls too.
- **rule:** Under `--connect`, a manifest without `metadata.namespace` is checked in `-n`, else the kubeconfig context's namespace, else `default` — never `""`. Validators read a failed lookup as "not applied yet", so an empty namespace made `--connect` warn about objects that existed (with client-go's raw *"an empty namespace may not be set"*) and pass checks it never made — a remote composite whose target lacks `remoteAliasKeystore` included. A reference the operator resolves before it acts is resolved here the same way, through the same function: a `Neo4jBackup`'s `spec.shardedDatabase` names a `Neo4jShardedDatabase` by its CR `metadata.name` in the backup's namespace, on the backup's `instanceRef`; anything else is an error at `spec.shardedDatabase` (the operator fails the backup for it), and a value that is a CR's logical name (`spec.name`, ignoring case) names that CR. A read that fails for another reason is a warning that the reference was not checked. Offline the summary says it was not checked.
- **why:** found by the v1.19.0 journey; confirmed against the dev Kind cluster: the old build printed the raw error and looked in namespace `""`, the new one checks in `default`. In the v1.22.0 journey `--connect` passed a backup whose `spec.shardedDatabase` named no `Neo4jShardedDatabase`, and the operator then failed it with `Neo4jShardedDatabase "<name>" not found`: the backup validator took no client and never looked. The logical name is the usual way to get there (release_verification.md's sharded backup row).
- **pinned-by:** `TestConnectedNamespace`, `TestValidate_Connected_CompositeKeystoreNeedsTheRightNamespace`, `TestValidate_Connected_BackupShardedDatabaseResolvesAsTheOperatorDoes` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate_test.go`); `TestShardedPreflightStatic` (`internal/controller/neo4jbackup_sharded_test.go`), the logical-name case.
- **enforcement:** unit test.

### id 117 — A comment or a blank line in the rendered config never restarts a server; the ConfigMap is written anyway
- **scope:** `ReconcileConfigMap`, `calculateConfigMapHash`, `isCommentOrBlankLine`, `normalizeNeo4jConfForRestart`, `normalizeStartupScript` (`internal/controller/configmap_manager.go`); `renderedConfStamp`, `standaloneConfSemanticHash`, `standaloneConfigSemanticHashAnnotation` and their use in `reconcileStatefulSet` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`).
- **rule:** Whether the servers restart is decided on a normalised rendering. Full-line comments and blank lines are dropped from `neo4j.conf` and `startup.sh`; `health.sh` does not count at all (id 181). A trailing comment after a command still counts, and so does adding or removing a runtime-variable line: its placeholder is not a comment. Whether the cluster ConfigMap is written is decided on the raw data, so a change that restarts nothing still lands for the next restart. Before this, the ConfigMap was written only when the hash changed. The standalone keeps stamping `sha256(raw neo4j.conf)` on its pod template, so an operator upgrade cannot change the stamp by itself. It records `neo4j.com/config-semantic-hash` (the normalised conf) on the StatefulSet, and keeps the live stamp while that hash is unchanged. Exception: a StatefulSet without the annotation (the first reconcile after the upgrade that introduced it) only records it. This is the foundation of the no-restart work in #465–#469: anything the operator decides it can defer is written to the ConfigMap without a restart stamp.
- **why:** #465. A comment-only edit to the rendered startup script restarted every server of every cluster on operator upgrade. The code even carried a deliberately wrong comment, "set to TOTAL_SERVERS", to avoid that; it is corrected in the same change. Walked live on Kind 2026-10-07:
  - **5.26 cluster, 3 servers:** created with the previous operator (`282bf84`), then upgraded. The ConfigMap got the corrected comment, the StatefulSet stayed at generation 1, and all three pods kept their UIDs.
  - **2026.08.1 standalone:** removing the annotation re-recorded it without touching the template (generation 1). A hand-added comment was overwritten without re-stamping, and the pod kept its UID. A changed `db.transaction.timeout` still re-stamped it and restarted the pod (generation 2).
- **pinned-by:** `TestCalculateConfigMapHash_IgnoresCommentsAndBlankLines`, `TestNormalizeNeo4jConf_IgnoresCommentsAndBlankLines`, `TestNormalizeStartupScript_ReplacesRuntimeVars`, `TestReconcileConfigMap_CommentOnlyChangeWritesWithoutRestart`, `TestReconcileConfigMap_SettingChangeRestarts` (`internal/controller/configmap_manager_test.go`); `TestReconcileStatefulSet_CommentOnlyConfChangeKeepsTheStamp` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_statefulset_test.go`).
- **enforcement:** unit test.

### id 118 — A neo4j.conf change of dynamic settings is applied to the running servers, then written; anything else restarts
- **scope:** `internal/controller/live_config.go`: `neo4jConfChanges`, `confValues`, `liveConfigBlocker`, `statefulSetSettled`, `boltLiveConfigApplier`, `boltStandaloneLiveConfigApplier`. Also `ConfigMapManager.onlyNeo4jConfChanged` and the live-apply step in `ReconcileConfigMap` (`configmap_manager.go`), `applyStandaloneConfLive` (`neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`), and `Client.SettingsDynamic` (`internal/neo4j/client.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **When it applies live:** only `neo4j.conf` changed (a `startup.sh` change runs only at container start; `health.sh` never restarts a server, id 181). Every changed setting must be one the server reports as `isDynamic` in `SHOW SETTINGS`; a setting it does not report counts as static. No changed setting may be set by a `NEO4J_*` env var, which the image applies over neo4j.conf. The deployment must be phase `Ready`, not `Degraded=True`, with every replica ready on the current revision, and every server must be reachable.
  - **What it does:** `dbms.setConfigValue` runs on every server, with `''` for a removed setting (reset to default), and then the ConfigMap is written.
  - **Order:** apply, then write. Written first, a crash before the apply leaves the servers on the old values with no difference left to act on.
  - **Fallback:** any blocker, refusal or unreachable server falls back to the restart that happened before. A partial apply is fine, because that restart makes every server consistent. Event `ConfigNeedsRestart` (Normal) carries the reason; a live apply raises `ConfigAppliedLive`.
  - **Standalone:** after a live apply, the new conf's semantic hash is recorded on the StatefulSet (rule 117), so the pod's stamp is kept.
  - **Diffing:** never use `resources.Neo4jConfSettings`, which skips the repeatable `server.jvm.additional` and would let a JVM-flag change skip its restart. `confValues` keeps every value and turns a non-`key=value` line into an unknown setting.
- **why:** #466. Every `spec.config` edit restarted every server, or took a standalone down, even for the roughly 50 settings Neo4j changes at runtime (52 of 307 on 5.26.28, 53 of 325 on 2026.08.1). `dbms.setConfigValue` changes one server and is not persisted, so the Operations Manual says to run it on every member and update neo4j.conf: exactly what this does. Walked live on Kind 2026-10-07:
  - **2026.08.1, 3 servers:** a changed and an added setting were applied to all three servers with no pod restarted. A removed setting reset to its default on all three. A static `db.tx_log.buffer.size` raised `ConfigNeedsRestart` naming it, and the servers restarted.
  - **5.26.31, 3 servers:** two dynamic settings were applied live.
  - **2026.08.1 standalone:** applied live with the same pod and stamp, and the value survived a pod delete.
  - **Bug found by the tests:** a line added with no value compared equal to an absent one, so the comparison checks presence as well.
- **pinned-by:** `TestNeo4jConfChanges`, `TestLiveConfigBlocker`, `TestBoltLiveConfigApplier`, `TestReconcileConfigMap_DynamicOnlyChangeAppliedLive`, `TestReconcileConfigMap_LiveApplyRefusedRestarts`, `TestReconcileConfigMap_StartupScriptChangeIsNeverAppliedLive`, `TestStandaloneDynamicConfChange` (`internal/controller/live_config_test.go`).
- **enforcement:** unit test; the Bolt calls are walked live.

### id 119 — Servers past the system primaries join as system secondaries, and adding them restarts nothing
- **scope:**
  - `buildSystemDatabaseModeBlock`, `RestartNeutralBegin`/`RestartNeutralEnd` and `SystemDatabaseModeFile` (`internal/resources/cluster.go`);
  - the restart-neutral skip in `normalizeStartupScript`, plus `isMembershipLine`, `membershipOnlyStartupChange`, `renderedServerCount`, `addedSystemPrimaries`, `scaleNeedsNoRestart` and their call in `ReconcileConfigMap` (`internal/controller/configmap_manager.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **Role:** a server's `system` role is decided at its first start (no system store, no record): PRIMARY below `EffectiveMinSystemPrimaries()`, SECONDARY at or past it. It is written to `/data/.neo4j-operator/system-database-mode` and read back on every later start, because `server.cluster.system_database_mode` is read at each start and defaults to PRIMARY, so without the record a secondary would turn primary at its first restart. A server with a store and no record predates this and keeps the default (PRIMARY): an existing server never changes role.
  - **Restart-neutral section:** the role block sits between the markers, and the restart hash skips the section. Adding it in an operator upgrade, or `OPERATOR_SYSTEM_PRIMARIES` changing, restarts nothing. Never put a setting a running server needs inside the markers. Never wrap lines that already exist unwrapped: an existing script still counts them, so its hash would change. Never change the marker text.
  - **Scale restart decision:** a change confined to the membership lines (`TOTAL_SERVERS=`, the discovery endpoint list on either line), with `neo4j.conf` unchanged (`health.sh` never counts, id 181), skips the restart when no added ordinal is below `EffectiveMinSystemPrimaries()`. That covers a scale-down, or a scale-up that adds only secondaries, and raises `ScaledWithoutRestart`. A scale-up that adds a system primary (2→3 by default) restarts, as before.
- **why:** #467. A scale-up rolled every existing server: about 60s each, measured 3m05s for 3→4 on 2026.08.1 and 2m49s on 5.26.31 with stalled writes. Neo4j's discovery rule (`clustering/setup/discovery.adoc`, both lines) asks for "each server hosting the `system` database in primary mode" in the list, so a new system secondary needs nothing from the running servers. The Operations Manual also advises three system primaries and secondaries for servers that come and go. Walked live on Kind 2026-10-07:
  - **2026.08.1:** a cluster created by the #466 operator was upgraded to this one with no restart; the ConfigMap gained the block and the StatefulSet stayed at generation 1.
    - Scale 3→5 took 21s to Ready under continuous writes, with 0 failures and servers 0–2 untouched. Servers 3 and 4 joined as secondaries with records.
    - Server-3 restarted, and then all five restarted together: the roles held, and everything was back in 60s.
    - Scale-down 5→3 took 31s, restarting nothing.
  - **5.26.31:** a fresh 4-server cluster formed with 3 primaries and 1 secondary. Scale 4→5 took 21s, restarting nothing, with no failed writes. The secondary kept its role across a restart.
- **pinned-by:**
  - `TestSystemDatabaseModeBlock` (runs the rendered block through bash for each case) and `TestStartupScript_ParsesAsBash` (`internal/resources/system_database_mode_test.go`);
  - `TestScale_RestartDecision` (both lines: 3→4, 3→6, 5→3 quiet; 2→3, 2→4 restart), `TestScale_OtherChangeStillRestarts` and `TestUpgrade_SystemDatabaseModeBlockRestartsNothing` (`internal/controller/scale_no_restart_test.go`).
- **enforcement:** unit test.

### id 120 — 5.26 clusters get async Raft sender channels at each server's next restart, decided per container from its own version
- **scope:** `buildAsyncRaftChannelsBlock`, `AsyncRaftChannelsSetting` and `AsyncRaftChannelsMinPatch` (`internal/resources/cluster.go`); `internal/controller/restart_pending.go` (`clusterDeferredSettings`, `supportsAsyncRaftChannels`, `stampDeferredSettings`, `pendingRestartServers`, `reconcileRestartPending`) and its call in `ReconcileConfigMap` and `Reconcile`; `GetLoadedComponents` (`internal/neo4j/client.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **The block:** a 5.26 image's startup script has a restart-neutral block (rule 119's markers). It appends `dbms.cluster.raft.async_channel_acquisition_enabled=true` only when that container's own kernel jar (`$NEO4J_HOME/lib/neo4j-kernel-<version>.jar`) is 5.26.29 or later, and only when `spec.config` does not already set the key.
  - **Why per container:** the setting does not exist before 5.26.29, and an unknown setting stops Neo4j starting. The `5.26-enterprise` tag floats, and nodes can hold different patches under it (this laptop's cache had 5.26.28 while the Kind node ran 5.26.31). Never decide it from the tag.
  - **CalVer:** nothing is rendered; it is on by default there.
  - **The record on the ConfigMap:** `neo4j.com/deferred-settings` names the settings applied at restart, and `neo4j.com/deferred-settings-since` is when the ConfigMap first carried exactly those.
  - **RestartPending:** True names every server whose Neo4j has the setting (`dbms.components()` on `system`, per pod) and whose `neo4j` container started before that moment. False means none is waiting. The condition is removed when nothing is deferred (CalVer, or a `spec.config` override) and the ConfigMap records no restart-effective change (id 137).
  - **Never use `SHOW SETTINGS` here:** on 5.26.31 it reports this setting `false` (`isExplicitlySet` false) on a server whose startup config dump in `debug.log` shows `true`.
  - **Never run `dbms.components()` without `DatabaseName: "system"` on a per-pod connection:** the default database (`neo4j`, one primary by default) is not on every server, so the call fails on the others.
- **why:** #468. Neo4j documents a 5.26 write stall on Kubernetes (`clustering/troubleshooting.adoc`, 5.x): the leader waits up to `dbms.cluster.network.connect_timeout` (30s) for a connection to a stopped member. It hits every roll, crash and drain. The user chose "at the next restart, with status" over restarting every 5.26 cluster on upgrade. Walked live on Kind 2026-10-07 (5.26.31):
  - **The stall:** with the setting `false` (set through `spec.config`), restarting a follower under writes stalled for **32s**; with it `true`, there was no gap across 75s. Earlier rolls without it stalled 32–37s.
  - **Upgrade from the #467 build:** the ConfigMap gained the block and the annotations, nothing restarted, and RestartPending named all three servers. After server-1 was deleted, it dropped from the condition and its conf has the line.
  - **Bugs found on the way:** `SHOW SETTINGS` was the first status design and was abandoned for the reason above. `GetLoadedComponents` without a database failed against servers not hosting `neo4j`.
- **pinned-by:** `TestAsyncRaftChannelsBlock` (runs the rendered block through bash against fake kernel jars: 5.26.28 none, 5.26.29/31 once, user value kept, no jar none; CalVer renders nothing) (`internal/resources/system_database_mode_test.go`); `TestClusterDeferredSettings`, `TestSupportsAsyncRaftChannels`, `TestStampDeferredSettings`, `TestPendingRestartServers`, `TestReconcileRestartPending` (`internal/controller/restart_pending_test.go`); `TestUpgrade_SystemDatabaseModeBlockRestartsNothing` covers the 5.26 upgrade.
- **enforcement:** unit test; the stall itself is walked live.

### id 121 — A renewed TLS certificate is reloaded on running servers (CalVer 2025.03+), never assumed reloaded
- **scope:**
  - `TLSReloadSetting`, `TLSReloadApplies`, `buildTLSReloadBlock` (`internal/resources/cluster.go`);
  - the standalone's `dbms.security.tls_reload_enabled` line, `standaloneDeferredConfKeys`, `withoutDeferredConf` and their use in `standaloneConfSemanticHash`, `renderedConfStamp` and `reconcileConfigMap` (`neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`);
  - `internal/controller/tls_reload.go` (`reconcileTLSReload`, `reloadRenewedCertificate`, `reloadOne`, `presentedCertFingerprint`, `tlsSecretOwnerRequest`), wired through `reconcileClusterTLSReload` and `reconcileStandaloneTLSReload` and the `-tls-secret` watches;
  - `Version.SupportsTLSReload`, `Client.ReloadTLS`, `Client.SettingValue`.
- **rule:**
  - **The setting:** CalVer 2025.03+ with cert-manager TLS gets `dbms.security.tls_reload_enabled=true`. A tag pins at least year and month, so the tag decides, unlike the 5.26 patch gate in rule 120. It is static and "requires a restart", so existing servers get it at their next restart: through the cluster's restart-neutral block, with `RestartPending` naming the servers, or as a single standalone conf line that the standalone's restart decision (semantic hash, live apply, and the first-upgrade stamp check) ignores.
  - **The renewal pass:** when `{name}-tls-secret` changes (watched by name; cert-manager owns it), each ready pod's presented certificate (TLS dial to pod IP:7687) is compared with the Secret's. For a mismatch, the operator reads `tls_reload_enabled` on that server; only if it is `true` does it call `dbms.security.reloadTLS()` and dial again.
  - **Never trust the procedure's success:** on 2026.08.1 it returns success and does nothing when reload is disabled.
  - **Outcomes:** reloaded → `TLSCertificateReloaded`. Not enabled, or still on the old certificate 10 minutes after the new one's `NotBefore` → `TLSCertificateNeedsRestart` (Warning). Still waiting → retry every 30s, throttled per deployment because the reconcile runs far more often. When none is waiting, the fingerprint is recorded on the StatefulSet (`neo4j.com/tls-certificate`), and nothing is dialled while it matches.
  - **5.26:** cannot reload; renewal still takes effect at restart, as the security guide says.
- **why:** #469. The security guide claimed "rolling restart on renewal", but nothing restarted, so a cluster that outlived its certificate kept serving the old one. Walked live on Kind 2026-10-07 (2026.08.1):
  - **TLS cluster, upgraded from the #468 build:** nothing restarted, and `RestartPending` named all three servers.
  - **First renewal:** a Warning named all three, which do not have reload enabled.
  - **After server-1 restarted:** a second renewal was presented by server-1 **91s later with no restart**, and the other two were reported.
  - **TLS standalone created fresh:** reload was enabled from the start, and a renewal was presented 83s later, same pod.
  - **Found on the way:** `reloadTLS()` silently succeeding while disabled, which the first design read as "files not projected yet" and retried for ever.
- **pinned-by:** `TestCertFingerprint`, `TestReloadRenewedCertificate` (already current, reloaded, files lag, not enabled → never reloaded, reload fails, unreachable), `TestReconcileTLSReload`, `TestReconcileTLSReload_ThrottleAndGiveUp`, `TestTLSSecretOwnerRequest`, `TestClusterDeferredSettings_TLSReload`, `TestStandaloneTLSReloadSetting` (rendering and the upgrade keeping the stamp) (`internal/controller/tls_reload_test.go`); `TestTLSReloadBlock` (`internal/resources/system_database_mode_test.go`); `TestSupportsTLSReload` (`internal/neo4j/version_test.go`).
- **enforcement:** unit test; the reload itself is walked live.

### id 122 — Diagnostics are written to status only when they change; a status write is a reconcile
- **scope:** `clusterDiagnosticsWriteNeeded`, `diagnosticsStale`, `diagnosticsRefreshInterval`, `sortServerDiagnostics` and the write in `CollectDiagnostics` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`); `standaloneDiagnosticsWriteNeeded` and `updateStandaloneDiagnostics` (`neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`); `sortDatabaseDiagnostics`, `withoutTransactionCounters` (`internal/controller/diagnostics_databases.go`); `updateBackupStatus` (`internal/controller/neo4jbackup_controller.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **What counts as a change:** `status.diagnostics` (and the ServersHealthy/DatabasesHealthy conditions) is written only when something other than `lastCollected` changed, or the stored `lastCollected` is at least 5 minutes old. Not changes:
    - rows in a different order: SHOW DATABASES and SHOW SERVERS return them unordered, and database rows carry no address, so the lists are sorted before writing and comparing;
    - the databases' `lastCommittedTxn` and `replicationLag`, which move with every write.
  - **Why it matters:** the cluster and standalone controllers have no event filter, so any status write re-enqueues the object at once. A per-pass write is a reconcile loop. Any new per-reconcile status field must follow the same compare-before-write rule; the Aura controllers' `status.lastSyncedTime` now does (id 156).
  - **A time is stamped on the transition it records:** a `Neo4jBackup`'s `status.lastRunTime` is set when the backup enters `Running` and `lastSuccessTime` when it enters `Completed`, and `updateBackupStatus` sends nothing when the status would not change. It set `lastRunTime` on every write of a `Running` backup, so every pass changed status: a write about every 27s for the whole run in the v1.22.x walk, each one another reconcile.
- **why:** #475. Each pass wrote a fresh `lastCollected`, so a Ready cluster reconciled continuously: 25–37 a minute on Kind in dev mode, each dialling every server for split-brain detection.
  - The first fix still left 13–29 a minute: the `writer` flag moved between database rows on every pass. Sorting the rows fixed it.
  - After the fix, measured 2026-10-08 on 2026.08.1 with 3 servers: 5–8 a minute in dev mode, all from the dev cache's 30s resync (`configureDevelopmentCache`), and **2 a minute in production mode**, the 30s requeue.
- **pinned-by:** `TestClusterDiagnosticsWriteNeeded`, `TestStandaloneDiagnosticsWriteNeeded`, `TestDiagnosticsWriteNeeded_IgnoresRowOrderAndTxnCounters` (`internal/controller/diagnostics_write_test.go`); `TestUpdateBackupStatus_StampsTheRunOnceAndWritesOnlyChanges` (`internal/controller/backup_walk_test.go`: a Running backup written again keeps its `lastRunTime`, and the same status three times is no write).
- **enforcement:** unit test; the rate is measured live.

### id 123 — A scale-down drain decides from the system leader's view, and "already underway" is progress
- **scope:** `ListServersFromLeader` (`internal/neo4j/client.go`); `drainAlreadyUnderway` and its uses in the pre-flight dry run, the DEALLOCATE step and the DROP step, `deallocateRefusal`, and the order of the drain's servers in `reconcileScaleDownDrain` (`internal/controller/scale_down.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **Read from the leader:** the drain reads `SHOW SERVERS` from the system database's leader, through a write-routed session. A read session goes to a follower, whose copy of `system` can lag the leader where CORDON, DEALLOCATE and DROP SERVER take effect.
  - **Already underway is progress:** a Neo4j refusal saying a server "is already deallocating", "already deallocated" or "already dropped" means the step was already taken. The pass ends quietly and the next one reads again; it never raises `ScaleDownBlocked`. Every other refusal still blocks: an infeasible topology, a single-primary database, the voting floor.
  - **The advice follows the cause** (`deallocateRefusal`, `onlyOnePrimaryRefusal`, `requiredTopologyRefusal`): a refused deallocation, the pre-flight dry run's or the DEALLOCATE step's, is `ScaleDownBlocked` with Neo4j's error and advice for what it names. Neo4j's texts, the same in `neo4j-dbms-enterprise` 5.26.32 and 2026.09.0: "Can't move databases with only one primary: %s." gets "give it a second primary first (ALTER DATABASE <db> SET TOPOLOGY 2 PRIMARIES), or keep the servers"; "Required topology for '%s' is %s primaries and %s secondaries." (after "Unable to reallocate '<db>(<mode>)'.", on 5.26 "Unable to reallocate '<db>'.") gets "lower its topology, or keep enough servers for it (at least <primaries + secondaries>)". A refusal that names neither gets advice covering both, and the manual's other two ("Managing servers in a cluster": a database offline, a quorum of a database's primaries cordoned). Before the cordon the message ends "No server has been cordoned; replicas are held until this is resolvable.", after it "Replicas are held until this is resolvable.". The drain takes its servers in id order, so a refusal that quotes the statement is the same text on every pass and one Warning (id 145).
- **why:** #476. And #619 (v1.24.0 journey, 3 × 2026.08.1, 2026-10-29): a 5 → 3 scale-down with database `wide` on four primaries was refused by the pre-flight dry run ("Unable to reallocate 'wide(PRIMARY)'. Required topology for 'wide' is 4 primaries and 0 secondaries."), and `ScaleDownBlocked` advised "Give single-primary databases an additional primary"; the DEALLOCATE step's message gave the other advice whatever Neo4j said. The two servers were named in the map's order, and the one refusal was two Warnings a second apart. Acting on a lagging read, a pass re-ran the dry run on servers the leader had already started deallocating or had dropped. Neo4j refused it, and the operator raised `ScaleDownBlocked` while the scale-down went on to complete. Measured on Kind 2026-10-08 (2026.08.1, 3↔5 servers, with the #475 reconcile loop still present to make the race likely): on main, one of three rounds raised the false warning; with the fix, none of five rounds did, and the fallback never fired, so the leader read alone kept the view current.
- **pinned-by:** `TestDrainAlreadyUnderway` (the live error texts, plus a real refusal that must still block), `TestPlanScaleDownStep` (`internal/controller/scale_down_test.go`); `TestDeallocateRefusal_AdviceFollowsTheCause` (the journey's 2026.08 text, its 5.26 form, the single-primary text with one database and a list, a cause it does not name), `TestScaleDownDrain_ARefusedDryRunSaysWhyOnce` (twenty passes of the refused dry run: nothing cordoned, the topology advice, one Warning), `TestDeallocateRefusal_KeepsNeo4jsError` (`internal/controller/scale_down_refusal_test.go`). Under `go test -overlay`, the fixed advice back fails all three; the drain's servers in the map's order fail the second (twenty runs of twenty). Which servers the drain takes, a server that is down among them: id 213.
- **enforcement:** unit test; the race is walked live.

### id 124 — Deleting a TLS deployment deletes the Secret cert-manager issued for it, and only that
- **scope:** `deleteIssuedTLSSecret` (`internal/controller/tls_secret_cleanup.go`); its calls in the cluster's `handleDeletion` and the standalone's `handleDeletion`.
- **rule:**
  - **What is deleted:** on deletion, `{name}-tls-secret` is deleted when its `cert-manager.io/certificate-name` annotation names this deployment's Certificate. That is `{name}-tls` for a cluster and `{name}-tls-cert` for a standalone; the two Kinds name it differently.
  - **What is kept:** a Secret the user created, or one issued for another Certificate.
  - **Failure:** logged, never blocks deletion.
  - **Retention:** data retention does not apply, since a re-created deployment is issued a new certificate.
- **why:** #479. The operator owns the Certificate, so garbage collection removes it, but cert-manager leaves the Secrets it issues unless it runs with `--enable-certificate-owner-ref` (default false). Every deleted TLS deployment left its certificate and private key behind, and #469's name-based Secret watch then reconciled deployments that no longer existed. Walked live on Kind 2026-10-08:
  - TLS standalone and TLS cluster deleted: their Secrets were gone.
  - Non-TLS standalone with a hand-made `ux-tls-secret`: the Secret was kept, with a log line saying why.
  - The first cut assumed `{name}-tls` for both Kinds, and the live walk caught the standalone's `{name}-tls-cert`.
- **pinned-by:** `TestDeleteIssuedTLSSecret`, `TestHandleDeletion_DeletesTheIssuedTLSSecret` (both Kinds' real deletion paths) (`internal/controller/tls_secret_cleanup_test.go`).
- **enforcement:** unit test.

### id 125 — A Ready deployment polls on its own interval; a Ready cluster's full split-brain check runs at most every `--split-brain-check-interval` unless a server pod changed
- **scope:** `readyPollInterval`, `serverPodsFingerprint`, `splitBrainCheckSkippable`, `skipSplitBrainCheck`, `recordCleanSplitBrainCheck` (`internal/controller/reconcile_cadence.go`); their use in `verifyNeo4jClusterFormation` and in the Ready return of the cluster and standalone `Reconcile`; flags `--ready-poll-interval` and `--split-brain-check-interval` (`cmd/main.go`, Helm `readyPollInterval`, `splitBrainCheckInterval`).
- **rule:**
  - **Ready poll:** only the Ready return uses `--ready-poll-interval` (default 30s). Every other return keeps `RequeueAfter`: forming, rolling, short of a server, upgrading. A long poll must never slow a deployment that is converging. A shorter TLS-reload requeue still wins.
  - **When the full check is skipped:** all of these must hold.
    - The cluster's phase is `Ready`. `Degraded`, `Forming` and recovery check in full.
    - `SHOW SERVERS` through the client Service lists at least `spec.topology.servers` servers as members (`Enabled` or `Cordoned`, id 224) and `Available`.
    - A clean full check is on record within the interval, against the same server pods. The fingerprint is each pod's name, UID, phase, readiness, container restart counts and whether it is being deleted, so a pod that is deleted or recreated, restarts a container or changes readiness checks on that pass.
  - **What is recorded:** only a full check that compared every server's view (phase `Ready`) and found the cluster whole. The full path clears the record first, so a split-brain, a shortfall, an error or the legacy fallback leaves nothing, and the next pass checks in full.
  - **In memory:** an operator restart or a leader change checks in full on its first pass.
  - **Flags:** `--split-brain-check-interval=0s` checks on every pass; a negative value, or a `--ready-poll-interval` that is not positive, stops the operator at startup.
- **why:** with the loop of #475 fixed (id 122), a Ready cluster still reconciled twice a minute, and every pass opened a Bolt connection to each server for split-brain detection. Across a fleet of large clusters that is N connections per cluster every 30 seconds. The fingerprint keeps detection prompt where it matters: a server that comes back and forms its own cluster does so when its pod is recreated or restarts. Measured on Kind 2026-10-08, production mode, 2026.08.1, 3 servers:
  - **Defaults, quiet cluster, 11 minutes:** 24 reconciles, 2 full checks five minutes apart, 6 per-pod connections instead of 72.
  - **A server pod deleted:** full checks on every pass while it was down, one when its pod started and one the moment it turned ready (its fingerprint changed), then skips. The phase stayed `Ready`.
  - **A container restarted (`kill 1` in the neo4j container):** full checks when it went down, when it restarted (its restart count changed) and when it turned ready.
  - **Operator restarted:** its first pass checked in full.
  - **`--ready-poll-interval=2m`, 10 minutes:** 7 reconciles. That is 5 polls, plus 2 passes that follow the 5-minute diagnostics refresh write of id 122. There was 1 full check.
  - **Standalone with `--ready-poll-interval=2m`, 10 minutes:** 7 reconciles, the same pattern: 5 polls two minutes apart, plus 2 passes after the diagnostics refresh.
  - **`--split-brain-check-interval=0s`:** 5 minutes, 11 reconciles, 11 full checks and 33 per-pod connections: the previous behaviour.
  - **First cut:** a pass skipped while a server pod was terminating, before its replacement existed. The rollout check still held the cluster in the shortfall path, but a pod being deleted is a change, so `deletionTimestamp` joined the fingerprint.
- **pinned-by:** `TestReadyPollInterval`, `TestServerPodsFingerprint`, `TestSplitBrainCheckSkippable`, `TestSkipSplitBrainCheck_TracksTheLastCleanCheck` (`internal/controller/reconcile_cadence_test.go`).
- **enforcement:** unit test; the cadence is measured live.

### id 126 — Neo4j's logs reach standard output through an operator-rendered server-logs.xml read from the live ConfigMap mount
- **scope:** `internal/resources/server_logs.go` (`StdoutLogs`, `BuildServerLogsXML`, `ServerLogsConfigLine`, `ClusterServerLogsConfigPath`, `StandaloneServerLogsConfigPath`); its use in `BuildConfigMapForEnterprise` and the cluster `neo4j.conf` (`internal/resources/cluster.go`), and in `createConfigMap` / `standaloneConfigMapData` / `reconcileConfigMap` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`); `ValidateStdoutLogs` (`internal/validation/config_validator.go`); `spec.monitoring.logs.stdout` (`MonitoringLogsSpec`); `examples/observability/otel-collector.yaml`.
- **rule:**
  - **What Neo4j offers, both lines:** no OTLP export. Metrics only as CSV, JMX, Graphite or a Prometheus endpoint. Logging is Log4j 2 (`server-logs.xml` for debug/http/query/security, `user-logs.xml` for `neo4j.log`); a `<Console target="SYSTEM_OUT">` appender is honoured in console mode, which is how the container runs Neo4j; `JsonTemplateLayout` with Neo4j's `QueryLogJsonLayout.json`, `StructuredJsonLayout.json` and `StructuredLayoutWithMessage.json` works on 5.26.31 and 2026.08.1; the file is re-read every 30 seconds (`monitorInterval="30"`).
  - **The file is the operator's own**, never a copy of Neo4j's (the shipped one is marked a commercial add-on): the same four file appenders, loggers, layouts and rollover as each line's default — `debug.log` JSON (`StructuredLayoutWithMessage`) on CalVer, `Neo4jDebugLogLayout` text on 5.26 and on a tag that does not parse — plus one Console appender per listed log, added to that log's logger only. Never change the file appenders' formats: Neo4j support reads `debug.log`.
  - **Delivery:** the file is a ConfigMap key (`server-logs.xml`) present only while a log is listed, and `server.logs.config` points at the **mount** (`/operator-conf/server-logs.xml` on a cluster, `/conf/server-logs.xml` on a standalone), never at startup.sh's copy in `/conf`, which does not change. The standalone's ConfigMap update sets or deletes the key explicitly (it updates keys in place); the cluster replaces its data wholesale.
  - **Restarts:** `server.logs.config` is static (`isDynamic=false` on both lines), so turning the feature on or off restarts each server once through the normal `ConfigNeedsRestart` path. The cluster's restart hash covers only `neo4j.conf`, `startup.sh` and `health.sh`, so a changed list restarts nothing; it reaches the servers when the kubelet refreshes the mount, then Log4j's next check.
  - **Validation:** `spec.config` may not set `server.logs.config` while a log is listed (it would replace the operator's path and the logs would silently never arrive).
  - **Metrics:** per-database metric names carry the database (`neo4j_database_<db>_<family>_…`); dots and dashes in a database name become underscores, so the example's relabel rule moves only names of letters and digits, anchored on the per-database families (`causal_clustering`, `check_point`, `cluster`, `cypher`, `db`, `ids_in_use`, `index`, `log`, `neo4j`, `pool`, `store`, `transaction`) so a dashed name stays whole rather than being split wrongly.
- **why:** getting database telemetry into an OpenTelemetry pipeline. The query, security and debug logs were files only, invisible to a node-level collector; the user chose database telemetry over tracing the operator. Every assumption was checked against the Operations Manual pages for both lines and then live. Walked on Kind 2026-10-09:
  - **Upgrade:** a 2026.08.1 cluster created under the previous operator kept its StatefulSet generation, pods and ConfigMap byte for byte.
  - **2026.08.1 cluster, 3 servers:** on → one rolling restart (`ConfigNeedsRestart`: "Neo4j reads server.logs.config only at startup"); list changed → no pod restarted, generation unchanged, security and debug records in the Collector after 45s; off → one roll, key and setting gone.
  - **5.26.31 standalone:** the same, with the change applied after 92s; `debug.log` stayed text while its standard-output copy was JSON.
  - **Collector (contrib 0.162.0):** query records with `database`, `elapsedTimeMs`, `queryLang`, pod attributes, Neo4j's timestamp and severity; metrics only from Neo4j pods (cert-manager carries the same annotations — hence the label filter), with the `database` attribute.
  - **Found on the way:** the Console appender's "only used by neo4j console" in Neo4j's comments; the 5.26 page names `user-log.xml` where the image ships `user-logs.xml`; the CRD's first "within about a minute" was optimistic (45–92s measured).
- **pinned-by:** `TestStdoutLogs`, `TestBuildServerLogsXML_RoutesEachListedLogToStdout`, `TestBuildServerLogsXML_FilesKeepNeo4jDefaults`, `TestClusterConfigMap_StdoutLogs` (`internal/resources/server_logs_test.go`); `TestClusterRestartHash_IgnoresServerLogsXML`, `TestStandaloneConfigMap_StdoutLogs` (`internal/controller/stdout_logs_test.go`); `TestValidateStdoutLogs` (`internal/validation/config_validator_test.go`).
- **enforcement:** unit test; walked live on both lines.

### id 127 — A rolling upgrade moves to the next server only when the restarted one's databases have caught up
- **scope:** `upgradeRollObservation.LastRolledDatabasesCaughtUp` and `planUpgradeRollStep`, the gate block in `stepUpgradeRolling`, `isServerAvailable` (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade_statemachine.go`); `evaluateDatabaseGate`, `lastRolledDatabasesCaughtUp`, `addressIsPod`, `databaseGateMessage`, `databaseNameList`, `upgradeGates` (`internal/controller/upgrade_database_gate.go`); `Client.ClusterStatusCheck`, `Client.DatabaseAllocations` (`internal/neo4j/client.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **The gate, per step, in order:** the StatefulSet rollout for the step settled, every pod Ready; the restarted server `Enabled`/`Available` in `SHOW SERVERS`; and its databases caught up, read **on that server**.
  - **"Caught up":** `dbms.cluster.statusCheck([])` — every row for the server itself (`requester`) `replicationSuccessful` and `memberStatus` `APPLYING` ("can replicate and is actively applying"; `REPLICATING` "cannot apply" and `UNAVAILABLE` do not pass), no `error` — and `SHOW DATABASES`: every database on that server at its requested status, which covers single-primary databases and secondaries statusCheck does not judge. Available on 5.24+ and CalVer with the same columns.
  - **Matching a server to a pod is by host, never substring:** `addressIsPod` — `x-server-1` must not match `x-server-10` (the old `strings.Contains` in `isServerAvailable` did, from 11 servers up).
  - **While waiting:** requeue; `status.upgradeStatus.message` names the databases (written only when it changes — a status write re-queues). The step's `upgradeTimeout` still applies, and a timeout while databases lag fails the upgrade naming them, not the server's rejoin. The check is bounded at 20s so a silent server cannot hold the controller's only worker.
- **why:** #488. Available is a server-level state; a restarted server's databases can still be starting or catching up, and the readiness probe checks less still (HTTP 7474 or formation log lines). The only margin was the probe's 45s `initialDelaySeconds`. Measured on Kind 2026-10-12, operator in production mode, writes to each database every 100–200ms through routing, statusCheck sampled on every server every 2s:
  - **Before (v1.20.0):** 1 database (2026.08.1→2026.09.0) margins +14s/+4s; 15 databases (5.26.30→5.26.31) +14s/+5s; **30 databases: −5s** — the operator restarted server-0 while server-1 had 29 of 30 copies applying (`d02` not), leaving `d02` one working copy for ~5s.
  - **After:** the same 30-database upgrade waited on exactly the copies the sampler saw lagging (`d16`, `d27` on server-1; `d21` on server-0), moved on 7–10s after all 30 were applying, 10,861 writes with none failed, the whole upgrade ~13s longer.
  - **Leadership needs no gate, and the operator cannot move it:** a graceful shutdown hands each database the server leads to a follower (`LeadershipTransferRequest`; the follower leads 3–30 ms later), and an upgrade step is a graceful pod delete. Measured 2026-10-12 (5.26.31, 3 servers, 10 three-primary databases under write load): 16 of 16 handovers to writable followers across four restarts. Neo4j has no procedure to move leadership; balancing, `priority_tag` and `server.databases.read_only` all move one database per 15s (`LeaderTransferJob`).
  - **Never drain leadership with `server.databases.read_only`:** a leader that is read-only fails writes with `Neo.DatabaseError.Statement.ExecutionFailed` (WriteOnReadOnly), which drivers do not retry, until the 15s job moves it — 320 managed transactions failed in the spike. And a shutdown handover aimed at a follower that is read-only for that database is lost (Neo4j does not try another), leaving the database leaderless for `dbms.cluster.raft.leader_failure_detection_window` (~20s): 4 of 4 times.
  - **What clients see per restart (all retryable):** at shutdown, errors (EOF, `RemoteExecutionTransientError`) and pauses of a few seconds; ~2s `NotALeader` pauses while balancing moves leaderships back to the restarted server. The ~10s stalls that came 30–50s after a restart were the cluster connect timeout meeting the replaced pod's new IP — see id 128.
- **pinned-by:** `TestEvaluateDatabaseGate`, `TestAddressIsPod`, `TestDatabaseGateMessage`, `TestStepUpgradeRolling_WaitsForTheRolledServersDatabases`, `TestStepUpgradeRolling_TimeoutNamesTheLaggingDatabases` (`internal/controller/upgrade_database_gate_test.go`); `TestPlanUpgradeRollStep` (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade_statemachine_test.go`).
- **enforcement:** unit test; walked live under write load.

### id 128 — A cluster's servers give up a connection after 5s, not Neo4j's 30s
- **scope:** the base conf in `buildNeo4jConfigForEnterprise` (`internal/resources/cluster.go`).
- **rule:** The rendered `neo4j.conf` of every cluster sets `dbms.cluster.network.connect_timeout=5s` (5.17+ and CalVer). It is an operator default, not an operator-managed key: `spec.config` overrides it, and `DedupeNeo4jConf` keeps the one line (the user's). It goes in the rendered conf, never the startup script, so it de-duplicates and takes the live-apply path.
- **why:**
  - **The mechanism:** a replaced pod comes back on a new IP, but the other servers' JVMs can resolve its name to the old IP for up to 30s (Java's default DNS cache; CoreDNS itself had the new IP 7s after the delete). Nothing holds the old IP, so a connection attempt waits out the whole connect timeout (`ConnectTimeoutException: connection timed out after 30000 ms: <pod>…/<old ip>:7000` in `debug.log`). When leadership balancing moves a database to the restarted server in that window, the leader stops accepting writes for the transfer (`TransferringLeadership`), the request never arrives, and writes on that database stall ~10s (7.4s transfer timeout plus a replication retry).
  - **Not the 5.26 stall Neo4j documents** (`clustering/troubleshooting.adoc`, fixed by async Raft channel acquisition, id 120): that one blocks replication; this one blocks a leadership transfer, with async acquisition on.
  - **Measured on Kind 2026-10-13** (3 servers, 10 three-primary databases, writes every 200ms per database through the `-client` Service; worst write per restart):
    - 30s default: 5.26.31 12.2s / 2.4s / 10.0s; 2026.08.1 5.1s / 10.0s / 10.0s.
    - 5s on every surviving server: 5.26.31 4.3 / 3.6 / 2.3 / 2.3s; 2026.08.1 2.3 / 2.3 / 3.0 / 2.8s. No write failed in either.
  - **Per-server ClusterIP Services are not the fix — tried 2026-10-14, not shipped.** Advertising each server's own ClusterIP Service (a stable virtual IP, as Neo4j's Helm chart does, with LIST endpoints left on pod FQDNs) formed and ran on 2026.08.1, and a down server's VIP mostly refused connections at once. But the VIP is black-holed too, briefly, while the replacement pod starts: kube-proxy routes it to the new pod before that pod's network accepts connections. One restart's burst of Raft connects at ~+11s hung the full timeout (782 `connection timed out after 30000 ms: <cluster>-server-0.<ns>.svc.cluster.local/<VIP>:7000`). Worst write per restart: 30s timeout 5.1 / 2.0 / **10.0s**; 5s timeout 2.3 / 3.0 / 2.2s — no better than pod FQDNs with 5s (above). What bounds the stall is this connect timeout, whatever the address; per-server Services would add a migration for every cluster, certificate SANs (5.26 cannot reload them) and a change to INV-4's wording for no measured gain. Revisit only with new evidence.
  - **Upgrading the operator restarts nothing:** the setting is dynamic, so the conf change is applied live (`ConfigAppliedLive`, pod UIDs unchanged, walked on 2026.08.1). But Raft connections read it only at startup: in the first rolling restart afterwards, a step whose surviving servers still started with 30s stalled 10s as before (4 of 4). From id 137 on, an operator that adds or changes the line records it, and `RestartPending` names those servers until each restarts. The change v1.21.0/v1.21.1 made live was never recorded, so those clusters are not named.
- **pinned-by:** `TestClusterConfLowersTheClusterConnectTimeout`, `TestClusterConnectTimeoutIsOverridable` (`internal/resources/cluster_network_test.go`).
- **enforcement:** unit test.

### id 129 — Clusters are reconciled in parallel; state kept across reconciles must be safe for that
- **scope:** `controllerOptions` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`); `DefaultClusterMaxConcurrentReconciles`, `DefaultClusterReconcileTimeout` (`internal/controller/reconcile_cadence.go`); `--cluster-max-concurrent-reconciles`, `--cluster-reconcile-timeout` (`cmd/main.go`, Helm `clusterMaxConcurrentReconciles`, `clusterReconcileTimeout`).
- **rule:**
  - **Workers:** the cluster controller reconciles `MaxConcurrentReconciles` clusters at once (default 4; zero means one). controller-runtime never reconciles the same cluster twice at once, so per-cluster code stays serial; different clusters run in parallel.
  - **Shared state:** anything the reconciler, a helper it holds, or a package variable keeps from one reconcile to the next must be safe for concurrent use by different clusters: a `sync.Map`, a mutex, or `sync.Once`. Today: the reconciler's `splitBrainChecks`, `deferredUpgradeTargets`, `connectivityFailures`, `availabilityAnnounced`, `fleetFailures` (`sync.Map`), `deletedServerStatefulSets` (`deletedStatefulSets`, a `sync.Map`; id 171); `ConfigMapManager.lastUpdateTime`, `restartPendingChecker.seen`, `serverAddressBook`, `auraClients`, `metrics.serverHealthSeen` (mutex); `tlsReloadAttempts` (`sync.Map`); `restartPendingOnce`. A value stored per cluster key (e.g. `*connectivityFailureStreak`) may be mutated without a lock only because only that cluster's reconcile touches it. Kubernetes objects a reconcile creates are named per cluster (`{cluster}-discovery`, `{cluster}-server`, …); never share one between clusters.
  - **envtest fixtures:** a spec that needs a Ready cluster creates it with `standInLabels()` and calls `markClusterReady` (`internal/controller/envtest_ready_test.go`). The suite's cluster controller leaves stand-in clusters alone through its `Ignore` seam (nil in production): envtest runs no pods, so it would rightly take them out of Ready — with one worker it usually got there after the spec's controller had looked, so a single status patch passed by luck. With four, two backup specs failed on CI's two cores; re-patching until Ready held did not help, because the controller kept demoting the cluster (60s per spec, and the package hit its 10-minute limit). With the seam, the backup and restore specs passed 5 runs in 5 at `GOMAXPROCS=2 -race`, the whole controller package in 106s.
  - **Deadline:** each pass's context gets `ReconcileTimeout` (default 10m; zero: none) through controller-runtime's `ReconciliationTimeout`. A pass cut off returns an error and is retried with the rate limiter's backoff; reconciles are idempotent, so a half-done pass is finished by the next one.
- **why:** with one worker, every cluster's Bolt work ran one cluster after another: a cluster whose servers answered slowly delayed every other, and a pass stuck on a server that accepted a query and stopped answering (Bolt connects and queries have driver timeouts, but the reconcile context had no deadline) stalled the whole controller. The audit before raising it (2026-10-13) found every cross-reconcile structure already guarded; the rate limiter's shared bucket paces only failed and `Requeue: true` passes, not `RequeueAfter` or watch events; it was one retry per 6s (burst 10), which after a shared failure held the 20th cluster's first retry for a minute, and is now controller-runtime's default (10/s, burst 100) — a storm guard, with each cluster's 5–30s backoff doing the pacing (`TestClusterRetriesAreNotThrottledAcrossClusters`).
- **pinned-by:** `TestClusterControllerOptions`, `TestReconcileLeavesIgnoredClustersAlone` (`internal/controller/reconcile_cadence_test.go`); the envtest spec "Neo4jEnterpriseCluster parallel reconciles" (`internal/controller/cluster_parallel_reconcile_test.go`), and every envtest cluster spec, run with `DefaultClusterMaxConcurrentReconciles` workers under `-race` in `make test-unit` — an unguarded counter planted in `Reconcile` made the parallel spec fail with 11 data races.
- **enforcement:** unit test (race detector) + code review.

### id 130 — The server pods' ServiceAccount is bound to no Role; no `{cluster}-discovery` Role, RoleBinding or Service
- **scope:** the ServiceAccount step and `deleteLegacyDiscoveryObjects` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`, `internal/controller/legacy_discovery_objects.go`); `BuildDiscoveryServiceAccountForEnterprise`, `LegacyDiscoveryName` (`internal/resources/cluster.go`); the roles/rolebindings RBAC markers (get, list, watch, delete only).
- **rule:** The cluster's server pods run under `{cluster}-discovery` (kept for its name and for `spec.podServiceAccountAnnotations`), and the operator binds it to no Role: LIST discovery resolves pod FQDNs through DNS and Neo4j never calls the Kubernetes API with it. Each reconcile deletes a `{cluster}-discovery` Role, RoleBinding or Service **only if the cluster controls it** (owner reference); one someone else created is kept. The Service was a ClusterIP on the V1 discovery port 5000, which nothing listens for. The operator's own ClusterRole only reads and deletes Roles and RoleBindings — it creates none (the backup controller's grant was removed with them). Never reintroduce a pod-side API grant without a reader of it — K8S discovery would be one, and it would change INV-4.
- **why:** releases up to v1.20 created a Role granting get/list/watch on services and endpoints, a leftover from Neo4j's K8S resolver, which the operator stopped using long ago (the startup script's comment blamed "K8S ClusterIP returns a single VIP", a past single-Service misuse; Neo4j's K8S resolver needs one Service per server). Nothing in the server pod used it — Neo4j with LIST, the trust-store and plugin init containers (the plugin download authenticates with its own mounted Secret), the startup and health scripts — so the pods held API credentials for no reader. Deleting a Role or RoleBinding changes no pod template: nothing restarts.
- **pinned-by:** `TestDeleteLegacyDiscoveryObjects` (`internal/controller/legacy_discovery_objects_test.go`); the envtest spec "creates the ServiceAccount and no discovery Role, RoleBinding or Service" (`internal/controller/cluster_server_rbac_test.go`).
- **enforcement:** unit test + envtest.

### id 131 — Diagnostics keep running during an upgrade; nothing else of the normal pass does
- **scope:** the upgrade branch of `Reconcile` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`); `collectDiagnosticsDuringUpgrade`, `upgradeDiagnostics` (`internal/controller/upgrade_diagnostics.go`).
- **rule:** While an upgrade is active, a pass runs the upgrade step and then `CollectDiagnostics` — at most once per `--ready-poll-interval` per cluster, only once the cluster has formed, never with `spec.monitoring.enabled: false`, never fatal. From the same `SHOW SERVERS` it writes the `Degraded` condition, with monitoring off too (id 214). Nothing else of the normal pass runs: the StatefulSet apply would undo the partition freeze, split-brain detection restarts pods, the availability logic would mark the cluster Degraded for servers that are down on purpose, and live config, fleet registration and the rest change things. Diagnostics read Neo4j and write only `status.diagnostics`, `ServersHealthy`, `DatabasesHealthy` and `server_health`, which nothing in the upgrade owns.
- **why:** the upgrade branch used to return straight after the step, so those fields kept their pre-upgrade values for the whole upgrade (up to `upgradeTimeout` per server): `ServersHealthy=True` and `server_health=1` while servers were restarting, and a server that never came back showed healthy until the upgrade gave up. The rate limit is because a status write reconciles the cluster again, and servers change state often during an upgrade. The documented alerts wait 5 minutes, longer than a server takes to restart.
- **pinned-by:** `TestCollectDiagnosticsDuringUpgrade_Gating`, `TestReconcile_CollectsDiagnosticsDuringAnUpgrade` (`internal/controller/upgrade_diagnostics_test.go`) — removing the call or the interval fails them; the `Degraded` condition, `TestReconcile_DegradedFollowsTheServersDuringAnUpgrade` (id 214).
- **enforcement:** unit test.

### id 132 — Cluster and standalone clients share one driver per deployment and credentials
- **scope:** `driverCache`, `drivers`, `tlsMaterialDigest`, `ForgetCluster`, `ForgetStandalone` (`internal/neo4j/driver_cache.go`); `NewClientForEnterprise`, `NewClientForEnterpriseStandalone`, `Client.Close`, `tlsSecretNames` (`internal/neo4j/client.go`); the deletion handlers of both reconcilers; `getClusterAdminSecretName` (`internal/controller/plugin_controller.go`), `dialClusterPod` and `createPodSpecificNeo4jClient`.
- **rule:**
  - `NewClientForEnterprise` and `NewClientForEnterpriseStandalone` take their driver from the process-wide cache; each still returns a Client of its own (own circuit breaker), and `Close` releases the driver instead of closing it. `NewClientForPod` (per-pod, split-brain/upgrade gates/live config) keeps a driver of its own.
  - **The key is everything the driver is built from:** the deployment's UID, the URI, the username, and SHA-256 digests of the password and of the TLS material (`tlsMaterialDigest` reads the same Secrets as `buildTLSConfig`, via `tlsSecretNames`). A deployment whose key changes gets a new driver; the old one is closed by its last holder. Add any new driver input to the key, or a stale driver is reused.
  - Drivers unused for 10 minutes are closed (a sweep on each acquire), and deletion forgets the deployment's at once. The cache is safe for the cluster controller's parallel workers and the database, user and role controllers using the same clients.
  - **Every client reads the admin Secret the servers were given:** `spec.auth.adminSecret`, or `neo4j-admin-secret` when it is unset (`getClusterAdminSecretName`, the name the StatefulSet mounts). That includes the per-pod clients, `dialClusterPod` (`internal/controller/live_config.go`: live config, TLS reload, restart-pending) and `createPodSpecificNeo4jClient` (`internal/controller/splitbrain_detector.go`: the split-brain check and the upgrade's per-server database gate). Both refused a cluster without `spec.auth.adminSecret`, which the CRD allows, so none of those reached its servers; pinned by `TestPodClients_UseTheAdminSecretTheServersWereGiven` (`internal/controller/pod_client_admin_secret_test.go`), which fails on the refusal.
- **why:** every pass built fresh drivers and closed them: new TCP connections, TLS handshakes and a login for each, each a `security.log` line — 35 logins in 2 minutes on one quiet 3-server cluster (2026-10-13, Kind, 5.26.31), now 3 in 4 minutes (the per-pod split-brain checks); a standalone made 0 in 3 minutes. A server restart: diagnostics showed it Unavailable 5s later and Available again 11s after that, with no operator errors — pooled connections to a gone pod are discarded (`ConnectionLivenessCheckTimeout`).
- **pinned-by:** `TestDriverCache`, `TestDriverCacheConcurrent`, `TestNewClientForEnterpriseSharesTheDriver` (`internal/neo4j/driver_cache_test.go`) — dropping the password or the TLS digest from the key, or retiring nothing on a key change, fails them; the TLS case is a renewed CA with the URI unchanged.
- **enforcement:** unit test (race detector).

### id 133 — A backup-mode replica's region check reads every source of the server's environment
- **scope:** `missingObjectStoreEnv`, `readServerEnvironment`, `envFromNames`, `serverEnvironment` (`internal/controller/neo4jreplicadatabase_controller.go`); `resolveReplicaTargetEnv`, `addEnvFromNames` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/preflight.go`).
- **rule:** Before a backup-mode replica asks Neo4j to seed from `s3://`, the operator — and `kubectl neo4j preflight` — look for `AWS_REGION` or `AWS_DEFAULT_REGION` in `spec.env`, the live server container's `env`, **and every variable an `envFrom` source supplies** (`spec.extraEnvFrom` and the live container's `envFrom`), with the source's prefix applied. A source that does not exist supplies nothing; one that cannot be read means the check cannot know, so the operator lets Neo4j try (as under workload identity) and preflight warns instead of reporting a problem. The two read the same sources: preflight must never report a problem the operator would not act on, nor stay silent on one it would.
- **why:** both read only env names, while `spec.extraEnvFrom` is documented for exactly this — a credential bundle with `AWS_REGION` in it. A cluster whose Secret carried the region had its replica `Failed` with "AWS_REGION is not set" (v1.21.0 journey), and the message's first suggestion, `source.credentialsSecretRef`, meant recreating the replica because `spec.source` is immutable. The message now lists the fixes that leave the replica alone first.
- **pinned-by:** `TestMissingObjectStoreEnv`, `TestReadServerEnvironment` (`internal/controller/neo4jreplicadatabase_controller_test.go`); `TestPreflightReplica` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/preflight_test.go`) — reading only env names, ignoring the prefix, or failing on an unreadable source fails them.
- **enforcement:** unit test.

### id 134 — A conflict retry re-applies the desired object, never what the last attempt read
- **scope:** `createOrUpdateResource` / `createOrUpdateResourceInternal` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`).
