- **rule:** `createOrUpdateResource` keeps a deep copy of the desired object and resets `obj` to it before every retry. `controllerutil.CreateOrUpdate` reads the live object into `obj`, so after a conflict `obj` is the previous attempt's live state; any code that derives "desired" from `obj` (the StatefulSet path captures `desiredSpec` from it) must see the real desired object on every attempt.
- **why:** the cluster controller writes the server StatefulSet on most passes (fields the API server defaults differ from the desired object), and the Neo4jPlugin controller patches the same StatefulSet. When the plugin's patch landed in between, the retry took the live template it had just read — the plugin's `NEO4J_PLUGINS` in it — as desired, applied it, and recorded every name in it as cluster-owned; the next pass then removed `NEO4J_PLUGINS` as "owned but no longer desired". The plugin stayed `Ready` with APOC gone, and nothing re-added it (CI, 2026-10-14: "Should install APOC plugin on Neo4jEnterpriseCluster" waited 40 minutes). Present since the conflict retry was added.
- **pinned-by:** `TestCreateOrUpdateResource_ConflictRetryKeepsTheDesiredSpec` (`internal/controller/sts_conflict_retry_test.go`) — it emulates the API server's defaulting and the plugin's mid-update patch; without the reset the owned set gains `NEO4J_PLUGINS` and the next pass deletes it.
- **enforcement:** unit test.

### id 135 — A Ready plugin is re-checked whenever its target's pod template changes, and puts back only what is gone; a server it cannot reach mid-install is not a failure
- **scope:** `SetupWithManager`, `readyPluginsForStatefulSet`, `podTemplateChanged`, the settled branch and the failure branches of `Reconcile`, `missingFromTarget`, `pluginEnvDrift`, `applyPluginEnv`, `writePluginToTarget`, `pluginEnvOwnedError`, `retryablePluginError`, `installFailed` (`internal/controller/plugin_controller.go`); `EventReasonPluginMissing` (`internal/controller/events.go`); `IsAdditiveConfEnvName` (`internal/resources/cluster.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **The watch:** the plugin controller watches StatefulSets. A created StatefulSet, or one whose pod template changed, enqueues the **Ready** `Neo4jPlugin`s in its namespace whose `clusterRef` names it (`{ref}-server` or `{ref}`); status updates do not. A plugin in another phase is part way through an install and requeues on its own, or waits for an edit to its CR, and its passes write the StatefulSet: waking it on that StatefulSet's changes would let a controller that reverts the write restart the servers in a loop (the cluster controller did that to a `VerifiedDownload` init container until id 141). No periodic requeue: the informer delivers every template change (a relist replays a missed one as an update), Create covers a recreated StatefulSet, and an operator restart reconciles every plugin.
  - **A settled pass** (phase `Ready`, `observedGeneration` current) makes the plugin's edits (`applyPlugin`, the code the install writes with) on a copy of the live StatefulSet. Nothing to put right: it returns — no Bolt, no pod-template write, no status write, so another controller's rollout never flips the plugin out of `Ready`; the one write it may make is the plugin's record of its variables, a patch of the StatefulSet's metadata that restarts nothing, on its first pass after an upgrade (id 155). Otherwise: one `PluginMissing` Warning, phase `Installing` ("Restoring the plugin: …"), then the normal install path, whose write restarts the servers — or, while the target is not `Ready` with every server available, phase `Waiting` until it is (id 141).
  - **What is put right (`pluginEnvDrift`, `pluginPodDrift`):** a variable the plugin sets that is absent; `NEO4J_PLUGINS` not listing a name the plugin needs; on a standalone, an env copy of one of the plugin's neo4j.conf settings an earlier operator left (id 114); a `VerifiedDownload` init container or volume of the plugin's that is absent, by name (id 141); its CA volume and the init container's mount of it once they no longer follow the target's `spec.trustedCASecrets` — without a `PluginMissing` Warning, since the user changed the list (id 147). **Never a value someone else gave a variable the plugin sets:** two writers that disagree on one (two plugins setting the same key) would restart the servers in turn for ever, each woken by the other's write. Nor, on a cluster, a shared procedure list holding a token no plugin of the target asks for any more, or a `NEO4J_PLUGINS` name the plugin's record says it no longer adds: those go with the plugin's next write (`setClusterPluginLists`, `applyPluginEntries`; id 155), and a settled pass — also the first one after an operator upgrade — restarts nothing for them (`pluginEnvDrift`). Nor an init container that is there but differs: the live one carries the API server's defaults.
  - **No write without a change:** an install pass whose edits change nothing does not update the StatefulSet. A variable the plugin recorded and no longer sets is not "missing": it is removed (id 155), without a `PluginMissing` Warning.
  - **A cluster's own variables are refused:** a change to a variable the StatefulSet's `neo4j.com/cluster-controller-env-vars` annotation lists (one the cluster renders from its own spec — `NEO4J_PLUGINS` in `spec.env`, say) is not written (`pluginEnvOwnedError`). The cluster controller puts back any live value that differs (`envVarsEqual`), so the two would restart the servers in turn. The plugin is `Failed`, naming the variable and the fix; a standalone's controller does not revert live values, so nothing is refused there. Aura Fleet Management's merge keeps the same rule on both kinds, without a failure: a deployment whose own `spec.env` sets `NEO4J_PLUGINS` carries `fleet-management` in it as it renders it, and the merge never writes it (id 223; the merge had no guard, and a cluster restarted its servers without end, #608). The refusal is decided from the StatefulSet before the write's gate and its Bolt check (`installChangesTarget`), reads "Plugin installation refused: …", and is logged once at info from the status write, never as an ERROR (id 228).
  - **Nothing is set at runtime, and a pass that waits asks Neo4j nothing:** every setting of a plugin reaches the servers when they start (an env var; on a standalone also its neo4j.conf), so the pass that finds its roll done goes `Ready` without a configuration step. The Bolt functional check (`deploymentFunctional`) runs only on a pass about to write the StatefulSet (`installChangesTarget`), never on one waiting for the restart its own write caused.
  - **Waiting is not failing:** an install whose StatefulSet write ran out of conflict retries (`retryablePluginError`: a Conflict, `IsConnectivityError` from `internal/neo4j/error_classify.go`, a deadline), reports `Installing` and requeues after `RequeueAfter`, with no Warning. Anything else is `Failed`, with one `PluginInstallFailed` Warning when the failure is new, and is retried every `RequeueAfter` without a status write or another Warning — the cause can lie outside the plugin (a Secret, the cluster's spec). A refusal the user must fix — the cluster's own variable above, a `NEO4J_PLUGINS` that is not a JSON list — is logged once at info and V(1) after; a real failure keeps its ERROR (id 228).
  - **`Installing` is announced only where an install starts:** not by a settled pass, nor by a pass continuing an install of the same generation (phase `Installing` or `Failed`).
- **why:**
  - **#508 (id 134):** a conflict retry of the cluster controller deleted `NEO4J_PLUGINS`. The plugin stayed `Ready` with APOC gone from the servers, because the plugin controller watched only `Neo4jPlugin` and a `Ready` plugin does not requeue: only an operator restart reconciled it again.
  - **v1.21.0 journey, a transient `PluginInstallFailed` while the servers restarted after an APOC install** — diagnosed from the code, not reproduced live: the install write is what restarts the servers, but the old pods report Ready until they stop, so `arePodsReady` let `configurePlugin` run in the same pass. With `spec.security` (the documented APOC example) it opens a Bolt client — a standalone's verifies connectivity — and the refused connection was reported as a failed install. The `Failed` write re-queued the plugin and the next passes waited for the pods and reached `Ready`. A second path to the same Warning: the StatefulSet write running out of its four conflict attempts (`retry.DefaultBackoff`, about 300ms) while the StatefulSet controller rewrote status at every pod transition — every waiting pass wrote the StatefulSet, changed or not.
  - **Found on the way:** a status write re-queues the plugin, so while the pods restarted each pass wrote "Installing plugin" then "Waiting for pods" and was re-run at once, and a lasting failure flipped `Failed` → `Installing` → `Failed` with a Warning as fast as passes ran.
  - **#548 and #530 (2026-10-24, from the code):** the configuration step called `dbms.setConfigValue` for every `spec.config` key but APOC's. Neo4j takes only a dynamic setting there ("The setting is not dynamic and cannot be changed at runtime", Operations Manual "Update dynamic settings", `5.x` and `dev`), and on a cluster only on the server the call runs against, while the servers already had the value from their env: a static key (`gds.model.store_location`) turned the plugin `Failed`, retried every `RequeueAfter`. For APOC with `spec.security` the step opened a Bolt client only to log "Skipping" — the client behind the v1.21.0 Warning above. The step and its dead helpers (`hasRuntimeSecurityConfiguration`, `PluginTypeHybrid`) are gone. And a standalone's plugin went `Waiting` ("to be functional") and `Installing` in turn while its one pod restarted, because the functional check ran before the rollout wait on every pass.
- **pinned-by:** `TestPluginController_ReAddsARemovedNeo4jPlugins` (envtest: without the watch `NEO4J_PLUGINS` never comes back), `TestReconcile_SettledPluginLeavesAnIntactTargetAlone`, `TestReconcile_SettledPluginReAddsWhatWasRemoved`, `TestReconcile_SettledStandalonePluginDropsALeftoverConfCopy`, `TestPluginEnvDrift`, `TestReadyPluginsForStatefulSet`, `TestPodTemplateChanged`, `TestInstallPluginViaEnvironment_WritesNothingWhenNothingChanged`, `TestInstallPluginViaEnvironment_LeavesClusterOwnedVariablesAlone`, `TestReconcile_InstallConflictIsNotAFailure`, `TestReconcile_ARealFailureIsAnnouncedOnceAndRetried`, `TestReconcile_WaitingForTheServersWritesNothingMore`, `TestRetryablePluginError` (`internal/controller/plugin_self_heal_test.go`); `TestReconcile_NothingIsSetAtRuntime` (a static GDS key on a cluster, an APOC allowlist on a standalone, neither target with an admin Secret: `Failed` with the configuration step), `TestReconcile_ARestartingStandaloneIsNotAWait` (`internal/controller/plugin_runtime_config_test.go`); `TestReconcile_SettledPluginLeavesAStaleListToItsNextWrite` (`internal/controller/plugin_shared_lists_test.go`); `TestIsAdditiveConfEnvName` (`internal/resources/cluster_test.go`).
- **enforcement:** unit test + envtest; not walked live yet.

### id 136 — A state-transition event is emitted on the transition, not on every pass
- **scope:** `reportDatabaseReady`, `updateDatabaseStatus` (`internal/controller/neo4jdatabase_controller.go`); `updateBackupStatus`, `scheduledBackupMessage` and the `BackupScheduled` emission in `handleScheduledBackup`, and the `BackupCompleted` and Job-failed `BackupFailed` of `handleExistingBackupJob` (`neo4jbackup_controller.go`); `transitionStatus`, the `ShardedDatabaseReady` / `SeedBackupPending` / `SeedProxyStarting` emissions, `shardCreateNeeded` and the create step of `reconcileShardedDatabase` (`neo4jshardeddatabase_controller.go`), `shardFamilyExists` and `observeShardFamily` (`neo4jshardeddatabase_status.go`); `learnOutcome.added` in `learnDesired` (`neo4jrole_learn.go`); every `ValidationFailed` emission and the status helper it reads the transition from — `updateDatabaseStatus`, `transitionStatus`, `updateClusterStatus` (`neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`), `setFailedStatus` (`neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`), `updatePluginStatus` (`plugin_controller.go`), and `setStatus` in `neo4jcompositedatabase_controller.go`, `neo4jdatabasealias_controller.go`, `neo4jreplicadatabase_controller.go`, `neo4juser_controller.go`, `neo4jrole_controller.go`, `neo4jrolebinding_controller.go` and `neo4jauthrule_controller.go`; the message those carry, from `ValidateShardedDatabase` (`internal/validation/shardeddatabase_validator.go`) and the cluster's validation return in `Reconcile`; the log line of a refused cluster or standalone spec, `logRefusal` (`internal/controller/refusal_log.go`); `RolesResolved` — `reportResolvedRoles` (`internal/controller/neo4juser_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jrolebinding_controller.go`), `setResolvedRolesCondition` and `resolvedRolesMessage` (`internal/controller/role_resolution.go`), condition `ConditionTypeRoleNamesResolved` (`events.go`); the sharded database's read of its object through `reader` (`APIReader`, wired in `cmd/main.go`); the Aura state events and the status writers they read the transition from — `syncStatus` (`auracustomermanagedkey_controller.go`: `AuraCustomerManagedKeyReady`; `auraipfilter_controller.go`: `AuraIPFilterReady`; `auradatabase_controller.go`: `AuraDatabaseReady`), `setStatusChanged` (`aurainvite_controller.go`: `AuraInviteReady`; `auraorganizationmember_controller.go` and `auraprojectmember_controller.go`: `AuraMemberReady`), the status write in `Reconcile` (`auraproviderconfig_controller.go`: `AuraCredentialsValidated`) — and `ensureToken` (`aura_fleet_provision.go`: `AuraFleetTokenRotated`); `ClusterFormationStarted` — `writeClusterStatus` and `clusterStatusWrite` (what a cluster status write replaced) and `reconcileServerShortfall` (`neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`); `ClusterReady` — `writeClusterStatus`, `clusterStatusWrite.entered` and `recordClusterReady` (`neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`), `completeUpgrade` (`rolling_upgrade_statemachine.go`); the Normal events nothing in status records — `lastAnnounced` (`internal/controller/event_memory.go`), `TopologyPlacementCalculated` in `placementAnnouncement` (`internal/controller/topology_scheduler.go`), `UpgradeDeferred` in `holdImageDriftUntilReady` (`deferredUpgradeTargets`) and `announceUpgradeDeferredByDrain`, forgotten by `setDrainServers` (`internal/controller/scale_down.go`); the property-sharding message, `validatePropertyShardingConfiguration`.
- **rule:**
  - **Transitions:** an event that reports a state — `DatabaseReady`, `BackupScheduled`, `ShardedDatabaseReady`, `SeedBackupPending`, `SeedProxyStarting`, and the auth kinds' `UserReady`, `RoleReady` and `BindingCreated` (id 158), a plugin's `PluginInstalled` and `PluginDisabled` (`updatePluginStatus`, `reportDisabled`) — is emitted only when the status write that records the state changed it: the Ready condition's status or reason, or the phase or message. The status helper reports that from the attempt whose `Status().Update` succeeded, re-fetched inside `retry.RetryOnConflict` (a stale read fails with a conflict and is read again), so the persisted status is the memory and an operator restart announces nothing. When what an event reports can change in place, put it in the status message so the change is a transition: a scheduled backup's message names its CronJob and schedule. A warning that stays true is a different case: id 102's `advisoryWarnings`. Every other Warning about a lasting condition follows the same rule: id 145.
  - **A failure in status is a state too:** `ValidationFailed`, on every kind that emits it, is emitted when the status write that records the failure changes the phase, the message or the Ready condition's status or reason — when the failure starts or its message changes — and not on the passes in between, which keep coming (`RequeueAfter`, resyncs, other watches) until someone fixes the spec. Write the status first and emit from what the write reports; never emit before it. The message carries the validation errors, so a spec edited into a different invalid state is announced. The cluster's and the replica's helpers also count an `observedGeneration` change, so there an edit that leaves the same message is announced once more. It says "validation failed" once: a validator returns its errors alone and the reconciler adds the prefix, and the sharded database's event carries its status message. The cluster's `PropertyShardingValidationFailed` too: `validatePropertyShardingConfiguration` says what is wrong, and the reconciler adds "Property sharding validation failed: ".
  - **…and so is its log line:** a refused spec — the cluster's and the standalone's validation failure, the cluster's server role hints and property sharding, either's missing StorageClass, the database kinds' refusals and waits (id 197), a restore's refused spec (id 215), and a `Neo4jPlugin`'s refusals — its refused spec, a duplicate, a name its target's image cannot install, an install its target's spec refuses (id 228) — is logged by `logRefusal` (`internal/controller/refusal_log.go`) from the same bool: at info on the pass whose status write announced it, at V(1) on the passes in between, never as an ERROR. A refusal is a status the user has to fix, not a controller error; logged as an ERROR before the status write, it was one line per pass, every 6–8 seconds (28 in about four minutes on the #562 walk, for a tag below what the servers ran).
  - **Entering a phase is read from the write:** `ClusterFormationStarted` is emitted when the status write that enters `Forming` replaced another phase of a cluster that, as the API server held it, had never formed — `writeClusterStatus` reports the status it replaced, from the attempt whose write succeeded. Judged from the pass's own copy, the pass after the one that wrote `Forming` read a cache that had not seen that write, and every new cluster announced formation twice. There is no `ClusterFormationFailed`: the formation check returned an error on no path, and formation waits rather than fails; a cluster that stays unreachable raises `ConnectivityDegraded`. `ClusterReady` is emitted by `writeClusterStatus` itself, when the write took the cluster into `Ready` from another phase as the API server held it (`clusterStatusWrite.entered`) — whichever pass writes it: the normal pass (`recordClusterReady`, which reports it), a formed cluster short of a server within its grace (from `Forming` once a majority is back), an upgrade's completion (`completeUpgrade`) on a cluster its walk repaired. A Ready write that keeps the phase announces nothing, whatever else it moves — the message of an upgrade that completed (`UpgradeCompleted` says so) or of a server back within the grace, the `Degraded` condition, a generation, `status.version` — and neither does a pass on a copy from before the Ready write, nor an operator restart.
  - **What no status write records is remembered:** a Normal event about something the operator computes but does not write — `TopologyPlacementCalculated` (the zones a zoned cluster's placement spreads over), `UpgradeDeferred` (an image change the Ready gate or a scale-down drain holds back) — is emitted when what it reports changes, from a per-cluster memory in the reconciler (`lastAnnounced`, `deferredUpgradeTargets`), not on every pass. The memory is forgotten when the condition ends (a drain releasing its hold, a placement with no zones) and when the cluster is deleted. An operator restart announces each once more; that is accepted, as for id 102's advisories. An event that reports a state the operator does write is announced from that write, never from such a memory.
  - **A state that changes in place is recorded:** `RolesResolved` reports which `Neo4jRole` CR names in a `Neo4jUser`'s or `Neo4jRoleBinding`'s `spec.roles` were granted as which Neo4j role. The mapping is the `RoleNamesResolved` condition (present only while there is one, removed silently when there is none), and the event is emitted when the write that records it changed it — a new or different mapping — never on a pass that resolved the same names again.
  - **A pass decides from what the last pass wrote:** the transition is only as good as the status the pass starts from. A `Neo4jShardedDatabase` pass reads its object through the uncached `APIReader` (id 94): from the cache, the pass that the Ready write's own watch event started could still read "Validating", write "Creating" over `Ready` from its step that advances the phase, and announce `ShardedDatabaseReady` again in the same second. A Warning announced from a pass's own copy rather than from the write has the same defect (id 145).
  - **…and goes on from what it wrote itself:** the sharded `transitionStatus` copies the status its write stored into the pass's own copy. Without it, the first pass of a new CR wrote "Validating" and kept a copy with no phase, so its step for "Validating" never wrote "Creating" (#549). A first pass now writes `Validating`, `Creating`, then `Ready` (or the wait it meets).
  - **An upgrade announces nothing a status already says:** the state is read from status, so an operator that announced on every pass (v1.21.1 announced `ShardedDatabaseReady` on each) leaves a status from which the new operator's first pass writes nothing new and announces nothing. A `ShardedDatabaseReady` at the boundary (#553, once, on main's first pass after an upgrade from v1.21.1) means the status had left `Ready` — a pass that found the cluster not accepting work writes `Waiting`, silently, and the next `Ready` is a transition — or came from the old operator's own last pass; neither is new in this operator, and it is left as it is.
  - **Aura states too:** an Aura controller's Normal state event — `AuraCustomerManagedKeyReady`, `AuraIPFilterReady`, `AuraDatabaseReady`, `AuraInviteReady` (which names the invite's phase, so sent → accepted is announced), `AuraMemberReady`, `AuraCredentialsValidated` — is emitted when the status write that records it changed the phase or the Ready condition, read inside the conflict retry; the writers write nothing when nothing changed (id 156), and report no change then. `AuraFleetTokenRotated` is emitted once the rotated token is stored: a token that cannot be stored is lost, the next pass rotates again, and the Warning repeated on each; the status message says the token was rotated and why it could not be kept (id 88).
  - **Privileges counted, not grants sent:** `PrivilegesApplied` counts, in learn mode, the statements whose grant created a row (`learnOutcome.added`), never every grant sent. Learn mode re-learns a role, granting every one of its statements once more, when its rendering scope changes — the image, or an alias its statements name created, dropped or re-pointed; any alias, for a role with a statement the operator cannot read (id 142) — and when an unattributed row goes away. Those grants are no-ops unless the statement's stored form moved.
  - **Sharded CREATE runs once:** `shardCreateNeeded` decides. A `Neo4jShardedDatabase`'s `CREATE DATABASE` is sent on the destructive `replaceExisting` pass, which always creates after its drop, and while the readiness `SHOW DATABASES` lists no family (the virtual database and its `-g000` graph shard). When the family IS listed it is sent only under `ifNotExists: false` for a family this CR did not create: that setting means "create, do not adopt", and Neo4j's "already exists" refusal is its answer (the validator ties `replaceExisting` to `ifNotExists: false`, not the converse). "This CR created it" is `status.virtualDatabase.name` equal to `spec.name` ignoring case, written from SHOW DATABASES only after this CR's own CREATE succeeded (or `IF NOT EXISTS` adopted the family), and persisted, so a restart keeps it. Every family name comparison (`shardFamilyExists`, `observeShardFamily`, this one) ignores case: the validator admits capitals in `spec.name` and SHOW DATABASES lists the database lower-cased, so an exact match recorded no shard status for such a name and refused its own family on every pass after creation. Never `creationTime` or `shardingReady`: neither is tied to a name, and `spec.name` is mutable. A name held by an ordinary database (no graph shard) is not a family; CREATE still runs and reports it as before.
- **why:** every event spends the object's event budget (client-go's spam filter: a burst of 25 per object, then one every five minutes). In v1.20.0 `SeedBackupPending` repeated on every pass and `SeedCredsAutoInherited` never reached the API. The v1.21.0 journey saw `DatabaseReady` and `BackupScheduled` about 6 a minute, the sharded controller's `CREATE … IF NOT EXISTS` and `ShardedDatabaseReady` on every pass, and `PrivilegesApplied "7 added"` six times on one unchanged role. The privilege GRANTs did run — once per alias-layout change, not on every pass — and were no-ops; the count was the defect. The sharded CREATE hid a worse one: without `IF NOT EXISTS`, which `replaceExisting` requires, Neo4j refuses CREATE on an existing name ("the default behavior [is] to fail with an error if the database already exists", Operations Manual `/current/` "Create databases"; a sharded database's virtual database carries the name, `/current/` "Showing sharded property databases"). So the pass after a successful destructive restore failed with "already exists" and turned the CR `Failed`. The local-only integration spec stops at the generation stamp, before that pass. `ValidationFailed` was emitted before (or regardless of) the status write on every kind: an invalid `Neo4jShardedDatabase` repeated it every `RequeueAfter` (found while fixing the above), and the same held for the eleven other kinds that emit it — every pass for the ten that requeue, every resync for `Neo4jPlugin`. `PluginInstalled` followed the Ready write whatever the write did (#548, from the code): a pass on a copy from before the last Ready write wrote nothing and announced the install again. The v1.22.0 journey found three more: `RolesResolved` on every pass that resolved a CR name (40 in five minutes on one `Neo4jUser`, 7 on one `Neo4jRoleBinding`); `ShardedDatabaseReady` twice in one second, from a pass that read a stale copy; and "Validation failed: validation failed: …" on a refused cluster, where the error the reconciler wrapped already said it (the sharded database's status did the same). #525 (2026-10-24, from the code) found the Aura states: `AuraMemberReady`, `AuraInviteReady`, `AuraDatabaseReady`, `AuraIPFilterReady` and `AuraCustomerManagedKeyReady` on every 5-minute re-observe — twice or more, with the pass their own `lastSyncedTime` write started (id 156) — `AuraCredentialsValidated` on every 30s revalidation, and `AuraFleetTokenRotated` on every pass while the token Secret could not be written. The v1.22.0 review and re-walk found four more (#527, #549, #553): `TopologyPlacementCalculated` on every pass of a zoned cluster, `UpgradeDeferred` every 15 seconds while a scale-down drain held an upgrade back, `ClusterFormationStarted` with count 2 at the creation of every new cluster (on main and on v1.21.1), and "Property sharding validation failed: property sharding version validation failed: …". `ClusterFormationFailed` was documented in the events guide and never emitted. #606 (the walk of the #594 fixes, d35c5aa9, 2026-10-27): the `ClusterReady` count went from 3 to 4 over an upgrade of a 3-server CalVer cluster retargeted from 2026.09.9, a tag never published, to 2026.09.0, with the phase `Ready` throughout. `Reconcile` announced every Ready write whose phase, message, Ready condition or generation moved; the normal pass after the completion wrote "Neo4j cluster is fully formed and ready" over "Rolling upgrade completed successfully". From the code, the same held for every completed upgrade, every server back within the grace ("Neo4j cluster is serving. …" replaced) and every spec edit of a Ready cluster (its generation).
- **pinned-by:** `TestClusterReady_ARetargetedUpgradeOfAReadyClusterAnnouncesNothing` (the walk: Rolling towards 2026.09.9 with server-2 down and `Degraded=True`, the retarget, the walk to the end, the completion, three normal passes — `recordClusterReady` reports nothing to announce, no `ClusterReady`; it fails on the code before the fix under `go test -overlay`) and `TestClusterReady_IsAnnouncedByTheWriteThatEntersReady` (from `Forming`, `Degraded`, `Failed`, a majority back within the grace, an upgrade completing on a repaired cluster: one event, none on the passes after; a server back within the grace, a spec edit, a stale copy, a restarted reconciler: none) (`internal/controller/cluster_ready_event_test.go`); `TestReportDatabaseReady_AnnouncesTheTransitionNotEveryPass`, `TestBackupReconcile_AnnouncesTheScheduleOnce` (`internal/controller/transition_events_test.go`); `TestReconcile_ReadyIsAnnouncedOnce` (`internal/controller/plugin_runtime_config_test.go`: a pass whose cache serves the copy from before the Ready write); the `PluginDisabled` count of `TestReconcile_DisablingAPluginTakesItOffItsTarget` (`internal/controller/plugin_disabled_test.go`); `TestReconcile_ValidationFailedIsAnnouncedOnce` (`internal/controller/validation_failed_events_test.go`), one subtest per kind: three passes, one event; a new message, one more; a restarted reconciler, none; `TestReconcile_ARefusedSpecIsLoggedOnceNotAsAnError` (`internal/controller/refusal_log_test.go`: the cluster and the standalone, three passes, no ERROR, one info line, two at V(1)); `TestShardedReconcile_CreatesOnceAndAnnouncesReadyOnce`, `TestShardedReconcile_ExistingFamilyIsNotCreated`, `TestShardedReconcile_IfNotExistsFalseRefusesAFamilyItDidNotCreate`, `TestShardedReconcile_IfNotExistsFalseSkipsTheFamilyItCreated`, `TestShardedReconcile_IfNotExistsFalseSkipsTheFamilyItCreatedWithAMixedCaseName`, `TestShardedReconcile_StaysReadyAfterADestructiveRestore`, `TestShardedReconcile_AnnouncesTheSeedWaitOnce`, `TestShardedReconcile_AnnouncesTheSeedProxyWaitOnce` (`internal/controller/neo4jshardeddatabase_create_once_test.go`); `TestLearnDesired_RelearnCountsOnlyGrantsThatAddedARow` (`internal/controller/neo4jrole_learn_test.go`); `TestReportResolvedRoles_AnnouncesTheMappingOnce` (`internal/controller/auth_repeated_warnings_test.go`), for both kinds: the same mapping three times, one event; a changed mapping, one more; none, the condition gone; `TestShardedReconcile_AStaleCacheDoesNotAnnounceReadyTwice` (`internal/controller/neo4jshardeddatabase_create_once_test.go`), a pass whose cache serves the copy from before the Ready write; `TestReconcile_ValidationFailureWording` and `TestShardedReconcile_ValidationFailureSaysSoOnce` (`internal/controller/cluster_validation_message_test.go`), which count the prefix; `TestShardedReconcile_TheFirstPassWritesEveryPhase` (`internal/controller/restore_sharded_announce_once_test.go`: the phases a new CR's first pass writes, "Creating" missing without the copy-back) and `TestShardedReconcile_AReadyDatabaseFromAnEarlierOperatorIsNotAnnounced` (same file: the status v1.21.1 left, one pass, no event — it passes with and without the copy-back, and guards the boundary); `TestAuraReconcile_ASteadyStateWritesAndAnnouncesNothing` and `TestAuraProviderConfig_ValidatedIsAnnouncedOnTheTransition` (`internal/controller/aura_status_writes_test.go`: three passes, one Ready event; a restarted reconciler and a lastSyncedTime refresh, none; valid → invalid → valid, one event each), `TestFleetProvision_ARotationIsAnnouncedOnceItsTokenIsStored` (`internal/controller/aura_fleet_provision_test.go`: three passes whose Secret write fails, no event; the pass that stores the token, one); `TestClusterReconcile_TopologyPlacementIsAnnouncedWhenTheZonesChange` (three full passes, one event; other zones, one more), `TestStartRollingUpgrade_TheDrainDeferralIsAnnouncedOnce` (three asks, one event; a new target, one more; a later drain, one more), `TestReconcileServerShortfall_FormationStartedIsAnnouncedOnceFromAStaleCopy` (two passes on a copy from before the first one's write, one event; a copy from before formation of a cluster that has formed, none), `TestClusterReconcile_PropertyShardingFailureSaysSoOnce` and `TestEventsGuide_ListsOnlyReasonsTheOperatorHas` (every reason the events guide lists is a constant in `events.go`) (`internal/controller/cluster_events_once_test.go`). Emitting unconditionally, counting every grant, sending CREATE on every pass, skipping it for a family the CR did not create under `ifNotExists: false`, not skipping it for one it did, reading the sharded database from the cache, or a validator that adds the prefix itself, fails them.
- **enforcement:** unit test. There is no machine check that a NEW state event follows the rule — convention + code review. `ClusterReady` (#606) walked live on 2026-10-28 (#606 walk, `5043c68f`): its count stayed the same across an upgrade retargeted from an unpublished tag, a server back within the grace and a dynamic `spec.config` edit, and rose by exactly one for a `Degraded` → `Ready` return and for a scale-up.

### id 137 — A live change of a setting that takes full effect only at a restart is recorded, so `RestartPending` names the servers still waiting
- **scope:** `restartEffectiveSettings`, `restartEffectiveRecord`, `stampRestartEffectiveSettings`, `deferredSetting.supports` and `reconcileRestartPending` (`internal/controller/restart_pending.go`); the stamp's call in `ReconcileConfigMap` (`configmap_manager.go`); `ClusterConnectTimeoutSetting` (`internal/resources/cluster.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **Which settings:** `restartEffectiveSettings` lists dynamic settings that `dbms.setConfigValue` changes only in part. Today it holds only `dbms.cluster.network.connect_timeout`: id 118 applies it to the running servers and `SHOW SETTINGS` then reports the new value, but Neo4j's Raft connections take it up only when the server restarts (id 128). Add a setting here only on evidence that a live change is incomplete. A setting a live apply changes fully stays out, or every edit of it would raise `RestartPending`.
  - **What is recorded: a change, never a presence.** When a ConfigMap write gives one of these a value different from the running ConfigMap's `neo4j.conf` (the line added, edited or removed — an operator upgrade or a `spec.config` edit, both lines compared with `confValues`), the setting and its new value are added to `neo4j.com/restart-effective-settings` (`name=value;…`, empty for Neo4j's default) and `neo4j.com/restart-effective-settings-since` moves to that pass. An unchanged value carries the record forward untouched. A new ConfigMap records nothing, because its servers start with what it carries. The change itself is applied live (or restarts) exactly as before: recording it never rolls anything.
  - **A record of its own:** never fold these into `neo4j.com/deferred-settings`. That signature is the startup-script settings' (id 120), and adding or dropping an entry there would move their `-since`. The unreleased first cut did that, and it named servers for an async Raft setting they already ran.
  - **What it reports:** each record has its own since. A server is named for a setting when its `neo4j` container started before that setting's record, and it drops out when it restarts. With neither record, the condition is absent (id 120). A cluster whose only record is a restart-effective change keeps `RestartPending=False` once every server has restarted.
  - **No connection for an ungated setting:** a `deferredSetting` with nil `Supported` exists on every Neo4j the operator runs (5.26 and CalVer; the connect timeout is 5.17+). When nothing recorded is version-gated, the pass decides from the pods alone and dials no server, so CalVer clusters gain no Bolt connections (ids 125, 132).
  - **Accepted gap:** v1.21.0 and v1.21.1 applied the 5s line live to clusters upgraded from v1.20.x and recorded nothing. Upgrading such a cluster to this release changes no value, so a server that has not restarted since that upgrade is not named. The v1.21.0 migration note already says Raft takes the value up at each server's next restart. This was chosen over naming every server of every v1.21.x cluster: most of those servers need no restart, including every cluster created on v1.21.x.
- **why:** the v1.21.0 journey: an upgraded 5.26 cluster still stalled writes ~10s when a restarted server rejoined, because the survivors' Raft connections kept the 30s they started with. `RestartPending`, the condition that says which servers wait for a restart, said nothing, because a setting applied live was never recorded. The Operations Manual labels the setting dynamic on both lines (`configuration/configuration-settings.adoc` on `5.x` and `dev`, `configuration/dynamic-settings.adoc`) and does not say which parts of the server honour a runtime change. The startup-only Raft behaviour is the live measurement of id 128: 4 of 4 steps on 2026.08.1, and the same 10s stall when server-1 rejoined an upgraded 5.26 cluster in the v1.21.0 journey. The first cut put the value in the deferred signature and named every server of every cluster that already had the line; review caught it.
- **pinned-by:** `TestStampRestartEffectiveSettings` (new ConfigMap, unchanged value, added line, kept since, later edit, removed line, startup record untouched), `TestRestartPending_ConnectTimeout_UnchangedValueNamesNobody` (the v1.21.x upgrade on both lines: no record, no condition on CalVer, `False` and an unmoved async-Raft `-since` on 5.26; it fails on the first cut), `TestRestartPending_ConnectTimeout_FreshCluster`, `TestRestartPending_ConnectTimeout_ChangedValueNamesTheServersUntilTheyRestart` (the v1.20.x upgrade on both lines: applied live, no restart, only the connect timeout named, servers drop out as they restart), `TestRestartPending_ConnectTimeout_SpecConfigChange` (each edit moves the since) (`internal/controller/restart_pending_test.go`). They run the real `ReconcileConfigMap` and `reconcileRestartPending` against a fake client. With the list empty, all but the unchanged and fresh cases fail.
- **enforcement:** unit test.

### id 139 — A cluster's spec is checked against what it runs, read from status; there is no previous spec to compare with
- **scope:** the validation block of `Reconcile` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`); `ValidateWithWarnings`, `ValidateCreateWithWarnings`, `validateAgainstRunning` and `validateCluster` (`internal/validation/cluster_validator.go`); `ValidateVersionUpgrade`, `ValidateTagAgainstRunning` and `ValidateUpgradeStrategy` (`internal/validation/upgrade_validator.go`); `validateVersionCompatibility` (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade.go`); the `Neo4jEnterpriseCluster` entry and `withLiveClusterStatus` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **No "old" object.** Without a webhook a reconcile sees only the object as it is now. Reading the cluster again returns the same spec, so never fetch a second copy to call it "old", and never write a rule as `old.Spec` against `new.Spec`. A rule that needs the previous state reads what the operator recorded about the running cluster in status.
  - **One entry point.** The reconciler and `kubectl neo4j validate` both call `ValidateWithWarnings`: the spec rules (`ValidateCreateWithWarnings`) plus `validateAgainstRunning`. A rule that needs only the spec goes in `validateCluster`, where the CLI runs it offline; the `spec.upgradeStrategy` durations are there. The wording of a refusal still follows `clusterEverAccepted` ("Validation failed" until a pass has accepted the cluster, then "Update validation failed").
  - **The version rule.** `spec.image.tag` is checked against `status.version`, which is stamped at the first Ready, after each verified upgrade, and at a Ready on an image that reached the servers while none was ready (id 144), and stays at the version the servers ran before an upgrade while it is in flight or Paused or Failed. Moving the tag back to it is allowed only while that upgrade has not restarted a server: once one runs the target, a tag below `status.upgradeStatus.targetVersion` is refused as well (`validateTagAgainstUpgrade`, id 149). A standalone is held to the same rule (id 144). Refused: a downgrade, CalVer to 5.x, CalVer from a 5.x other than 5.26, and 4.x. Not judged: an empty or unparseable `status.version`, a tag the image rule already refuses, and the patch when either tag names none (a patch-less tag floats to the newest patch) — for a patch-less tag the patch is judged against what the servers report instead, once recorded (`status.runningVersion`, `status.upgradeStatus.resolvedTargetVersion`, id 153). It runs on every pass, mid-upgrade included: a target changed mid-flight re-stages with no check of its own (this rule and id 149's are what refuse a downgrade then), and a cluster with no ready server takes the new image straight into the StatefulSet. The upgrade machine's own check, when an upgrade starts (`validateVersionCompatibility`), calls the whole rule — `UpgradeValidator.ValidateTagAgainstRunning`, which `validateAgainstRunning` is — so the two cannot disagree; it called only `ValidateVersionUpgrade`, without the reported versions of ids 149 and 153 (#550, `TestValidateVersionCompatibility_HoldsTheTagToWhatTheServersReport`). Its pre-upgrade check then holds the tag to what the servers report at that moment (id 153).
  - **No server-count rule.** At least 2 servers is a spec rule; a scale-down is the drain's to carry out or refuse (`scale_down.go`); whether added servers fit is the scheduler's call (`kubectl neo4j preflight` estimates it). The never-run `ResourceValidator` was deleted rather than wired in: it compared cluster-wide totals (a pod lands on one node), its 80% "warning" was an error, and it would have refused every scale-up a node autoscaler serves and every one whose node or pod list was forbidden.
  - **The CLI.** Offline, it drops any status in the manifest (apply does too) and its summary says the tag rule needs `--connect`. Connected, it copies in the status of the live cluster with the same name; a cluster not created yet has none; one it cannot read gives a warning naming the check that did not run.
- **why:** review found `isUpdate` true for every object read from the API server and the "current" cluster fetched with the same `r.Get`, so `ValidateUpdateWithWarnings(currentCluster, cluster)` compared a spec with itself, and `ValidateScaling` was called with `(newCluster, newCluster.Spec.Topology)` as well. The scale-up, servers-below-2 and version rules never fired. `ValidateUpgradeStrategy` did run, on every pass, but only there, so `validate` passed `upgradeTimeout: "5 minutes"` and the operator then refused the cluster. A downgrade was refused only by the upgrade machine when an upgrade started (`Failed`, then `Ready` again on the next formation check, and refused again); a target changed mid-flight to below the running version was re-staged, which the envtest spec shows on the old code (`targetVersion` became `2025.10.0-enterprise`). Neo4j (Upgrade and Migration Guide, `/current/`, read 2026-10-16): "Neo4j does not support downgrades. If the upgrade or migration is not successful, you have to do a full rollback, including restoring a pre-upgrade or a pre-migration backup"; "All upgrades from earlier than 5.26 must first upgrade to Neo4j 5.26 LTS"; "You can upgrade from any version in the 2025–2026 series to any subsequent version". The old rule refused the step from an earlier 5.x to 5.26, which Neo4j requires, and a move from `5.26.12-enterprise` onto `5.26-enterprise`.
- **pinned-by:** `TestValidateWithWarnings_ChecksTheTagAgainstStatusVersion`, `TestValidateWithWarnings_LeavesTheServerCountToTheSpecRules`, `TestValidateCreateWithWarnings_ChecksTheUpgradeStrategy` (`internal/validation/cluster_running_validation_test.go`); `TestValidateVersionUpgrade` (`internal/validation/upgrade_validator_test.go`); `TestValidateVersionCompatibility`, `TestValidateVersionCompatibility_HoldsTheTagToWhatTheServersReport` (`internal/controller/upgrade_detection_test.go`); the envtest spec "Neo4jEnterpriseCluster image tag against the version it runs" (`internal/controller/cluster_running_version_test.go`), which drives a real `Reconcile` against the API server; `TestValidate_ChecksTheUpgradeStrategyLikeTheOperator`, `TestValidate_Connected_ChecksTheTagAgainstTheRunningVersion`, `TestValidate_Offline_SaysTheTagIsCheckedOnlyWhenConnected` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate_running_cluster_test.go`). Each fails on the code before the fix.
- **enforcement:** validator (inline in the reconciler and the CLI) + unit and envtest tests.

### id 140 — A `spec.config` value of a type the operator knows is parsed the way Neo4j parses it, and refused only when Neo4j would refuse it
- **scope:** `typedSettings`, `ValidateSettingValues` and the readers `readNeo4jByteSize`, `readNeo4jDuration`, `readJavaInteger`, `readOneOf` (`internal/validation/setting_values.go`); the call from `ConfigValidator.ValidateConfigMap` (`config_validator.go`), which both the cluster and the standalone validator run, and the cluster's `spec.propertySharding.config` call in `validateCluster` (`cluster_validator.go`, only with sharding enabled — the block is rendered only then); `MemoryValidator.parseMemoryToBytes` (`memory_validator.go`), which reads sizes with the same reader; `kubectl neo4j validate` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate.go`), which runs the same validators.
- **rule:** For a setting in `typedSettings`, a value Neo4j cannot parse — or one below a documented minimum — is a validation error on `spec.config[<key>]` naming the value, the expected format and an example. Everything else passes: an unlisted setting, a value written as `$(command)` or containing a backslash (Neo4j expands or unescapes it first: `Config.isCommand`, `java.util.Properties`), and any spelling the readers mark unknown (non-ASCII text, NumberFormat grouping such as `1,024K`, a possible exponent, `NaN`). Readers follow Neo4j's code, not its prose: values are trimmed as `String.trim` does; byte sizes are `ByteUnit.parse` (a NumberFormat number, optional whitespace, then one of 25 case-sensitive unit names, every one a power of 1024; text after the unit is ignored; exactly 2^63 bytes saturates); durations are `TimeUtil.parseTimeMillis` (int amounts each with a lower-cased unit `ns`/`us`/`μs`/`ms`/`s`/`m`/`h`/`d`, a bare number is seconds, truncated to milliseconds before a minimum is compared); integers and longs are `Integer.parseInt`/`Long.parseLong`; booleans and enums are `equalsIgnoreCase`. Maximums are not encoded. The memory rules (heap + page cache vs the limit) read sizes with `readNeo4jByteSize` and skip a size they cannot read rather than reporting it a second time. **Adding a setting** — all four must hold, or leave it out: (1) `configuration-settings.adoc` gives it identical "Valid values" on branches `5.x` and `dev` of `neo4j/docs-operations` (fetch `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/neo4j/docs-operations/<branch>/modules/ROOT/pages/configuration/configuration-settings.adoc` and read the `|Valid values` row under `=== \`<name>\``; the rendered page truncates); (2) its declaration — `newBuilder("<name>", <PARSER>, …)` and every `addConstraint` — is the same in `neo4j/neo4j` on `release/5.26.x` and the current CalVer branches (`GraphDatabaseSettings`, `BootloaderSettings`, `ServerSettings`, `BoltConnector` under `community/`), with a parser the readers model (`BOOL`, `INT`, `LONG`, `DURATION`, `BYTES`, `ofEnum` — whose constants are the enum's names); (3) no setting migrator rewrites its value (`SettingMigrators`, `ServerSettingsMigrator`, `BoltSettingsMigrator`); (4) the renderer writes the user's value into `neo4j.conf` unchanged (not an auth-generated or dropped key). Enterprise-only settings have no public declaration, so condition (2) cannot be checked and they are not listed. A setting whose type differs between the lines needs a version gate first: `ValidateConfigMap` does not know the image tag today.
- **why:** the v1.21.0 verification walk set `db.tx_log.buffer.size: "1MiB"` on a 5.26.31 standalone. The setting is a long (`LONG`, `min(kibiBytes(128))`), and static, so Neo4j read it only at startup: validation passed, the server refused to start ("Error evaluating value for setting", whatever `server.config.strict_validation` says — that setting covers unknown names and duplicates), the pod crash-looped, and after the value was fixed the StatefulSet took about four minutes to replace the crash-looping pod. Sources, read 2026-10-16: the `5.x` and `dev` AsciiDoc ("A long that is minimum `131072`." on both; rendered at `https://neo4j.com/docs/operations-manual/5/configuration/configuration-settings/#config_db.tx_log.buffer.size` and `/current/`), and Neo4j's source on `release/5.26.0`, `release/5.26.31`, `2025.01`, `.04`, `.07`, `.10`, `2026.01`, `.04`, `.06`, `.08` and `.09`, where the 46 listed declarations, `ByteUnit`, `TimeUtil` and the `SettingValueParsers` used are identical. The narrow table is deliberate: a false refusal blocks a deployment that would run, so the check refuses only what it can prove. Found alongside: the memory rules had their own size parser, which refused `1GiB` and `512MB` (valid Neo4j sizes, "invalid memory format" whenever a memory limit was set) and read `2Gi` (which Neo4j refuses); the journey's crash-loop recipe (`db.tx_log.rotation.size: "not-a-size"`) is now refused up front, and uses `db.tx_log.rotation.retention_policy: "forever"` instead.
- **pinned-by:** `TestConfigValidator_RefusesAByteSizeForALongSetting` (`internal/validation/config_validator_test.go`; uses only entry points that predate the check, and fails on the code before it); `TestReadNeo4jByteSize`, `TestReadNeo4jDuration`, `TestReadJavaInteger`, `TestReadOneOf`, `TestValidateSettingValues`, `TestValidateSettingValues_TransformedValuesAreNotJudged`, `TestValidateSettingValues_UnlistedSettingsPassUntouched`, `TestValidateSettingValues_ReportsEveryBadValueInKeyOrder`, `TestSettingTypeExpectation`, `TestTypedSettingsExamplesAreValid`, `TestTypedSettingsAreUserSettings`, `TestOperatorRenderedConfPassesTheValueCheck`, `TestMemoryRulesLeaveFormatErrorsToTheValueCheck`, `TestClusterValidator_TypedValuesInPropertyShardingConfig` (`internal/validation/setting_values_test.go`); the parity subtest "typed values are judged alike" in `TestStandaloneAndClusterAgreeOnNeo4jRules` (`internal/validation/standalone_validator_test.go`); `TestMemoryValidator_parseMemoryToBytes` (`internal/validation/memory_validator_test.go`); `TestStandaloneCreateConfigMap_OperatorValuesPassTheValueCheck` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_conf_test.go`); `TestValidate_CatchesValuesNeo4jCannotParse` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate_test.go`); `TestPublishedManifestsPassOurOwnValidators` keeps every example applicable.
- **enforcement:** validator (inline in both reconcilers and the CLI) + unit test. Whether a NEW table entry meets the four conditions is convention + code review: no test can fetch the docs or Neo4j's source.

### id 141 — The cluster controller keeps the init containers and volumes it did not render; a plugin writes to the servers only when its target is Ready with every server available and has rolled out its latest spec, and is Ready only once its own roll is done
- **scope:** `createOrUpdateResourceInternal`, `isTemplateChangeSignificant`, `hasSignificantTemplateChanges`, `initContainersEqual`, `volumesEqual`, `readOwnedTemplateItems`, `writeOwnedTemplateItems`, `withoutForeign` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`); `mergeOwnedByName` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`); the settled branch, the availability gate and the rollout wait of `Reconcile`, `getTargetDeployment`, `deferInstall`, `trustedCAUpdate`, `updatePluginStatus`, `installChangesTarget`, `writePluginToTarget`, `applyPluginToTemplate`, `applyVerifiedDownload`, `missingFromTarget`, `pluginPodDrift` (`internal/controller/plugin_controller.go`); `withoutPluginInitContainer` (`internal/controller/plugin_env_ownership.go`); `targetRolloutPending`, `statefulSetRolloutPending`, `rolloutPending`, `failingPods` (`internal/controller/plugin_target_gate.go`); `EventReasonPluginInstallDeferred` (`internal/controller/events.go`); `PluginCAVolumeName` (`internal/resources/plugin_init_container.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **Init containers and volumes are owned by name, as env vars are:** a template apply of the cluster controller merges the server pod template's init containers and volumes with `mergeOwnedByName`. What it renders is applied; what it rendered on its last apply and no longer renders is dropped (its record: `neo4j.com/cluster-controller-init-containers` and `neo4j.com/cluster-controller-volumes` on the StatefulSet, written from the desired template, never the merged one — id 134 — and the merged env goes into a copy of it, not into the template the record is read from, id 155); anything else is kept. `initContainersEqual` and `volumesEqual` leave the kept ones out (`withoutForeign`), so they never make a stable cluster's template "changed", and the template hash covers the desired template only.
  - **A StatefulSet without that record** (an earlier operator's, which replaced both lists wholesale on every apply): every init container on it is the cluster's except those the plugin controller lists in its pod-template annotation `neo4j.com/plugin-init-containers`, and every volume is except those only such an init container mounts. Reading a missing record as "owns nothing" would keep for ever an init container the cluster stops rendering — the truststore one, still reading a CA Secret the user then deletes.
  - **The plugin writes once, and only a change:** its env edits and a `VerifiedDownload` init container and volumes go in one StatefulSet update (`applyPluginToTemplate`). The live init container is replaced only when a field the plugin sets differs from it (`equality.Semantic.DeepDerivative`), because the API server defaults the others. A `Ready` plugin puts back its init container and volumes when they are gone (`pluginPodDrift`, by name). Uninstalling the last `VerifiedDownload` plugin of a target removes the shared `plugin-ca` volume, which nothing else would now remove; while installed, the plugin keeps that volume in line with the target's `spec.trustedCASecrets` (id 147).
  - **No write to the servers on a target that is not fully available:** when the target is not `Ready` with every server available (`IsReady` from `getTargetDeployment`: a cluster in phase `Ready` with no `Degraded=True`; a standalone in phase `Ready`) and the plugin's edits would change its StatefulSet (`installChangesTarget`), the plugin is `Waiting` ("Waiting until Neo4jEnterpriseCluster … is Ready with every server available: installing the plugin restarts every server", or "updating the plugin's trusted CAs restarts every server" when all it writes is the CA wiring, `trustedCAUpdate`; id 147), looks again after `RequeueAfter`, and says so once: a `PluginInstallDeferred` Normal event, or, for a `Ready` plugin whose target lost what it set, one `PluginMissing` Warning that says it waits. The event follows the status write that starts the wait (`updatePluginStatus` reports it; id 136), so the passes that go on waiting and an operator restart say nothing; every pass words the wait the same way, from the same `trustedCAUpdate`. Not held: a pass with nothing to write (one waiting for the servers its own write restarted, which turned the cluster `Forming`), a settled plugin with nothing missing, the uninstall of a deleted plugin, and the write that repairs the plugin's own failing init container (id 152). The removal of a disabled plugin is a write like an install and is held (id 160).
  - **A `Ready` the target has not acted on is not `Ready`:** `IsReady` also needs the target to have rolled out its latest spec (`targetRolloutPending`): its controller recorded it (`status.observedGeneration` equal to `metadata.generation`, on the cluster and the standalone alike), and its StatefulSet has no rollout in flight (`statefulSetRolloutPending`: `status.observedGeneration` at least `metadata.generation` — the API server moves the generation on every template write, before the StatefulSet controller has looked — `currentRevision` equal to `updateRevision`, and updated and ready replicas equal to `spec.replicas`). Between a spec change and the status write of the pass that acts on it, a cluster still reads `Ready`: it writes its StatefulSet first and turns `Forming` only once it has checked formation, and the plugin, woken by that write, read the `Ready` in between. A standalone read `Ready` while its one pod did; since #548 its own `Ready` needs the roll done as well (`updateStatus` in `internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`: the pod ready and `statefulSetRolloutPending` empty, else `Pending`, "Waiting for the standalone to restart on its latest pod template: …"), so everything that goes by a standalone's `Ready` — databases, users, roles, a plugin — waits for the roll as a cluster's dependents wait while it is `Forming`. The plugin keeps its own check, which also covers the pass before the standalone's status write. The servers a `Paused` or `Failed` upgrade holds below its partition count as a rollout in flight here (unlike for the cluster's phase, id 149): a plugin written then would reach only the servers above the partition.
  - **`Ready` only once its own roll is done:** after its write the plugin stays `Installing` ("Waiting for pods to be ready after plugin installation") until its target's StatefulSet reports the new template rolled out (`rolloutPending`, then `arePodsReady`), and only then goes `Ready` — there is no configuration step (id 135) — with its one `PluginInstalled` event, from the status write that makes it `Ready` (id 136). The old pods report `Ready` until they stop, so pod readiness alone let a plugin read `Ready` in the second it wrote. The pass that writes judges the roll against the generation its write created (`writePluginToTarget` returns it): the StatefulSet it reads next comes from the informer cache, which may not have seen that write and still reads fully rolled out, so a cached generation below it is a roll not started. While the roll is stuck on pods the plugin's own init container keeps from starting, the message names them ("…; cannot start: c-server-2 (init container plugin-init-apoc exited with code 60)"; `failingPods`), and pods whose neo4j container keeps exiting the same way (id 155); it changes only when that does, so it is no status-write loop.
- **why:**
  - **The init container (reported on #515):** `createOrUpdateResourceInternal` replaced the pod template wholesale but for env vars and pod-template annotations, and `initContainersEqual` / `volumesEqual` compared the lengths of the live and desired lists. A `VerifiedDownload` plugin's init container therefore made the next pass of a stable cluster a "significant change", and the apply removed it: the servers restarted without the plugin's JAR. A plugin still `Installing` put it back, which restarted them again, until it reached `Ready` — after which nothing put it back. Seen in the code, not live; the envtest shows the StatefulSet's generation moving on every pass of either controller without the fix. The standalone controller had been fixed for the same thing (`TestReconcileStatefulSet_PreservesForeignInitContainersAndVolumes`), the cluster not.
  - **The wait:** `getTargetDeployment` computed `IsReady` (Ready, not Degraded) and nothing read it, while the plugin docs said installs wait while a cluster is Degraded. An install restarts every server; on a cluster already short of one that can cost it its majority (id 105).
  - **Found on the way:** the env edits and the init container were two StatefulSet writes — two rollouts — and the init container was written on every pass, changed or not.
  - **The stale `Ready` and the early `Ready` (v1.22.0 journey, 2026-10-17, CalVer 2026.08.1, 3 servers):** two CA Secrets added to `spec.trustedCASecrets` at 09:33 CEST; the plugin wrote its CA update in the same second as the cluster's truststore write, having read the cluster's `Ready` from the pass before its new generation, and never showed `Waiting` or `Installing`, so the servers rolled to both changes at once. Separately, a cluster `VerifiedDownload` install read `Ready` at 09:04:09 while the roll it caused finished at 09:07:43, and a standalone `PluginMissing` repair read `Ready` mid-restart. Walking the fix live (Kind, 13:46 CEST) found one more way: a wrong-checksum edit was written, the same pass read the StatefulSet from the cache as it was before the write, and the plugin was `Ready` for 46 seconds while server-2 crash-looped in its init container. The unit tests' fake client has no cache; `TestReconcile_ThePassThatWritesWaitsForTheGenerationItWrote` gives it one.
- **pinned-by:** `TestCreateOrUpdateResource_KeepsAPluginsInitContainerAndVolumes`, `TestCreateOrUpdateResource_ReadsAnEarlierOperatorsTemplate`, `TestClusterAndPluginLeaveEachOthersItemsAlone_APIServer` (envtest) (`internal/controller/sts_template_ownership_test.go`); `TestIsTemplateChangeSignificant_PluginAddedInitContainer` (`internal/controller/template_comparison_test.go`); `TestReconcile_InstallWaitsUntilTheTargetIsFullyAvailable`, `TestReconcile_DeferredInstallProceedsWhenTheClusterRecovers`, `TestReconcile_RestoringWaitsUntilTheClusterIsFullyAvailable`, `TestReconcile_SettledPluginIgnoresADegradedCluster`, `TestReconcile_WaitingForItsOwnRestartIsNotDeferred`, `TestInstallPluginViaEnvironment_VerifiedDownloadWritesOnce`, `TestReconcile_SettledVerifiedDownloadPluginRestoresItsInitContainer`, `TestPluginPodDrift`, `TestUninstall_RemovesTheSharedCAVolumeOnceUnused` (`internal/controller/plugin_target_availability_test.go`); `TestReconcile_AReadyTheTargetHasNotActedOnIsNotReady` (four ways a `Ready` is stale), `TestReconcile_ACAUpdateThatWaitedIsWrittenAsOne`, `TestReconcile_ReadyOnlyOnceItsOwnRollIsDone` (a cluster install and a standalone `PluginMissing` repair), `TestReconcile_ThePassThatWritesWaitsForTheGenerationItWrote` (a cache that has not seen the write), `TestReconcile_TheWaitNamesThePodsItsInitContainerKeepsFromStarting`, `TestStatefulSetRolloutPending`, `TestPluginWritesFollowTheTargetsRollout_APIServer` (envtest: real generation semantics, an install then a CA change) (`internal/controller/plugin_target_gate_test.go`); `TestStandaloneUpdateStatus_ARollInFlightIsNotReady` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_status_test.go`: a template write the StatefulSet controller has not seen, and the old pod still ready, are `Pending`); `TestPluginController_ReAddsARemovedNeo4jPlugins` (envtest, with a stand-in StatefulSet controller, `simulateStatefulSetController`, since envtest runs none). Each fails with its fix switched off; `TestReconcile_SettledPluginIgnoresADegradedCluster` and `TestReconcile_WaitingForItsOwnRestartIsNotDeferred` fail against a gate that waits whenever the target is not Ready.
- **enforcement:** unit test + envtest; the stale-`Ready` wait and the `Ready`-after-the-roll walked live on Kind (2026-10-17, see id 152).

### id 142 — A role re-learns its privileges only when an alias it names changes; an unreadable alias layout is no change
- **scope:** `roleRenderingScope`, `roleAliasFingerprint` and the uncached path of `renderStatement` (`internal/controller/neo4jrole_normalise.go`); the unknown-scope branch of `learnDesired` (`neo4jrole_learn.go`); `resolvableDatabaseNames` (`neo4jrole_controller.go`); `PrivilegeAliasDependence`, `scanPrivilegeScopes`, `parseName`, `privilegeTokens` (`internal/neo4j/privileges.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **The scope:** a role's rendering scope — `status.privilegeRenderingScope` in learn mode, the cache key in probe mode — is the server's UID, its image, and the targets of only the aliases the role's statements name, matched case-insensitively against SHOW ALIASES, a constituent by the qualified name it is listed under (`comp.c`). `ON DBMS`, `ON HOME` / `ON DEFAULT` and the `*` wildcard name no alias: each is stored as the keyword or the `*`, never as a database (recorded fixture), so no alias change can move it. A grant on a composite is stored on the composite, so the composite's constituents are not in the scope; a constituent the role names is. Only a statement `PrivilegeAliasDependence` cannot read (no `ON`, an `ON` it does not know such as `LOAD ON ALL DATA`, a name it cannot take apart) puts the whole role on every alias: the key it had before, unchanged. The scope is per role, so a spec change that adds or removes a statement naming an existing alias changes it too: one re-learn of the role, as for an alias change. Never widen a role that names its databases, or a role on `*`, back to every alias.
  - **Names as Neo4j lists them:** a name is read as dot-separated parts, each bare or backtick-quoted, a doubled backtick being one literal backtick, and returned with the quotes resolved — `` `my-comp`.`latest x` `` is `my-comp.latest x`, the name SHOW ALIASES lists. The same names feed the does-this-database-exist check (`PrivilegeDatabaseTargets`) and the composite warning (`PrivilegeGraphTargets`). A closing quote does not end a token, and `CanonicalisePrivilegeStatement` keeps a doubled backtick inside its name.
  - **Unknown is not empty:** when SHOW DATABASES or SHOW ALIASES fails, `roleRenderingScope` returns `""`. Learn mode keeps the scope the renderings were learned in, so that pass re-learns nothing and the next pass that reads the layout compares against it; probe mode probes without the cache, reading and filling nothing. Never fingerprint a failed listing as an empty layout.
  - **Upgrade:** the narrowed key differs from the old one wherever the cluster has an alias the role does not name — for a role on `*` too, whose key was every alias and is now none. Such a role re-learns once after upgrading — one round of no-op grants, which `PrivilegesApplied` does not count (id 136) — and then settles. On a cluster without aliases the keys are equal, and a role with a statement that cannot be read keeps the cluster-wide key: neither re-learns.
- **why:** the re-learn exists for one case. Neo4j resolves a local alias when the command runs and stores the privilege against its target ("Privileges are defined on the target database, and not the local database alias", Operations Manual 5 and `/current/`, "Manage database aliases"), and keeps those rows when the alias is re-pointed — so the presence check still passes and only a re-grant learns the new target. An alias a role does not name cannot move its stored form. The scope fingerprinted every alias on the cluster, so any alias created, dropped or re-pointed, any constituent added or removed, and the aliases a database recreate drops (`DROP DATABASE … CASCADE ALIASES`) and recreates, made every role re-send every grant — roles × statements GRANTs per change, each a `security.log` line, all no-ops but one. A failed SHOW ALIASES fingerprinted as empty: two more rounds, on the failure and on the recovery. The tokenizer ended a token at every closing quote, so `` `my-comp`.`x` `` was read as two names, `my-comp` and `` .`x` ``: the second never resolved, so the grant was skipped as naming a missing database, and the first raised the "GRAPH privileges on a composite" warning for a grant on a constituent. And the canonicaliser read an escaped backtick as two quoted names back to back, so the name a`b (written quoted, its backtick doubled) canonicalised to `ab` — another name's canonical form.
- **pinned-by:** `TestLearnDesired_AnAliasTheRoleDoesNotNameSendsNoGrants`, `TestLearnDesired_AChangeToAnAliasTheRoleNamesRelearns`, `TestLearnDesired_AWildcardRoleRelearnsOnlyForTheAliasesItNames`, `TestLearnDesired_AnUnreadableStatementRelearnsOnAnyAliasChange`, `TestLearnDesired_AnUnreadableAliasLayoutIsNotAScopeChange`, `TestLearnDesired_UpgradeRelearnsOnceThenSettles` (`internal/controller/neo4jrole_learn_test.go`); `TestRoleAliasFingerprint`, `TestRoleRenderingScope_UnknownIsNotEmpty`, `TestNormaliseDesired_UnknownAliasLayoutIsNotCached` (`internal/controller/neo4jrole_normalise_test.go`); `TestUnresolvedPrivilegeDatabases` (`internal/controller/neo4jrole_controller_unit_test.go`); `TestPrivilegeAliasDependence`, `TestPrivilegeDatabaseTargets`, `TestPrivilegeGraphTargets_OnlyGraphScope`, `TestCanonicalise_EscapedBacktickStaysInTheName`, `TestPrivilegeStatementMatchesRole` (`internal/neo4j/privileges_test.go`). Fingerprinting every alias, putting `*` back on every alias, missing a named alias or constituent, reading a failed listing as empty, splitting a quoted constituent or merging an escaped backtick fails them.
- **enforcement:** unit test.

### id 143 — What a reconcile reads for status is applied to the object the status write re-reads, and written only when it changes
- **scope:** `ensureDatabase`, `observeDatabase`, `databaseObservation`, `updateDatabaseStatusObserved` and `reportDatabaseReady` (`internal/controller/neo4jdatabase_controller.go`); `GetDatabases` and `DatabaseInfo.Address` (`internal/neo4j/client.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **Into the closure:** a value the reconcile reads from Neo4j for status — `Neo4jDatabase` `status.state` and `status.servers` — is handed to the status helper, which applies it to the object it re-reads inside `retry.RetryOnConflict`: the object it writes. Never set it on the reconcile's own copy; the helper's re-read throws that copy's status away.
  - **Only on change:** the helper compares the status before and after (`apiequality.Semantic.DeepEqual`) and skips the write when nothing differs. A status write is a watch event that reconciles the object again (id 122).
  - **Not on row order:** SHOW DATABASES lists one row per copy in no stable order, so the reading must not depend on it. The servers are the copies' `address` values, sorted. The state folds every copy through `summarizeShardCopies`: `online` when every copy is, otherwise the first other status by name. Names match ignoring case, as for sharded families (id 136) and every name a spec gives (id 146).
  - A read that fails, or lists no copy of the database, observes nothing, and the persisted values stay.
- **why:** `ensureDatabase` set `status.state` and `status.servers` on the in-memory object, and `updateDatabaseStatus` re-fetched a fresh copy and wrote that. They reached the API only through the first pass's `dataImported` write, so a database with `initialData` or `seedURI` kept its first values for good: one that was stopped still said `online`, one that moved still listed the servers it left. A database with neither never reported them at all. The state was the first row's status, so once written on change it would have flipped with the row order whenever one copy lagged. Found while fixing id 136's `DatabaseReady`.
- **pinned-by:** `TestDatabaseReconcile_StatusFollowsShowDatabases`, `TestDatabaseReconcile_SeededDatabaseStatusDoesNotGoStale`, `TestDatabaseReconcile_WritesStatusOnlyWhenItChanges`, `TestDatabaseReconcile_ObservesAMixedCaseName`, `TestObserveDatabase` (`internal/controller/neo4jdatabase_observed_status_test.go`). Setting the fields on the reconcile's copy, writing when nothing changed, taking the first row's state, or matching the name exactly fails them.
- **enforcement:** unit test (fake client).

### id 144 — `status.version` is what the servers run; every image change is checked against it, and no strategy keeps an image from the servers
- **scope:** `rolledImageTag`, `imageTag`, `clusterVersionToRecord` (`internal/controller/running_version.go`); `recordClusterReady`, `runningServerImageTag`, `handleRollingUpgrade` and the escape hatch in `holdImageDriftUntilReady` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`); `buildStatefulSetForEnterprise` (`internal/resources/cluster.go`); the Recreate entry of `ClusterValidator.NoEffectWarnings` (`internal/validation/noeffect_warnings.go`); the validation block of `Reconcile`, `createStatefulSet`, `updateStatus` and `isStandaloneUpgradeRequired` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`); `StandaloneValidator.Validate` and `validateAgainstRunning` (`internal/validation/standalone_validator.go`); `validateUpgradeTimeout` and `validateVersionMove` (`internal/validation/upgrade_validator.go`); `upgradeTimeoutOrDefault` (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade.go`); `ApplyDefaults` (`internal/validation/cluster_validator.go`); the cluster and standalone entries of `validators`, `withLiveStandaloneStatus` and the usage text (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **No strategy holds an image back.** A cluster's server StatefulSet is always `RollingUpdate`, and every image change on a Ready cluster goes through the rolling-upgrade state machine, whatever `spec.upgradeStrategy.strategy` says. Neo4j documents only rolling upgrades for a cluster, so `Recreate` on a cluster is accepted with a `ValidationWarning` (`NoEffectWarnings`, id 102) and does what `RollingUpgrade` does. A standalone's StatefulSet is `RollingUpdate` too: with one replica that is what `Recreate` promises, the pod deleted and created again on the new template. Never `OnDelete`: the operator deletes a cluster's server pods only to repair a split-brain, and a standalone's only through a restore.
  - **`status.version` follows the servers.** It is the image tag of a StatefulSet whose controller has observed the current template (`observedGeneration`) with every replica on that revision and ready (`rolledImageTag`); the template and its status come from one read. It stays a tag: for a tag without a patch, the version the servers report is recorded beside it in `status.runningVersion`, and the tag rule holds `spec.image.tag` to that too (id 153). A cluster's Ready pass records it when it differs from `status.version` — at the first Ready, and after an image reached the servers without the state machine through the zero-ready escape hatch — except while a `Failed` or `Paused` upgrade record targets that same tag: the machine re-enters a Failed upgrade and records the version once it has verified the servers, and Paused waits for the user (ids 139, 149). A standalone's `updateStatus` records it whenever its pod is ready on the current template, and never from `spec.image.tag`.
  - **A standalone has the cluster's tag rule.** `StandaloneValidator.Validate` is its one entry point, run by the reconciler on every pass and by `kubectl neo4j validate`: the spec rules (`ValidateCreate`), the admin password's shape, and `spec.image.tag` against `status.version` with the cluster's rule (`validateVersionMove`). There is no update-only method: without a webhook there is no old object (id 139). The pre-upgrade check and the `UpgradeStarted` event belong to the pass that applies a new image, the one on which the StatefulSet's template does not carry it yet (id 136), read from the API server (`isStandaloneUpgradeRequired` through `apiReader`): read from the cache, the pass after the template write could see the template from before it and announce the upgrade again (#565).
  - **Upgrade timeouts are above zero.** `upgradeTimeout`, `healthCheckTimeout` and `stabilizationTimeout` are read with `time.ParseDuration` and refused at zero or less; the state machine's getters fall back to the default for such a value.
  - **The CLI judges a cluster as the operator does.** `validate` applies `ApplyDefaults` to the decoded copy it validates, and `ApplyDefaults` adds no field an error can then be reported on (no nameless `spec.tls.issuerRef`). The operator applies no defaults to a standalone, so the CLI does not either. Each validator reports every error it finds; `Neo4jShardedDatabase`'s stops when its `clusterRef` cannot be read, and the usage text says so.
- **why:** a review found, and the code confirmed: `handleRollingUpgrade` returned before the state machine for `Recreate` on a Ready cluster, and `Reconcile` returns what it returns, so the "regular reconciliation" it logged never ran. The cluster kept its old image, and no other spec change was applied while the tag differed; under the `OnDelete` strategy `Recreate` also selected, no template change reached a running server. On a standalone the same `OnDelete` kept a new image, a configuration change and a plugin from the pod, while `updateStatus` had already written the new tag to `status.version` from the spec. The cluster's Ready pass wrote `status.version` only when it was empty, so after the escape hatch had moved every server on, it kept the older version, and moving the tag back to it passed the version rule. The standalone controller ran `ValidateCreate` only: `ValidateUpdate` was never called, its image rule allowed everything, and its storage-class rule had no old object to compare (a `className` change on either kind still has no effect: the claim template is not updated). `"0s"` and `"-5m"` parse, and a step's deadline is its start plus the timeout, so every upgrade failed at its first check (Staging checks its deadline before it does anything). The operator applied its defaults before validating and `validate` did not: a cluster naming `authorizationProviders` without `authenticationProviders` had its providers replaced by the operator's defaults and was refused by `validate`. `validate`'s usage text and its page still said "the operator stops validating after certain critical errors", untrue for the cluster since #354. Neo4j (Upgrade and Migration Guide, `/current/`, `version-5/upgrade-minor` and `version-2025-2026` pages, read 2026-10-16) documents two ways to upgrade a cluster, "In-place rolling upgrade: Each server is taken offline and upgraded one at a time" and "New server rolling upgrade", and "you must ensure that the cluster returns to a stable state before moving on to the next"; it describes no upgrade that stops every server at once.
- **pinned-by:** `TestBuildServerStatefulSetForEnterprise_AlwaysRollingUpdate` (`internal/resources/cluster_test.go`); `TestReconcileStatefulSet_RecreateReplacesThePod` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_statefulset_test.go`); `TestClusterValidator_NoEffectWarnings`, `TestStandaloneValidator_NoEffectWarnings` (`internal/validation/noeffect_warnings_test.go`); `TestRolledImageTag`, `TestClusterVersionToRecord`, `TestRecordClusterReady_RecordsTheImageTheEscapeHatchApplied`, `TestRecordClusterReady_LeavesTheVersionWhileARolloutIsInFlight`, `TestStandaloneUpdateStatus_VersionFollowsThePod`, `TestIsStandaloneUpgradeRequired_OnlyUntilTheTemplateCarriesTheImage`, `TestIsStandaloneUpgradeRequired_ReadsTheStatefulSetAsStored` (`internal/controller/upgrade_failed_cycle_test.go`: a cache that still holds the template from before the write) (`internal/controller/running_version_test.go`); the envtest specs "Neo4jEnterpriseCluster with upgradeStrategy Recreate" and "Neo4jEnterpriseStandalone image tag against the version it runs" (`internal/controller/upgrade_running_version_test.go`), which drive a real `Reconcile` against the API server; `TestStandaloneValidator_Validate_ChecksTheTagAgainstStatusVersion`, `TestStandaloneValidator_Validate_RunsTheSpecRulesAndTheAdminPassword` (`internal/validation/standalone_validator_test.go`); `TestValidateUpgradeStrategy` (`internal/validation/upgrade_validator_test.go`);
