`TestUpgradeTimeouts_NotAboveZeroUseTheDefault` (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade_version_test.go`); `TestClusterValidator_ApplyDefaults_LeavesAMissingIssuerRefMissing` (`internal/validation/cluster_validator_test.go`); `TestValidate_JudgesAClusterAfterTheOperatorsDefaults`, `TestValidate_ReportsNoFieldTheUserDidNotWrite`, `TestValidate_Connected_ChecksAStandalonesTagAgainstTheRunningVersion`, `TestValidate_Offline_SaysAStandalonesTagIsCheckedOnlyWhenConnected`, `TestValidate_UsageSaysOneRunListsEveryError`, `TestValidate_RefusesAnUpgradeTimeoutOfZeroOrLess` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate_operator_parity_test.go`). Each fails on the code before the fix, or does not compile against it; the standalone `Recreate` case of `TestStandaloneValidator_NoEffectWarnings` pins that a standalone gets no warning, as before. The cluster's Ready pass is pinned at `recordClusterReady`, the function `Reconcile` calls there: envtest runs no Neo4j, and the formation check before it asks Neo4j for every cluster of two or more servers, which is every cluster the CRD admits.
- **enforcement:** validator (inline in the reconcilers and the CLI) + unit and envtest tests.

### id 145 — A Warning about a condition that lasts is announced when it starts, and a precondition the user can fix is a wait, not a failure
- **scope:** every `Recorder.Event*` with `corev1.EventTypeWarning` in `internal/controller/` whose condition outlives one pass, and the status helper it reads the transition from: `updateClusterStatus` (`neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`: `ServerRoleValidationFailed`, `PropertyShardingValidationFailed`, `StorageClassNotFound`, `TopologyPlacementFailed`; `storage_expansion.go`: `StorageExpansionFailed`, `storageExpansionRetry`); `setFailedStatus` and `standaloneReconcileFailedMessage` (`neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`: `StorageClassNotFound`, `ReconcileFailed`; `standalone_storage_expansion.go`: `standaloneFailedWith`, `standaloneStorageExpansionRetry`); `updateDatabaseStatus` (`neo4jdatabase_controller.go`: `ClusterNotFound`, `SeedCredsMissing`, `CreationFailed`, `DataImportFailed`); `transitionStatus` (`neo4jshardeddatabase_controller.go`: `ClusterNotFound`, `ClusterNotReady`, `ClientCreationFailed`, `SeedBackupResolutionFailed`, `ReconcileFailed`; `neo4jshardeddatabase_pvc_proxy.go`: `ensureClusterSeedConfig`, `SeedCredsMissing`); `setStatus` (`neo4jreplicadatabase_controller.go`: `ReplicaVersionTooOld`, `ConnectionFailed`, `ReplicaObjectStoreEnvMissing`); `updateBackupStatus` (`neo4jbackup_controller.go`: `BackupFailed` for an invalid spec) and `runWarnings` (`BackupShardedDatabasesExcluded`, `BackupShardedDifferential`); `updatePluginStatus` (`plugin_controller.go`: `PluginDuplicate`); `reportRefused` (`plugin_refused.go`: `PluginUnknownToImage`, announced when the write moves the plugin into the refusal, not when the message of a refusal it already carries changes; id 170); the `fail()` helpers, `reportPendingRoles`, `reportUserNotFound`, `reportNameBlocked`, `setNamedConditionChanged` and the gated `ConnectionFailed`, `AuthRuleVersionTooOld`, `OIDCProviderNotConfigured` of `neo4jauthrule_controller.go`, `neo4jrole_controller.go`, `neo4jrolebinding_controller.go`, `neo4juser_controller.go`, `neo4jcompositedatabase_controller.go` and `neo4jdatabasealias_controller.go`; `startPromoting`, `promoteCallFailed`, `fail` and `setStatus` (`neo4jreplicapromotion_controller.go`); `blockScaleDown` and `setScaleDownConditionPersisted` (`internal/controller/scale_down.go`: `ScaleDownBlocked`, from the `ServersPendingDrain` condition); `auraConditionChanged` (`aura_common.go`) and the `fail()`, `setConditionChanged` and `setStatusChanged` of every `aura*_controller.go`; the retried drop in the `Neo4jDatabase` deletion, `handleDeletion` (`neo4jdatabase_controller.go`: `DeletionFailed`), and the retried drop or revoke in the `handleDeletion` of `neo4juser_controller.go` (`UserDeletionFailed`), `neo4jrole_controller.go` (`RoleDeletionFailed`), `neo4jrolebinding_controller.go` (`BindingFailed`), `neo4jauthrule_controller.go` (`AuthRuleFailed`) and `neo4jcompositedatabase_controller.go` (`CompositeDatabaseFailed`); `awaitSeedConfig` and `transitionRestoreStatus` (`neo4jrestore_controller.go`: `SeedEndpointNotProjected`), and the one `RestoreStarted` of `startRestore` (`restoreJobStartedMessage`) in the same file; `finishAllDatabasesRestore` (`neo4jrestore_alldatabases.go`: `RestoreCompleted`, `RestoreFailed`); `refuseUpgradeStart` (`upgrade_start.go`: the `UpgradeFailed` or `UpgradePaused` of an upgrade a check refused to start, announced when the write changed the upgrade record, id 149). The advisories that go through `advisoryWarnings` (`advisory_warnings.go`, `recordScoped`): `TopologyZoneDiscoveryDegraded` (`recordZoneDiscovery`), `RouteAPINotFound`, `MCPApocMissing` (`mcpMissingAPOCWarnings`), the standalone's pre-upgrade `UpgradeFailed` (`preUpgradeHealthCheck`), `ReplicationSourceCaveat` (Warning), `BackupValidateUnsupported` (`warnValidateUnsupported`), the backup's `ServiceAccountAnnotationConflict`, `RestoreFromChainParent` (`warnIfChainParent`), `RestoreShardedDatabasesNotCovered`, `PrivilegesDriftKept` (`revokeUnwanted`). Reasons in `events.go`: `EventReasonSeedCredsMissing`, `EventReasonSeedCredsAutoInherited`, `EventReasonSeedBackupResolutionFailed`, `EventReasonShardedDatabaseDropped`, `EventReasonBackupValidateUnsupported`, `EventReasonReplicaObjectStoreEnvMissing`.
- **rule:**
  - **Generalises id 136 from state events and `ValidationFailed` to every Warning about a lasting condition.** A precondition that is not met, a refusal, a failure the controller retries (on `RequeueAfter` or with error backoff): write the status that records it first, and emit the Warning only when that write changed the phase, the message or the Ready condition's status or reason — from the attempt whose `Status().Update` succeeded, re-read inside `retry.RetryOnConflict`. So the same condition is one event however many passes meet it, a changed message is one more, and a restarted operator emits nothing for a condition already in status. A helper that has no transition bool gets one (the Aura `setStatusChanged` / `setConditionChanged`, the authrule/rolebinding/user `setNamedCondition`, the promotion `setStatus` returning `(changed, entered)`); bookkeeping such as `lastSyncedTime` does not count as a change. Where nothing recorded the condition, record it (an `AuraInstance` whose `deletionProtection` blocks deletion now sets `Synced=False`, reason `DeletionProtected`).
  - **Announce from the write, not from your copy.** The pass's own copy of the object may predate the last pass's write (the informer cache lags), so it may spare a write but never decides an event: the restore's `awaitSeedConfig` announced `SeedEndpointNotProjected` whenever its copy was not yet waiting, and two passes on a copy from before the first one's write both announced the wait, in the same second. It announces when `transitionRestoreStatus`, whose write re-read the restore, changed the phase or message.
  - **A cleanup the deletion retries is a lasting failure too.** A `Neo4jDatabase` whose deletion retries a refused `DROP DATABASE` (until it succeeds or `finalizerDeletionGracePeriod` ends) records the failure — phase `Error`, Ready reason `DeletionFailed`, the message — and announces `DeletionFailed` when that write changed something; the drop is still retried on every pass and the finalizer stays. The deletion that releases its finalizer stays a single event. The `Neo4jUser`, `Neo4jRole`, `Neo4jRoleBinding` and `Neo4jAuthRule` deletions do the same through their `setStatus` — phase `Failed`, Ready reason `UserDeletionFailed`, `RoleDeletionFailed`, `BindingFailed` or `AuthRuleFailed` — and so do a `Neo4jCompositeDatabase`, a `Neo4jDatabaseAlias` and a `Neo4jReplicaDatabase` through theirs. The composite's retried a refused `DROP COMPOSITE DATABASE` for ever and never left `Terminating` (#526); after the grace period it now releases its finalizer with a single Warning, as the others do. A target that is not Ready or cannot be reached is the same lasting failure, announced once with the drop's (id 178).
  - **An event that says something started follows the checks that can refuse it,** and says what the operator does: the restore's `RestoreStarted` is emitted once the existence check, the pre-restore hooks and the stop (or, with `stopCluster: false`, the check that the standalone is not running) have passed and the Job exists; for an offline standalone restore it says why it is offline, and with `stopCluster: false` that the standalone is stopped, since the operator stops nothing.
  - **…once, from the write that starts it:** one `RestoreStarted` per attempt — "Restore job … started", with the offline reason in the same message (`restoreJobStartedMessage`) — emitted when the status write that makes the restore `Running` changed the phase or message. The offline reason used to be an event of its own: with `stopCluster: true` at the stop, so one offline restore emitted two `RestoreStarted` in the same second (2026-10-25 re-walk, #573); with `stopCluster: false` on each pass of a restore that came back through `Pending` (#549). An online restore's one `RestoreStarted` ("Online restore into …") says too that `stopCluster` is ignored, which was a `RestoreStarted` of its own (id 215). "Restore job … started" is announced from that write, not from the pass's copy: a pass that read the restore from before it — with `stopCluster: true` after waiting out the stop's 10s poll — found the Job (`AlreadyExists`, backup-restore.md Rule 46) and announced it a second time (#553, v1.22.0 re-walk).
  - **A retry is not a new attempt:** a pass that retries an attempt the persisted status already records as failed at this generation does not write the optimistic in-progress status first (`storageExpansionRetry`, `standaloneStorageExpansionRetry`, `lastPromoteCallFailed`, as `announceSeeding` does for a replica). Alternating `Expanding` → `Failed` (or `Promoting` → failure) on every pass made each pass two transitions and two events, and each write a watch event that ran the next pass at once.
  - **A warning that stays true while the object is otherwise fine is advisory** (id 102): it goes through `advisoryWarnings.record` — announced when it starts to apply, again every 30 minutes while it does, forgotten when it stops. Pass the full list each pass (an empty list forgets); where two checks share a reason, use `recordScoped` with a scope each, or each check's pass makes the other's warning look new. An operator restart announces such a warning once more; that is accepted.
  - **Warnings about a backup run** (`BackupShardedDatabasesExcluded`, `BackupShardedDifferential`) are collected while the run is built (`runWarnings`) and emitted after the status write commits, only for runs the write kept in `status.history` — not inside the conflict retry, not on a pass that defers the run for its Pod log, not for a run the ten-entry cap trims and the next pass rediscovers.
  - **Waiting is not failing.** A precondition the user can fix, that the controller retries on its own, and that comes before anything is asked of Neo4j, is phase `Pending` with a message naming what is missing — not `Failed` (backup-restore.md Rule 138 for a restore): a `Neo4jDatabase` or `Neo4jShardedDatabase` whose host lacks the seed credentials (or, sharded, the custom S3 endpoint) — `SeedCredsMissing`, as `Pending` with `SeedCredsAutoInherited` while the opted-in host rolls out; a `Neo4jShardedDatabase` whose `clusterRef` names no cluster (`ClusterNotFound`; another read error is retried with backoff and reported nowhere, as for `Neo4jDatabase`) — its validator, which runs first, must not refuse it: `ShardedDatabaseValidator.Validate` reports the missing cluster as `Pending`, never as an error, and runs every spec rule whatever the cluster read does (#532: it returned the read error alone, so with the operator's validator wired in the wait was unreachable and the database `Failed`, and the spec errors already found were dropped); every other kind whose `clusterRef` names no deployment yet (id 182); a backup-mode `Neo4jReplicaDatabase` whose downstream servers have no `AWS_REGION` (reason `ReplicaObjectStoreEnvMissing`, no longer `ReplicaFailed`; id 133). What stays `Failed`: a spec the validator refuses, a target that cannot serve the request as it is (a cluster without property sharding, a replica's downstream older than 2026.08), a missing `seedBackupRef` backup (as for a restore, Rule 43), a StorageClass that does not exist (documented as `Failed`, before anything is created), and anything Neo4j refused or that failed once a database was touched. A `Neo4jDatabase` whose host lacks the seed Secret was phase `Unknown`; it is `Pending`.
  - **A Warning no status write records is remembered per object** (`lastAnnounced`, `internal/controller/event_memory.go`): `AuraFleetManagementPluginPatchFailed` (`fleetPluginPatchFailures` on the cluster and standalone reconcilers, `reconcileAuraFleetManagement`) is announced when the error's text changes, forgotten when the patch goes through — so a failure that comes back is announced again — and when the deployment is deleted. It was emitted on every pass while a StatefulSet lacked its neo4j container or the API server refused the write (#565). It is logged the same way (`logRefusal`, with what `lastAnnounced` said): at info when announced, at V(1) on the passes in between, never as an ERROR — the live walk of the stack (2026-10-25) counted about eight ERROR lines a minute beside the one event (id 164). `reconcileAuraFleetManagement` returns no error for it, so there is no `Reconciler error` line either, and the pass keeps its requeue. An operator restart announces a lasting one once more. Plugin-only Aura Fleet Management — `enabled: true` with neither `tokenSecretRef` nor `provision`, a state the user chose — is logged the same way (`logFleetRegistrationDeferred`, `fleetRegistrationDeferred`, `logAnnounced`, on both reconcilers): "…neither tokenSecretRef nor provision is configured; plugin installed, registration deferred" at info once, at V(1) on the passes after; a token source configured, Fleet Management turned off, or the deployment deleted ends it, so it is said once more if it comes back. It was an info line on every pass (#608; `TestFleetRegistrationDeferred_IsLoggedOnce`, `internal/controller/fleet_enable_test.go`: three passes, one info line and two at V(1), on both kinds; off and on, one more — failing with the line at info restored, and with the forgetting on the off pass taken out).
  - **Leave as they are:** an emitter whose condition is a single event (a Job that failed, a terminal phase the next pass returns on, a deletion that releases its finalizer), the `fleetFailureTracker` and `ConnectivityDegraded` streak, and `RestoreSeedProgress`, which repeats on purpose — the restore's progress summary every 10 minutes while it seeds, saying how long the seed has stood still, from a source of its own (id 166).
- **why:** every event spends the object's event budget — client-go's spam filter allows a burst of 25 per object, then one every five minutes — and a Warning repeated on every pass crowds out the events that report real changes. After id 136, the #517 author found the rest: the cluster's `ServerRoleValidationFailed`, `PropertyShardingValidationFailed` and `StorageClassNotFound`, the standalone's `StorageClassNotFound`, the database's `ClusterNotFound` and `SeedCredsMissing`, and five sharded Warnings, each on every `RequeueAfter` or backoff retry; an audit of all 158 Warning call sites found the same in the auth, composite, alias, promotion and Aura `fail()` paths (the invite and member ones about twice every 30s, their `lastSyncedTime` write waking the next pass — that write is now made only on a change or a refresh, id 156), the role's `PrivilegesDriftKept` (one per kept row, every pass), `RolePending` (emitted before the status write), and advisories on every pass (`MCPApocMissing`, `RouteAPINotFound`, zone discovery, a backup CronJob's `BackupValidateUnsupported` because `CreateOrUpdate` reports `Updated` whenever the API server's defaults differ, a restore's chain-parent and sharded advisories on every pass of its seed-credentials wait). A refused cluster storage expansion flipped `Expanding`/`Failed` in a tight loop. And the sharded database and the replica reported `Failed` while they retried a precondition the user could fix — the restore had moved to `Pending` for the same thing (#512, Rule 138). Raw reason strings (`"SeedCredsMissing"`, `"SeedCredsAutoInherited"`, `"SeedBackupResolutionFailed"`, `"ShardedDatabaseDropped"`, `"BackupValidateUnsupported"`, `"UpgradeDeferred"`) became constants with the same text. The v1.22.0 journey found `DeletionFailed` about ten times a minute on a `Neo4jDatabase` during teardown — every `RequeueAfter` and every status change of its host, which a teardown makes often — `SeedEndpointNotProjected` twice in one second, and an offline point-in-time restore into a running 5.26 standalone with `stopCluster: false` that announced "Restoring offline with neo4j-admin, which stops standalone" and was then refused. Once that fixed `Neo4jDatabase`, the user, role, rolebinding and authrule deletions still emitted their "will retry" Warning on every retry (#549). The cluster's `ScaleDownBlocked` was gated on the pass's own copy of the `ServersPendingDrain` condition, so two passes on a copy from before the first one's write announced the same block twice (#565); it is announced from the condition write, once per cause (its message).
- **pinned-by:** `TestClusterReconcile_PreconditionFailuresAreAnnouncedOnce`, `TestClusterStorageExpansion_ARefusedExpansionIsAnnouncedOnce`, `TestClusterStorageExpansion_AShrinkIsAnnouncedOnce`, `TestAdvisoryEmitters_AnnounceOnceWhileTheyApply`, `TestStandaloneReconcile_PreconditionFailuresAreAnnouncedOnce`, `TestDatabaseReconcile_WaitsAreAnnouncedOnce`, `TestShardedReconcile_LastingFailuresAreAnnouncedOnce`, `TestShardedReconcile_AMissingClusterIsAWaitThatEnds`, `TestShardedReconcile_AMissingClusterIsAWaitWithTheOperatorsValidator` and `TestShardedReconcile_ASpecErrorIsReportedWhileTheClusterIsMissing` (`internal/controller/neo4jshardeddatabase_validator_wait_test.go`, with the operator's validator; both fail with the earlier validator), `TestShardedDatabaseValidator_ReportsEverySpecErrorWhateverTheClusterRead`, `TestShardedDatabaseValidator_AMissingClusterIsNotAnError` (`internal/validation/shardeddatabase_validator_test.go`), `TestValidate_Connected_ShardedDatabaseReportsEveryErrorWithoutItsCluster` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate_sharded_test.go`), `TestShardedReconcile_MissingSeedCredentialsAreAWait`, `TestReplicaReconcile_MissingRegionIsAWait`, `TestReplicaReconcile_LastingFailuresAreAnnouncedOnce`, `TestBackupReconcile_LastingWarningsAreAnnouncedOnce`, `TestRunWarnings_EmitOnlyForRecordedRuns`, `TestRestoreReconcile_AdvisoriesWhileWaitingAreAnnouncedOnce`, `TestPluginReconcile_DuplicateIsAnnouncedOnce` (`internal/controller/repeated_warnings_test.go`); `TestReconcile_PersistentWarningIsAnnouncedOnce`, `TestFail_AnnouncesTheSameFailureOnce`, `TestReportPendingRoles_AnnouncesTheWaitOnce`, `TestReportUserNotFound_AnnouncesTheWaitOnce`, `TestReportNameBlocked_AnnouncesOnce`, `TestRevokeUnwanted_KeptDriftIsAnnouncedOnce`, `TestPromotion_StartAndFailureAreAnnouncedOnce` (`internal/controller/auth_repeated_warnings_test.go`); `TestAuraReconcile_FailureWarningIsAnnouncedOnce` (`internal/controller/aura_repeated_warnings_test.go`); `TestFleetPluginPatchFailed_IsAnnouncedOncePerCause` (`internal/controller/aura_fleet_followups_test.go`: three passes, one event, for both kinds; one more once it comes back after a patch went through); `TestFleetPluginPatchFailed_IsLoggedOnceNotAsAnError` (same file: three passes, no ERROR, one info line and two at V(1), no reconcile error); the `Pending` case of `TestEnsureClusterSeedConfig` (`internal/controller/neo4jshardeddatabase_seed_pvc_diff_test.go`); `TestDatabaseDeletion_ARetriedDropIsAnnouncedOnce` (`internal/controller/repeated_warnings_test.go`: three retries, one event, the drop retried each time and the finalizer kept; a new failure, one more; a restarted reconciler, none); `TestAuthDeletion_ARetriedDropIsAnnouncedOnce` (`internal/controller/auth_deletion_retry_test.go`: the same, one subtest per auth kind, against a scripted server behind each controller's client seam) and `TestCompositeDeletion_ARefusedDropReleasesTheFinalizerAfterTheGracePeriod` (same file: kept and announced once within the grace period, released after it); `TestAwaitSeedConfig_TwoPassesOnAStaleCopyAnnounceOnce` (`internal/controller/neo4jrestore_seed_config_wait_test.go`: two calls on a copy from before the first write, one event); `TestStartRestore_OfflineRestoreIsAnnouncedOnlyOnceItsChecksPass` (`internal/controller/neo4jrestore_online_standalone_test.go`: a refused restore emits no `RestoreStarted`); `TestStartRestore_APassOnAStaleCopyDoesNotAnnounceTheJobAgain` (a second pass on the restore as it was before the first one's write: no event) and `TestStartRestore_WithoutStopClusterARetryThroughPendingAnnouncesOnce` (`stopCluster: false`, the previous Job still terminating: nothing on the `Pending` pass, one event naming the Job and the offline reason when it starts; it waits out `createRestoreJob`'s 10s poll) (`internal/controller/restore_sharded_announce_once_test.go`); `TestStartRestore_AnOfflineRestoreWithStopClusterIsAnnouncedOnce` and `TestRestoreJobStartedMessage` (`internal/controller/restore_job_attempt_test.go`: `stopCluster: true`, one `RestoreStarted` naming the Job, the stop and the reason; fails with the reason announced at the stop, as on the re-walk; waits out the stop's 10s poll). Each fails with the earlier emission restored. Most run several passes through `Reconcile` and a restarted reconciler; each fails with its gate switched off, and the phase cases fail with `Failed` (or `Unknown`) restored. `TestRunWarnings_EmitOnlyForRecordedRuns` pins the helper only: the fake clientset cannot script the backup Pod log the run paths parse. `RouteAPINotFound` is pinned by `TestReconcileRoute_NoRouteAPI` (`internal/controller/unstructured_content_test.go`) and, through whole passes against an API server without the Route API, `TestReconcile_RoutesWithoutTheRouteAPI_APIServer` (`internal/controller/route_apply_test.go`); `reconcileRoute` could not be driven in a test until the Route builder held JSON types only (id 148). `TestBlockScaleDown_IsAnnouncedOncePerCause` (`internal/controller/scale_down_blocked_test.go`: the same block from a stale copy twice, one event; another cause, one more; a restarted reconciler, none — two events with the in-memory gate restored).
- **enforcement:** unit test. There is no machine check that a NEW Warning follows the rule — convention + code review: an emitter that runs on a pass which requeues needs a status transition or `advisoryWarnings`.

### id 164 — A routine transient is neither a Warning nor an ERROR
- **scope:** `statementTransient`, `transientStreaks`, `transientRetry` (`internal/controller/transient_retry.go`) and the `fail()` of `internal/controller/neo4juser_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jrole_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jrolebinding_controller.go` and `internal/controller/neo4jauthrule_controller.go`; `reconcileAuraFleetManagement` of `internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go` and `internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go` (a server StatefulSet that is not there yet); `logDiagnosticsQueryFailure` (`internal/controller/diagnostics_users_roles.go`), used by `CollectDiagnostics`, `collectStandaloneDiagnostics` and `collectUsersAndRoles`; `IsTransientError` and `IsConnectivityError` (`internal/neo4j/error_classify.go`), `isTransientNeo4jError` (`internal/neo4j/client.go`).
- **rule:** What every deployment passes through on its way up, or during a routine restart, is not reported as a failure. `AuraFleetManagementPluginPatchFailed` is not raised for a server StatefulSet that does not exist yet — a new cluster merges the plugin before it writes its StatefulSet, and a new standalone reads the one it just created from a cache that has not seen it — since the write that creates the StatefulSet already lists the plugin (id 175); anything else the patch meets (a StatefulSet without a `neo4j` container, a conflict that outlasts its retries) is still a Warning. A diagnostics query (SHOW SERVERS, SHOW DATABASES, SHOW USERS, SHOW ROLES) that fails transiently (`IsTransientError`) is logged at info: a server that could not be reached (`IsConnectivityError`: a driver `ConnectivityError` such as EOF, refused, reset, a timeout, no routing table), or Neo4j answering "not now" with an error the driver itself retries — a `Neo.TransientError.*` (a database unavailable while it restarts or fails over, replication under way), `Neo.ClientError.Cluster.NotALeader`, `Neo.ClientError.General.ForbiddenOnReadOnlyDatabase`, a 2025+ "conflicting transaction state" — read with `errors.As`, so a wrapped one counts. Any other failure stays an error: a syntax error, a missing privilege, an unknown database, and `Neo.TransientError.Transaction.Terminated`, which the driver reclassifies as a client error. Either way it is in `status.diagnostics.collectionError`, and the cluster's status describes the restart (id 105). A user, role, binding or auth rule whose target reads Ready but refuses the connection follows the same principle: id 172. So does a statement Neo4j refuses with an error it asks the client to retry (`statementTransient`: `IsTransientError` — `Neo.TransientError.*` such as `Neo.TransientError.Transaction.DeadlockDetected`, `NotALeader`, a conflicting transaction state — and not the target being down, which has its own wait): the `fail()` of `Neo4jUser`, `Neo4jRole`, `Neo4jRoleBinding` and `Neo4jAuthRule` retries it after `transientRetry` (5s), logged at info, with nothing on status and no event, while the object's passes have met such errors for no longer than `targetUnreachableGrace` (`transientStreaks`, in memory, per object; a pass two minutes clear of one ends the streak). Past that it is the object's failure, reported as any other. It went `Failed` with a `UserSyncFailed` (`RoleSyncFailed`, `BindingFailed`, `AuthRuleFailed`) Warning and back to controller-runtime as an error — logged at ERROR, retried on backoff — before the retry succeeded. A deletion's cleanup does the same within its own grace period (id 178). A cluster's or standalone's deletion whose finalizer release finds the object gone — a pass that read it before an earlier pass released the finalizer — is done, logged at V(1): it logged an ERROR and returned the error, a second ERROR ("Reconciler error"), on every cluster deletion (#562 re-walk; `handleDeletion` in `internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go` and `internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`). Every kind's release now does the same, in `removeFinalizer` (id 189).
- **why:** #531: every new cluster's first pass raised `AuraFleetManagementPluginPatchFailed` ("get StatefulSet …: not found"), which `kubectl neo4j diagnose` then reported on a healthy cluster. #549: deleting a server pod (v1.22.0 journey, Phase 2) logged one `ERROR Failed to collect SHOW SERVERS … ConnectivityError: EOF`, while the verification matrix says a routine restart logs no errors. Classifying only the connection failures, the walk of the v1.22.x fixes still logged `Failed to collect SHOW …` at ERROR during server restarts and a lost majority, 25 lines in one second, and once for SHOW USERS during an upgrade: Neo4j's own transient codes.
- **pinned-by:** `TestHandleDeletion_AnObjectAlreadyGoneIsNotAnError` (`internal/controller/walk_noise_test.go`: the cluster's and the standalone's deletion of an object the API server has no more, no error returned or logged); `TestUserReconcile_ATransientOnAlterIsRetriedQuietly` and `TestAuthFail_ATransientIsQuietUntilItLasts` (`internal/controller/deletion_transient_test.go`: a deadlock on ALTER USER through `Reconcile`, no error, no Warning, `Ready` when the retry succeeds; each auth kind's `fail()` quiet on a fresh streak and the failure past the grace — the quiet cases fail with `transientStreaks.quiet` switched off); `TestReconcileAuraFleetManagement_NoStatefulSetYetIsNotAFailure` (`internal/controller/cluster_events_once_test.go`: no StatefulSet, no Warning, cluster and standalone; the plugin added once it exists; a StatefulSet without a `neo4j` container, one Warning); `TestCollectDiagnostics_AServerThatHangsUpIsNotAnError` (`internal/controller/diagnostics_log_level_test.go`: the whole diagnostics pass against a listener that hangs up on every connection, no error logged, the failure in `collectionError`) and `TestLogDiagnosticsQueryFailure` (same file: Neo4j's transient and cluster codes, wrapped as the client wraps them, at info; a missing privilege and an unknown database at error); `TestIsTransientError` (`internal/neo4j/error_classify_test.go`). Each fails with the earlier code restored.
- **enforcement:** unit test. There is no machine check that a NEW emitter or log line treats a routine transient as a failure — convention + code review.

### id 175 — A plugin the deployment's own spec installs is in the write that creates its StatefulSet
- **scope:** `addFleetManagementPlugin`, `clusterInstallsFleetManagement`, `standaloneInstallsFleetManagement` (`internal/controller/fleet_plugin.go`); the create branch of `createOrUpdateResourceInternal` and `mergeFleetManagementPlugin` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`); the template apply of `reconcileStatefulSet` and `mergeFleetManagementPlugin` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`); `MergeNeo4jPluginList` (`internal/controller/plugin_controller.go`); `setFleetEntryRecord` (`internal/controller/fleet_disable.go`).
- **rule:** With `spec.auraFleetManagement.enabled`, the write that creates the cluster's server StatefulSet, or the standalone's StatefulSet, already lists `fleet-management` in the neo4j container's `NEO4J_PLUGINS`. It is merged with `MergeNeo4jPluginList` into what is written, after the record of the env the deployment owns and its template hash are taken from the rendered template, so `NEO4J_PLUGINS` stays out of the static template (the builders never render it) and out of that record (the plugin, fleet and Aura writers share the variable; id 155). The fleet merge of every pass then finds it listed and writes nothing. A StatefulSet created again (a storage expansion orphan-deletes it) carries it too. Enabling Fleet Management on a running deployment still restarts its servers once, through the write that rolls them for the enable's conf change — a cluster's restart stamp, a standalone's StatefulSet write with its new conf hash — which carries the name; the merge waits for it (id 226); a template change the deployment applies later keeps the entry as a foreign one. The create and the merge record the name as Fleet Management's in the write that adds it (`neo4j.com/aura-fleet-management-entries`, StatefulSet metadata), so turning the feature off takes it out again (id 222). Never on an image whose entrypoint does not list `fleet-management`: the validator refuses the spec before anything is written (id 191). Not on an image with Fleet Manager built in (2026.03 and later, id 192): `addFleetManagementPlugin` adds nothing there, judged by the template's own image, and never removes a name an earlier write put there. A `NEO4J_PLUGINS` the deployment's own `spec.env` sets is the deployment's: a cluster's carries the name as the cluster renders it, from the create on, and a standalone's gets it in its own template write; one from a Secret or ConfigMap, or not a JSON list, gets nothing, with a `ValidationWarning`, and no longer fails the write (id 223). A cluster's template apply that stops owning the variable keeps the name in it, in the same write.
- **why:** the walk of the v1.22.x fixes: a new cluster with `spec.auraFleetManagement.enabled: true` had its StatefulSet created without `fleet-management`, then patched a second later by the next pass's merge (after #531 the first pass no longer warned that the StatefulSet was missing), so the servers restarted during their first formation. A new standalone did the same: its merge runs after the StatefulSet write in the same pass, reads the cache that has not seen it, and the next pass's merge restarted the new pod.
- **pinned-by:** `TestClusterReconcile_CreatesTheServersWithTheFleetPlugin` (the first pass creates the StatefulSet once, with the plugin, not in the cluster's owned-env record; three more passes write no StatefulSet; a later env change keeps the plugin), `TestStandaloneReconcile_CreatesThePodWithTheFleetPlugin`, `TestAddFleetManagementPlugin` (`internal/controller/fleet_plugin_test.go`). The two reconcile tests fail with the create-time merge switched off. The record in the create: `TestClusterReconcile_DisablingFleetManagementTakesItsEntryOut`, `TestStandaloneReconcile_DisablingFleetManagementTakesItsEntryOut` (`internal/controller/fleet_disable_test.go`, id 222).
- **enforcement:** unit test; not walked live yet.

### id 182 — A reference to a deployment that does not exist yet is a wait on every kind, never a validation error
- **scope:** `LookupDeployment`, `DeploymentPending`, `DeploymentUnreadable` (`internal/validation/deployment_ref.go`); the clusterRef checks of `DatabaseValidator.Validate` (`database_validator.go`), `UserValidator.validateClusterRef`, `RoleValidator.validateClusterRef`, `RoleBindingValidator.validateClusterRefAndUserOverlap`, `AuthRuleValidator.validateClusterRef` and their results' `TargetPending`; `ShardedDatabaseValidator.Validate`'s `Pending` (id 145); the `!target.Found` wait of the user, role, rolebinding, authrule, composite database, alias and replica reconcilers and `resolveDatabaseHost`'s of the database reconciler; `withTargetPending` and `deploymentRefFindings` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate.go`); the `else` branch of every self-managed kind in `docs/gitops/argocd-health-checks.yaml`.
- **rule:**
  - **Validators:** a `clusterRef` (a backup's `instanceRef`) that names no `Neo4jEnterpriseCluster` or `Neo4jEnterpriseStandalone` is reported as pending (`TargetPending`, `DeploymentPending`), never as an error, and every rule of the spec still runs: the rules that need the deployment are skipped, the others are not (the database validator no longer returns at a failed lookup; a topology is held to non-negative counts until the deployment says cluster or standalone). A read that fails otherwise is a warning that those rules were not checked. An empty `clusterRef` is still `Required`.
  - **Reconcilers:** a deployment that does not exist is phase `Pending` (`Waiting` for a backup), Ready reason `ClusterNotFound` (a backup's and a restore's Ready condition follows their phase, id 198), with one `ClusterNotFound` Warning from the status write that enters the wait (id 145; for a dependent whose target went away rather than never existed, only once it has outlasted the target by a minute, id 216) — before any other wait: a `Neo4jUser` whose password Secret is missing too reports the deployment first, and `SecretPending` once the deployment exists (it reported `SecretPending` and began the `ClusterNotFound` wait only once the Secret was there; v1.22.x walk); the reconcile requeues, and the cluster's or standalone's creation enqueues its dependents (`EnqueueDependentsForClusterChange`), so the object carries on by itself. A spec the validator refuses is still `ValidationFailed` (database) or `Failed`, with its own errors and nothing about the cluster.
  - **CLI:** `kubectl neo4j validate --connect` reports the missing deployment as pending on every kind that names one — the five validators' `TargetPending`, the sharded database's `Pending`, and for the alias, composite, replica, backup and plugin `deploymentRefFindings`, which resolves the reference as their reconcilers do. Offline it is not checked, and the summary says so.
  - **Argo CD:** `Pending` and `Waiting` fall to each self-managed kind's `Progressing` branch, so an Application that applies a database or a user with its cluster syncs and waits.
- **why:** #566. The database, user, role, rolebinding and authrule validators refused a missing `clusterRef` (`spec.clusterRef: Not found`) before their reconcilers' documented wait, so the wait was unreachable with the operator's validators wired in. Applying one of them with its cluster in one `kubectl apply -f dir/` or one GitOps sync — the common case — made it `ValidationFailed` (database) or `Failed` with a `ValidationFailed` Warning, and `Degraded` to Argo CD, until a later pass (the requeue, or the cluster's creation enqueuing it) found the cluster and went on: it recovered, but announced a refusal that was not one. The reconcilers' waits were pinned only by tests that ran without a validator. The composite, alias and replica reached their wait but announced nothing, and `validate --connect` said nothing about their deployment; the backup's and plugin's CLI side neither.
- **pinned-by:** `TestReconcile_AMissingClusterIsAWaitThatEnds` (`internal/controller/missing_cluster_wait_test.go`: each of the eight kinds with the validator the operator wires in — `Pending`, one `ClusterNotFound` over three passes, no `ValidationFailed`, nothing from a restarted operator, past the wait once the standalone is created; every kind fails on the earlier code), `TestReconcile_ASpecErrorIsReportedWhileTheClusterIsMissing` (same file; the five validator kinds fail on the earlier code, whose message carried `spec.clusterRef`); `TestUserReconcile_AMissingClusterIsReportedBeforeAMissingSecret` (same file: neither the Secret nor the cluster, then the cluster, then both; fails with the Secret wait reported first); `TestValidators_AMissingDeploymentIsPendingBesideTheSpecErrors`, `TestDatabaseValidator_ChecksTheSpecWithoutItsHost` (`internal/validation/deployment_ref_test.go`); `TestValidate_Connected_AMissingDeploymentIsPendingOnEveryKind` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate_missing_deployment_test.go`: ten kinds, every one fails on the earlier code); `TestArgoCDHealthChecks_AWaitIsProgressing` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/argocd_health_wait_test.go`, a guard: the health checks already sent both phases to `Progressing`).
- **enforcement:** unit test. `Neo4jPlugin`'s reconciler, which reported a missing target `Failed` (id 177), was fixed with the plugin work (#573); `Neo4jBackup` (phase `Waiting`) and `Neo4jRestore` (phase `Pending`) announce the wait as the others do since round 8 (id 198).

### id 197 — A refusal or a wait of a database kind is logged once per cause at info, never as an ERROR on every requeue
- **scope:** `logRefusal` (`internal/controller/refusal_log.go`); in `Reconcile` of `internal/controller/neo4jdatabase_controller.go`, the validation failure (`Database validation failed`), its validation warnings, and the wait for the host's seed credentials (`Host missing seed credentials projection`); in `Reconcile` of `internal/controller/neo4jshardeddatabase_controller.go`, the validation failure, a cluster without property sharding, and a `seedBackupRef` that cannot be resolved.
- **rule:** id 136's "…and so is its log line", for the database kinds: a refused spec, a capability the target lacks, a wait the user can fix — every condition the controller meets again on each `RequeueAfter` and announces once through its status write — is logged by `logRefusal` from that write's bool: at info on the pass that announces it (it starts, or its message changes), at V(1) on the passes in between, never as an ERROR. The validation warnings of a `Neo4jDatabase`, which `advisoryWarnings` announces, are logged at V(1). A failure the controller returns as an error (a create Neo4j refused, a connection that fails) stays an ERROR: controller-runtime logs it and backs off. The composite, alias and replica reconcilers log no refusal at all.
- **why:** "Database validation failed" was logged at ERROR before the status write on every requeue — 60 lines in ten minutes on the round-7 walk — while its `ValidationFailed` event fired once. The sharded database did the same for its validation failure, a cluster without property sharding and an unresolvable `seedBackupRef`, and the database for a host without the seed credentials Secret, a wait it announces once.
- **pinned-by:** `TestReconcile_ARefusedSpecIsLoggedOnceNotAsAnError` (now with `Neo4jDatabase` and `Neo4jShardedDatabase`) and `TestReconcile_ADatabaseKindsLastingRefusalIsLoggedOnceNotAsAnError` (`internal/controller/refusal_log_test.go`: three passes through `Reconcile` into a level-capturing logger — no ERROR, one info line, two at V(1)); each fails with the earlier logging restored.
- **enforcement:** unit test. No machine check that a NEW refusal is logged this way — convention + code review: a condition that requeues and is announced once is logged from the same bool.

### id 198 — A backup or restore whose target does not exist yet announces the wait once; an ended restore is not reopened by its target going away
- **scope:** the `errors.IsNotFound` branch of `getTargetCluster` in `Reconcile` (`internal/controller/neo4jbackup_controller.go`) and of `getClusterRef` in `Reconcile` (`internal/controller/neo4jrestore_controller.go`), with the status writers they announce from (`updateBackupStatus`, `transitionRestoreStatus`); the terminal guard of the restore's `Reconcile` (`restoreEndedAtGeneration`, `internal/controller/restore_outcome.go`; quiet once ended, id 215); `EventReasonClusterNotFound` (`events.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **Id 182's wait, announced:** a `Neo4jBackup` or `Neo4jRestore` whose `spec.instanceRef` names no cluster or standalone emits one `ClusterNotFound` Warning ("Waiting for target to appear: …") from the status write that enters the wait, and its log line at info then, at V(1) after (id 197); the passes in between, an operator restart and a resync announce nothing. It carries on by itself once the target exists.
  - **Phases, decided:** the restore waits in `Pending`, as every other kind. The backup keeps `Waiting`: it is the phase its API reference documents for a missing target and for one not accepting work, `kubectl neo4j status` and the Argo CD check read it as in progress, and alerts and scripts written against it would break on `Pending`, which a backup already uses for a different wait (a `chainFromBackup` parent, a running Job in the chain). The Ready condition follows the phase as before.
  - **An ended restore stays ended:** `Reconcile` returns on a restore `Completed` or `Failed` at its generation before it looks the target up, as a one-time backup does (#116). Checked after the lookup, a target deleted after the restore wrote `Pending` over `Completed` or `Failed`, and the target created again under the same name ran the restore a second time — for a Job restore into a standalone, over the new standalone's data.
- **why:** round 8: every kind that names a deployment announces a missing one (id 182) except these two, which waited in silence — a backup or restore applied before its cluster said nothing about why it was not running. The re-run was found reading the same branch.
- **pinned-by:** `TestBackupReconcile_AMissingTargetIsAnnouncedOnce`, `TestRestoreReconcile_AMissingTargetIsAnnouncedOnce` (one event over several passes, none from a restarted reconciler; the backup `Waiting`, the restore `Pending`) and `TestRestoreReconcile_AnEndedRestoreIsNotReopenedByAMissingTarget` (`Completed` and `Failed` stay so) (`internal/controller/backup_restore_target_wait_test.go`); each fails on the earlier code.
- **enforcement:** unit test.

### id 206 — An all-databases restore waits out a target that cannot serve it for a moment, and resumes where it was; its start is announced once per attempt
- **scope:** `allDatabasesTargetAway`, `awaitAllDatabasesTarget`, `allDatabasesTargetAnswered`, the connection and `DatabaseExists` steps of `startAllDatabasesRestore`, and the start announcement of `markDatabaseResult` (`internal/controller/neo4jrestore_alldatabases.go`); `targetUnreachable`, `targetUnreachableGrace` and `targetAnswered` (`internal/controller/target_wait.go`); `IsTransientError` (`internal/neo4j/error_classify.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **A wait, as ids 172 and 183 have it:** a connection to the restore's target, or its `DatabaseExists` check, that fails because the target cannot serve it now — unreachable (`targetUnreachable`: refused, reset, a timeout, `Neo.TransientError.General.DatabaseUnavailable`) or a Neo4j error the driver would retry (`IsTransientError`) — keeps the restore's phase and its `status.databaseResults`, records `ClusterNotReady=True` (reason `ClusterUnreachable`; its start is the condition's `lastTransitionTime`), is logged at info, and requeues with no error and no event. The next pass that reaches the target writes `ClusterNotReady=False` and goes on from the database it was at.
  - **Bounded, reported if it lasts:** past `targetUnreachableGrace` (5 minutes) the condition's message says so and one `ConnectionFailed` Warning is announced, from the write that changed it; the restore goes on waiting rather than failing — the seeds run on the servers, and a failed restore would have to be started again. Any other connection failure is as before: an ERROR, and `Pending` with the error.
  - **One start per attempt:** `RestoreStarted` is announced by the write that moves the restore into `Running` only while no database of the attempt has left `Pending`. A wait that writes `Pending` mid-way (the seed proxy restarting, a non-transient check failure) no longer makes the next write a second start.
- **why:** round-8 walk: an all-databases restore with `replaceExisting` recreated `neo4j`, the database the driver's connectivity check uses, and the next pass's connection failed with `DatabaseUnavailable`. It was logged as ERROR "Failed to connect for all-databases restore" twice, the restore went back to `Pending`, and the next write that made it `Running` announced `RestoreStarted` again (count 2). Every database still completed. Pre-existing since the all-databases loop gained its connection check (#558).
- **pinned-by:** `TestAllDatabasesRestore_ATargetBrieflyUnavailableIsAWait` (three passes against a target that answers `DatabaseUnavailable` after the recreate: `Running` throughout, `ClusterNotReady=True`, then `Completed` with one `RestoreStarted`, no `ConnectionFailed`, no ERROR), `TestAllDatabasesRestore_ATargetAwayForLongIsReportedOnce` (past the grace: one `ConnectionFailed`, still `Running`, then `Completed`) and `TestMarkDatabaseResult_AnnouncesTheStartOnlyBeforeAnyDatabaseStarted` (`internal/controller/restore_alldatabases_target_wait_test.go`); each fails on the earlier code.
- **enforcement:** unit test.

### id 215 — A restore says how it ended: a refused spec is a status logged once at info, a failed Job says why (a backup's too), an online restore starts once, its Ready condition carries a restore's reasons, and once it has ended it is quiet
- **scope:** `refuseRestore`, `restoreRefusal`, `isRestoreRefusal`, `apiReadFailure`, `restoreReadyCondition`, `restoreJobFailure`, `restoreEndedAtGeneration` (`internal/controller/restore_outcome.go`); the terminal guard of `Reconcile` and its `spec.instanceRef` refusal after it (`internal/controller/neo4jrestore_controller.go`); `jobFailureMessage`, `jobPodFailure`, `jobCondition`, `messageTail`, `backupContainerName` (`internal/controller/job_failure.go`); in `internal/controller/neo4jbackup_controller.go` the `JobFailed` branch of `handleExistingBackupJob`, the failed run's `error` in `recordOneShotBackupRun` and `reconcileScheduledHistory`, and the backup container's `TerminationMessagePolicy` in `createBackupJob` and `createBackupCronJob`; in `internal/controller/neo4jrestore_controller.go` the Neo4j version check of `Reconcile`, the validation, database-existence and running-standalone checks of `startRestore` (`checkDatabaseExists`, `refuseRestoreIfPodsRunning`), the spec refusals and the existing-database refusal of `startClusterCypherRestore`, `onlineRestoreStartedMessage`, the `JobFailed` branch of `checkRestoreProgress`, the restore container's `TerminationMessagePolicy` in `createRestoreJob`, and `transitionRestoreStatus`; `onlineRestoreClient` and `onlineClient` (`internal/controller/restore_target.go`, the seam tests script); `ConditionReasonRestoreComplete`, `ConditionReasonRestoreFailed`, `ConditionReasonRestoreRunning` (`internal/controller/conditions.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **A refused spec is a status, not a controller error** (id 136's "…and so is its log line", for the restore): a restore the operator refuses as it is — `validateRestore` (`database: system`, a missing `backupRef`, …), a Neo4j version it does not support, an offline restore into a running standalone without `stopCluster` (`refuseRestoreIfPodsRunning`, a `restoreRefusal`), a database that exists without `replaceExisting` (`checkDatabaseExists` and the online path), a `source.type: storage` shape the online path cannot seed — is `Failed` with the reason in `status.message`, logged by `logRefusal` from the status write (info when it records the refusal, V(1) after), and the pass returns no error. A read that fails on the way (a pod list, a Bolt connection, an API read inside the validation, `apiReadFailure`) stays an ERROR. The Failed restore is terminal at its generation, so the returned error only added "Reconciler error" and a retry that did nothing. Messages and phases are as before.
  - **A failed restore Job says why:** `status.message` and the `RestoreFailed` event read "Restore job <name> failed (<the Job's Failed reason>: <message>): neo4j-restore exited with code <n>: <tail>" — the Job's condition, and the restore container of the Job's most recently failed pod (one the Job controls, so a pod of an earlier attempt's Job of the same name is never read): its exit code and the last five non-empty lines of its termination message, at most 600 characters. The container's `TerminationMessagePolicy` is `FallbackToLogsOnError`, so the termination message is the end of its log when it writes none. With no pod left, the Job's condition alone. **A backup Job too** (`jobFailureMessage`, shared): a one-time backup's `status.message` and `BackupFailed` event read "Backup job <name> failed (…): backup exited with code <n>: <tail>", and every failed run in `status.history` — one-time and scheduled (a CronJob child) alike — carries the same text in its `error`, which was never set. The backup container (in the Job and in the CronJob's template) is `FallbackToLogsOnError` too; a scheduled backup's CronJob template gains the field on the operator's next pass, which runs nothing.
  - **An online restore announces its start once:** its one `RestoreStarted`, "Online restore into …", carries "spec.stopCluster is ignored …" for a standalone restored online with `stopCluster: true`. Decided against an advisory `ValidationWarning` (id 102): the field is read — it stops a standalone for the offline cases — and whether it applies is known only once the restore has picked its path, which the start already reports.
  - **A restore's Ready condition has a restore's reasons** (`restoreReadyCondition`): `Completed` → `True`/`RestoreCompleted`, `Failed` → `False`/`RestoreFailed`, `Running` → `Unknown`/`RestoreRunning`, `Pending` → `Unknown`/`Pending`. `transitionRestoreStatus` took them from `PhaseToConditionStatus`, which gives a completed restore the backup's `BackupSucceeded` and a failed one `ReconciliationFailed`. The `ClusterNotReady` condition of an all-databases restore (id 206) is unchanged.
  - **Once it has ended, it is quiet:** a restore `Completed` or `Failed` at its generation (`restoreEndedAtGeneration`) is still reconciled — by every resync (every 30 seconds in dev mode, `configureDevelopmentCache` in `cmd/main.go`; in production the controller-runtime default of ten hours, five minutes with `--cache-strategy=lazy`), an event of its Job (its five-minute TTL removing it) and an operator restart — and that pass returns at the guard (id 198) with no requeue and no write, announces nothing about how it ended, and logs "Restore already completed" / "Restore previously failed; not retrying …" at V(1). The write that ended it already said how: its status, its `RestoreCompleted` or `RestoreFailed`, a refusal's one line at info. A restore without `spec.instanceRef` is refused through `refuseRestore` after that guard, so it is written and logged at info once; before, its check came first and every pass logged "Invalid Neo4jRestore spec" at info and wrote its status again. A new generation runs the restore again, as before. The restore Job's `ttlSecondsAfterFinished` is Kubernetes' to act on: no requeue of the restore removes it.
- **why:** #594, the v1.23.0 pre-release journey on dd429120: a refused restore (the pod-identity offline case into a running standalone, `database: system`) logged an ERROR and a "Reconciler error" each; an offline restore whose Job failed read "Restore job failed" and nothing else, so the reason was in a pod the Job's five-minute TTL removes — and so did a failed backup Job ("Backup job failed"), whose history entry had no `error` at all; an online restore into a standalone with `stopCluster: true` emitted two `RestoreStarted`, the notice under the same reason; and a completed restore's `Ready` read `BackupSucceeded`, copied from the backup's mapping. #606, the walk of those fixes on d35c5aa9 (2026-10-27, dev mode): the refused restore's refusal was logged once, then "Restore previously failed; not retrying until spec changes or resource is recreated" at info about every 30 seconds — not a requeue and not a status write, the dev cache's 30-second resync running the terminal pass, which logged at info.
- **pinned-by:** `TestRestoreReconcile_ARefusedSpecIsLoggedOnceNotAsAnError` (three passes through `Reconcile` into a level-capturing logger for each refusal: no error returned, no ERROR, one info line, `Ready` reason `RestoreFailed`; the existing-database one raises one `RestoreFailed`), `TestCheckRestoreProgress_AFailedJobSaysWhy` (the last failed pod of the Job, not an earlier one or another Job's; the Job's condition alone with no pod), `TestStartRestore_TheJobReportsTheTailOfItsLog` (`FallbackToLogsOnError`; `Running` reads `RestoreRunning`), `TestRestoreReconcile_AnOnlineRestoreWithStopClusterStartsOnce` (through `Reconcile` against a scripted server behind `newOnlineRestoreClient`: one `RestoreStarted` with the notice, `Completed` reading `RestoreCompleted`), `TestRestoreReadyCondition`, `TestMessageTail` (`internal/controller/restore_outcome_test.go`); `TestHandleExistingBackupJob_AFailedJobSaysWhy` (status, event and history `error`; the last failed pod of the Job only), `TestReconcileScheduledHistory_AFailedRunSaysWhy` (a failed CronJob child's history `error`), `TestBackupJobs_TheContainerReportsTheTailOfItsLog` (Job and CronJob template) (`internal/controller/backup_job_failure_test.go`), each failing with `neo4jbackup_controller.go` as before: the message `Backup job failed`, an empty `error`, no termination-message policy. With `neo4jrestore_controller.go` as on 4eb6d2ac (plus the client seam) each of the first four fails: the refusal returned as an error, logged at ERROR, reason `ReconciliationFailed`; "Restore job failed"; no termination-message policy and `Running` reading `Pending`; two `RestoreStarted` and `BackupSucceeded`. `TestStartRestore_OfflineRestoreIsAnnouncedOnlyOnceItsChecksPass` (`internal/controller/neo4jrestore_online_standalone_test.go`) now expects no error from the running-standalone refusal. The quiet end: `TestRestoreReconcile_AnEndedRestoreIsQuietOnTheNextPasses` (`internal/controller/restore_ended_quiet_test.go`: a refused restore — `database: system`, and one without `spec.instanceRef` — whose ending pass logs one line at info, then a failed and a completed restore whose Job is still there; three more passes each: no requeue, no write of any kind, no event, nothing at info, the guard's line at V(1)), failing with `neo4jrestore_controller.go` as on 0e72c207 (`go test -overlay`): the guard's lines at info on every pass, and without `instanceRef` its old refusal line at info, its status written and the seed proxy's three deletes sent on every pass; `TestRestoreReconcile_ANewGenerationRunsAnEndedRestoreAgain` (`Failed` and `Completed` at generation 1, the restore at 2: it looks its target up and waits, `observedGeneration` 2) and `TestRestoreJob_IsRemovedByItsTTLNotByARequeue` (same file) guard what the fix keeps and pass on both.
- **enforcement:** unit test. No machine check that a NEW restore refusal goes through `refuseRestore` — convention + code review. The quiet end was walked live on 2026-10-28 (#606 walk, `5043c68f`) with a development-mode operator (30 s resync): a refused, a completed and an `instanceRef`-less restore logged nothing at info and kept their `resourceVersion` for two minutes; at debug level the guard's line came about every 30 s.

### id 188 — `kubectl neo4j validate --connect` compares itself with the operator that is running, or says it cannot tell
- **scope:** `warnOnVersionSkew`, `runningOperator`, `holdsLeaderLease`, `deployedOperatorVersion` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate.go`).
- **rule:** Of the Deployments labelled `app.kubernetes.io/name: neo4j-operator`, one with no available replica (`status.availableReplicas`) runs nothing and is not compared with. One that runs is the operator. Several that run one release need no choice. Of several on different releases, the one whose pod holds a current `coordination.k8s.io` Lease in its namespace (the leader election: `holderIdentity` starts with the pod name, `<deployment>-…`; `renewTime` plus `leaseDurationSeconds` not past) is the one reconciling. When none runs, or several do on different releases and no single one holds its lease, the command prints `version skew not checked:` with why, instead of picking one. With no Deployment to read (another namespace, no permission) it stays silent, as before.
- **why:** #573, the v1.22.0 re-walk: the skew warning compared the CLI with a v1.22.0 operator Deployment scaled to 0 in another namespace, the first the list returned, not with the operator running.
- **pinned-by:** `TestWarnOnVersionSkew_ComparesWithTheRunningOperator` (fails on the earlier code, which named the scaled-down Deployment), `TestRunningOperator` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/version_skew_test.go`).
- **enforcement:** unit test.

### id 181 — A cluster server is Ready when it is available in the cluster, in its own view, for at most five minutes after it starts; the check reaches it at its next restart
- **scope:** `buildHealthScript`, `ReadyWindowSeconds` (`internal/resources/health_script.go`); the probes `buildReadinessProbe`, `buildLivenessProbe`, `buildStartupProbe` (`internal/resources/cluster.go`); `normalizeConfigContent` (the `health.sh` case) and `onlyNeo4jConfChanged` (`internal/controller/configmap_manager.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **What Ready means.** A cluster server's `/conf/health.sh` passes, while it waits, only when the server is available in the cluster as it sees itself: `GET /db/system/cluster/available` on its own HTTP port answers 200 (it hosts `system`, as every server does, Free ones included, and it is available on it), and `GET /dbms/cluster/status` reports no database with `healthy` (or `isHealthy`) `false` and none it hosts as a primary (`core: true`) with `participatingInRaftGroup: false`. A secondary takes part in no Raft group and is not held for it; nor is a single-primary database (the default `neo4j`, 1 primary), which reports `core: false` / `participatingInRaftGroup: false` on its primary (walked on 2026.08.1 and 5.26.21); a `404` from the combined endpoint skips the per-database part. Both endpoints are documented with the same behaviour on 5.26 and on CalVer (Operations Manual "Monitor cluster endpoints for status information", `/5/` and `/current/`, raw `clustering/monitoring/endpoints.adoc` on the `5.x` and `dev` branches, read 2026-10-25). They need credentials while `dbms.security.cluster_status_auth_enabled` is `true`, its default on both lines, so the script sends `DB_USERNAME`/`DB_PASSWORD`, which the container already has from the admin Secret, to `127.0.0.1` through bash's `/dev/tcp` (HTTP/1.0): the images ship no curl (the Debian ones purge it), and nothing puts the password on a command line. `dbms.cluster.statusCheck` and `SHOW SERVERS` were not used: either needs `cypher-shell`, a JVM per probe.
  - **Bounded, and never a restart.** The script cannot tell which probe runs it, so it waits only where waiting cannot restart a server: before the server has been seen available in this container, and for at most `ReadyWindowSeconds` (300) of the container's age (`ps -o etimes= -p 1`). Only the startup probe runs then (readiness and liveness start once it passes), and its budget (30s + 60 × 10s) is longer. Once the server has been available, or the window is over, the script records it in `/tmp/.neo4j-operator-available`, keyed by when PID 1 started (`ps -o lstart= -p 1`), so a restarted container starts afresh; from then on, and whenever the container's age cannot be read, it checks what it checked before #564 — the process, and 7474 answering or a cluster-formation line — so the liveness probe never restarts a server for being out of the cluster.
  - **First formation, scale-up, recovery are not held back.** The server StatefulSet uses `Parallel` pod management and the headless Service publishes not-ready addresses, so every server starts and finds the others whatever their readiness; readiness only paces the StatefulSet's rolling update, one pod at a time. A cluster that cannot form keeps its servers unready for at most the window.
  - **What reads pod readiness.** `serverStatefulSetFullyRolled`, the shortfall assessment's ready-pod count and `notReadyServerPods` (id 105), the plugin gate (id 141), live config (id 118), TLS reload and the upgrade machine's `ReadyReplicas` wait (id 127) all read a Ready pod as "serving", which it now is. The upgrade machine still checks `SHOW SERVERS` and the database catch-up itself: readiness is one server's own view and stops waiting after the window.
  - **Restart-neutral.** `health.sh` reaches a server only when its container starts (the copy into `/conf`), so the restart decision ignores it entirely (`normalizeConfigContent` returns nothing for it): an operator upgrade writes the new script to every cluster's ConfigMap and restarts nothing, and each server picks it up at its next restart. The probes, and so the pod template, are unchanged. A standalone keeps its own script (id 6).
- **why:** #564. Pod Ready was not Neo4j `Available` (id 6), and every roll but an upgrade — a plugin install, a config change, TLS, an env change, the follow-ups of a storage expansion — was paced by it: on a 3-server cluster the StatefulSet controller could restart the next server before the previous one had rejoined, and the cluster lost its majority for a while. Rolling upgrades have gates of their own (id 127).
- **pinned-by:** `TestHealthScript_ReadyMeansAvailableInTheCluster` (`internal/resources/health_script_test.go`: the rendered script under bash, with `pgrep` and `ps` faked on PATH and the two endpoints served locally — not ready while `system` is not available, a database is unhealthy, a primary is out of its Raft group, the port does not answer or the credentials are refused; ready for a secondary, without the combined endpoint, with the credentials sent; the check before once the window is over, once available in this container, or when the age cannot be read; a record from an earlier container start ignored), `TestHealthScript_CredentialsStayOffTheCommandLine`, `TestReadyWindowFitsTheStartupProbe`, `TestServerProbes_Unchanged` (same file); `TestReconcileConfigMap_NewHealthScriptRestartsNothing` and `TestCalculateConfigMapHash_IgnoresCommentsAndBlankLines` (`internal/controller/configmap_manager_test.go`). The script cases fail with the earlier script, the restart cases with `health.sh` counted again.
- **enforcement:** unit test. Walked live on Kind (2026.08.1 and 5.26.21, the v1.22.x walk): the endpoint field names as the manual gives them; an operator upgrade restarted nothing (pod UIDs and StatefulSet generation kept, the ConfigMap new, the pods on the old script until their restart); a Managed APOC roll under a write loop never had two servers unavailable and lost no write; a deleted pod was `Ready` only once `Available`; a stopped database did not hold readiness; the five-minute bound held with no liveness restart. Servers were available about 18s after they started, inside the startup probe's 30s initial delay, so the probe's waiting message shows only for a slower server.

### id 146 — A name from a spec is compared with the names Neo4j reports ignoring case
- **scope:** `FindDatabase` and `DatabaseExists` (`internal/neo4j/client.go`), `GetDatabaseInfo` (`replicas.go`), `ShowAlias` (`aliases.go`), `UnqualifyConstituent` (`composite.go`); `classifyAliases`, `undesiredConstituents`, `localConstituentDrifted`, `remoteConstituentDrifted`, `aliasInNamespace` and the constituent lookup in `reconcileConstituents` (`internal/controller/neo4jcompositedatabase_controller.go`); `aliasTargetDiffers` (`neo4jdatabasealias_controller.go`); the home database in `computeAlter` (`neo4juser_controller.go`); `compositesAmong` and `distinctPrivilegeTargets` — the de-duplication in `privilegeDatabaseNames` and `compositeGraphTargets` (`neo4jrole_controller.go`); `storedFormRows` (`neo4jrole_learn.go`) and `FoldPrivilegeDatabaseNames` (`internal/neo4j/privileges.go`); the sharded guard of `validateRestore`, `artifactForDatabase`, `standaloneRestoreArtifact` and `standaloneRestoreFromPath` (`neo4jrestore_controller.go`), `restoreSeedArtifacts` (`restore_target.go`); `parseStandardArtifactFromLog`, `parseArtifactTypesFromLog` and `artifactTypeOf` (`neo4jbackup_log_parser.go`); `shardedGlobPoisoning` (`neo4jbackup_sharded.go`); `findFamilyArtifacts` (`neo4jshardeddatabase_seed.go`); the constituent rules of `CompositeDatabaseValidator.validateConstituents`, the self-target rule of `AliasValidator.Validate` and the same-name warning of `ReplicaValidator.Validate` (`internal/validation/`); the scoped-name check of `validateChainParent` (`neo4jbackup_controller.go`); the shard-name lookup of `mergeShardArtifactsFromLog` (`neo4jbackup_log_parser.go`). Lower-cased where Neo4j matches exactly: `latestArtifactSubstitution`, `latestArtifactGlob` and `resolveLocalPVCFromPath` (`neo4jrestore_controller.go`); `shardedLogicalNameForBackup`, `expectedShardArtifactsForBackup` and `shardedBackupPatterns` (`neo4jbackup_sharded.go`), so a sharded backup's patterns, its expected shard names and the names in its log agree; `shardSeedKey`, `buildPerShardCloudURIs`, `resolvePVCShardedSeed` (`neo4jshardeddatabase_seed.go`) and the per-shard keys of `buildShardedDatabaseOptions` (`neo4jshardeddatabase_controller.go`). Already so before: `observeDatabase` (id 143), `shardFamilyExists`, `observeShardFamily` and `shardCreateNeeded` (id 136), `resolvableDatabaseNames` and `roleAliasFingerprint` (id 142), `rolesGrantingOn`.
- **rule:**
  - **Any spelling is one name.** A database, alias, composite or constituent name, or a user's home database, taken from a spec is compared with a name Neo4j reports — SHOW DATABASES, SHOW ALIASES, SHOW USERS, and the names neo4j-admin writes into artifacts and logs, which the operator records in a backup's status — ignoring case: `strings.EqualFold`, or a map keyed `strings.ToLower`. So are two names in specs that must not name the same database or alias (two constituents of a composite, an alias and its target, a replica and its upstream). Comparisons between two names Neo4j reported are left exact.
  - **Only comparisons:** the name is still sent to Neo4j, written to status and shown in events and messages as the spec writes it; Neo4j accepts it in any case.
  - **…except where Neo4j matches exactly, and there it is lower-cased:** two names are matched with the case Neo4j itself gives them, not compared by the operator. The artifact a PVC restore with no recorded artifact globs for: neo4j-admin names it `<database>-<yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH-mm-ss>.backup` with the database lower-cased, so the shell glob takes the source database lower-cased and the timestamp's shape (`latestArtifactGlob`), which also keeps `orders-eu-…` out of `orders` (a bare `orders-*.backup` matched it, and it sorts last). And a per-shard seedURI map's keys: Neo4j derives `<database>-g000` / `-pNNN` from the lower-cased name and takes each out of the key set exactly, so the operator sends every key lower-cased, whether it built the map from `seedBackupRef` or the user wrote `spec.seedURIs`.
  - **One lookup for the client:** `neo4j.FindDatabase` is the SHOW DATABASES lookup by name; `DatabaseExists` and `GetDatabaseInfo` go through it, and a test double of either must too, or a Reconcile test against the double says nothing about the real client.
  - **Composite constituents** are keyed by their short name lower-cased (`classifyAliases`), and spec names are lower-cased to find them (`undesiredConstituents`, the lookup in `reconcileConstituents`).
  - **Privilege statements:** a role's database names are listed once whatever their spelling, the first spelling kept (`distinctPrivilegeTargets`), and learn mode matches a statement's own text against a stored row with only its database and graph names folded to lower case (`FoldPrivilegeDatabaseNames`); labels, properties and the role keep their case, as Neo4j compares them.
- **why:** Neo4j database names "are case-insensitive and normalized to lowercase" (Operations Manual 5 and `/current/`, "Naming rules for databases"; `naming-databases.adoc` on `5.x` and `dev` of `neo4j/docs-operations`, read 2026-10-16). Alias names are the same `NormalizedDatabaseName` (`DatabaseReference.alias()`), and so is a home database (`AdministrationCommandRuntime` on `release/5.26.0` and `2026.08`); neo4j-admin compiles a `--database` pattern `CASE_INSENSITIVE` (`DatabaseNamePattern`). The validators admit capitals in all of these names (`neo4jDatabaseNamePattern` is `^[a-zA-Z][a-zA-Z0-9.\-]*$`). With exact comparisons, read from the code and reproduced in the tests, not walked live: a `Neo4jDatabase` named `Orders` sent `CREATE DATABASE … IF NOT EXISTS` on every pass, and with `ifNotExists: false` turned `Failed` with "already exists" on the second; a restore into an existing database spelled another way took the create path — CREATE refused, `replaceExisting` never consulted — and a point-in-time restore went online over it; a `Neo4jReplicaDatabase` was created again on its second pass and went `Failed`, and a promotion said the database did not exist; an alias was created (`IF NOT EXISTS`, with an `AliasCreated` event) and re-pointed (`AliasRetargeted`) on every pass, and one whose target was capitalised waited for that target for ever; a capitalised composite re-sent every constituent's `CREATE ALIAS`, with an event, on every pass, a capitalised target was re-pointed on every pass, and under `enforceConstituents` a capitalised constituent was dropped on one pass and created on the next, so it was missing half the time; a home database was re-set, with a `UserUpdated` event, on every pass; the sharded backup's glob guard matched no database for a capitalised name and let `productsales` into `Products*`; the sharded restore guard let a capitalised `spec.database` through; and a capitalised name found no artifact in an all-databases backup. Found after (#529), from neo4j-admin's and Neo4j's own code rather than assumed: neo4j-admin backs up the databases the server lists that match the pattern, and names each artifact from the database's layout, `FileManager`'s `"%s-%s.backup"` with `yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH-mm-ss` (`OnlineBackupExecutor`, `BackupWorkspace.nextArtifact` in `neo4j-backup` 5.23.0 and 2025.09.0, read with `javap`), whose name `PlainDatabaseLayout` normalizes with `NormalizedDatabaseName` (`toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT)`, `neo4j/neo4j` `release/5.26.0`) — so the PVC glob `$(ls …/Products-*.backup | tail -1)` found nothing for `spec.database: Products`; and a sharded `CREATE DATABASE` checks the seedURI map with `InputValidators.validateSharedSeedURI` — expected names from the normalized database name, `HashSet.remove` of each, "entry is missing for 'products-g000'" otherwise — and seeds each shard by an exact `Map.get` (`DatabaseSeedInputParams.seedURIForShard`, 2025.12.0), so the keys the operator built from `spec.name: Products` (`Products-g000`) were refused. Two `Neo4jBackup`s chaining one database spelled two ways were refused as different targets, though neo4j-admin writes one chain of `orders-…` artifacts for both. Left after #521 and fixed since: a role's warnings listed `TOPODB` and `topodb` as two databases (#529), and learn mode held `ACCESS ON DATABASE TOPODB` ambiguous when another statement had granted its row, which kept every out-of-band row on the role unattributed (#551). The v1.22.x walk found the sharded backup's own names: `expectedShardArtifactsForBackup` built `Products-g000` while neo4j-admin wrote `products-g000`, so the exact lookup in `mergeShardArtifactsFromLog` recorded no file name for any shard (backup-restore.md Rules 53, 174).
- **pinned-by:** `TestDatabaseExists_IgnoresCase`, `TestGetDatabaseInfo_IgnoresCase`, `TestShowAlias_IgnoresCase`, `TestFindDatabase` (`internal/neo4j/database_name_case_test.go`: the real client methods over a scripted driver); `TestQualifyAndUnqualifyConstituent` (`internal/neo4j/composite_test.go`); `TestDatabaseReconcile_MixedCaseNameIsCreatedOnce`, `TestReplicaReconcile_MixedCaseNameIsCreatedOnce`, `TestDecidePointInTimePath_MixedCaseNameOverAnExistingDatabase`, `TestValidateRestore_ShardedGuardIgnoresCase`, `TestRestoreArtifactLookup_IgnoresCase`, `TestClassifyAliases_IgnoresCase`, `TestUndesiredConstituents`, `TestConstituentDrift_IgnoresTheTargetsCase`, `TestAliasInNamespace_IgnoresCase`, `TestAliasTargetDiffers`, `TestComputeAlter_HomeDatabaseIgnoresCase`, `TestCompositesAmong_IgnoresCase`, `TestBackupLogParsers_IgnoreCase`, `TestShardedGlobPoisoning_IgnoresCase`, `TestFindFamilyArtifacts_IgnoresCase` (`internal/controller/database_name_case_test.go`); `TestPrivilegeTargets_DeduplicateIgnoringCase` (`internal/controller/neo4jrole_requeue_test.go`), `TestLearnDesired_ACaseVariantOverlapIsAttributed` (`internal/controller/neo4jrole_learn_test.go`), `TestFoldPrivilegeDatabaseNames` (`internal/neo4j/privileges_names_test.go`); `TestCompositeDatabaseValidator_NamesDifferingInCaseAreOneName`, `TestAliasValidator_SelfTargetInAnotherCaseRejected`, `TestReplicaValidator_UpstreamNameInAnotherCaseWarns` (`internal/validation/database_name_case_test.go`); `TestLatestArtifactSubstitution_FindsTheArtifactNeo4jAdminWrote` (runs the generated `ls … | tail -1` with `sh` against files named as neo4j-admin names them: `Products`, `products`, `Products-EU`, `products-2025`), `TestResolveLocalPVCFromPath_LowerCasesTheDatabase`, `TestPerShardSeedKeys_AreTheLowerCaseShardNames` (cloud, PVC and `spec.seedURIs`), `TestValidateChainParent_OneDatabaseInAnotherCase` (`internal/controller/backup_restore_name_case_test.go`). Each fails with its comparison made exact again, or its name sent as the spec spells it (and the glob with the bare `-*.backup`); `TestShardedBackup_ACapitalisedNameRecordsItsShards` (`internal/controller/backup_walk_test.go`: `spec.name: Products`, the shard names expected, and an earlier operator's `Products-g000` stub, matched against neo4j-admin's log lines).
- **enforcement:** unit test; not walked live. No machine check that a NEW comparison ignores case — convention + code review.

### id 147 — A VerifiedDownload plugin's CA volume follows its target's `spec.trustedCASecrets`, written by the plugin controller alone; its CAs extend the downloader's defaults
- **scope:** `setPluginCAVolume`, `sameTrustedCAs`, `trustedCAProjections`, `applyVerifiedDownload`, `deploymentTrustedCASecrets`, `pluginPodDrift`, `missingFromTarget`, `trustedCAUpdate` and the settled branch of `Reconcile` (`internal/controller/plugin_controller.go`); `BuildPluginCAVolume`, `BuildPluginVerifiedDownloadInitContainer`, `pluginInitScript` (`internal/resources/plugin_init_container.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **Custom CAs extend the defaults, never replace them:** curl's `--cacert` is its only trust store, so the init script builds it from the init image's default CA bundle followed by every certificate in `/etc/plugin-ca`, each file ended by a newline (two PEM blocks never share a line). The image is configurable (Helm `pluginInitContainer.image`, an air-gapped mirror), so the default bundle is found, not assumed: `$CURL_CA_BUNDLE` when it names a non-empty file (`curlimages/curl:8.5.0` sets it to `/cacert.pem`), else the first non-empty of `/etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt` (Debian, Ubuntu, Alpine — also in `curlimages/curl`), `/etc/pki/tls/certs/ca-bundle.crt` (RHEL, UBI), `/etc/ssl/cert.pem`, `/cacert.pem`. The log line names the bundle used; an image with none of them trusts the custom CAs only and logs a `WARNING` saying so. The JVM truststore the target renders from the same list keeps the JDK's default CAs too (`examples/clusters/cluster-with-trusted-cas.yaml`), so both now mean the same: these CAs as well. The checksum is still what vouches for the JAR.
  - **One writer:** the `plugin-ca` volume — a projected volume of the target's `spec.trustedCASecrets`, mounted at `/etc/plugin-ca` by every `VerifiedDownload` init container of the target — is written by the plugin controller only. The cluster and standalone controllers keep it as a volume they did not render (id 141); never render or refresh it there: the plugin adds it in the write that adds a mount of it (the API server refuses a mount without its volume), and two writers of one volume are a tug of war. Every `VerifiedDownload` plugin of a target builds the same volume from the same list, so the first plugin pass after a change updates it and the others find nothing to change.
  - **It follows the list:** `applyVerifiedDownload` (through `setPluginCAVolume`) adds the volume when the list is not empty, replaces it when it projects other Secrets, keys or paths than the list (in any order), and removes it when the list is empty and no container mounts it any more. The init container's mount of it and its CA step come and go with the list, in the same write; a mount another plugin's init container still has keeps the volume until that plugin's pass rebuilds its own. The list is the one the target's JVM truststore is built from: `spec.trustedCASecrets` with the legacy `spec.auth.trustStore` folded in (`resources.CollectTrustedCASecrets`, id 28; `deploymentTrustedCASecrets`). It used to be `spec.trustedCASecrets` alone, so a CA a target named the legacy way was trusted by Neo4j and not by the download (#530); a `VerifiedDownload` plugin on such a target updates its CA volume on its first pass after the operator upgrade (one restart, no `PluginMissing`).
