  - **Only a change is written:** the comparison is what the volume projects (`trustedCAProjections`), not the API server's defaults (`defaultMode`) nor whether a projection is optional, so a volume an earlier operator wrote — its projections required — is left alone while the list stays the same. A settled pass counts the volume and the init container's mount of it (`pluginPodDrift`, "outdated"), never another difference of the init container (its script, its image), so an operator upgrade restarts nothing.
  - **A change, not a loss:** a settled plugin whose only drift is outdated CA wiring writes it without a `PluginMissing` Warning (phase `Installing`; its "Updating the plugin's trusted CAs: spec.trustedCASecrets of … changed (…)" message is replaced in the same pass by the rollout wait's); like every write to the servers it waits while the target is not `Ready` with every server available and rolled out (`Waiting`, "…: updating the plugin's trusted CAs restarts every server", one `PluginInstallDeferred`; id 141), and the pass that writes after the wait still says "Updating the plugin's trusted CAs" (`trustedCAUpdate`). The volume absent while the plugin's init container is there is a list that was empty; absent with the init container, both are missing (id 141).
  - **What wakes it:** the target's own controller renders its JVM truststore from the same list (the `truststore-init` init container and a `trusted-ca-<name>` volume per Secret), so a list change changes the pod template and re-queues every `Ready` plugin (id 135). That pass finds the target not yet rolled out — it has just written its StatefulSet and may still read `Ready` from the pass before (id 141) — so the plugin waits. The servers restart twice: the target's roll, then the plugin's write once the target is `Ready` and rolled out again. Kept on purpose (#530): one restart would need either the plugin's write to land before the target's roll has replaced any pod — a race the target's own controller starts — or the target's controller to render the plugin's volume, a second writer of it. The plugin is `Ready` again only once its own roll is done (id 141). A write that repairs the plugin's own init container while a server crash-loops in it does not wait (id 152).
  - **A removed CA cannot stop a server:** every projection is optional, and the init script falls back to the default CAs when `/etc/plugin-ca` holds no certificate. Between the target's write and the plugin's the volume still names a removed CA; a Secret deleted then must not keep a restarting server from starting — a required one does, and the plugin's write waits for a `Ready` target that never comes. The JAR's checksum is what vouches for it, with or without the CA.
  - **Accepted gap:** a volume and init container an earlier operator wrote keep required projections and the old script until the list next changes (that write carries the new ones). Until then, deleting a removed CA's Secret before the plugin is `Ready` again can still hold a restarting server in `ContainerCreating`; the plugins guide says to wait. Clearing the list costs one write per `VerifiedDownload` plugin of the target, each rebuilding its own init container.
- **why:**
  - **The volume:** `applyVerifiedDownload` added the volume only when it was absent and nothing compared its content, so a CA added to the list never reached the download (a fetch from a newly trusted mirror failed TLS), a removed one stayed trusted, and clearing the list left the volume projecting Secrets the user was free to delete. With the projections required, the target's own roll of that change gave a server that could not mount the deleted Secret, and the plugin waited for a `Ready` target for ever. Found by the #519 author in review; diagnosed from the code, not seen live.
  - **The bundle (v1.22.0 journey, 2026-10-17, CalVer 2026.08.1, 3 servers):** the script passed the custom CAs alone as curl's `--cacert`. Once the volume followed the list, adding two CA Secrets to a cluster whose APOC plugin downloads from its GitHub release URL put server-2 in `Init:CrashLoopBackOff` (curl exit 60, 6 restarts) and the cluster stayed `Forming`; a test pod confirmed custom CAs only → exit 60, default CAs plus custom → exit 0. v1.22.0's upgrade notes promise one `plugin-ca` update after the upgrade for a plugin whose target's list changed since its install, so a public-URL plugin that works on v1.21.1 would have crash-looped on the upgrade; that write now carries the script that keeps the defaults.
- **pinned-by:** `TestReconcile_SettledPluginFollowsTheTargetsTrustedCAs`, `TestReconcile_ClearedTrustedCAsTakeTheCAVolumeAway`, `TestReconcile_FirstTrustedCAAddsTheCAVolume`, `TestReconcile_UnchangedTrustedCAsWriteNothing`, `TestReconcile_PluginsShareTheCAVolume`, `TestReconcile_TrustedCAUpdateWaitsForTheTarget`, `TestReconcile_StandalonePluginFollowsItsTrustedCAs`, `TestReconcile_PluginTrustsTheLegacyTrustStore`, `TestPluginPodDrift_TrustedCAs`, `TestPluginCAVolumeFollowsTrustedCASecrets_APIServer` (envtest: the cluster controller and two plugins through four list changes, no pass changing the template after each) (`internal/controller/plugin_trusted_ca_test.go`); `TestReconcile_ACAUpdateThatWaitedIsWrittenAsOne` (`internal/controller/plugin_target_gate_test.go`); `TestBuildPluginCAVolume_ProjectionsAreOptional`, `TestPluginInitScript_TrustedCAs` and `TestPluginInitScript_CustomCAsExtendTheDefaults` (both run the init script under `sh`, with the image's default bundle paths moved into a temp directory: `$CURL_CA_BUNDLE` first, each fallback path, an empty bundle skipped, none at all, PEM blocks on their own lines) (`internal/resources/plugin_init_container_test.go`). All but `TestReconcile_UnchangedTrustedCAsWriteNothing` — which guards the other way — fail with the fix switched off; the two script tests fail on the custom-only script.
- **enforcement:** unit test + envtest; walked live on Kind 2026-10-17 (id 152's enforcement line has the run).

### id 148 — Hand-built unstructured content holds JSON types only
- **scope:** `buildRoute` (`internal/resources/route.go`, behind `BuildRouteForEnterprise`, `BuildRouteForStandalone`, `BuildMCPRouteForCluster`, `BuildMCPRouteForStandalone`); `buildExternalSecret`, `jsonStringMap` (`internal/resources/cluster.go`); `setupMetricsCollection`, `setupAlertingRules` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`); `reconcileServiceMonitor` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`); the Route writes, `applyDesiredRoute` (`internal/controller/route_apply.go`) in `reconcileRoute` and `reconcileMCPRoute` of both controllers.
- **rule:** An object the operator builds as `unstructured.Unstructured` content (or as a `map[string]any` that becomes it) holds only what JSON decoding would produce: `map[string]interface{}`, `[]interface{}`, `string`, `int64`, `float64`, `bool`, `nil`. Never a `map[string]string` (labels, annotations: use `SetLabels` / `SetAnnotations`, or convert), a `[]map[string]any` (use `[]any`), an `int` or `int32` (use `int64`), or a named Go type. `runtime.DeepCopyJSONValue`, under `Unstructured.DeepCopy`, panics on anything else, and the accessors (`GetLabels`, `NestedStringMap`, `NestedInt64`, `NestedSlice`) read such a value as absent. Converting a typed struct with `runtime.DefaultUnstructuredConverter.ToUnstructured` is the other safe way; the operator has no typed Route (no `openshift/api` dependency), so the Route is built from JSON types by hand. The JSON the API server receives is the same either way. **A Route is written when it changes, once per pass:** the mutate step of every Route's `CreateOrUpdate` is `applyDesiredRoute`, which writes what the operator renders — labels, annotations, `spec.to`, `spec.port`, `spec.path`, `spec.host` when the user names one, `spec.tls.termination` and `insecureEdgeTerminationPolicy` — and keeps the rest of the live Route: what OpenShift's API server fills in (`spec.wildcardPolicy: None`; a generated `spec.host` and its `openshift.io/host.generated` annotation — OpenShift ignores an update that clears the host) and a certificate added to the Route by hand. The standalone reconciles its Route at the end of `reconcileService` and nowhere else.
- **why:** `buildRoute` put `map[string]string` labels and annotations, an `int` weight and an `int32` targetPort into the Route, and `reconcileRoute` / `reconcileMCPRoute` (cluster and standalone) start with `route.DeepCopy()`. Every pass of a deployment with `spec.service.route.enabled` or an MCP Route panicked there ("cannot deep copy map[string]string"), with or without the Route API. controller-runtime recovered it as a reconcile error ("panic: … [recovered]", stack trace in the log, `controller_runtime_reconcile_panics_total` up) and requeued with backoff; the status write in the error branch never ran, so a new cluster stayed `Initializing` ("Starting cluster reconciliation") and a new standalone had no phase. Both controllers reconcile the Route before they write the server StatefulSet, so such a deployment never got any servers; one that enabled a Route later kept its last status while nothing after the Route step (the StatefulSet, status, diagnostics) ran again. Present since v1.13.0, reported by a review agent. The ServiceMonitors, the PrometheusRule and the ExternalSecrets made the same mistake; nothing deep-copies them on the way to the API server, so they did not panic in production, but a fake client or a future `DeepCopy` would, and their labels read as absent. Found while fixing the panic (#536, by code reading): the standalone sent its Route to the API server twice per pass, the second time with an unconditional Get+Update; and replacing `spec` wholesale dropped what the API server fills in, so on OpenShift every pass would send an update the API server turns back into the same object (inferred from OpenShift's API behaviour).
- **pinned-by:** `TestBuiltRoutes_DeepCopy`, `TestBuiltRoutes_ReadThroughTheAccessors`, `TestBuildExternalSecret_ContentIsJSON`, `TestBuiltRoutes_RenderTheSameJSON` (the JSON the old builders rendered, so the fix changes no field) (`internal/resources/unstructured_content_test.go`); `TestReconcileRoute_Cluster`, `TestReconcileRoute_Standalone`, `TestReconcileRoute_NoRouteAPI`, `TestClusterReconcile_WithRoutesWritesTheStatefulSet`, `TestStandaloneReconcile_WithRoutesWritesTheStatefulSet`, `TestHandBuiltObjects_ContentIsJSON` (`internal/controller/unstructured_content_test.go`), with the Route GVK registered as unstructured in a fake client. All but `TestBuiltRoutes_RenderTheSameJSON` — which guards the other way — fail with the previous builders. The writes: `TestReconcileRoute_LeavesWhatTheAPIServerFilledIn` (four Routes filled in as OpenShift would, three passes, no write; a rendered change, one write each, the filled-in fields kept), `TestApplyDesiredRoute`, `TestStandaloneReconcile_WritesItsRouteOnlyWhenItChanges` (one write on the first pass, none after; two, then one per pass, before), and against an API server without the Route API, as on Kind, `TestReconcile_RoutesWithoutTheRouteAPI_APIServer` (`internal/controller/route_apply_test.go`).
- **enforcement:** unit test + envtest; not walked live on OpenShift.

### id 149 — A Paused or Failed upgrade keeps its partition until it goes on, a tag below what a server started on is refused, and a patch-less tag matches any patch of its line
- **scope:** `upgradeHeld`, `upgradeWaitsForUser`, `keptUpgradePartition`, `heldRolloutPending`, `partitionForNewServerStatefulSet`, `sameHeldUpgrade`, `newUpgradeRecord`, `clusterReadyMessage`, `serverImageInPlace`, `announceHeldImageChange` (`internal/controller/upgrade_hold.go`); `progressReleasedAServer`, `containerStartedOn`, `targetStartedOnAServer`, `recordStartedTarget`, `forgetAbandonedUpgrade` (`internal/controller/upgrade_started_target.go`); `UpgradeStatus.StartedTargetVersion` (`api/v1beta1/neo4jenterprisecluster_types.go`); `announceUpgradeDeferredByDrain` (`internal/controller/scale_down.go`); `refuseUpgradeStart`, `startRefusedRecently`, `preUpgradeCheck`, `noteUpgradeInClusterMessage`, `clusterShortUpgradeMessage` (`internal/controller/upgrade_start.go`); `splitImage` (`internal/controller/running_version.go`); the partition code of `createOrUpdateResourceInternal`, `isUpgradeRequired`, `holdImageDriftUntilReady`, the retarget in `handleRollingUpgrade`, the held-upgrade calls in `Reconcile`, `serverStatefulSetFullyRolled`, `recordClusterReady` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`); `serverRolloutInFlight` (`internal/controller/cluster_availability.go`); `startRollingUpgrade`, `stepUpgradeRolling`, `failUpgrade` (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade_statemachine.go`); `versionsMatch`, `parseVersion`, `normalizeKernelAlias` (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade.go`); `setOwnerReference` (`internal/controller/configmap_manager.go`); `Client.ValidateUpgradeSafety` (`internal/neo4j/client.go`); `GetImageVersion`, `ParseVersion` (`internal/neo4j/version.go`); `validateAgainstRunning` (`internal/validation/cluster_validator.go`); `validateTagAgainstUpgrade`, `upgradeFloor`, `upgradeUnfinished`, `upgradeRestartedAServer`, `parseVersion` (`internal/validation/upgrade_validator.go`); `VersionLabelValue` (`internal/resources/version_label.go`) and its callers `getLabelsForEnterpriseServer`, `getLabelsForEnterprise` (`internal/resources/cluster.go`) and the MCP label builders (`internal/resources/mcp.go`); `tagFromImage` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **The hold.** Once the upgrade state machine stops — `Paused` (`autoPauseOnFailure`) or `Failed` — the normal pass runs again, and every StatefulSet write keeps the live `RollingUpdate.Partition` (the builder renders none). Only the machine moves it, so no server the upgrade has not rolled restarts onto the target except through the machine's per-server gates (id 127). A server StatefulSet created again while an upgrade is held for `spec.image.tag` (a storage expansion orphan-deletes it) starts at `currentPartition`, or frozen at `replicas` if the upgrade had not staged. The rest of the normal pass runs — services, ConfigMap, diagnostics, split-brain, the Ready status — unlike an active upgrade (id 131); a change of `spec.topology.servers` waits (id 163).
  - **The image hold of a cluster that is not Ready** (`holdImageDriftUntilReady`, #262) reads the image in place from the server StatefulSet, or, while a storage expansion has orphan-deleted it, from the orphaned server pods — the highest ordinal's, which carries the template rolled out last (`serverImageInPlace`). It found nothing there and let the held image into the StatefulSet created again, with no partition: every server rolled onto it, ungated, on a cluster that was not Ready (#538). It splits the image as the operator built it (`splitImage`): a tag may carry a digest, and splitting on the last colon pinned the repo `neo4j:2026.08.1-enterprise@sha256` (#531). `GetImageVersion` and the CLI's `tagFromImage` split the same way.
  - **A tag pinned to a digest is accepted, and works.** The image rules accept `2026.08.1-enterprise@sha256:…`, so everything the operator derives from the tag handles it: the `app.kubernetes.io/version` label of every object that carries it (the cluster's StatefulSet, pod template, ConfigMap, Services, Certificate and ServiceAccount; a cluster's or standalone's MCP Deployment and Service, from the MCP tag) is `VersionLabelValue(tag)` — the digest dropped, only label characters kept, cut to 63 and to an alphanumeric end. It was the tag itself, which the API server refused (`@`, `:`, over 63 characters), so the ConfigMap write failed and the cluster went `Failed` (#562 walk). A standalone's own objects carry no tag in a label. `ParseVersion` and the upgrade validator's `parseVersion` drop the digest before reading the version, so `5.26.31@sha256:…` is 5.26.31, as the operator runs it, not 5.26.0 or a tag without a patch. Its `UpgradeDeferred` event is announced on the second pass that holds the same image, once (`announceHeldImageChange`): the pass after a refused tag is fixed still reads the refusal's `Failed` phase, writes `Ready`, and the next pass starts the upgrade, so the first pass's deferral was announced every time (#550). A change an upgrade the machine stopped already names (its record's target) is not announced. The wait for a scale-down drain is announced once per target (`announceUpgradeDeferredByDrain`), not on every retry. In-memory: an operator restart announces once more.
  - **Ready while held.** The servers below the kept partition are not a rollout in flight (`heldRolloutPending`): `serverStatefulSetFullyRolled` needs only the pods at or above it on the template and every pod ready, and `serverRolloutInFlight` reports the same, so a held cluster with every server available is `Ready` (`clusterReadyMessage` names the held upgrade) and one with a server down is short of a server (id 105), not `Forming`. When the machine starts, resumes or retargets an upgrade on such a cluster it writes the message itself, "its upgrade to X is in progress" (`noteUpgradeInClusterMessage`): only the normal pass wrote it, which does not run during an upgrade, so a resumed upgrade said "is paused" for its whole walk (#553). On a formed cluster short of servers — the repair walk of id 162, on a cluster `Ready` with `Degraded=True`, `Degraded`, or `Forming` once it lost a majority — it writes `clusterShortUpgradeMessage` ("upgrade to X is in progress (status.upgradeStatus): the servers that could not start are replaced first, then the rest one at a time") and keeps the phase, which the next normal pass judges: the message the normal pass wrote before the walk named the server down then ("c-server-2 (Unavailable)") and kept naming it after it was replaced, and never said the upgrade was in progress (#562 walk). Dependents keep working, as they do during an active upgrade, whose cluster phase stays `Ready`. `status.version` is not recorded: `rolledImageTag` sees a split revision (id 144). For a target without a patch, what the servers at or above the partition report is (`status.upgradeStatus.resolvedTargetVersion`, id 153). A template write the StatefulSet controller has not observed is still a rollout in flight here (`heldRolloutPending`, and id 153 for the non-held path).
  - **What goes on.** `Failed`: the operator retries once the cluster is `Ready` with every server available (`isUpgradeRequired`). The status alone cannot say so after a stop: the upgrade stopped on a cluster that read `Ready` with every server available, and the normal pass, which records a server short, does not run while the machine does. So the retry also waits until every server pod is Ready on the server StatefulSet (`ReadyReplicas`, the measure of its first gate, `validateStatefulSetsReady`), and, after an attempt the pre-upgrade check refused, for `upgradeStartRetry` (`startRefusedRecently`, as for a refused start). Meanwhile the normal pass runs and records the shortfall as for any formed cluster (id 105): `Ready` with `Degraded=True` naming the server, then, past the grace period, phase `Degraded` with one `ClusterDegraded` — after which `isUpgradeRequired`'s first check (`Ready`, no server unavailable) holds the retry until the normal pass sees every server again; a new tag still goes on at once through id 162's repair. `Stalled` stays `True` throughout, and the Argo CD check reports Degraded either way (the phase, or the `Failed` record in its `Ready` branch). The walk of be1e0a6f: server-2 in `ImagePullBackOff`, `autoPauseOnFailure` off, for over 10 minutes the retry started on every pass and stopped at that gate ("Upgrade waiting for StatefulSet readiness"), the normal pass never ran, and the `Degraded` condition kept saying "All 3 servers are available" while SHOW SERVERS and `ServersHealthy=False` said server-2 was not; the phase never turned `Degraded`. A `Paused` upgrade, which is not retried, recorded it. `startRollingUpgrade` runs its gates again, and Staging, the template already carrying the target, keeps the partition the upgrade reached (id 162: raising it over the servers on the target would put one that restarted back on the version they left), so the walk re-verifies the server at it and rolls the rest. A stopped upgrade is logged once at info when its record is written, with the `UpgradePaused` or `UpgradeFailed` Warning; it is not an ERROR, and a `Failed` one is not returned as a reconcile error (which controller-runtime logs as an ERROR too) but requeued for the normal pass (#562 re-walk). `Paused`: waits while `upgradeWaitsForUser` — `autoPauseOnFailure` still set and `spec.image.tag` the record's `targetVersion` (or a record with no target, which an older operator wrote when the pre-upgrade check paused). It goes on when the user changes the tag (image drift starts the machine towards it) or turns `autoPauseOnFailure` off (then it is retried as `Failed`). There is no other resume; a new tag goes on even while the servers the upgrade restarted cannot start on its target (id 162).
  - **A start a check refuses.** `startRollingUpgrade`'s checks — the version rule (`validateVersionCompatibility`, id 139), then the pre-upgrade check (`preUpgradeCheck`: `Client.ValidateUpgradeSafety` — health, consensus, a leader — and the target against what SHOW SERVERS reports, id 153) — run before anything is written to the StatefulSet. A refusal (`refuseUpgradeStart`) records the attempt, `Failed` or `Paused` with the cause, and announces `UpgradeFailed` or `UpgradePaused` (and counts the failure metric) only when the write changed the record's phase, target or error. The cluster stays as it was — it was `Ready` with every server available, or the machine would not have started — and a `Ready` one has its message name the stopped upgrade, so the next normal pass writes nothing and no `ClusterReady`. A `Failed` one is tried again once `upgradeStartRetry` (1 minute) has passed since the attempt (`startRefusedRecently` in `isUpgradeRequired`), with the normal pass, the image change held, running in between; `Paused` waits for the user. It used to go through `failUpgrade`: the cluster turned `Failed` (dependents paused), the next pass held the image and wrote `Ready`, the one after tried again — a check that kept refusing flipped the cluster between `Failed` and `Ready` with an `UpgradeFailed` and a `ClusterReady` every cycle (#538, #531), and retried on every pass it would have starved the normal pass. A failure after Staging still goes through `failUpgrade`.
  - **A stop mid-walk is a state, announced once.** `failUpgrade` writes the record `Paused` or `Failed` with `lastError`, and leaves the cluster's phase as it is — `Ready` with its message naming the stopped upgrade (`noteUpgradeInClusterMessage`, `clusterReadyMessage`), or what the next normal pass reports for a cluster short of a server. It set the phase to `Paused` or `Failed`, which the cluster documents for neither: the next pass wrote `Ready` again with a `ClusterReady`, and a `Failed` upgrade the operator retried that failed the same way cycled `Failed` → `Ready` → retry with an `UpgradeFailed` and a `ClusterReady` every cycle (#565). `UpgradePaused` and `UpgradeFailed` are announced once per target and cause: the retry of the same target keeps the record's `lastError` (`newUpgradeRecord`), and a stop with that same error is logged at V(1) and not announced; a new cause or a new target is. The retry itself is kept, unbounded: a `Failed` upgrade is retried while every server is available (the documented recovery), each attempt restarts no server it already rolled (Staging keeps the partition, id 162) and waits a full step timeout before it stops, so the cycle is quiet and slow; the failure metric still counts every attempt.
  - **…and every reader of health reads the upgrade record.** With the phase `Ready`, a reader keyed on `status.phase` alone reports a stopped upgrade as healthy, and a GitOps user loses the only sign that an image change did not land (v1.22.0's `Failed` phase was Degraded in Argo CD). The published Argo CD check for `Neo4jEnterpriseCluster` (`docs/gitops/argocd-health-checks.yaml`) reads `status.upgradeStatus.phase` inside its `Ready` branch: `Failed` is Degraded, `Paused` is Suspended (Argo CD's "waiting for a human"), each with `status.message`, which names the stopped upgrade; an upgrade in progress stays Healthy. `kubectl neo4j status` flags such a cluster under `--problems` (`stoppedUpgrade`, `phaseCell` "Ready (upgrade Failed)", the last error under the table; `cmd/kubectl-neo4j/status.go`), and `diagnose` reports it as a problem with what to do (`stoppedUpgradeSymptom`, `cmd/kubectl-neo4j/diagnose.go`). The standalone has no upgrade record: its upgrade replaces one pod, a downgrade is a refused spec (phase `Failed`), and a pre-upgrade check that holds it back is an advisory `UpgradeFailed`, as in v1.22.0. For kstatus readers — Flux's health checks — the cluster carries a `Stalled` condition (`syncStalledCondition`, `internal/controller/upgrade_stalled.go`; constants `ConditionTypeStalled`, `ConditionReasonUpgradeFailed`, `ConditionReasonUpgradePaused`, `ConditionReasonUpgradeNotStopped` in `internal/controller/conditions.go`): `True` while the record is `Failed` (reason `UpgradeFailed`) or `Paused` (`UpgradePaused`), its message naming the target and the last error; `False` (`UpgradeNotStopped`) once the upgrade goes on, completes or its record is dropped; absent on a cluster whose upgrade never stopped. `Ready` stays as it is. kstatus checks the generic conditions before anything else and returns Failed for `Stalled=True`, whatever `Ready` says; a `Stalled` that is `False`, or absent, is passed over and `Ready` decides (`checkGenericProperties` in `pkg/kstatus/status/generic.go` of `sigs.k8s.io/cli-utils`, master, read 2026-10-25, and the same function in Flux's fork `github.com/fluxcd/cli-utils`, main) — so a Flux Kustomization health-checking the cluster fails after an image change that did not land, as with v1.22.0's `Failed` phase. It is set in the status write that records the upgrade's state — `patchUpgradeStatus` (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade_statemachine.go`), `updateUpgradeStatus` (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade.go`, the `Completed` write) and the record's drop in `forgetAbandonedUpgrade` — through `SetNamedCondition`, so it adds no write and no event (id 136).
  - **The record remembers.** Every attempt `startRollingUpgrade` makes writes a record naming its target, also when a gate refuses it before Staging (`failUpgrade`'s `prepare`): the record used to keep the previous upgrade's target, so a Paused one could not be told from a changed tag. An attempt that takes up a held upgrade from the same `status.version` (`sameHeldUpgrade`) keeps its `startTime` and `progress`.
  - **No going back past a server that started on a target.** A server whose Neo4j container started on a target has had its store opened by it; one that never started has not. The operator records which: `status.upgradeStatus.startedTargetVersion` is `previousVersion` when the record is written (`newUpgradeRecord`), and becomes the target once a server pod's neo4j container runs that tag and has started on it — running, or exited after running, or restarted (`containerStartedOn`; a crash loop counts, it may have touched the store) — never while it waits on `ErrImagePull`, `ImagePullBackOff`, `InvalidImageName` or `CreateContainerConfigError`. `recordStartedTarget` reads the pods on every Rolling pass, before a retarget, before `startRollingUpgrade` takes a held upgrade up, and on the normal passes that hold one (the image may have become pullable); it writes only when the field changes. A record that takes up a held upgrade from the same `status.version` keeps it, so a retarget does not forget a version an earlier target started a server on. While the upgrade is unfinished (any phase but `Completed`), `validateAgainstRunning` refuses a `spec.image.tag` below it (`validateTagAgainstUpgrade` through `upgradeFloor`, same ordering as id 139, patch only when both name one): rolling it back is a downgrade. The message names the field ("servers this upgrade restarted have run 2026.09.0-enterprise (status.upgradeStatus.startedTargetVersion)"), or, when it is the target, keeps the earlier wording. A tag between it and `targetVersion` is allowed: a Paused upgrade to a tag that was never published (`2026.09.9-enterprise`, server-2 in `ErrImagePull`) goes on towards an earlier fixed tag (`2026.09.0-enterprise`) or back to `status.version` — the "fixed image" the upgrades guide promises — through id 162's repair. The retarget in `handleRollingUpgrade` reads the pods and checks the new tag again before it re-stages: validation may have read the record before a server started, and a refused tag waits for the next pass's validation to report it. A record an earlier operator wrote (no `startedTargetVersion`) keeps that operator's rule, read from progress: `progress.inProgress` or `progress.upgraded` above zero means the walk lowered the partition for a server, and the tag is held to `targetVersion` (`upgradeRestartedAServer`); the field is filled in the first time a pass sees a server started on the target. With an earlier release's CRDs installed the API server drops the field, so the earlier rule applies until they are applied; every pass that sees a started server sends it again, a write that changes nothing (the resourceVersion does not move), logged at V(1) rather than at info on every pass, as for id 153's reported versions. Before anything started, the tag may go back to `status.version`; the machine then runs an upgrade to that version, which every server still runs (read from the code, not walked live).
  - **An abandoned record is dropped.** A `Paused` or `Failed` record whose target `spec.image.tag` no longer names, that no server started on (`startedTargetVersion` is `previousVersion`, or an earlier operator's record released no server), that resolved no version, and whose target the server StatefulSet's template does not carry (a check refused it before Staging, or the tag moved back before it went on), is set to nil on the next normal pass (`forgetAbandonedUpgrade`, re-read inside `RetryOnConflict`, only if it is still that record). Nothing would take it up — the image change it was for is gone — and the cluster's message said "its upgrade to X failed …, and the operator takes it up again" for as long as it stayed (#562 walk: still there 5 minutes after the tag went back to `status.version`). A record with no target (an earlier operator's pause before the pre-upgrade check) waits for the user as before; one whose template carries its target is the machine's to finish. **A target without a patch** is not ordered against a patch of its line, so on its own this let the v1.22.0 journey set `5.26-enterprise` (5.26.32) back to `5.26.31-enterprise` and roll server-2 down: the tag is also held to what a restarted server reports, `status.upgradeStatus.resolvedTargetVersion` (id 153).
  - **Floating tags verify.** `versionsMatch` (Verifying) compares major and minor (year and month) after the 2025.01 ↔ 5.27 kernel alias, and the patch only when the tag names one, as id 139 orders patch-less tags. Version comparisons in the upgrade path, checked: `versionsMatch` (changed); `ValidateVersionUpgrade`/`validateVersionMove`/`isDowngrade` (already patch-aware); `validateTagAgainstUpgrade` (new, same); `isUpgradeRequired`'s retry, `clusterVersionToRecord`, `isStandaloneUpgradeRequired`, the mid-flight retarget in `handleRollingUpgrade` (tag strings against tag strings — exact on purpose: they ask whether the image changed, not whether two versions are one); `Client.CheckUpgradeCompatibility` — removed from the pre-upgrade check (`ValidateUpgradeSafety` judges health only): it compared `dbms.components()` with the tag string, never equal for an `-enterprise` tag so it refused nothing there, while a bare tag (`5.26.31`) whose repo alone changed was refused as "the same as current version" and the upgrade `Failed` (#538); a same-version image change is one the servers need, and the version is the version rule's to judge — the start check now holds the tag to what SHOW SERVERS reports instead (id 153); `neo4j.ParseVersion` (no patch semantics; its callers gate features on major.minor, or read a server's real version).
- **why:** found in review and confirmed from the code (`createOrUpdateResourceInternal` kept the live partition only under `upgradeStateMachineActive`), reproduced in envtest: one normal pass after a Paused or Failed upgrade that had rolled server-2 set the partition from 2 to 0, so the StatefulSet controller rolled server-1 and server-0 onto the target with only the readiness probe between them — the #488 hazard. The cluster went `Forming` meanwhile (dependents paused), then `Ready` with `status.version` still the old version, so moving the tag back to it passed id 139's rule and rolled every server back down; and a Paused upgrade could never go on (`isUpgradeRequired` returned on Paused, `holdImageDriftUntilReady` pinned any new tag), so the docs' "left as-is for you to investigate" was untrue twice. Verifying compared the patch even for a patch-less tag, so an upgrade to `2026.08-enterprise` — on Docker Hub the same digest as `2026.08.1-enterprise` (read 2026-10-17) — failed with "running 2026.08.1, expected 2026.08-enterprise". Neo4j (Upgrade and Migration Guide, `/current/`, read 2026-10-17): "Neo4j does not support downgrades. If the upgrade or migration is not successful, you have to do a full rollback, including restoring a pre-upgrade or a pre-migration backup"; a rolling upgrade takes servers "offline and upgraded one at a time", and "you must ensure that the cluster returns to a stable state before moving on to the next" (`version-2025-2026/upgrade/in-place-rolling`); "From version 5.9, the `system` database is automatically upgraded when safe" (`version-5/upgrade-minor/in-place-rolling`), so a cluster stopped half-way runs mixed versions as Neo4j's procedure expects. Operations Manual `/current/` `clustering/servers`: SHOW SERVERS `version` is the "Neo4j version the server is running". The manuals do not describe patch-less image tags (Docker introduction page, `/current/`). Found later, by code reading (#538, #531, #550, #553, 2026-10-24) and reproduced in unit tests and envtest: the refused-start flap, the ungated roll after a storage expansion, the digest split, the dead `CheckUpgradeCompatibility`, the `UpgradeDeferred` after a fixed refusal and the drain's on every retry, and the "paused" message of a resumed upgrade, each described above. Found on the way: `ConfigMapManager.setOwnerReference` built the ConfigMap's owner reference from the cluster's `TypeMeta`, which a read through an uncached reader leaves empty, and the API server refuses an owner reference without an apiVersion and kind; it now names the operator's own kind (`metav1.NewControllerRef`). The live walk of the combined build (#562, 2026-10-24) found the rest: a Paused upgrade to `2026.09.9-enterprise`, which was never published (server-2 in `ErrImagePull`), could not be pointed at `2026.09.0-enterprise` — "has already restarted servers on it", because progress said the walk had released server-2 — and the cluster went `Failed`, although the guide promised that a fixed image goes on; the repair walk's message named "clb-server-2 (Unavailable)" and never said the upgrade was in progress; the record of a refused start stayed after the tag went back, with its "takes it up again" message; and a cluster on `2026.08.1-enterprise@sha256:…` went `Failed` at once, the ConfigMap refused for its `app.kubernetes.io/version` label.
- **pinned-by:** `TestFailedUpgrade_AServerShortIsRecordedThenDegraded` (`internal/controller/upgrade_failed_shortfall_test.go`: the walk's cluster, `Failed` with server-2's pod not Ready — no retry, the normal pass's shortfall `Ready` with `Degraded=True` naming server-2, then phase `Degraded` with one `ClusterDegraded`, `Stalled` `True` throughout, no retry while short, and the retry once every server is back), `TestFailedUpgrade_ARefusedRetryWaitsForTheInterval` (same file), and the every-pod-Ready precondition of `TestIsUpgradeRequired_FailedRetry` (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade_statemachine_test.go`); `TestStalled_FollowsTheUpgradeRecordInTheSameWrite` (`internal/controller/upgrade_stalled_test.go`: absent while the upgrade runs; `True` with `UpgradeFailed` or `UpgradePaused` and the last error once it stops, `Ready` still `True` and no write of its own; `False` when it is taken up again, and after the `Completed` write), `TestStalled_ClearedWhenAnAbandonedUpgradeIsDropped` (same file), the `Stalled` entry of `TestConditionGuidanceCoversEveryConditionType` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/explain_test.go`); `TestArgoCDHealthChecks_AStoppedUpgradeIsNotHealthy` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/argocd_health_test.go`: the cluster check's `Ready` branch, read by its conditions: `Failed` Degraded, `Paused` Suspended, both with `status.message`, Healthy only otherwise; it fails on the v1.22.0 check), `TestStatusProblems_ReportsAClusterWhoseUpgradeStopped`, `TestDiagnose_AClusterWhoseUpgradeStoppedHasAProblem` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/stopped_upgrade_test.go`); `TestFailUpgrade_ARetriedFailureIsAnnouncedOncePerTargetAndCause` (`internal/controller/upgrade_failed_cycle_test.go`: four failures of one target with one cause, one `UpgradeFailed` and no `ClusterReady`; a new cause and a new target, one more each); `TestFailUpgrade_AStoppedUpgradeIsNotAnError` (`internal/controller/walk_noise_test.go`: Paused and Failed, no ERROR, one info line, one event, no reconcile error) and `TestFailUpgrade_RoutesPausedAndFailed` (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade_statemachine_test.go`: the cluster stays `Ready`, its message naming the paused or failed upgrade); `TestContainerStartedOn`, `TestNewUpgradeRecord_StartedTargetVersion`, `TestRecordStartedTarget`, `TestRecordStartedTarget_IsReadWhereTheTagRuleActs` (a pass of the walk, a held upgrade taken up, a normal pass that holds one), `TestRecordStartedTarget_ADroppedFieldIsNotAnInfoLineOnEveryPass`, `TestHandleRollingUpgrade_ARetargetBelowWhatAServerStartedOnWaits`, `TestStartRollingUpgrade_ARepairOnADegradedClusterSaysTheUpgradeIsInProgress`, `TestForgetAbandonedUpgrade` (one subtest runs a whole `Reconcile`) (`internal/controller/upgrade_started_target_test.go`); `TestValidateWithWarnings_HoldsTheTagToWhatAServerStartedOn` (`internal/validation/upgrade_hold_validation_test.go`: six cases, an earlier record's rule among them); the envtest spec "replaces the servers that cannot start on the target when the tag moves on" (`internal/controller/upgrade_held_servers_test.go`), now a table with the entry "an earlier fixed tag, after a pause on one that was never published": a real `Reconcile` takes the Paused upgrade to `Rolling` towards the earlier tag, never `Failed`; `TestVersionLabelValue`, `TestEveryObjectLabelledWithTheTagHasAValidLabel` (`internal/resources/version_label_test.go`); `TestParseVersion` (`internal/neo4j/version_test.go`) and `TestValidateVersionUpgrade` (`internal/validation/upgrade_validator_test.go`), their digest cases. Each fails with its part reverted under `go test -overlay`. Since #538: `TestHoldImageDriftUntilReady_ATagWithADigestIsPinnedWhole`, `TestHoldImageDriftUntilReady_HoldsTheOrphanedServersImage`, `TestHoldImageDriftUntilReady_AnnouncesADeferralThatOutlastsAPass` (`internal/controller/cluster_upgrade_gate_test.go`); `TestStartRollingUpgrade_ARefusedStartLeavesTheClusterReadyAndIsAnnouncedOnce`, `TestStartRollingUpgrade_ARefusedStartPausesWithTheClusterReady`, `TestStartRollingUpgrade_AResumedUpgradeIsNoLongerCalledPaused`, `TestStartRollingUpgrade_AWaitForADrainIsAnnouncedOnce` (`internal/controller/upgrade_start_test.go`); the envtest spec "creates the StatefulSet a storage expansion deleted on the image the servers run" (`internal/controller/upgrade_held_servers_test.go`); `TestSetOwnerReference_NeedsNoTypeMeta` (`internal/controller/configmap_manager_test.go`); `TestValidateUpgradeSafety_JudgesHealthNotTheTag` (`internal/neo4j/upgrade_safety_test.go`: on the earlier client it fails, the bare tag `5.26.31` refused as the version the servers already run); `TestGetImageVersion` (`internal/neo4j/version_test.go`); `TestDeployedOperatorVersion` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate_test.go`). Each fails with its part reverted under `go test -overlay`. Earlier: the envtest specs "Neo4jEnterpriseCluster whose upgrade is Paused or Failed" (`internal/controller/upgrade_hold_test.go`: a real `Reconcile` against the API server keeps partition 2 for Paused and Failed while the ConfigMap and Services are reconciled, and refuses the tag moved back to `status.version` with the partition and template unchanged); `TestCreateOrUpdateResource_KeepsTheHeldUpgradesPartition`, `TestIsUpgradeRequired_HeldUpgrade`, `TestStartRollingUpgrade_TakesUpAHeldUpgrade`, `TestStartRollingUpgrade_ARefusedAttemptNamesItsTarget`, `TestServerStatefulSetFullyRolled_HeldUpgrade`, `TestClusterReadyMessage`, `TestStepUpgradeRolling_ARetriedUpgradeRollsTheRestThroughTheGates` (`internal/controller/upgrade_hold_unit_test.go`); `TestVersionsMatch_FloatingTags`, `TestParseVersion` (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade_version_test.go`); `TestValidateWithWarnings_ChecksTheTagAgainstAnUpgradeThatRestartedAServer` (`internal/validation/upgrade_hold_validation_test.go`). Each wiring switched off fails its tests: the update-branch partition (both envtest entries and the update cases), the create branch, `isUpgradeRequired` (three cases), the record carry-over and the refused attempt's record, the held-rollout readiness (four cases), the validator rule (six cases and the envtest refusal), and the old `versionsMatch` (six cases).
- **enforcement:** unit test + envtest. Walked live on be1e0a6f: B1, `Stalled`, the Argo CD check (run in gopher-lua) and the CLI passed; a `Failed` upgrade short of a server was the finding fixed above (its retry waits for every server).

### id 150 — The spec a user applied is the spec the API server keeps: defaults stay in the reconcile's copy, and every write to a CR is a patch of what it changed
- **scope:** `patchObject`, `mergePatchSince`, `addFinalizer`, `removeFinalizer` (`internal/controller/object_patch.go`), and every finalizer add and release, annotation write and spec write of an operator CR in `internal/controller/`: the cluster and standalone reconcilers' `Reconcile` and `handleDeletion` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`); `setDrainServers` (`internal/controller/scale_down.go`); `setDeploymentAnnotation` (`internal/controller/aura_fleet_provision.go`); `EnsureSeedCredsProjected` (`internal/controller/cluster_seed_creds.go`); `updateTargetSpec` (`internal/controller/restore_target.go`); the user, role, rolebinding, authrule, database, composite database, database alias, replica database, backup, restore and plugin reconcilers' `Reconcile` and `handleDeletion`; the restore's annotation writers (`setRestoreInProgressAnnotation`, `clearRestoreInProgressAnnotation`, `markCypherRestoreIssued`, `markRestorePreHooksRan`, `markCypherRestoreObservedOffline`, the seed-proxy wait anchor, `setRestoreAnnotation` in `internal/controller/restore_point_in_time.go`); the one write helper of each Aura controller (`patchInstance`, `patchCMK`, `patch`); `ApplyDefaults` and `applyAuthProviderDefaults` (`internal/validation/cluster_validator.go`); `AuthenticationProvidersSetting`, `AuthorizationProvidersSetting` and `BuildAuthConfig` (`internal/resources/cluster.go`); the cluster entry of `validators` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **No Update of a CR.** Everything the operator writes to one of its custom resources (a finalizer, an annotation, the opted-in seed-credentials `extraEnvFrom` entry, a restore's `env` and `extraEnvFrom` on its target) is a JSON merge patch computed between two copies of the object, carrying its resourceVersion (`patchObject`), so a concurrent write is a conflict as it is for an Update. The exceptions are a finalizer's removal (`removeFinalizer`: a JSON patch that tests the finalizer at its index and removes it there, id 189) and its addition (`addFinalizer`: a JSON patch that tests the list the copy has and appends to it, id 199), so a status write since the copy was read is no conflict and another writer's finalizer is still never lost. Never `Update` one: an Update sends the whole object as the Go types encode it. That writes the cluster reconcile's defaults into the stored spec. It turns a `false` the CRD defaults to `true` back into `true`, because a non-pointer `bool` with `omitempty` is left out and the API server defaults it: `Neo4jRole.enforcePrivileges`, `Neo4jAuthRule.enforceRoles`, `Neo4jPlugin.enabled`, `Neo4jDatabase.ifNotExists` and `wait`, a cloud identity's `autoCreate.enabled`, `monitoring.enabled`, `upgradeStrategy.preUpgradeHealthCheck`, `postUpgradeHealthCheck`, `autoPauseOnFailure`, `mcp.readOnly`. It drops a `false` the CRD has no default for (`AuraInstance.deletionProtection`, `paused`). And it rewrites a quantity in its canonical spelling (`0.5` becomes `500m`). Status goes through the status subresource, which ignores the spec. Child objects the operator renders (StatefulSets, Services, ConfigMaps, Jobs) are not CRs and are not covered here.
  - **Defaults stay in memory, and only fill gaps.** `ApplyDefaults` works on the copy the reconcile uses and on the copy `kubectl neo4j validate` decodes, and nothing writes either back. It never replaces a value the user set. The auth provider lists get Neo4j's default, `native` (each on its own: configuration settings, Operations Manual `/5/` and `/current/`, read 2026-10-17): both lists when the user names no authentication provider, the authorization list only when the user set it nowhere. A setting in `spec.config` counts as set, because a list the operator writes takes its setting from `spec.config` (`BuildAuthConfig`). A cluster that names authentication providers and no authorization providers gets no authorization line, as before: writing `native` would change its neo4j.conf, and restart its servers on an operator upgrade, for the value Neo4j uses anyway.
  - **A schema default is the schema's.** The defaults `ApplyDefaults` shares with the CRD (`image.pullPolicy`, `storage.retentionPolicy`, the mode of a `tls` block, the type of a `service` block) say what the `+kubebuilder:default` markers say: the API server fills them in before the operator reads an object, so the CLI needs them to judge a manifest as the operator does. A `tls` block without a mode is `cert-manager`. No `tls` block is TLS disabled and no `service` block a ClusterIP Service; those are the operator's reading of an absent block, not schema defaults.
  - **The CLI judges what the API server stores, for every kind.** `kubectl neo4j validate` applies every default the kind's CRD declares to the document before the kind's validator runs (`withSchemaDefaults`, `applyDefaults` in `cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate_schema_defaults.go`), from the CRDs embedded in the binary (`config/crd/embed.go`, package `crd`: `bases/neo4j.neo4j.com_neo4j*.yaml`; only the CLI imports it, the operator image does not carry it), with the API server's structural defaulting: a property absent, or null where the schema does not allow null, takes a fresh copy of its default; a defaulted block gets its own defaults; arrays and maps are walked element by element. The cluster's `ApplyDefaults` then runs as before. No `k8s.io/apiextensions-apiserver` code: the walk is short and pinned by its own test. Before it, only the cluster had defaults in the CLI, four of them: a standalone's `tls: {}` had no mode where the operator reads `cert-manager` and requires an `issuerRef`, and a bool the CRD defaults to `true` read `false` (#540).
  - **Objects an earlier operator wrote:** nothing is rewritten back. Their stored spec keeps what the earlier operator's Update left: the cluster's defaults, a `true` over the user's `false`, a dropped `false`, canonical quantities. Every kind then behaves as its stored spec says, as it did before the upgrade, so an operator upgrade changes nothing a deployment runs and restarts nothing. The user's next apply puts back each field their manifest sets, and that change takes effect as any edit would: a role whose `enforcePrivileges: false` is put back stops revoking, a plugin whose `enabled: false` is put back is taken off its target (id 160). The rest stays until they remove it; removing a default restarts nothing.
- **why:** the cluster reconciler applied its defaults to the object it then sent back with the finalizer, on every cluster's first accepted pass. The stored spec gained fields the user never wrote, which a GitOps diff of the live object shows, and a wrong default became permanent: `ApplyDefaults` replaced `authorizationProviders` with `[native]` whenever `authenticationProviders` was empty, so a cluster mapping LDAP or OIDC groups over native sign-in ran with native authorization and its stored spec said so, and the native lists it filled in also took the provider settings out of `spec.config`. Driving the first passes against envtest showed the whole-object Update did the same to every kind: a cluster's and a standalone's `monitoring.enabled: false` came back `true`, so the diagnostics and metrics the user switched off came back; a `Neo4jRole`'s `enforcePrivileges: false` came back `true`, so the role controller revoked privileges the user had chosen to keep; a `Neo4jPlugin`'s `enabled: false` came back `true`, which writes its settings (procedure allowlists) into its target's neo4j.conf; a `Neo4jDatabase`'s `ifNotExists` and `wait`, a `Neo4jAuthRule`'s `enforceRoles`, and a backup's or restore's `autoCreate.enabled` likewise; an `AuraInstance` lost its explicit `deletionProtection: false`; `cpu: "0.5"` came back `500m`. The scale-down, Aura fleet and restore-in-progress annotation writes, the seed-credentials projection and a restore's env write sent the whole object too. `ApplyDefaults` also gave a `tls` block without a mode `disabled` where the CRD, whose default the operator reads, says `cert-manager`, so `kubectl neo4j validate` skipped every TLS rule the operator applies to it. Diagnosed from the code and envtest, not walked live.
- **pinned-by:** `TestReconcile_LeavesTheSpecAsApplied_APIServer` (`internal/controller/stored_spec_test.go`: envtest with the CRDs and their defaults; a cluster and a standalone applied as JSON, then every writer above, comparing the stored spec with the applied one after each; each writer's step fails with its Update restored); `TestFinalizers_LeaveEveryKindsSpecAsApplied_APIServer` (same file: the user, role, rolebinding, authrule, plugin, database, backup, restore, composite database, alias and replica database applied as JSON with every bool the CRD defaults to `true` set to `false`, through their first and deletion passes, and `patchInstance` for the Aura helpers; the role, authrule, plugin, database, backup, restore and AuraInstance cases fail with the earlier Updates restored, the others have no such field and guard the same contract); `TestClusterValidator_ApplyDefaults_KeepsTheUsersAuthProviders`, `TestClusterValidator_ApplyDefaults_AgreesWithTheCRDDefaults` (reads the CRD YAML), `TestClusterValidator_ApplyDefaults_KeepsEverySetValue`, `TestClusterValidator_ApplyDefaults_RendersAsTheDefaultsAnEarlierOperatorStored` (`internal/validation/cluster_defaults_test.go`); `TestValidate_ATLSBlockWithoutAModeIsCertManager`, `TestValidate_JudgesTheAuthorizationProvidersAsWritten` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate_operator_parity_test.go`); `TestValidate_AStandaloneTLSBlockWithoutAModeIsCertManager` (fails without the schema defaults), `TestWithSchemaDefaults_FillsWhatTheAPIServerStores`, `TestApplyDefaults_Structural` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate_schema_defaults_test.go`). The auth, CRD-default and CLI tests fail on the earlier `ApplyDefaults`; `KeepsEverySetValue` and `RendersAsTheDefaultsAnEarlierOperatorStored` guard the other way: no set value replaced, no restart on upgrade.
- **enforcement:** unit test + envtest. No machine check that a NEW writer patches — convention + code review: a write of a CR goes through `patchObject`, `addFinalizer` or `removeFinalizer`. The tests write the CRs the same way, which the advisory `make check-test-cr-updates` checks (id 205).

### id 151 — Every ExternalSecrets block the operator builds from is validated like the TLS one
- **scope:** `ValidateExternalSecretsConfig` (`internal/validation/external_secrets_validator.go`), its callers in `tls_validator.go` and `AuthValidator.Validate` (`internal/validation/auth_validator.go`); the `SecretStoreRef == nil` guard in `BuildExternalSecretForTLS` / `BuildExternalSecretForAuth` (`internal/resources/cluster.go`).
- **rule:** A cluster builds an ExternalSecret from `spec.tls.externalSecrets` and from `spec.auth.externalSecrets`, and the builder dereferences `secretStoreRef`. Both blocks, when enabled, go through `ValidateExternalSecretsConfig`: a `secretStoreRef` with a name and a supported kind, a parseable refresh interval, and at least one complete `data` mapping. The builders also refuse to build without a store, so an unvalidated path cannot panic. The standalone never builds from `spec.auth.externalSecrets`, so it is not judged there.
- **why:** only the TLS block was validated. `spec.auth.externalSecrets.enabled: true` without `secretStoreRef` reached `buildExternalSecret` and panicked with a nil dereference on every reconcile pass (found while fixing the Route panic, rule 148).
- **pinned-by:** `TestAuthValidator_ChecksTheAuthExternalSecretsLikeTLS` (`internal/validation/external_secrets_validator_test.go`); `TestBuildExternalSecret_WithoutAStoreBuildsNothing` (`internal/resources/external_secret_store_test.go`) — both fail without the fix.
- **enforcement:** unit test.

### id 152 — A plugin write that repairs its own failing init container does not wait for the target it keeps unavailable, and nothing else is let through
- **scope:** `selfRepair`, `podsDownOnlyIn`, `unavailableServerOutside`, `initContainerFailure`, `initContainerWriteChanges`, `podInitContainer`, `statefulSetOrdinal`, `failingPods`, `replaceStuckPods` (`internal/controller/plugin_target_gate.go`); the `mayWrite` closure, the availability gate, the functional check and the repair announcement of `Reconcile` (`internal/controller/plugin_controller.go`); `EventReasonPluginInitContainerFailing` (`internal/controller/events.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **The exception to id 141's gate:** a write of a `VerifiedDownload` plugin goes to a target that is not `Ready` with every server available when all of these hold, and only then (`selfRepair`):
    1. the write changes the plugin's init container (`plugin-init-<name>`) or a volume that container mounts (`initContainerWriteChanges`: a new checksum, URL, auth Secret or CA list — what the container runs with). A write that leaves the failing container as it is repairs nothing and waits.
    2. Every server pod that is not `Ready` is failing in that init container: it ran and exited non-zero and has not succeeded since (`initContainerFailure`: `Init:Error`, or `Init:CrashLoopBackOff` waiting or running again after a failed run). Not a container still starting, pulling its image, or failing in another init container. No pod may be missing or being deleted.
    3. Those pods are the highest ordinals, at or above the StatefulSet's partition (`podsDownOnlyIn`). The StatefulSet controller (Parallel pod management, the cluster's) replaces the highest-ordinal pod not on the new revision first, healthy or not, and the next only once that one is ready; so it replaces the failing pods before it touches a server that is up.
    4. On a cluster, Neo4j's last view agrees: no server in `status.diagnostics.servers` is anything but `Available` unless its address names one of those pods (`unavailableServerOutside`; an unknown address counts as outside). A pod can read `Ready` while its server is out of the cluster (the readiness probe passes during cluster formation), and the roll after the repair goes by pod readiness. No diagnostics (`spec.monitoring.enabled: false`) means only the pod view decides.
  - **The same for the plugin's settings (id 155):** conditions (1) and (2) also hold, for a plugin in any install mode, when the write changes the env it gives the neo4j container and every pod that is not `Ready` keeps failing in its neo4j container while running a variable the write changes; (3) and (4) as above.
  - **Why it cannot roll a cluster short of a server for an unrelated reason:** a server down for any other reason — a pod not `Ready` without this failure, a pod missing or terminating, a server Neo4j reports unavailable — fails (2) or (4), and the write waits as before. A failing pod below a `Ready` one fails (3): the roll would take the `Ready` one first (measured on Kind v1.34: with `par-1` failing and `par-2` healthy, the fixing write deleted `par-2` first, two pods down at once). When the exception holds, the write replaces only servers that are already down until they are back, and then rolls the rest one at a time, as every plugin install does.
  - **What the pass does:** no functional (Bolt) check before the write — the servers it repairs are down, and a standalone whose one server is down answers nothing; phase `Installing`, "Writing the plugin to Neo4jEnterpriseCluster … although it is not Ready: its init container keeps c-server-2 (init container plugin-init-apoc exited with code 60) from starting, and this write changes that container; the roll replaces those pods first", with one `PluginInitContainerFailing` Warning from the status write that records it (ids 136, 145). The write is then waited for like any other (id 141); the next passes have nothing to write and say nothing more.
  - **It judges the plugin's next write** (`desiredTarget`): its install edits, or, for a plugin set to `spec.enabled: false`, its removal (id 160) — disabling the plugin whose init container or setting keeps the down servers failing goes through at once, as removing the setting does.
  - **`MaxUnavailableStatefulSet` (a note, #548):** condition (3) and the roll after the repair rely on the StatefulSet controller replacing the highest-ordinal pod not on the update revision whether it is healthy or not — its behaviour while the feature gate `MaxUnavailableStatefulSet` is off, as measured here on Kind v1.34. With the gate on, the controller of 1.35.0–1.35.3 holds a rolling update while `maxUnavailable` pods (1 by default) are already unavailable, so the repair write did not replace a cluster's stuck pod — only a standalone's, which the plugin deletes itself (`replaceStuckPods`); 1.37's replaces old-revision pods that are not available first. Read from the controller source in round 8, and fixed: the cluster deletes such a pod itself once the StatefulSet controller has seen the repair (`reconcileStuckServerPods`, one `ServerPodReplaced` event; id 204). The gate is alpha and off through Kubernetes 1.34 (the Kind anchor, `kindest/node:v1.34.0`), beta and on by default in 1.35.0–1.35.3, beta and off again in 1.35.4–1.36, and on by default from 1.37 (`pkg/features/kube_features.go` at those tags). Walk a cluster repair on `kindest/node:v1.35.1` (release_verification.md).
  - **A standalone's stuck pod is deleted:** its StatefulSet uses OrderedReady pod management, which waits for a pod that is not `Ready` before it rolls a new template out — Kubernetes' documented "forced rollback" (measured on Kind v1.34: the pod stayed on the broken revision a minute after the fixing write). `replaceStuckPods` deletes a standalone's pod that is failing in this plugin's init container, not `Ready`, and on an older revision than the StatefulSet's `updateRevision`, once the StatefulSet's controller has seen its latest template (`status.observedGeneration`) — before, the pod would come back on the broken template. One `PluginInitContainerFailing` Normal event per deletion; the UID precondition keeps a recreated pod safe; volume claims stay. A cluster's pods are its StatefulSet's to replace.
- **why:** the v1.22.0 journey (2026-10-17, CalVer 2026.08.1, 3 servers) deadlocked at 09:53 CEST: with server-2 in `Init:Error` in the plugin's init container (its CA bundle could not verify GitHub, id 147), removing the CAs from `spec.trustedCASecrets` left the plugin `Waiting` with one `PluginInstallDeferred` — its target was not `Ready` with every server available — while `plugin-ca` still projected the removed CA, so server-2 could not start, and only the plugin's write would change that. Only deleting the CA Secret by hand (the projections are optional) recovered it. The same wait held any fix of an install that broke a server (a wrong checksum fixed in the CR): the old gate waited for a server that only the fix could bring back.
- **pinned-by:** `TestReconcile_ARepairOfItsOwnInitContainerDoesNotWait` (trusted CAs removed from a Ready plugin's target; a checksum fixed during the install that broke the server), `TestReconcile_ARepairWaitsWhenSomethingElseIsDown` (ten ways something else is down, each waiting; each fails against an exception without the guard that protects it — any failing pod, no diagnostics check, any write), `TestReconcile_DisablingThePluginThatKeepsServersDownDoesNotWait` (`internal/controller/plugin_disabled_test.go`: waits when the exception judges the install instead of the removal), `TestReconcile_AStandalonesStuckPodIsReplacedAfterTheRepair`, `TestReplaceStuckPods`, `TestInitContainerFailure`, `TestStatefulSetOrdinal`, `TestPluginWritesFollowTheTargetsRollout_APIServer` (envtest: the journey's deadlock against a real API server, and the wait while server-0 is also down) (`internal/controller/plugin_target_gate_test.go`). Without `selfRepair` the repair tests fail with the plugin `Waiting`, as on the journey; without `replaceStuckPods` the standalone's pod stays.
- **enforcement:** unit test + envtest; walked live on Kind v1.34 (2026-10-17, CEST), a 3-server 2026.08.1 cluster and a `VerifiedDownload` APOC 2026.08.1 core JAR: (1) installed from its GitHub release URL, `Installing` through its roll (13:21:42–13:25:16), `Ready` 13:25:22. (2) `corp-ca` added to `spec.trustedCASecrets` at 13:26:40: `Waiting`, one `PluginInstallDeferred`, in that second; the cluster's truststore roll to 13:30:07; the plugin's write at 13:30:12; `Ready` after its roll, 13:33:35; every server's `plugin-init-apoc` "trusting the default CAs (/cacert.pem) and the custom CAs", `apoc.version()` on all three. (3) The CA removed and its Secret deleted at once (13:34:20): `Waiting`, then `Installing` "Updating the plugin's trusted CAs …" at 13:37:56, `Ready` 13:41:19, no pod in `ContainerCreating`. (4) The plugin pointed at an in-cluster HTTPS mirror signed by a CA nothing trusted: server-2 `Init:Error` (curl exit 60), plugin `Installing` "… cannot start: live-server-2 (… exited with code 60)", cluster `Forming`; the mirror's CA added at 13:42:33: the cluster's truststore write replaced server-2, still failing; the plugin's repair at 13:42:46 with one `PluginInitContainerFailing` Warning; server-2 running 13:42:56, servers 1 and 0 rolled after it; `Ready` 13:46:10, the JAR from the mirror on all three. (5) A wrong checksum, then its fix (13:52:30 / 13:53:24): the repair was written in the same second, only server-2 restarted, `Ready` 13:54:43. Step (5)'s first walk found the cache read fixed in id 141 (the plugin `Ready` for 46 s while server-2 crash-looped); the second walk, on the fixed build, had none.

### id 153 — A tag without a patch is held to the version the servers report; a template write the StatefulSet controller has not seen is a rollout in flight
- **scope:** `Neo4jEnterpriseClusterStatus.RunningVersion`, `UpgradeStatus.ResolvedTargetVersion`, `Neo4jEnterpriseStandaloneStatus.RunningVersion` (`api/v1beta1/neo4jenterprisecluster_types.go`, `api/v1beta1/neo4jenterprisestandalone_types.go`); `tagFloats`, `runningVersionFor`, `resolvedTargetVersionFor`, `heldTargetServerVersion`, `highestReportedVersion` (`internal/controller/reported_version.go`); `recordClusterReady`, `reportedVersionsToRecord`, `updateClusterStatusRecording`, `serverStatefulSetFullyRolled`, `serverTemplateWriteUnobserved`, `noteServerStatefulSetWrite` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`); `serverRolloutInFlight` (`internal/controller/cluster_availability.go`); `stepUpgradeRolling`, `isServerAvailable` (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade_statemachine.go`); `verifyVersionUpgrade`, `updateUpgradeStatus` (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade.go`); `newUpgradeRecord` (`internal/controller/upgrade_hold.go`); `recordStandaloneRunningVersion` (`internal/controller/standalone_running_version.go`) and `updateStatus` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`); `reportedVersionsOutcome` and its use in `recordClusterReady` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`), `upgradeStaged` (`internal/controller/upgrade_hold.go`), `preUpgradeCheck` (`internal/controller/upgrade_start.go`); `GetServerList` (`internal/neo4j/client.go`); `validateTagAgainstUpgrade`, `validateTagAgainstRunningVersion`, `belowReported`, `TagBelowReportedVersion` (`internal/validation/upgrade_validator.go`), `validateAgainstRunning` (`internal/validation/cluster_validator.go`, `internal/validation/standalone_validator.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **The tag is not the version.** `status.version` and `status.upgradeStatus.targetVersion` stay image tags (id 144's contract). A tag that names a patch says the version; one that does not (`5.26-enterprise`, `2026.08-enterprise`) floats to the newest patch of its line, and the version rule cannot order a patch of the same line against it (ids 139, 149). For such a tag the operator records what the servers report in SHOW SERVERS `version` (the "Neo4j version the server is running", the source `Verifying` already trusts), the highest one when servers report different patches, and the version rule refuses a `spec.image.tag` below it, naming the version and the field: `the servers run 5.26.32 (status.runningVersion, what 5.26-enterprise in status.version resolved to)`, `they run 5.26.32 (status.upgradeStatus.resolvedTargetVersion)`. A tag without a patch on the same line is not below it. Nothing recorded yet: not checked, as before.
  - **`status.runningVersion`** describes `status.version`. Cluster: written on a Ready pass from the SHOW SERVERS the formation check already runs on every pass (`GetServerList` yields `version`), so it costs no query, and written only when it changes (id 122); also in the `Completed` write, from the list `Verifying` read, in the same write that moves `status.version`. While an upgrade is held, only the servers below the kept partition run `status.version`, and only theirs counts; a Paused or Failed record whose template never carried its target (a check refused it before Staging, `upgradeStaged`) holds nothing, and every server counts — it was left unrecorded for as long as such a record stayed (#550). A `status.version` that names a patch clears it. With an earlier release's CRDs installed, the API server drops the field and every Ready pass sends it again (the first pass after the CRDs are applied records it); that write changes nothing and is logged at V(1), as the standalone's, not at Info on every pass (`reportedVersionsOutcome`, #550). Standalone: read once its pod is Ready, with one SHOW SERVERS, and read again only when its container runs another image (pod UID + `imageID`; a floating tag with `pullPolicy: Always` can change the patch at any restart); the status is compared with that read on every pass and written while it does not say it, so a field the old CRD dropped is recorded on the first pass after the CRDs are applied, with no new query (`standaloneVersionRead`; the re-walk of 2026-10-23 found it recorded only after an operator restart when the read alone was remembered); `updateStatus` clears it when `status.version` moves.
  - **`status.upgradeStatus.resolvedTargetVersion`** describes a floating `targetVersion`: written when a restarted server passes the Rolling step's availability check (same SHOW SERVERS row), and, for a Paused or Failed upgrade, on the next Ready pass from the servers at or above the kept partition once the template carries the target (the v1.22.0 incident paused before the check saw server-2). Kept across a retarget and when a held upgrade is taken up (`newUpgradeRecord`); a new upgrade starts without it. `validateTagAgainstUpgrade` holds the tag to it once the upgrade has restarted a server.
  - **When an upgrade starts,** its pre-upgrade check holds the target to what SHOW SERVERS reports at that moment (`preUpgradeCheck`, `TagBelowReportedVersion`): a tag below the highest version a server reports is refused before Staging (id 149's refused start), naming the version. The fields above are written from a Ready pass, so a floating tag pulled again (`imagePullPolicy: Always`, a new node) can run a newer patch before they say so; the start is where that would turn into a rollback. **Still unchecked:** a target changed mid-upgrade before the restarted server's availability check has recorded what it runs (the re-stage asks Neo4j nothing); a standalone between a restart on a newer patch and its next pass; and an upgrade with `preUpgradeHealthCheck: false`, whose start reads nothing from Neo4j.
  - **Stale Ready.** `serverStatefulSetFullyRolled` and `serverRolloutInFlight` count a template write the StatefulSet controller has not observed (`observedGeneration < generation`), or a cached read older than the generation this pass wrote (same UID), as a rollout in flight. `status.observedGeneration` (and the conditions') is the generation the pass read and acted on, not the one the status write re-reads — the standalone's too (`updateStatus`; it stamped the re-read generation until #550). Rule 149's hold is unchanged: servers below a Paused or Failed upgrade's partition are not a rollout in flight.
- **why:** the v1.22.0 journey (2026-10-17): a 3-server cluster on `5.26.31-enterprise` with `upgradeStrategy {autoPauseOnFailure: true, upgradeTimeout: 30s}` was moved to `5.26-enterprise` (5.26.32 that day); the upgrade paused after server-2 restarted on 5.26.32; setting `5.26.31-enterprise` back was accepted and the operator rolled server-2 down to 5.26.31 (`UpgradeStarted "Rolling upgrade resumed after it was Paused: 5.26.31-enterprise -> 5.26.31-enterprise"`, `dbms.components()` 5.26.31), and `kubectl neo4j validate --connect` said ok. After the upgrade completes the same hole is open against every server: `status.version` reads `5.26-enterprise`. Neo4j (Upgrade and Migration Guide, `/current/`): "Neo4j does not support downgrades." Separately, the cluster could read its own stale Ready: right after its template write the StatefulSet's status still described the previous template, every replica updated and ready.
- **pinned-by:** `TestValidateWithWarnings_ChecksTheTagAgainstWhatAFloatingTargetResolvedTo`, `TestValidateWithWarnings_ChecksTheTagAgainstTheVersionTheServersReport`, `TestValidateWithWarnings_ChecksTheTagAgainstAnUpgradeThatRestartedAServer` (`internal/validation/upgrade_hold_validation_test.go`); `TestStandaloneValidator_Validate_ChecksTheTagAgainstTheVersionTheServerReports` (`internal/validation/standalone_validator_test.go`); `TestValidate_Connected_ChecksTheTagAgainstWhatTheServersReport` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate_floating_tag_test.go`); `TestReportedVersionsToRecord`, `TestRecordClusterReady_RecordsWhatTheServersReport`, `TestRecordClusterReady_RecordsWhatAHeldUpgradesTargetResolvedTo`, `TestStepUpgradeRolling_RecordsWhatTheRestartedServerReports`, `TestUpdateUpgradeStatus_CompletedRecordsWhatTheServersReport`, `TestNewUpgradeRecord_KeepsWhatTheTargetResolvedTo`, `TestRecordStandaloneRunningVersion_ReadsOncePerImage`, `TestRecordStandaloneRunningVersion_RecordsOnceTheCRDKeepsTheField`, `TestStandaloneUpdateStatus_ClearsTheRunningVersionWhenTheTagMoves` (`internal/controller/reported_version_test.go`); `TestServerStatefulSetFullyRolled_ATemplateWriteNotYetObservedIsInFlight`, `TestUpdateClusterStatus_RecordsTheGenerationThePassActedOn` (`internal/controller/stale_ready_test.go`); the envtest spec "Neo4jEnterpriseCluster on a tag without a patch" (`internal/controller/floating_tag_downgrade_test.go`: a real `Reconcile` refuses the tag set back below a Paused upgrade's resolved target with partition and template unchanged, refuses a patch below `status.runningVersion` after `Completed`, accepts the patch the servers run, and shows the API server keeps both fields). Each fails with its part of the fix switched off. Since #550: `TestStartRollingUpgrade_RefusesATargetBelowWhatAServerReports` (`internal/controller/upgrade_start_test.go`), the "had not staged, from a floating status.version" case of `TestReportedVersionsToRecord`, `TestRecordClusterReady_ADroppedReportedVersionIsNotAnInfoLineOnEveryPass` (`internal/controller/reported_version_old_crd_test.go`, which captures the log levels), `TestStandaloneUpdateStatus_RecordsTheGenerationThePassActedOn` (`internal/controller/stale_ready_test.go`); each fails with its part reverted under `go test -overlay`.
- **enforcement:** validator (inline in the reconcilers and the CLI) + unit and envtest tests.

### id 162 — A held upgrade whose restarted servers cannot start on its target goes on towards a new tag, replacing only those servers first
- **scope:** `heldUpgradeRepair`, `serversDownOnTarget`, `serversDown`, `podServerImage`, `podCannotStart`, `imageChanges` (`internal/controller/upgrade_repair.go`); the not-Ready branch of `isUpgradeRequired` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`); the `repair` path of `startRollingUpgrade`, and in `stepUpgradeStaging` the stored-record check (`storedUpgradeStatus`), the uncached StatefulSet read and the partition it freezes at (`stagingPartition`, `serverImage`) (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade_statemachine.go`); the helpers it shares with id 152, `podsDownOnlyIn`, `unavailableServerOutside`, `containerCrashLooping`, `initContainerFailure`, `statefulSetOrdinal` (`internal/controller/plugin_target_gate.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **When:** an upgrade is held (`Paused` or `Failed`, id 149), the cluster has formed (id 105), a `Paused` one does not wait for the user (`upgradeWaitsForUser`: `spec.image.tag` is not its target), and `spec.image.tag` names another image than the down servers run — the same image cannot start there either; it asks the pods, not the template, which after a StatefulSet created again or a pod recreated below the partition can name another image than a down server runs (#562 re-walk). Every server pod that is not `Ready` cannot start on the image it runs (`podCannotStart`: its neo4j container or an init container waits on an image pull that fails — `ErrImagePull`, `ImagePullBackOff`, `InvalidImageName` — or the neo4j container exited non-zero and has not run since, or an init container failed); those pods are the highest ordinals; no pod is missing or being deleted, every pod below them is `Ready` (`podsDownOnlyIn`); and Neo4j's last view (`status.diagnostics.servers`) names no other server unavailable (`unavailableServerOutside`; none recorded leaves the pods to decide). They are servers an upgrade restarted: at or above a partition between zero and the replica count, or, with every server frozen (the partition at the replica count), running another image than every server that is up — created from a revision the walk had not put them on. A server down on the image the others run is down for another reason. Then `isUpgradeRequired` starts the machine although the cluster is not `Ready` with every server available. The new tag may be later or earlier than the target: when no server started on the target — an image that cannot be pulled never runs — the version rule holds it only to what a server did start on (id 149, `status.upgradeStatus.startedTargetVersion`), so an upgrade paused on an unpublished tag goes on towards the earlier fixed one or back to `status.version`; the walk's message says the upgrade is in progress (id 149).
  - **What it does:** `startRollingUpgrade` runs the version rule, skips the "every replica ready" gate and the pre-upgrade health check (the cluster is short of exactly the servers being replaced), keeps the held upgrade's record (progress, start, resolved version: `newUpgradeRecord`) and announces `UpgradeStarted` "Rolling upgrade resumed after it was Paused: A -> C; c-server-2 (container neo4j: ImagePullBackOff) cannot start on B and is replaced first". Staging, whenever `serversDownIn` finds such servers — from the pods as the API server has them (id 203) — running another image than the target — this covers a target changed mid-walk while the server just restarted cannot start, too — freezes the partition at the lowest of them instead of at the replica count — or lower, at the lowest server that runs a newer revision than the StatefulSet's current one, which never goes below the partition (id 180). The StatefulSet controller replaces them on the new template before anything else (highest first); a server that is up restarts only after them. Where it does not — `MaxUnavailableStatefulSet` on, Kubernetes 1.35.0–1.35.3 — the walk deletes them itself (id 204). The walk goes on from there through its gates (id 127): the pods at or above the partition updated, every pod ready, the server at the partition available with its databases caught up.
  - **Staging runs once per target.** It reads the upgrade record as stored and the StatefulSet uncached (`APIReader`), and leaves everything as it is when the stored record has moved on from Staging or names another target: the pass after Staging's write is started by that write's watch event, and its cached copy could still say Staging. When the live template already carries the target — Staging run again after a crash between its two writes, a held upgrade retried towards the same target, a legacy resume — it keeps the live partition if that is lower than the replica count (`stagingPartition`), never raising it over servers that run the target or are being replaced on it. A server being replaced (deleted, not yet created again) below a raised partition comes back on the StatefulSet's current revision: the version the upgrade leaves, then a second restart onto the target, or, after a StatefulSet was created again on the held target, the unpullable image again (id 171). The retry of a held upgrade then re-verifies the servers from the live partition down, not from the replica count; Stabilizing and Verifying check every server, as before.
  - **What it does not:** a server down for another reason, a `Ready` pod above a down one (the partition would restart it while the other is down), a server below the partition down, or a server Neo4j reports unavailable outside them — the upgrade waits as before. The servers above the lowest repaired one are gated by Kubernetes readiness only: the StatefulSet replaces them highest first, each after the one before is ready.
