- **why:** #538, by code reading: a server that crash-loops or cannot pull its image on the target blocked going on towards any tag. `isUpgradeRequired` needs a `Ready` cluster with every server available, which such a cluster never is again; `holdImageDriftUntilReady` pinned the new tag to the template; and had the machine started, Staging froze every pod and the walk waited for all of them to be ready, so the crashed pod was never replaced. Changing the tag, the documented way to resume a `Paused` upgrade, did nothing; the upgrades guide documented the limitation. An unpullable target (a tag not published yet, a typo) is the likeliest way in. Same shape and answer as a plugin's failing init container (id 152). The #562 re-walk (2026-10-25): resuming an upgrade paused on a tag never published, Staging ran twice in one second — partition 2, then, on a cached copy that still said Staging and a template that already carried the target, 3 — so server-2, deleted for the first write, came back on the StatefulSet's current revision: on a fresh cluster the version it left (2026.08.1), then onto 2026.09.0 a minute later, an extra restart; after a storage expansion the unpullable held target (id 171), and the walk paused on "timed out waiting for server ordinal 3". With every server frozen the repair refused (the partition at the replica count), and the template already named the tag asked for, so every tag change only deferred: the cluster was stuck until the partition was patched by hand.
- **pinned-by:** `TestPodCannotStart`, `TestIsUpgradeRequired_RepairsAHeldUpgradesServersThatCannotStart` (ten cases: two that go on, eight that wait), `TestStartRollingUpgrade_TheRepairReplacesTheServersThatCannotStartFirst` (`internal/controller/upgrade_repair_test.go`); `TestStepUpgradeStaging_RunsOncePerTarget` (a pass on a cached copy that still says Staging, a Staging run again on the same template, a copy from before a retarget), `TestHeldUpgradeRepair_AServerDownOnAnotherImageWithEveryServerFrozen` (goes on, Staging replacing server-2 first; a Paused upgrade waiting for the user, a server down on the version the others run and one down on the tag asked for wait), `TestHeldUpgrade_AnExpansionThenTheFixedTagWalksToTheEnd` (`internal/controller/upgrade_expansion_resume_test.go`); the envtest spec "replaces the servers that cannot start on the target when the tag moves on" (`internal/controller/upgrade_held_servers_test.go`: a real `Reconcile` against the API server takes a Paused upgrade whose server-2 is in `ImagePullBackOff` to `Rolling` on the new tag, the template carrying it, partition 2 — for a later fixed tag, and for an earlier one after a pause on a tag that was never published, which went `Failed` before id 149's `startedTargetVersion`). Reverted under `go test -overlay`, each part fails its tests: the not-Ready start, the Staging partition, the skipped readiness gate; since the re-walk, the stored-record check, the kept partition (and both together, the walk), the frozen case, the down servers' image in place of the template's, and the other-image condition.
- **enforcement:** unit test + envtest; not walked live yet.

### id 163 — A change of spec.topology.servers waits while an upgrade is in flight or keeps a partition
- **scope:** `holdTopologyChange`, `holdTopologyWhileUpgradeHolds`, `topologyDeferral`, `reportTopologyDeferral` (`internal/controller/upgrade_topology.go`); its call in `Reconcile` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`); the replica count in `stepUpgradeStaging`, the report in `stepUpgradeRolling` and the walk's server count in `startRollingUpgrade` (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade_statemachine.go`); `ConditionTypeTopologyChangeDeferred`, `ConditionReasonWaitsForUpgrade`, `ConditionReasonNoTopologyChangeWaits` (`internal/controller/conditions.go`); its CLI guidance (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/explain.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **In flight:** Staging writes the target template with the StatefulSet's own replica count, never `spec.topology.servers`; the walk's `progress.total` is that count. `stepUpgradeRolling` reports a difference in either direction in the `TopologyChangeDeferred` condition (it reported only a scale-down, in `ServersPendingDrain` with reason `ScaleDownDeferredByUpgrade`, which is gone).
  - **Held:** while a `Paused` or `Failed` upgrade keeps a partition on the server StatefulSet (`keptUpgradePartition`), the normal pass works on the StatefulSet's replica count: `holdTopologyWhileUpgradeHolds` sets the pass's copy of `spec.topology.servers` to it, the in-memory hold `holdImageDriftUntilReady` applies to the image. So the builder renders no new replica count, the ConfigMap keeps its discovery endpoints, the drain sees nothing to drain, and the formation check expects the servers there are (a cluster held at 3 that expected 4 would turn `Forming`, and a `Failed` upgrade retries only on a `Ready` cluster). A drain already in progress is left to finish. A held record whose template never carried its target (a start a check refused) keeps no partition and holds no topology.
  - **The condition:** `TopologyChangeDeferred` `True`, reason `WaitsForUpgrade`, names both counts and the upgrade; `False` (reason `NoTopologyChangeWaits`) once nothing waits, and only if it was set. Written when it changes, on a copy re-read inside `RetryOnConflict`. Once the upgrade completes, the next normal pass applies the change as it would have: a scale-up's servers start on the new version, a scale-down drains first. The normal pass's one call is `holdTopologyChange`: this hold first, then a raise's wait for the removed servers' pods and volumes (reason `WaitsForRemovedServers`, id 229), and `False` only when neither holds.
- **why:** #538, by code reading; and Staging's write, found on the way. Staging applied the builder's StatefulSet, whose replica count is `spec.topology.servers`: a scale-down in the same apply as an image change — or made before a retarget — removed servers with no drain at all; a scale-up added servers mid-upgrade on the target. Once an upgrade was held, the normal pass ran: a scale-up added servers that started on the held target (pods at or above a partition get its update revision), and one added while the upgrade had staged but restarted nothing ran the target while the record said no server did, so the version rule (id 149) still let the tag go back below it; a scale-down's drain cordoned and deallocated the rolled servers first, mid-upgrade. Neo4j's rolling upgrade restarts the servers it has, one at a time, and "you must ensure that the cluster returns to a stable state before moving on to the next" (Upgrade and Migration Guide, `/current/`).
- **pinned-by:** `TestHoldTopologyWhileUpgradeHolds`, `TestStepUpgradeRolling_ReportsADeferredTopologyChange` (`internal/controller/upgrade_topology_unit_test.go`); `TestStepUpgradeStaging_FreezesEveryServerAndKeepsTheReplicaCount` (`internal/controller/upgrade_repair_test.go`); the envtest spec "a change of spec.topology.servers waits while the upgrade holds its partition" (`internal/controller/upgrade_held_servers_test.go`: a real `Reconcile` keeps 3 replicas and partition 2 for a scale-up to 4 and a scale-down to 2, starts no drain, and sets the condition); `TestConditionGuidanceCoversEveryConditionType` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/explain_test.go`). Reverted under `go test -overlay`: Staging's replica count, the hold's call in `Reconcile` (both envtest entries: 4 replicas; the drain annotation), and the Rolling report each fail their tests.
- **enforcement:** unit test + envtest; not walked live yet.

### id 154 — The CLI judges a resource by the status the operator wrote: every refusal phase is a problem, and a refused resource's missing workload points at its status
- **scope:** `AllPhases` and `PhaseValidationFailed` (`api/v1beta1/phases.go`); `problemPhases` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/status.go`); `phaseGuidance` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/explain.go`); `PhaseToConditionStatus` (`internal/controller/conditions.go`); `updateDatabaseStatusObserved` (`internal/controller/neo4jdatabase_controller.go`), which writes the phase; `noWorkloadAction` and `diagnoseInstance` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/diagnose.go`); the self-managed health checks in `docs/gitops/argocd-health-checks.yaml`.
- **rule:** A phase a controller writes when it refuses or fails a resource is a constant in `api/v1beta1/phases.go`, listed in `AllPhases`, and the controller writes the constant, not an event reason that happens to read the same. Then `kubectl neo4j status --problems` classifies it (the completeness test over `AllPhases` forces the decision), `explain` explains it, and `PhaseToConditionStatus` maps it to Ready `False`. `ValidationFailed`, a `Neo4jDatabase`'s name for `Invalid`, is one. A phase this CLI has never heard of still counts as healthy and `explain` says it may be newer than the CLI. `diagnose` reads a resource the operator has written status to as reconciled: a cluster or standalone with no StatefulSet and no pods under such a status is one the operator chose not to build, and the hint points at the status message (or, without one, at `kubectl describe`'s conditions and events) and `explain`; it stays a problem. Only a resource with no status at all is told to check that the operator runs and watches the namespace. **Argo CD judges the same way:** the published health check of every self-managed kind maps every phase in `problemPhases` — `Failed`, `Error`, `Invalid`, `ValidationFailed`, `Degraded`, `Unknown` — to `Degraded`, whether that kind writes it today or not, so adding a problem phase to the CLI without the health checks fails a test. The Aura kinds key off their `Ready` condition and stay out (their phase is Aura's open vocabulary).
- **why:** v1.22.0 journey. A `Neo4jDatabase` the operator refused sat in phase `ValidationFailed`, which was in no list, so `status --problems` reported every resource healthy and left out the message that said what to fix. `diagnose` on a refused cluster said "The operator has not built the workload yet. Check that it is running" — wrong about the operator, which had written the refusal right above the hint. The Argo CD health check mapped the same `ValidationFailed` (and a retried deletion's `Error`) to Progressing: a refused spec does not progress, so Argo CD showed the Application still syncing and a later sync wave waited for ever (#528). Of the self-managed kinds only `Neo4jDatabase` wrote a refusal phase its health check did not map; the others were made uniform with it so the next one cannot.
- **pinned-by:** `TestStatusProblems_ReportsAValidationFailedDatabase`, `TestResourceStatus_EveryPhaseInTheVocabularyIsClassified` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/status_test.go`); `TestEveryPhaseConstantHasGuidance` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/explain_test.go`); `TestPhaseToConditionStatus_Table` (`internal/controller/conditions_unit_test.go`); `TestDiagnose_NoWorkloadOfARefusedClusterPointsAtItsStatus` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/diagnose_test.go`); `TestArgoCDHealthChecks_EveryProblemPhaseIsDegraded` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/argocd_health_test.go`: parses each self-managed check's Degraded branch; fails on the earlier file, `Neo4jDatabase` first). The status and diagnose tests fail with the earlier lists and hint.
- **enforcement:** unit test for the phases in `AllPhases`, and for the health checks against `problemPhases`. A controller that writes a phase literal outside it is caught by nothing — convention + code review. The `Neo4jAuthRule`'s `PendingDependencies` was one: `explain` said it "may be newer than this CLI" (#549). It is `PhasePendingDependencies` now, in `AllPhases`, written by `neo4jauthrule_controller.go`, explained, not a problem for `status --problems` (a wait, like `Pending`) and Ready `Unknown`/`Pending` for `PhaseToConditionStatus`, as before; `TestExplainResource_AnAuthRuleWaitingForRolesIsExplained` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/explain_test.go`) fails without its guidance. The Lua itself is not executed (no Lua interpreter in the module); the test reads the conditions it is written with.

### id 168 — `kubectl neo4j diagnose` marks a Warning only while it describes the resource's current state
- **scope:** `warningEvents`, `warningIsCurrent`, `readySince`, `eventLastSeen`, `transitionSlack`, `advisoryCurrentFor`, the `earlierWarnings` line of `renderOne` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/diagnose.go`); `AdvisoryWarningReasons` and `AdvisoryWarningRefresh` (`internal/controller/advisory_warnings.go`).
- **rule:** A `Warning` event on a resource is marked `⚠` when it was last seen (`lastTimestamp`, else the series' or the event's time) at or after the resource's `Ready` condition last changed, less `transitionSlack` (5s: the operator writes the status that records a state and emits the event that reports it in the same pass, status first, both in whole seconds); or when its reason is in `AdvisoryWarningReasons` — the reasons the reconcilers announce through `advisoryWarnings`, again every 30 minutes while they apply (ids 102, 145) — and it was seen within `AdvisoryWarningRefresh` plus 5 minutes. Any other Warning describes a state the resource has left: it is not a symptom, `--quiet` leaves a resource with nothing else out, the exit code ignores it, and one line counts such Warnings and points at `kubectl describe`. A resource without a `Ready` condition keeps every Warning marked. A reason recorded through `advisoryWarnings` belongs in `AdvisoryWarningReasons`, except `UpgradeFailed`, which is a one-time failure everywhere but the standalone's pre-upgrade block.
- **why:** v1.22.0 re-walk (#553): on a healthy cluster `diagnose` marked every Warning of the last hour (the API server keeps events an hour) with `⚠`, a `ClusterQuorumLost` from 40 minutes before the cluster was `Ready` again among them, and sent the reader after a problem that was over. The `Ready` condition's `lastTransitionTime` is the one timestamp every kind keeps for "the state changed"; the advisories needed the exception because they are announced when they start to apply, usually before the resource first turns `Ready`, and are still true.
- **pinned-by:** `TestDiagnose_AWarningFromBeforeTheLastTransitionIsNotMarked`, `TestDiagnose_WhichWarningsDescribeTheCurrentState` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/diagnose_events_test.go`; the first fails on the earlier `warningEvents`); `TestAdvisoryWarningReasons_ListsEveryRecordedReason` (`internal/controller/advisory_warning_reasons_test.go`: parses the package for `advisoryWarnings.record`/`recordScoped` and fails for a reason not listed).
- **enforcement:** unit test.

### id 169 — A Get replaces the object it decodes into; the writers that read again into a non-empty object rely on it
- **scope:** `setDeploymentAnnotation` (`internal/controller/aura_fleet_provision.go`), `EnsureSeedCredsProjected` (`internal/controller/cluster_seed_creds.go`), `reconcileMutableResource` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`), `updateServerStatefulSet` (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade.go`), the hook Job wait in `internal/controller/neo4jrestore_controller.go`; every read through the direct client (`--cache-strategy=none`, `--ultra-fast`: `createDirectClientManager` in `cmd/main.go`) and an `APIReader`.
- **rule:** These read an object again into one that already carries fields: a `DeepCopy` of the argument, the caller's object, a copy of the desired object, the object a failed conflict attempt read and changed, the Job they created. That is safe only because controller-runtime's typed client zeroes the target before it decodes (`targetZeroingDecoder` in its `pkg/client/apiutil/apimachinery.go`), on every Get, Update and Patch, for the direct client and the `APIReader` alike; the informer cache replaces the object too. Decoding JSON into a non-empty Go object keeps what the JSON leaves out — a map keeps its keys, an omitted field its value — so a client without that zeroing (a plain client-go REST client, an older controller-runtime) would make these writers keep, and write back, fields the server no longer has. Use the controller-runtime client for every read of a typed object; if an upgrade drops the zeroing, give each of these reads an empty object.
- **why:** #540 raised the stale read from code: `setDeploymentAnnotation`'s copy would have kept a deployment ID the stored object lost and never written it back; `EnsureSeedCredsProjected` would have written back an `extraEnvFrom` entry the user removed. Against envtest with the direct client none of it happens (the four writers' scenarios pass on the unchanged code); with a client-go REST client without the zeroing, the stale annotation survives the read.
- **pinned-by:** `TestDirectClientReadReplacesTheObject_APIServer` (`internal/controller/direct_client_read_test.go`: a map key, an omitted list and an omitted pointer, gone after a read into the object that had them).
- **enforcement:** envtest (library behaviour).

### id 155 — A plugin records the variables it writes to its target and takes away what it no longer sets; its uninstall takes away everything it wrote, in one write
- **scope:** `pluginEnvVarsAnnotation`, `pluginEntriesAnnotation`, `pluginRecordKey`, `applyPluginEntries`, `neo4jPluginsList`, `setClusterPluginLists`, `sameCSVSet`, `writeRemoval`, `pluginOwnedEnvNames`, `otherPlugins`, `targetOwnedEnvNames`, `envOwnedElsewhere`, `applyPlugin`, `withoutEnvNames`, `setPluginEnvRecord`, `recordPluginEnv`, `removePluginFromTarget`, `withoutPlugin`, `pluginListTokens`, `withoutPluginEntries`, `pluginEntries`, `fleetManagementEnabled`, `withoutPluginInitContainer` (`internal/controller/plugin_env_ownership.go`); `pluginListSettings` (`internal/controller/plugin_conf_settings.go`); the settled branch and the repair announcement of `Reconcile`, `targetDrift`, `installChangesTarget`, `writePluginToTarget`, `uninstallPlugin` (`internal/controller/plugin_controller.go`); `selfRepair`, `podsDownOnlyIn`, `containerFailure`, `containerCrashLooping`, `podRunsOtherSettings`, `rolloutPending`, `failingPods`, `replaceStuckPods` (`internal/controller/plugin_target_gate.go`); `EventReasonPluginSettingsFailing` (`internal/controller/events.go`); the env merge of `createOrUpdateResourceInternal` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **The record:** a plugin writes one variable per `spec.config` key onto its target's neo4j container (`NEO4J_<setting>`; on a standalone not the keys that go to neo4j.conf, id 114), and on a cluster each security setting that is not a list. It records those names on the target's StatefulSet under `neo4j.com/plugin-env-vars.<plugin CR name>` (a name too long for an annotation key is cut and ended with a hash), sorted and comma-separated like `neo4j.com/cluster-controller-env-vars`. It is StatefulSet metadata: writing it restarts nothing and wakes no plugin (id 135's watch looks at the pod template). The procedure and extension lists (`IsAdditiveConfKey`) are shared and never recorded: a plugin adds tokens to them.
  - **Names keep the setting's case:** `Neo4jSettingEnvVarName` writes `NEO4J_` + the key with `_` doubled and `.` turned into `_`, case kept (`apoc.export.file.enabled` → `NEO4J_apoc_export_file_enabled`): the image's entrypoint keeps the case too, so an upper-cased name sets a setting nothing reads. The published docs and examples name the variables so — the plugin examples' jsonpath checks named the APOC variable and the unrestricted list in upper case, and printed nothing (`TestPublishedDocsNameSettingVariablesAsTheImageReadsThem`, `internal/resources/setting_env_docs_test.go`).
  - **Removal:** every write of the plugin (`applyPlugin`, the code installs, repairs, `installChangesTarget`, `targetDrift` and `selfRepair` all compare against) removes the variables its record lists and it no longer sets, in the same StatefulSet update as its other edits, and writes the record anew. Never a variable owned elsewhere (`envOwnedElsewhere`): one the target renders itself (the cluster's or the standalone's own record — `spec.env`, its settings), one another plugin records, or one another plugin of the target sets (from its spec, so a plugin that has no record yet is covered), nor a list. A removal changes the pod template, so it waits like an install for a target that is `Ready` with every server available and rolled out (id 141), except as below. A record that changed while the template did not is written alone, as a merge patch of the StatefulSet's metadata.
  - **`NEO4J_PLUGINS` names are recorded too** (`neo4j.com/plugin-entries.<plugin CR name>`, `applyPluginEntries`): the names the plugin adds in Managed mode, its own and its dependencies' as the entrypoint knows them. A write removes those its record lists and it no longer adds — a dependency dropped from `spec.dependencies`, every name once the plugin leaves Managed mode — less those another enabled Managed plugin of the target adds and `fleet-management` while the target's `spec.auraFleetManagement` installs it, and none when NEO4J_PLUGINS is the target's own; then it records what it adds now. Without the record (#548, from the code) such names stayed: the entrypoint went on installing a dropped dependency at every start, and after a switch to `VerifiedDownload` it installed the plugin from NEO4J_PLUGINS beside the verified JAR. A settled pass counts a name the plugin needs and NEO4J_PLUGINS lacks as missing, and a name its write would take away as a removal, not a loss (`pluginEnvDrift`).
  - **A cluster's shared lists follow what the plugins ask for now** (`setClusterPluginLists`): in every write of a plugin, each `IsAdditiveConfKey` list on a cluster's neo4j container gains the writing plugin's tokens (`pluginListSettings`: its automatic settings, `spec.security`, and the list keys of its `spec.config`) and keeps a token already there only while that plugin or another enabled plugin of the target asks for it — a token nobody asks for goes, a list nobody puts anything in goes. Another plugin's tokens are never added (id 173). A list the target renders itself or reads from a Secret is left alone, and a list whose tokens do not change keeps its value and order. A plugin used to add its tokens and take them out only on its uninstall: tokens stayed when its `spec.security` changed, and a list key in a plugin's `spec.config` replaced the union outright (#548, from the code). The plugin's other `spec.config` keys are still set as given. On a standalone the lists are in its neo4j.conf (id 114).
  - **First pass after an upgrade:** a plugin installed by an earlier operator has no record. Its first pass records what it sets now and removes nothing: a variable a key removal left behind under that operator cannot be told from one someone else set. A settled plugin writes that record with a metadata patch and nothing else — no Bolt, no template write, no status write, no event (`recordPluginEnv`; id 135's settled pass otherwise writes nothing). Removals start with the plugin's next edit. A leftover from before the upgrade stays until removed by hand.
  - **Uninstall:** the finalizer takes off the target's StatefulSet, in one write (it was up to three, each a restart), everything the plugin wrote: the variables its record lists and those its spec sets now, less those owned elsewhere; on a cluster each shared list keeps only the tokens the target's other enabled plugins ask for, and gains nothing (`setClusterPluginLists`, id 173), never a list the target renders; on a standalone its tokens in the env copies an earlier operator left, less those another plugin puts there; its NEO4J_PLUGINS names — those its record lists and, in Managed mode, its own as the entrypoint knows it (`mapPluginName`: `gds` is `graph-data-science`) and its dependencies' — less those another Managed plugin of the target installs and `fleet-management` while the target's `spec.auraFleetManagement` installs it, and not at all when NEO4J_PLUGINS is the target's own; its VerifiedDownload init container, auth volume and the shared CA volume once nothing mounts it (id 141); its record. A NEO4J_PLUGINS value that is not a JSON list is left as it is and the CR still goes. A PreBaked plugin's NEO4J_PLUGINS is left alone, as before. The uninstall does not wait for the target (id 141). **On a standalone whose pod keeps failing on the plugin** (its init container, or settings Neo4j refuses), the finalizer stays after the write until the StatefulSet's controller has seen it and `replaceStuckPods` has deleted that pod (`uninstallWaitsForStuckPod`, rechecked every `stuckPodRecheck`, at most `pluginUninstallPodWait` — five minutes — after the deletion was asked for): an OrderedReady StatefulSet does not replace a pod that is not Ready (id 152), and once the finalizer is released the plugin has no pass left to delete it. The v1.22.0 re-walk of the combined build deleted such a plugin on a standalone: `NEO4J_PLUGINS` went back to `["apoc"]`, the pod stayed in CrashLoopBackOff until deleted by hand, and the standalone's `ConfigNeedsRestart` announced a restart that never came. A disabled or refused plugin's removal needs none of this: its CR stays and its next passes delete the pod.
  - **The settings repair, id 152's exception extended:** a write may go to a target that is not `Ready` with every server available when every server pod that is not `Ready` keeps failing in its neo4j container — exited non-zero and not running again (`containerCrashLooping`: a container that is running again may be starting fine) — while running a variable this write changes (`podRunsOtherSettings`: the pod's env differs from the written one in a changed name), and id 152's other conditions hold: no pod missing or being deleted, those pods the highest ordinals at or above the partition, and on a cluster no other server unavailable in `status.diagnostics.servers`. A server down for another reason, or one failing without running what the write changes, makes it wait. Phase `Installing`, "Writing the plugin to … although it is not Ready: every server that is down keeps failing with settings this write changes (c-server-2 (container neo4j exited with code 1)); the roll replaces those pods first", one `PluginSettingsFailing` Warning from the status write. On a standalone the plugin then deletes the pod (OrderedReady does not replace it, id 152) once the StatefulSet has seen the write, when the pod keeps exiting on an older revision whose env differs from the template in a variable the standalone does not render, and the plugin is not settled; one `PluginSettingsFailing` Normal event.
  - **The wait says why:** while its roll is pending, the plugin's message names the pods whose neo4j container keeps exiting ("…; cannot start: c-server-2 (container neo4j exited with code 1)"), for every install mode, as it names a failing VerifiedDownload init container (id 141).
  - **The cluster keeps what it did not render (ids 134, 141):** its template apply writes the merged env into a copy of the desired template, never into the desired template itself, so its record and template hash hold only what it renders. A struct assignment of the desired template shares its container array with the live one: writing the merged env there wrote the plugins' variables into "desired", the cluster recorded and hashed them as its own, and its next pass removed them.
- **why:** v1.22.0 journey (CalVer 2026.08.1, 3 servers): `examples/plugins/genai-plugin-example.yaml` set `genai.vector.enabled` and `genai.embeddings.enabled`, which no Neo4j version documents (GenAI plugin docs `/genai/plugin/5/` and `/current/`, Operations Manual `configuration/plugins.adoc` on `5.x` and `dev`, read 2026-10-23: credentials are procedure arguments; CalVer documents only `genai.openai.baseurl` and, from 2026.04, `genai.azure.openai.baseurl` — yet the GenAI plugin bundled with 2026.08.1 declares neither, and Neo4j refuses both as unrecognized, v1.22.0 re-walk 2026-10-23). Neo4j refused them as "Unrecognized setting" and server-2 crash-looped. Removing the keys from the plugin did not remove the variables: the plugin only ever added, the cluster controller keeps every variable it did not render, and the uninstall removed only the NEO4J_PLUGINS entry — the StatefulSet was edited by hand. Reproduced in unit tests on cluster and standalone before the fix (`TestReconcile_ARemovedConfigKeyLeavesTheServers`, `TestUninstall_TakesAwayWhatThePluginWrote`: the variables stayed). Found on the way: the uninstall removed `plugin.Spec.Name` from NEO4J_PLUGINS, not the name it added (an alias such as `gds` stayed), never its dependencies, subtracted procedure tokens another plugin needed, and made up to three StatefulSet writes; and with the removal written only once the target was `Ready`, the removal of a setting that kept a server down waited for that server for ever — the deadlock id 152 solved for the init container. Found walking it live (Kind, 2026-10-23 10:39 CEST): adding a CA to the cluster's `spec.trustedCASecrets` made the cluster apply its template twice in the same second, and the second apply removed a `VerifiedDownload` plugin's `NEO4J_apoc_export_file_enabled` and a `NEO4J_PLUGINS` (StatefulSet revision 10 against 9); the plugin's next write put its variable back, unannounced. The aliased container array, introduced with id 141's record (#519, unreleased), was the cause; before it the env record was taken before the merge.
- **pinned-by:** `TestReconcile_ARemovedConfigKeyLeavesTheServers` (cluster and standalone: one write, the record follows), `TestReconcile_TheFirstPassAfterAnUpgradeRecordsAndRemovesNothing`, `TestApplyPlugin_LeavesWhatSomeoneElseOwns`, `TestReconcile_ARemovalWaitsForAFullyAvailableTarget`, `TestReconcile_ARemovalThatRepairsCrashingServersDoesNotWait`, `TestReconcile_ASettingsRepairWaitsWhenSomethingElseIsDown` (seven ways something else is down), `TestReconcile_TheWaitNamesServersThatKeepExiting`, `TestReconcile_AStandalonesCrashingPodIsReplacedAfterTheRepair`, `TestReplaceStuckPods_Settings`, `TestContainerFailure`, `TestPluginEnvVarsAnnotation`, `TestUninstall_TakesAwayWhatThePluginWrote`, `TestUninstall_LeavesWhatOthersNeed`, `TestUninstall_VerifiedDownloadIsOneWrite`, `TestUninstall_ReplacesTheStandalonePodThatKeepsFailingOnThePlugin`, `TestUninstall_TheWaitForAStuckPodIsBounded`, `TestReconcile_DisablingReplacesTheStandalonePodThatKeepsFailingOnThePlugin` (`internal/controller/plugin_target_absent_test.go`), `TestReconcile_ClusterProcedureListsFollowThePlugins` (a narrowed allowlist; a list in `spec.config` added to, not replacing; the same tokens in another order written nothing), `TestReconcile_NEO4JPluginsFollowsThePlugin` (a dependency dropped; a switch to `VerifiedDownload` in one write; a name another plugin adds kept; the first pass after an upgrade records and removes nothing), `TestUninstall_TakesAwayTheRecordedNames`, `TestPluginEntriesAnnotation` (`internal/controller/plugin_shared_lists_test.go`), the removal-is-not-a-loss cases of `TestPluginEnvDrift` (`internal/controller/plugin_self_heal_test.go`), `TestPluginEnvFollowsItsConfig_APIServer` (envtest: the cluster controller's apply between the plugin's passes; a key removed in one roll and not brought back; a change of the cluster's own `spec.env` is one write and keeps the plugins' variables; two plugins deleted, the cluster's own env untouched) (`internal/controller/plugin_env_ownership_test.go`); `TestUninstall_RemovesTheSharedCAVolumeOnceUnused` (`internal/controller/plugin_target_availability_test.go`); `TestCreateOrUpdateResource_KeepsAPluginsEnvAfterATemplateChange` (`internal/controller/sts_template_ownership_test.go`; with the shared container array restored, it and the envtest's last two steps fail); `TestPublishedManifestsPassOurOwnValidators` keeps the GenAI example applicable. Each part switched off with `go test -overlay` fails its tests: the removal, the record, the settled pass's record, the uninstall's variables, its NEO4J_PLUGINS names, its token subtraction, the ownership guard, the settings repair (and its two guards: a crash in a container that runs nothing the write changes, a container running again), the standalone's pod replacement, and the crash naming.
- **enforcement:** unit test + envtest; walked live on Kind v1.34 (2026-10-23, CEST), a 2-server 2026.08.1 cluster with a `spec.env` entry: (1) a Managed APOC plugin with two `spec.config` keys and an allowlist, installed 10:24:48, `Ready` 10:26:47 after one roll, its record listing both variables. (2) One key removed at 10:27:01: written in that second (one roll), the variable gone and the record following, `Ready` 10:28:58, no `PluginMissing`. (3) A GenAI plugin with `genai.vector.enabled` at 10:29:08: server-1 crash-looped ("Unrecognized setting. No declared setting with name: genai.vector.enabled"), the plugin `Installing` "… cannot start: walk-server-1 (container neo4j exited with code 1)"; the key removed at 10:29:43 while the cluster was `Forming`: written in that second with one `PluginSettingsFailing` Warning, server-1 replaced first, `Ready` 10:31:40. (4) APOC deleted at 10:31:47: one roll, its variables, allowlist tokens and record gone, `NEO4J_PLUGINS` `["genai"]`, APOC procedures 0, the cluster-owned env unchanged; GenAI deleted at 10:34:03: `NEO4J_PLUGINS` `[]`. (5) A `VerifiedDownload` APOC plugin with a key, `Ready` 10:38:44; a CA added to `spec.trustedCASecrets` at 10:39:19 found the cluster-controller defect above; on the fixed operator a `spec.env` edit at 10:49:55 was one write, the plugin's variable stayed and the plugin stayed `Ready`. (6) That plugin deleted at 10:52:53: one roll, `plugin-init-apoc` and `plugin-ca` gone (the cluster's `truststore-init` stays), its variable and record gone, `/plugins` empty, the cluster-owned env unchanged.

### id 166 — A progress stream has an event source of its own and fits its budget, so every progress event and the outcome after it reach the API
- **scope:** `ProgressRecorder` and `progressRecorder` (`internal/controller/neo4jrestore_controller.go`), wired as `neo4j-restore-progress` in both controller registries of `cmd/main.go`; `seedProgressEvents`, `seedProgressRefresh`, `seedProgressSummary`, `roughDuration`, `allDatabasesRestoreClient`, `allDatabasesClient`, the `RestoreSeedProgress` of `startAllDatabasesRestore`, the `RestoreStarted` and `RestoreDatabaseFailed` of `markDatabaseResult` (with `allDatabasesRestoreStartedMessage`), and `finishAllDatabasesRestore` (`internal/controller/neo4jrestore_alldatabases.go`); `EventReasonRestoreSeedProgress`, `EventReasonRestoreDatabaseFailed` (`events.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **The budget:** client-go's spam filter (`getSpamKey` in `tools/record/events_cache.go`, v0.37.1) keys on the event's source component, host, involved object and TYPE — not its reason — and allows a burst of 25 per key, then one every five minutes; a deduplicated repeat (a count patch) spends a token like a new event. So every Normal event on one restore from one recorder shares one budget, and a Warning has its own. Its aggregator also combines more than ten events of one reason with different messages within ten minutes into one.
  - **Own source:** the per-database events of an all-databases restore — `RestoreSeedProgress`, and the Warning `RestoreDatabaseFailed` — go through `ProgressRecorder` (component `neo4j-restore-progress`), a recorder of its own from the manager's one broadcaster, so they spend their own budget. The restore's own events — its outcome above all — keep `neo4j-restore-controller`'s. A recorder that is nil falls back to `Recorder` (tests).
  - **Fewer events, not a way round the budget (#573):** a database starting is not announced — one `RestoreStarted` per database (30 for a 30-database restore) shared the progress source's budget with the progress, and 13 of 30 reached the API server. The restore announces its start once, on its own source, from the `markDatabaseResult` write that moves it into `Running` (how many databases, the first ten by name); a database written `Failed` is announced once, as `RestoreDatabaseFailed` (Warning: a budget of its own) from the progress source; the rest is `status.databaseResults`, the complete record. A source per database, or another event type, would get round the spam filter, which exists to protect the API server: not done.
  - **Progress is one summary per restore, every ten minutes (round 8):** `RestoreSeedProgress` is the restore's summary — databases restored and failed so far (from `status.databaseResults`), the database being seeded, its progress and how long it has shown it ("unchanged for 40m" is the slow-versus-stuck signal of id 92, said outright rather than read from an aggregated count) — on the restore's first seed poll and then every `seedProgressRefresh` (10 minutes) while it seeds (`seedProgressEvents`, `seedProgressSummary`; in memory, keyed by restore UID, forgotten when the restore finishes). Chosen over a per-database event (on change and every 10 minutes): that grew with databases × states, about three per database, so a 30-database restore emitted 101 and 55 reached the API server — no cadence per database fits a budget that refills one token every five minutes once the burst of 25 is spent. A summary every ten minutes spends half the refill whatever the size, and never meets the aggregator's ten similar events in ten minutes, so every one is delivered (the 30-database test: 21 emitted, 21 delivered). Also considered: an event only when a database stalls — it says nothing on a restore that is merely long, and the summary already shows a stall within ten minutes. Between summaries `status.databaseResults` is the record.
  - **The outcome is announced:** an all-databases restore ends with one `RestoreCompleted` (Normal) or `RestoreFailed` (Warning), from the status write that changed the phase (id 145). It used to end in status alone.
- **why:** #527: `RestoreSeedProgress` repeated on purpose, on every poll, and could spend the restore's event budget on a long seed so the final event never reached the API. Checked against client-go before fixing: the Warning `RestoreFailed` has a budget of its own (the type is in the key); the all-databases path — the only one that emits progress — emitted no `RestoreCompleted` at all; and with the per-database events on the restore's recorder, rate-limiting alone did not save it: a 30-database restore (the size of the #488 upgrade test) spends 30 `RestoreStarted` and about three progress states per database, past the burst, whatever the progress cadence. Only a budget of its own makes the outcome's delivery independent of the number of databases and the length of the seed.
- **pinned-by:** `TestAllDatabasesRestore_TheOutcomeIsAnnouncedAfterALongSeed` (`internal/controller/neo4jrestore_event_budget_test.go`: 30 databases, one seeding for two hours, through `Reconcile` with a scripted server and client-go's own `EventCorrelator` on a fake clock; `RestoreCompleted` emitted once and delivered; `RestoreSeedProgress` at most one per 10 minutes of the run, every one delivered — with the per-database progress restored it emits 101 and delivers 55). It fails three ways: with the original code (no `RestoreCompleted`), with the outcome announced but progress on every poll from the restore's recorder (`RestoreCompleted` emitted, dropped by the spam filter), and with the progress throttled but on the restore's recorder (dropped too); and, since #573, with a `RestoreStarted` per database (30 emitted, 13 delivered). `TestAllDatabasesRestore_AnnouncesItsStartAndEachFailedDatabaseOnce` (same file: one `RestoreStarted`, one `RestoreDatabaseFailed` for the database that exists without `replaceExisting`, one `RestoreFailed`; fails with the per-database `RestoreStarted` and no failure event restored) and `TestAllDatabasesRestoreStartedMessage`. `TestSeedProgressEvents` (same file) pins the summary: one at the first poll, none for a change in between, one a refresh period later saying how long the progress has stood, one per restore, and fails with the per-database events.
- **enforcement:** unit test. No machine check that a NEW repeating event gets a source of its own — convention + code review: a stream whose volume grows with time or with the size of the resource does not share a recorder with the resource's outcome.
### id 156 — An Aura status is written when it changes; a refusal on status is not asked again; a failure shows in the phase
- **scope:** `fail` and `snapshotTerminal` (`internal/controller/aurasnapshot_controller.go`); `auraSyncedTimeRefresh`, `auraStatusWriteNeeded` and `auraRefusalRecorded` (`internal/controller/aura_common.go`); every Aura status writer that stamps `status.lastSyncedTime` — `syncStatus` in `internal/controller/aurainstance_controller.go`, `internal/controller/auracustomermanagedkey_controller.go`, `internal/controller/auraipfilter_controller.go`, `internal/controller/auradatabase_controller.go` and `internal/controller/auradatabasebackup_controller.go`, `setStatusChanged` in `internal/controller/aurainvite_controller.go`, `internal/controller/auraorganizationmember_controller.go` and `internal/controller/auraprojectmember_controller.go`; the refusal guards — `refusalStands` (`aurainstance_controller.go`), the guard before `observeOrCreate` in `auradatabase_controller.go` (`auraReasonInstanceNotMultiDatabase`) and `aurainvite_controller.go` (`auraReasonOrganizationRoleRequired`); `fail` in `aurainstance_controller.go` and `auracustomermanagedkey_controller.go`.
- **rule:**
  - **A status write is a reconcile.** Every Aura controller watches its own kind and has no event filter, so each status write starts another pass at once (id 122). A writer builds the status it would write, and writes it only when it differs from the stored one in anything but `lastSyncedTime`, or the stored `lastSyncedTime` is missing or `auraSyncedTimeRefresh` (15 minutes) old. `lastSyncedTime` is therefore when the operator last recorded what it observed — updated with any change, and at least every 15 minutes while it polls and nothing changes. The refresh is longer than the 5-minute re-observe on purpose: a refresh is one write, and one more pass.
  - **A terminal refusal on status is not asked again.** A refusal returns without a requeue, but a resync, an operator restart or the refusal's own status write still runs the next pass. A pass that finds the refusal on status for the current generation — `Ready` False with the refusal's reason, `observedGeneration` equal to `generation` — stops before calling Aura. The guard and the writer share the reason constant (id 91). The spec edit that can change the answer bumps the generation. Where the cause is outside the spec, the guard looks at it too: an `AuraDatabase`'s refusal names the instance's Aura ID, so a replaced instance is asked again; an `AuraInstance` refused for want of an organization is asked again once one resolves, since `defaultOrganizationId` on the `AuraProviderConfig` can supply it. An `AuraInvite` refusal depends on the spec alone.
  - **A failure shows in the phase.** `fail()` writes phase `Error` with the `Ready` condition on every Aura kind; the `AuraInstance` and `AuraCustomerManagedKey` ones wrote the condition alone. Their phase otherwise mirrors Aura's own status, and the next observation writes it back. `AuraSnapshot` had no `fail()`: a create or a poll Aura refused (not a 409, not transient) wrote the condition alone and raised no event; it now goes through one, phase `Error`, announced once with `AuraSnapshotFailed` (#566, 2026-10-25). A snapshot in a terminal phase (`Completed`, `Failed`, `Cancelled`; `snapshotTerminal`) is not read again: a resync or a restart GETs nothing, and a credential revoked since cannot turn a finished snapshot into a failure retried every 30s.
- **why:** #525 (2026-10-24, from the code, not walked live). Stamping a fresh `lastSyncedTime` on every pass made every pass start the next: an invite or member that was failing or waiting ran about twice every 30s, each Ready Aura resource two or more passes every 5 minutes (a chain that stops only when two writes land in the same second), each spending the Aura API's rate limit — 25 calls a minute on a trial account — and announcing its Ready event again (id 136). The refusals re-ran on every such event: an `AuraInstance` refused for its tier sent the same refused create to Aura, an `AuraDatabase` against a single-database instance whose verdict was unknown sent the create Aura answers with `multi-db-only`, an invite listed the organization's invites. And `kubectl neo4j status` and the ArgoCD health check judge a resource by its phase (id 154): a failing `AuraInstance` or key read as healthy, or as Progressing forever.
- **pinned-by:** `TestAuraReconcile_ASteadyStateWritesAndAnnouncesNothing` (`internal/controller/aura_status_writes_test.go`: for each kind, a pass that observes the same state writes nothing, a restarted reconciler neither, a stale `lastSyncedTime` is refreshed once); `TestAuraInstance_ARecordedRefusalIsNotAskedAgain`, `TestAuraDatabase_ARecordedRefusalIsNotAskedAgain`, `TestAuraInvite_ARecordedRefusalIsNotAskedAgain` (`internal/controller/aura_api_calls_test.go`: three passes, one round of Aura calls; a spec edit, an organization from the provider config, a replaced instance, one more); `TestAuraFail_RecordsPhaseError` (`internal/controller/aura_status_writes_test.go`). Each fails with the earlier controllers swapped in by `go test -overlay`.
- **also pinned by:** `TestAuraSnapshot_AFailureIsPhaseErrorAnnouncedOnce` (`internal/controller/aura_fleet_followups_test.go`: a refused create and a refused poll are phase `Error` with one Warning, a restart announces nothing, the next good create is `Pending`; a `Completed` snapshot is not read), which fails on the earlier `internal/controller/aurasnapshot_controller.go`.
- **enforcement:** unit test. No machine check that a NEW Aura status field or writer compares before it writes, or that a new terminal refusal has a guard — convention + code review.

### id 157 — An Aura deletion resolves the credentials only to call Aura, and waits for them a bounded time
- **scope:** `handleDeletion` and the order of `Reconcile` in `internal/controller/aurainstance_controller.go`, `internal/controller/auracustomermanagedkey_controller.go`, `internal/controller/auraipfilter_controller.go` (with its `deletionTarget`) and `internal/controller/auradatabase_controller.go` (id 202); `auraDeletionNeedsCredentials`, `auraOrphanedWithoutCredentials` and `auraDeletionDeadline` (`internal/controller/aura_common.go`); `finalizerDeletionGracePeriod` and `deletionGracePeriodExceeded` (`internal/controller/finalizer_deletion.go`).
- **rule:** A terminating `AuraInstance`, `AuraCustomerManagedKey` or `AuraIPFilter` is handled before the credentials are resolved, and resolves them (and, for the filter, the organization) only when the deletion calls Aura: `deletionPolicy: Delete`, permitted by `managementPolicies`, with an external ID. `Orphan`, the default, calls nothing and releases the finalizer at once. A Delete that cannot resolve them records the wait — phase `Error`, `Ready` False with reason `CredentialsUnavailable`, a message naming the moment it stops waiting — announced once (id 145), and retries; once `finalizerDeletionGracePeriod` (5 minutes) has passed since the deletion was requested, it releases the finalizer without deleting and emits a Warning with the kind's `…Orphaned` reason saying the resource was left in Aura and must be deleted in the console. `deletionProtection` is checked before the credentials, and still holds the finalizer for as long as it is set. The `AuraDatabase` reconciler released its finalizer at once, with a Normal `Orphaning database` event, when its instance or credentials could not be resolved; it now follows this rule too, except for an `AuraInstance` CR that is gone, which releases at once with a Warning (id 202). The `AuraInvite`, `AuraOrganizationMember` and `AuraProjectMember` reconcilers released theirs at once and silently; they now follow this rule (id 178). A DELETE that Aura refuses, once the credentials resolve, is waited on the same way (id 201).
- **why:** #525 (2026-10-24, from the code, not walked live). The three reconcilers resolved the credentials first and returned `CredentialsUnavailable` before they looked at the deletion. A namespace teardown deletes the credentials Secret and the provider config alongside the CRs, in no order, so a CR whose Secret went first kept its finalizer for ever — whatever its deletion policy — and the namespace stayed `Terminating`. Releasing the finalizer of a Delete at once would leave a paid instance running without a word; waiting for ever wedges the namespace. The bounded wait is the rule the operator's Cypher cleanups already follow (`classifyFinalizerCleanup`): a CR that cannot be deleted is worse than a resource left behind and named.
- **pinned-by:** `TestAuraDeletion_OrphanNeedsNoCredentials`, `TestAuraDeletion_DeleteWithoutCredentialsWaitsThenOrphans` (`internal/controller/aura_deletion_credentials_test.go`), one subtest per kind; both fail with the earlier controllers swapped in by `go test -overlay` (the finalizer is kept). The `AuraDatabase`: `TestAuraDatabaseDeletion_AnUnresolvableTargetIsAWarning` (id 202).
- **enforcement:** unit test.

### id 201 — A DELETE Aura refuses is waited on, bounded; then the resource is named as left in Aura
- **scope:** `auraDeleteRefused`, `auraDeletionRefusedWait`, `auraOrphanedAfterRefusal` and `auraReasonDeletionRefused` (`internal/controller/aura_common.go`); the DELETE branch of `handleDeletion` in `internal/controller/aurainstance_controller.go`, `internal/controller/auracustomermanagedkey_controller.go` (with its `KeyInUse` wait), `internal/controller/auraipfilter_controller.go`, `internal/controller/auradatabase_controller.go`, `internal/controller/aurainvite_controller.go`, `internal/controller/auraorganizationmember_controller.go` and `internal/controller/auraprojectmember_controller.go`; `finalizerDeletionGracePeriod` and `deletionGracePeriodExceeded` (`internal/controller/finalizer_deletion.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **A refusal is waited on, bounded.** A DELETE that Aura answers with an error that is not "not found" (gone is what the deletion wanted: released with the kind's deleted or removed event), not a conflict (`aura.IsConflict`: a 409, an operation in flight) and not transient (`aura.IsTransient`: a 429, a 5xx, a network failure) keeps the finalizer and records the wait through the kind's `fail()`: phase `Error`, `Ready` False with reason `DeletionRefused`, the message `deletionPolicy is Delete, but Aura refused to delete <what> <id>: <error>; it is asked again until <time>, then the finalizer is released and <what> <id> is left in Aura` (without the request ID, id 184), announced once with the kind's `…Failed` Warning (id 145). It returns `RequeueAfter` with a nil error and asks again on each pass. Once `finalizerDeletionGracePeriod` (5 minutes) has passed since `deletionTimestamp`, the pass Aura refuses once more releases the finalizer with one Warning of the kind's `…Orphaned` reason: `Orphaning <what> <id> although deletionPolicy is Delete: Aura still refused to delete it 5m0s after the deletion was requested (<error>); <what is left>, so delete it in the Aura console`. What is left: an instance "the cloud instance keeps running"; a key "it stays registered in Aura"; a filter "it stays in place in Aura"; a database "it stays on Aura instance <instanceRef> with its data"; an invite "it stays pending in Aura and <email> can still accept it"; a member "they keep the role <role> in the organization" (or project).
  - **A conflict or an outage is not bounded.** Both are asked again on every pass, without a deadline, as before: the operation in flight ends, the outage passes, and releasing during either would leave behind a resource Aura was about to delete.
  - **A customer-managed key in use is bounded too.** `encryption-key-is-active` keeps its own wording — `Synced` False, reason `KeyInUse`, one `AuraCustomerManagedKeyDeleteBlocked` — and its message now names the deadline; after it the finalizer is released with a Warning `AuraCustomerManagedKeyOrphaned` ("it stays registered in Aura, still bound to an instance"). It waited for ever: a namespace whose instance had been orphaned (the instance's default policy) and still used the key stayed `Terminating` until someone deleted that instance in the console.
- **why:** round 8 of the post-v1.22.0 fixes (2026-10-25, from the code, not walked live): every Aura `handleDeletion` returned a refused DELETE as the reconcile's error — controller-runtime's error backoff for ever, a `Reconciler error` line each time, nothing on status, and a CR, with its namespace, that never went away. The credentials wait (id 157) and the Cypher cleanups (id 178) already bound the same shape: a CR that cannot be deleted is worse than a resource left behind and named.
- **pinned-by:** `TestAuraDeletion_ARefusedDeleteWaitsThenOrphans` (`internal/controller/aura_deletion_refused_test.go`: per kind — instance, key, filter, database, invite, organization member, project member — a 403 within the grace period: three passes, the finalizer kept, a nil error and a requeue, one `…Failed` Warning, `DeletionRefused` on status without the request ID; after it: released with one `…Orphaned` Warning and no deleted event), `TestAuraDeletion_AConflictOrOutageIsNotBounded` (same file: a 409 and a 503 past the deadline keep the finalizer), `TestAuraCMK_AKeyInUseIsWaitedOnBounded` (same file). The first and third fail with the earlier controllers put back by `go test -overlay`; the second passes on both, which is the point.
- **enforcement:** unit test; not walked live (the walk has no Aura account). No machine check that a NEW Aura kind's deletion classifies its DELETE through `auraDeleteRefused` — convention + code review.

### id 202 — An AuraDatabase deletion waits for what it needs to reach Aura; only an AuraInstance CR that is gone releases at once, and as a Warning
- **scope:** `handleDeletion`, `instanceGone` and the order of `Reconcile` (`internal/controller/auradatabase_controller.go`); `resolveAuraDBCoords`, `auraDeletionNeedsCredentials` and `auraOrphanedWithoutCredentials` (`internal/controller/aura_common.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **Deletion first.** A terminating `AuraDatabase` is handled before its target is resolved. `Orphan`, `managementPolicies` without `Delete`, or no database ID calls nothing and releases at once, with a Normal `AuraDatabaseOrphaned` when a database is left: what the user asked for.
  - **An `AuraInstance` CR that is gone releases at once, as a Warning.** The credentials, the organization, the project and the instance's Aura ID all come through the `AuraInstance` CR (`resolveAuraDBCoords`). Once it is gone, nothing will reach Aura for the database again: the target that is gone of id 178. The finalizer is released at once with a Warning `AuraDatabaseOrphaned`: "its AuraInstance <name> no longer exists, so the operator cannot reach Aura to delete it. It went with the instance if that was deleted in Aura; otherwise it is still there with its data, so check the Aura console". A Warning and not a Normal, because under the instance's default `Orphan` the database is still there, with its data. A read of the instance that fails with anything but NotFound is not "gone": it waits, as below.
  - **Anything else waits, bounded (id 157).** The credentials Secret or the provider config, the organization or the project, an instance not created yet: phase `Error`, `Ready` False with `CredentialsUnavailable`, one `AuraDatabaseFailed`; after the grace period, a Warning `AuraDatabaseOrphaned` ("it stays on Aura instance <name> with its data").
  - **A DELETE Aura refuses** follows id 201.
- **why:** round 8 of the post-v1.22.0 fixes (2026-10-25, from the code, not walked live): `Reconcile` resolved the target first and, when that failed for a terminating database, released the finalizer at once with a Normal "Orphaning database <id>; it stays in place in Aura" — a database left behind with its data and announced as routine, for causes that usually recover (a namespace teardown that deletes the Secret first). Ids 157 and 178 had kept it as an exception because "an instance that is gone took it with it", which holds only when the instance was deleted in Aura as well.
- **pinned-by:** `TestAuraDatabaseDeletion_AnUnresolvableTargetIsAWarning` (`internal/controller/aura_deletion_refused_test.go`: the instance CR gone — released at once, one Warning, no DELETE; the credentials missing — three passes keep the finalizer with one `AuraDatabaseFailed` and `CredentialsUnavailable`, then a Warning after the grace period; `Orphan` — Normal; everything in place — one DELETE and a Normal `AuraDatabaseDeleted`), and the `AuraDatabase` cases of `TestAuraDeletion_ARefusedDeleteWaitsThenOrphans`. The first three subtests fail on the earlier controller (`go test -overlay`); the last two pass on both.
- **enforcement:** unit test; not walked live.
### id 158 — A Ready user, role, binding or auth rule stays Ready while its target is not; its Ready event follows the write that enters Ready
- **scope:** `recordTargetWait` and `readyAtGeneration` (`internal/controller/target_wait.go`); `targetNotAwaited` (`internal/controller/neo4jauthrule_controller.go`); the `waitForTarget` of `internal/controller/neo4juser_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jrole_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jrolebinding_controller.go` and `internal/controller/neo4jauthrule_controller.go`; the `writeStatus` that reports `entered` in the first three, and what announces from it: `UserReady` (`Reconcile`), `RoleReady` (`reportReady`), `BindingCreated` / `BindingUpdated` (`Reconcile`).
- **rule:**
  - **A target that is not Ready is not a state of its dependents.** When the clusterRef target of a `Neo4jUser`, `Neo4jRole`, `Neo4jRoleBinding` or `Neo4jAuthRule` is neither `Ready` nor `Degraded` (id 105), the reconcile records `ClusterNotReady=True` and requeues. An object that is `Ready` at its generation — phase `Ready` and a `Ready=True` condition the phase writer stamped at the current generation — keeps its phase, message and `Ready` condition. So does one whose status records another outcome of its own for its generation — a failure, or a wait on something other than the target (id 189). Anything else (new, waiting on its target already, or with a spec change not yet applied) is phase `Pending` with `Ready=False`/`ClusterNotReady`, as before. One status write, re-read inside `retry.RetryOnConflict`.
  - **Entering Ready is announced from the write.** `UserReady`, `RoleReady` and `BindingCreated` are emitted when the status write that records `Ready` moved the persisted phase into `Ready` (`entered`; for a role, into `Ready` with reason `RoleReady`, which waits for statements skipped for a missing database, id 216), never from the pass's own copy (id 145); `BindingUpdated` when that write changed `status.grantedRoles` while the binding stayed `Ready`. So the first `Ready`, and a return from a failure of the object's own (a refused grant, a missing role, a connection failure that is not an outage or outlasted one), are announced once; a target restart, a stale cache and an operator restart are not.
  - **A target that reads Ready but cannot be reached is the same wait** (id 172): reason `ClusterUnreachable` instead of `ClusterNotReady`.
  - `ClusterNotFound` (the target is gone) still demotes to `Pending`: a target created again is a new one.
- **why:** what these objects describe lives in the target's `system` database and does not change while the target restarts, and the target announces its own state. Each restart of a standalone made every user on it `Pending` and the pass that found the standalone back moved it into `Ready` again, which `UserReady` announced — 28 times in 40 minutes of restarts on the v1.22.0 re-walk, none in the quiet windows between them (#553). `RoleReady` and `BindingCreated` did the same from the same code, and all three were emitted from the pass's copy (`user.Status.Phase != "Ready"`), so a pass on a cache from before the last write announced again. Each spends the object's event budget (id 136).
- **pinned-by:** `TestUserReconcile_ATargetRestartIsNotATransition`, `TestRoleBindingReconcile_ATargetRestartIsNotATransition`, `TestRoleReconcile_ATargetRestartIsNotATransition`, `TestAuthRuleReconcile_ATargetRestartKeepsItReady`, `TestAuthRuleReconcile_ARefusalOfAReadyTargetIsNotATargetWait` (an auth rule refused by its Ready target's version or OIDC configuration reads `ClusterNotReady=False` after a restart; it kept `True` before), `TestRecordTargetWait` (`internal/controller/auth_target_wait_test.go`): three target restarts and a pass on a stale copy, no new Ready event, and a restarted reconciler none. They fail with a Ready object demoted to `Pending` during the wait (one event per restart), and the user, role and binding cases with the event announced from the copy.
- **enforcement:** unit test. No machine check that a NEW dependent kind keeps its `Ready` through a target wait — convention + code review. The database kinds follow the same rule: id 183.
### id 172 — A target that reads Ready but cannot be reached is the rest of its outage, not a failure of its users, roles, bindings or auth rules
- **scope:** `targetUnreachable`, `targetUnreachableMessage`, `targetUnreachableGrace` and `recordTargetWait` (`internal/controller/target_wait.go`); the `unreachable`, `waitForTarget` and `fail` of `internal/controller/neo4juser_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jrole_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jrolebinding_controller.go` and `internal/controller/neo4jauthrule_controller.go`, and the connection step of each `Reconcile`; the reason `ConditionReasonClusterUnreachable` (`internal/controller/events.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **Unreachable is an outage.** A connection to a target that reads Ready (`clusterAcceptsWork`, or a standalone's `status.ready`) that fails because the target cannot be reached — `IsConnectivityError` (refused, reset, EOF, a timeout), `IsHostUnresolvableError`, or `Neo.TransientError.General.DatabaseUnavailable` — whether at connect or on a later call of the pass (`fail`), is recorded as id 158's wait with reason `ClusterUnreachable`: `ClusterNotReady=True`, an object `Ready` at its generation stays `Ready`, any other waits in `Pending`. No event; logged at info; returned as `RequeueAfter` with a nil error — an error is logged at ERROR by controller-runtime and retried within milliseconds. Any other connection failure (no admin Secret, a refused login, TLS) is still `Failed` with `ConnectionFailed` at once (id 145).
  - **Bounded.** The wait's start is the `ClusterNotReady` condition's `lastTransitionTime`, so `ClusterNotReady=False` is written only after a connection succeeds — written before it, every pass restarted the clock. One exception: a `Neo4jAuthRule` its Ready target's spec refuses (a version older than 2026.03, no OIDC provider listed) never connects, waits on nothing of the target's, and writes `ClusterNotReady=False` with the refusal (`targetNotAwaited`, id 158). An outage that outlasts `targetUnreachableGrace` (5 minutes) is the object's failure: `Failed`, reason `ConnectionFailed`, announced once; the pass that reaches the target again moves it back into `Ready` and announces it (id 158).
  - The status of a wait is written only when it changes.
- **why:** a standalone reads Ready a few seconds before its Bolt port accepts connections, and a `stopCluster` restore stops it for longer. On the v1.22.0 combined-build walk, after each standalone restart `bob`, `analyst` and `extuser-binding` went `Failed` with a `ConnectionFailed` Warning for about five seconds, then announced `UserReady`, `RoleReady` and `BindingCreated` again (counts 2, then 4 over the `stopCluster` restores), and about 180 ERROR lines were logged in two minutes (#559). Id 158 kept them `Ready` while the standalone said it was not; the connection failure that followed undid it.
- **pinned-by:** `TestReconcile_ATargetThatReadsReadyButRefusesIsAnOutage`, `TestFail_ALostConnectionIsAnOutage`, `TestReconcile_AnUnreachableTargetIsReportedAfterTheGrace` and `TestUserReconcile_ARestartThatRefusesBoltIsNotATransition` (`internal/controller/auth_unreachable_target_test.go`), one subtest per kind where it applies; `TestRecordTargetWait` (`internal/controller/auth_target_wait_test.go`). The first two fail with the connection failure written `Failed` again, the third with no grace or with `ClusterNotReady=False` written before the connection.
- **enforcement:** unit test. No machine check that a NEW dependent kind treats an unreachable target this way — convention + code review. The database kinds follow it (id 183), and so does the all-databases restore's loop (id 206).

### id 183 — A Ready database, composite, alias, replica or sharded database stays Ready while its target is not; its Ready event follows the write that enters Ready
- **scope:** `noteTargetWait`, `targetAnswered`, `readyAtGeneration` (which takes the kind's Ready phase) and `recordTargetWait` (`internal/controller/target_wait.go`); `waitForHost`, `hostUnreachable`, `hostAnswered` and the host-readiness, connection, `ensureDatabase` and import steps of `Reconcile` (`internal/controller/neo4jdatabase_controller.go`); `waitForTarget`, `unreachable`, `failPass`, `writeStatus` and `connect` (`internal/controller/neo4jcompositedatabase_controller.go`); `waitForTarget`, `unreachable`, `fail`, `writeStatus` and `connect` (`internal/controller/neo4jdatabasealias_controller.go`); `waitForTarget`, `unreachable`, `fail`, `writeStatus` and `reportReplicating` (`internal/controller/neo4jreplicadatabase_controller.go`); `waitForCluster`, `clusterUnreachable`, `clusterAnswered` and the cluster-readiness, connection and reconcile steps of `Reconcile` (`internal/controller/neo4jshardeddatabase_controller.go`); what announces from those writes: `DatabaseReady` (`reportDatabaseReady`), `CompositeDatabaseReady`, `AliasReady`, `ReplicaReady`, `ShardedDatabaseReady`. Not the deletions: `handleDeletion` and the composite's deletion `fail` are untouched.
- **rule:**
  - **Id 158 and id 172, for the database kinds.** When the clusterRef target of a `Neo4jDatabase`, `Neo4jCompositeDatabase`, `Neo4jDatabaseAlias`, `Neo4jReplicaDatabase` or `Neo4jShardedDatabase` is not Ready (for a sharded database: not `Ready`/`Degraded` with property sharding operational), the reconcile records `ClusterNotReady=True` and requeues. An object Ready at its generation — phase `Ready` (`Replicating` for a replica) and a `Ready=True` condition stamped at the current generation — keeps its phase, message and `Ready` condition. Anything else waits in its kind's waiting phase as before: `Pending` (`Waiting` for a sharded database, its Ready condition still derived from that phase). A promoted replica is never touched. One status write, re-read inside `retry.RetryOnConflict`, and only when it changes.
  - **A target that reads Ready but does not answer is the same wait** — reason `ClusterUnreachable` — at connect and on a later call of the pass (`ensureDatabase`, the initial-data import, the composite's and alias's statements, the replica's lookup and create, the sharded reconcile): no event, logged at info, `RequeueAfter` with a nil error. After `targetUnreachableGrace` (5 minutes, from the condition's `lastTransitionTime`) it is the object's failure: `Failed` with `ConnectionFailed` (`ClientCreationFailed` for a sharded database), announced once. A target that takes the connection and refuses the statements for now — a cluster that lost its majority — is the same wait (id 210), so `ClusterNotReady=False` (reason `ClusterReady`) is written only once the pass's statements answered, not when the connection is made, and only when it changes.
  - **Entering Ready is announced from the write.** `CompositeDatabaseReady`, `AliasReady` and `ReplicaReady` are emitted when the status write moved the persisted phase into `Ready` / `Replicating` (`entered`), never from the pass's copy; `DatabaseReady` and `ShardedDatabaseReady` already came from their status writers. So the first Ready, and a return from a failure of the object's own, are announced once; a target restart, a cluster rollout, a stale cache and an operator restart are not.
  - **The pass's copy follows the write** where the pass writes from it later: the database's `hostAnswered` takes the written status and resourceVersion (its data-import marker is an `Update` of the copy, and a conflict there would import the initial data again on the retry; since id 210 `hostAnswered` runs after the import, just before the Ready report); the sharded writers copy the status back, as `transitionStatus` does.
- **why:** #565: the shape #559 fixed for users, roles, bindings and auth rules was still in the database kinds. `DatabaseReady` turned `Pending` on a host not Ready and announced Ready again when it came back; the composite, alias and replica did the same, and announced from the pass's copy (`if cd.Status.Phase != Ready`); a sharded database wrote `Waiting` over `Ready` whenever its cluster was not `Ready` — every rollout of the cluster, which is `Forming` meanwhile — and announced `ShardedDatabaseReady` after it. A standalone reads Ready a few seconds before Bolt accepts connections (id 172), so without the unreachable half a database on it went `Failed` (`ConnectionFailed`) and back.
- **pinned-by:** `TestDatabaseKinds_ATargetRestartIsNotATransition` (three target restarts, a pass on a stale copy and a restarted reconciler: one Ready event in all, the object `Ready` with `ClusterNotReady=True` meanwhile), `TestDatabaseKinds_ATargetThatRefusesBoltIsAnOutage` (the target reads Ready and refuses the connection: no error, no event, `ClusterUnreachable`), `TestDatabaseKinds_AnObjectNotYetReadyStillWaits`, `TestDatabaseReconcile_AnUnreachableHostIsReportedAfterTheGrace` and `TestDatabaseReconcile_InitialDataIsImportedOnceAfterTheHostAnswered` (the import runs once; fails, with a conflict and a second import, when `hostAnswered` leaves the pass's copy as it was) (`internal/controller/database_target_wait_test.go`), one subtest per kind, through `Reconcile` against a scripted server behind each controller's client seam (the composite and alias seams are new). Each fails with the earlier code for its kind restored — the restart test with `Pending`/`Waiting` written over Ready (and, for the composite, alias and replica, the event from the copy), the outage test with the connection failure written `Failed`. `TestReplicaLagSeries_RemovedWhenLagCannotBeRead` and the connection case of `TestReplicaReconcile_LastingFailuresAreAnnouncedOnce` now use a refused connection as the outage it is and another failure for the backoff path.
- **enforcement:** unit test. No machine check that a NEW dependent kind keeps its Ready through a target wait — convention + code review: a kind with a clusterRef goes through `noteTargetWait` (or `recordTargetWait`) and announces Ready from its status write.

### id 210 — A cluster that lost its majority still reads Ready; a statement it asks to be retried is a wait of its databases, composites, aliases, replicas and sharded databases, not their failure
- **scope:** `targetAway` and `targetAwayMessage` (`internal/controller/target_wait.go`), on `statementTransient` (`internal/controller/transient_retry.go`); the connection and statement steps of each `Reconcile` that id 183 lists, and the writers that record the answer: `hostAnswered` (`internal/controller/neo4jdatabase_controller.go`), the `ClusterNotReady=False` write before the Ready `writeStatus` of `internal/controller/neo4jcompositedatabase_controller.go` and `internal/controller/neo4jdatabasealias_controller.go`, `targetServed` (`internal/controller/neo4jreplicadatabase_controller.go`), `clusterAnswered` (`internal/controller/neo4jshardeddatabase_controller.go`); the database's observation read (`GetDatabases`) and the sharded database's `updateShardStatus` read; `waitForNeo4jReadiness` and `readinessAttemptsFor` (`internal/controller/neo4jshardeddatabase_controller.go`). Not the deletions (id 178 has their own transient handling).
- **rule:**
  - **A statement Neo4j asks to be retried is id 183's wait.** An error `targetAway` accepts — what `targetUnreachable` accepts (id 172), an error `statementTransient` accepts (a `Neo.TransientError.*` other than the target being down, `NotALeader`, `ForbiddenOnReadOnlyDatabase`, `25N11`), or a message that names a `Neo.TransientError.` code once the wrapping lost its type (not `Transaction.Terminated` or `Transaction.LockClientStopped`, which the driver itself does not retry) — at connect or on any statement of the pass is recorded as `ClusterNotReady=True` with reason `ClusterUnreachable` and the message "… reads Ready but cannot serve the request: …". An object Ready at its generation keeps its phase, message and `Ready` condition. No event; logged at info; `RequeueAfter` with a nil error. The same reason as an unreachable target, so a cluster that alternates between refusing the connection and refusing the statements is one wait with one clock.
  - **Bounded, like id 172.** After `targetUnreachableGrace` (5 minutes, from the condition's `lastTransitionTime`) it is the object's failure: `Failed` with `ConnectionFailed` (`ClientCreationFailed` for a sharded database), announced once.
  - **Answered means the statements answered.** `ClusterNotReady=False` is written by the pass whose statements succeeded — the database's just before it reports Ready, the composite's and alias's just before their Ready write, the replica's after a create or before it reports `Replicating`, the sharded database's before its Ready transition. Written after the connection, as before, it reset the wait's clock on every pass of a cluster that takes connections and refuses statements, and the grace never ran out. A pass that ends waiting on something other than the target writes it too, where it stops: the alias whose target database does not exist yet, the composite whose name another database holds, the replica waiting for its upstream, its credentials' rollout or its servers' `AWS_REGION` (and the promoted one), the database waiting for its seed credentials, the sharded database waiting for its seed backup, seed proxy or seed configuration.
  - **A sharded database Ready at its generation, or already waiting out its cluster, asks once** (`readinessAttemptsFor`): the readiness loop's thirty one-second retries are for a cluster that has just formed. They held the reconciler's only worker (`cmd/main.go` runs it with `MaxConcurrentReconciles: 1`) thirty seconds a pass for every sharded database on a cluster that lost its majority. The loop's last error is wrapped (`%w`), so the caller sees what Neo4j answered.
  - **Ready is announced only from a real transition** (id 183): the recovery writes `ClusterNotReady=False` and nothing else.
- **why:** the v1.23 pre-release journey on main (dd429120) stopped two of a three-server cluster's servers. The cluster still read Ready for a while, and every `Neo4jDatabase`, `Neo4jDatabaseAlias` and `Neo4jCompositeDatabase` on it answered `Neo.TransientError.Statement.RemoteExecutionTransientError` ("Failed to obtain connection towards READ server"). `targetUnreachable` does not cover that code, so each kind took its failure branch: `Failed to ensure database` and `Reconciler error` nine or ten times per object, 158 ERROR lines in one second on 2026.08.1 and 232 in two seconds on 5.26.21; `CreationFailed`, `AliasFailed` and `CompositeDatabaseFailed` Warnings in bursts ("combined x16"); and `DatabaseReady`, `AliasReady` and `CompositeDatabaseReady` announced again after recovery. The release's own upgrade notes promise the database kinds keep their phase and do not repeat their Ready event (id 183) and that refusals are logged once, at info (id 197).
- **pinned-by:** `TestDatabaseKinds_ATransientFromAClusterThatLostItsMajorityIsAWait` (three outages of three passes each: no error, every pass under five seconds, the object `Ready` with `ClusterNotReady=True`/`ClusterUnreachable`, no event, no ERROR log; the recovery writes `ClusterNotReady=False` with no event), `TestDatabaseKinds_ATransientThatLastsIsReportedAfterTheGrace` (backdated past the grace: `Failed`, one `ConnectionFailed` / `ClientCreationFailed`, each pass under five seconds), `TestAliasReconcile_AWaitOnTheDatabaseIsNotATargetWait` (after a transient, a pass that waits on the missing target database writes `ClusterNotReady=False`), `TestTargetAway` (`internal/controller/database_target_wait_test.go`), one subtest per kind through `Reconcile`, with `Neo.TransientError.Statement.RemoteExecutionTransientError` (and `NotALeader` for the sharded database). With the five controllers as on dd429120 every subtest of the first two fails: the pass returns the transient as an error. With the sharded readiness loop asking thirty times, the sharded subtest fails the five-second bound (each pass took 29 seconds). Without the write on the alias's database wait, the alias test reads `True`.
- **enforcement:** unit test. No machine check that a NEW statement of these reconcilers routes its error through `targetAway` — convention + code review: a failure branch of a database kind's pass checks `targetAway(err)` first.

### id 190 — A Neo4jDatabase writes only the phases its API reference lists
- **scope:** `databasePhaseFor` and `updateDatabaseStatusObserved` (`internal/controller/neo4jdatabase_controller.go`); the `phase` row of `docs/api_reference/neo4jdatabase.md`; `PhaseUnknown` (`api/v1beta1/phases.go`).
- **rule:** the phase is `Ready` for a Ready condition `True`; otherwise `ValidationFailed` (a refused spec), `Pending` (a wait the next pass carries on from: host missing, not Ready or not answering, seed credentials not on the host yet), `Error` (a drop the deletion retries) or `Failed` — every other reason, including one added later without an arm of its own. There is no `Unknown`: the writer mapped an unlisted reason and a condition status other than `True`/`False` to it, no caller passed either, and the API reference listed it as "any other blocked state". `PhaseUnknown` stays in the shared vocabulary for the CLI and the Argo CD health checks (an older operator wrote it for a host without the seed Secret, id 145).
- **why:** #565 found the branch unreachable; a phase the documentation promises and the code never writes sends readers looking for a state that does not exist, and an unlisted reason is a failure, which is what the CLI's `status --problems` and the Argo CD checks report for `Failed` as for `Unknown`.
- **pinned-by:** `TestExplainTerm_UnknownSaysOnlyOlderOperatorsWriteIt` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/explain_test.go`: `kubectl neo4j explain Unknown` says only operators up to v1.21 write it, for a target without the seed credentials Secret, and no longer sends the reader to a cluster's `status.diagnostics.collectionError`); `TestUpdateDatabaseStatus_WritesOnlyTheDocumentedPhases` (`internal/controller/neo4jdatabase_phase_test.go`: every reason the controller writes, an unlisted one, and each condition status; fails with the `Unknown` arms restored).
- **enforcement:** unit test.

### id 160 — `spec.enabled: false` keeps a plugin off its target's servers; the CR stays, Disabled
