- **scope:** `reconcileDisabled`, `reportDisabled` (`internal/controller/plugin_disabled.go`); the `spec.enabled` branches of `Reconcile`, `deferInstall`, `installChangesTarget`, `desiredTarget` (`internal/controller/plugin_controller.go`); `otherPlugins`, `withoutPlugin`, `writeRemoval` (`internal/controller/plugin_env_ownership.go`); `selfRepair` (`internal/controller/plugin_target_gate.go`); `EventReasonPluginDisabled` (`internal/controller/events.go`); `ConditionReasonDisabled` and the `PhaseDisabled` arm of `PhaseToConditionStatus` (`internal/controller/conditions.go`); `PhaseDisabled` and `AllPhases` (`api/v1beta1/phases.go`); the `Enabled` field (`api/v1beta1/neo4jplugin_types.go`); the phase guidance and the status classification of `kubectl neo4j` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/explain.go`, `cmd/kubectl-neo4j/status.go`); the `Neo4jPlugin` health check (`docs/gitops/argocd-health-checks.yaml`). Already reading the field before: `unionPluginConfSettings` (`internal/controller/plugin_conf_settings.go`, a standalone's neo4j.conf) and `mcpMissingAPOCWarnings` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **Off the servers:** a plugin with `spec.enabled: false` is taken off its target by the write that uninstalls a deleted one (`withoutPlugin`, id 155) — its variables, its list tokens, its `NEO4J_PLUGINS` names, its `VerifiedDownload` init container and volumes, its records — and the CR stays. Phase `Installing` ("Removing the plugin from … (spec.enabled: false)") while the removal rolls out, then `Disabled` with one `PluginDisabled` event from the status write that makes it so (id 136). A plugin with nothing on its target (created disabled, a target without a StatefulSet, a target that does not exist) is `Disabled` at once and writes nothing. `Disabled` is a resting phase, not a fault: `Ready` is `False` with reason `Disabled`; `kubectl neo4j status --problems` does not list it; ArgoCD reports it `Healthy`.
  - **A write like any other:** unlike a deletion's uninstall, which must not wedge a finalizer, the removal waits like an install for a target that is `Ready` with every server available and rolled out (id 141; `Waiting`, one `PluginInstallDeferred`, "… removing the disabled plugin restarts every server"), with id 152's exception: `selfRepair` judges the plugin's next write (`desiredTarget`), so disabling the plugin whose init container or setting every down server keeps failing in goes through at once, with one `PluginInitContainerFailing` or `PluginSettingsFailing` Warning, and on a standalone the stuck pod is deleted (`replaceStuckPods`).
  - **Back on:** `spec.enabled: true` installs the plugin again — the next pass finds it neither settled nor part way through an install of its generation.
  - **A disabled duplicate:** a disabled CR is never held back by the duplicate check, and its removal leaves what an enabled CR with the same `spec.name` installs (id 195).
  - **Nothing is kept for a disabled plugin:** `otherPlugins` leaves disabled plugins out, so another plugin's write or uninstall keeps none of a disabled plugin's list tokens or `NEO4J_PLUGINS` names; what a disabled plugin still records on the StatefulSet is protected by its record until its own pass takes it away. A standalone's neo4j.conf union and the MCP APOC check already left disabled plugins out. A plugin refused by its target's image (id 170) is off its target too (`pluginOnTarget`), is left out the same way, and is taken off by the same write.
  - **On a standalone** a disabled plugin with neo4j.conf settings costs two restarts, as its install does: the standalone controller drops the settings from its conf at once and rolls; the plugin's removal waits for that roll, then rolls again.
- **why:** #540 (found while fixing #539, from the code): the plugin controller never read `spec.enabled`, so a plugin set to `false` was installed and stayed installed, while the standalone's conf union and the MCP check, which read it, treated it as absent — on a standalone the JAR was loaded without its allowlist. Knowledge rule 102 says such a field must work or warn; it now works. Until v1.22.0 a whole-object Update also stored `enabled: false` as `true` (id 150), so most disabled plugins only became disabled once re-applied.
- **pinned-by:** `TestReconcile_DisablingAPluginTakesItOffItsTarget` (cluster and standalone: one write, `Installing` until the roll is done, `Disabled` with one event, a quiet pass, then installed again), `TestReconcile_APluginCreatedDisabledIsNeverInstalled`, `TestReconcile_ARemovalOfADisabledPluginWaitsForTheTarget`, `TestReconcile_DisablingThePluginThatKeepsServersDownDoesNotWait`, `TestOtherPlugins_LeavesOutDisabledPlugins` (`internal/controller/plugin_disabled_test.go`); `TestPhaseToConditionStatus_Table` (`internal/controller/conditions_unit_test.go`); `TestResourceStatus_EveryPhaseInTheVocabularyIsClassified` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/status_test.go`) and `TestEveryPhaseConstantHasGuidance` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/explain_test.go`) for the new phase. Each of the controller tests fails with the `spec.enabled` branches and the `otherPlugins` filter switched off (`go test -overlay`); the repair test also with `selfRepair` judging the install.
- **enforcement:** unit test; not walked live yet.

### id 161 — A plugin whose procedures the target's allowlist hides says so; the shared list is not widened
- **scope:** `reportHiddenProcedures`, `hiddenProceduresMessage`, `effectiveAllowlist`, `allowlistLoadsAny`, `pluginProcedureNamespaces`, `setProceduresHidden` (`internal/controller/plugin_allowlist.go`); its call in `Reconcile` (`internal/controller/plugin_controller.go`); `ConditionTypeProceduresHidden`, `ConditionReasonNotInAllowlist` (`internal/controller/conditions.go`); `EventReasonPluginProceduresHidden` (`internal/controller/events.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **The list is one for the server:** `dbms.security.procedures.allowlist` (default `*`; an empty value loads nothing) holds for every plugin of the target; once any plugin's `allowedProcedures` or `spec.config`, or the target's own `spec.config`, sets it, the procedures and functions it does not match are not loaded at all (configuration settings and "Securing extensions", Operations Manual `5.x` and `dev`, read 2026-10-24).
  - **What the servers start with** (`effectiveAllowlist`): on a cluster, the union of the setting in its neo4j.conf (`{target}-config`: its own `spec.config` and Aura Fleet Management's entry) and the neo4j container's `NEO4J_dbms_security_procedures_allowlist` (its plugins' tokens), which its startup script joins before the entrypoint writes the variable in place of the conf's line (id 179). On a standalone, that variable when someone else set one — the entrypoint replaces the conf's line with it (`add_env_setting_to_conf`); its plugins and its own `spec.env` put their lists in its conf (id 193) — else the conf's setting, the union of its own `spec.config`, its `spec.env` and its plugins' settings. The conf is the one the target's controller renders for its spec now, not the ConfigMap as it is (id 194). A variable read from a Secret or ConfigMap cannot be judged and is not. The message names the plugins that set the list and, when its own spec does (`targetSetsAllowlist`: the key in `spec.config`), the target's own configuration. Aura Fleet Management only adds its entry to a list something else sets (id 192).
  - **Judged** (`pluginProcedureNamespaces`, `allowlistLoadsAny`): the plugins the operator knows — APOC and APOC Extended (`apoc.`), GDS (`gds.`), Bloom (`bloom.`), GenAI (`genai.` and, from 2025.11, `ai.`), N10s (`n10s.`), Fleet Management (`fleetManagement.`) — are hidden when no entry of the list can match a name in any of their namespaces. A list that loads part of a plugin (`apoc.coll.*`) is the user's choice and does not count. A custom plugin is not judged.
  - **Said, not changed:** a hidden plugin stays `Ready` (its JAR is on the servers) and carries the condition `ProceduresHidden` (`True`, reason `NotInAllowlist`) naming the list, where it is read and the enabled plugins that put entries in it (or the target's own configuration), with one `PluginProceduresHidden` Warning from the status write that adds the condition or changes its message (ids 136, 145). The condition goes, silently, when the list loads the plugin again or the plugin is disabled. Every pass computes it — a settled one too, which another plugin's write to the StatefulSet wakes (id 135) — and writes only a change, so an intact settled plugin still writes nothing.
  - **Not widened:** the operator does not add a plugin's namespace to the list. The list is a security boundary someone set; loading procedures they did not list is not the operator's call, and an automatic union would also hide the trap the next time someone narrows it.
- **why:** the v1.22.0 re-walk (#553, 2026-10-23): installing the published GenAI example (`allowedProcedures: genai.*, ai.*`) beside a `VerifiedDownload` APOC plugin with no `security` block made `apoc.version()` fail with 42N48 while APOC stayed `Ready`, and nothing said why. The trap was documented (the plugins guide's "an allowlist, not a grant") but invisible where it bit.
- **pinned-by:** `TestAllowlistLoadsAny`, `TestReconcile_APluginHiddenByAnotherPluginsAllowlistSaysSo` (cluster: the condition and one Warning, the plugin `Ready`, nothing written to the StatefulSet, a quiet second pass, the condition gone once the list loads APOC), `TestReconcile_AStandalonePluginHiddenByTheConfAllowlistSaysSo`, `TestReconcile_NoHiddenProceduresWithoutAKnownListAndNamespace`, `TestReconcile_ADisabledPluginCarriesNoHiddenProcedures` (`internal/controller/plugin_allowlist_test.go`). The reconcile tests fail with the call in `Reconcile` removed. A plugin refused by its target's image (id 170) carries no condition either, and is not named as a setter: `TestReconcile_ARefusedPluginCarriesNoHiddenProcedures` (`internal/controller/plugin_refused_test.go`).
- **enforcement:** unit test; not walked live yet.

### id 170 — A Managed plugin is judged by the names its target's image installs; a name it does not list is refused, and an earlier write of it taken off
- **scope:** `entrypointPluginLists`, `entrypointPluginNames`, `entrypointBrokenPlugins`, `brokenEntrypointPlugin`, `EntrypointPluginName`, `managedPluginEntries`, `managedNameUnknownToEveryImage`, `ManagedPluginImageErrors`, `ManagedPluginNameWarnings`, `PluginTargetImageTag` (`internal/validation/plugin_entrypoint.go`); the compatibility-matrix warning of `PluginValidator.Validate` (`internal/validation/plugin_validator.go`); `targetImageTag`, `pluginRefusal`, `pluginOnImage`, `pluginOnTarget`, `reconcileRefused`, `reportRefused` (`internal/controller/plugin_refused.go`); the refusal branch of `Reconcile`, `desiredTarget`, `mapPluginName`, `writePluginStatus` (`internal/controller/plugin_controller.go`); `otherPlugins` (`internal/controller/plugin_env_ownership.go`); `unionPluginConfSettings` (`internal/controller/plugin_conf_settings.go`) and its call in `collectStandalonePluginConf` (`internal/controller/standalone_conf.go`); `reportHiddenProcedures`, `hiddenProceduresMessage` (`internal/controller/plugin_allowlist.go`); `EventReasonPluginUnknownToImage` (`internal/controller/events.go`); the `Neo4jPlugin` validator of `kubectl neo4j validate` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **The lists are data, per release:** the Neo4j Docker entrypoint looks every `NEO4J_PLUGINS` name up in the image's `/startup/neo4j-plugins.json` and exits 1 on one it does not list ("is not a known Neo4j plugin", `install_neo4j_plugins`). `entrypointPluginLists` is that list per release range, read from `neo4j/docker-neo4j` at every release tag (5.26.0–5.26.32, 2025.01.0–2026.10.1): `apoc`, `apoc-extended`, `bloom`, `genai`, `graph-data-science` everywhere; `n10s` on 5.26 and CalVer before 2025.05.0; `fleet-management` from 5.26.11 and 2025.08.0; `graphql` nowhere. One table, one line after the other; add an entry when a release changes its list. Never encode a plugin name's support from memory (id 103).
  - **Judged by the target's tag:** a Managed, enabled plugin's names — its own and its dependencies', after the aliases `gds` and `neosemantics` (`EntrypointPluginName`, which `mapPluginName` delegates to) — are checked against the list of its target's `spec.image.tag`. A tag without a patch is its line's newest patch (the list a floating tag pulls; within 5.26 the newest list is the widest, so this never refuses what an older patch would install). A CalVer newer than the table is judged by its newest list — an assumption, stated in the table's comment: a later image could install a name the table does not know, and the table must then learn it. A tag that names no supported version is not judged (the image rule refuses it). `VerifiedDownload` and `PreBaked` never touch `NEO4J_PLUGINS` and are never refused; neither is a disabled plugin.
  - **Refused, nothing written:** the plugin is phase `Failed` with the field error (`spec.name: Invalid value: "graphql": not a plugin the neo4j:<tag> image can install: …; install its JAR with installMode: VerifiedDownload (source.url + source.checksum) instead`, or `spec.dependencies[i].name`) and one `PluginUnknownToImage` Warning, announced from the status write that moves it into the refusal (ids 136, 145; `reportRefused` reads the status it replaced from the write, so the removal's steps, which change the message, and a restarted operator announce nothing; a different refusal — the target's image changed — announces once more). It is looked at again every `RequeueAfter`, since the target's tag changes without an edit of the plugin. A refused plugin is not on its target (`pluginOnTarget`): `otherPlugins`, a standalone's neo4j.conf union and the allowlist check leave it out, as they leave out a disabled one (ids 160, 161).
  - **The repair:** what an earlier write left on the target — every operator up to v1.22.x wrote these names, and a target moved to an image that drops one (5.26 → CalVer with `n10s`) has it too — is taken off by the write a disabled plugin's removal makes (`withoutPlugin`, `desiredTarget` judging the removal; ids 155, 160): its `NEO4J_PLUGINS` names, its variables, list tokens and records. The names are those its record lists (`neo4j.com/plugin-entries.<plugin>`) **and** its own and its dependencies' as the entrypoint knows them: with no record — every plugin an earlier operator installed — the mapped name is exactly what that operator wrote (the mapping has not changed), and a name the image does not list can be needed by nothing else, since no server starts with it. Names another plugin on the target adds, `fleet-management` while the target installs it, and a `NEO4J_PLUGINS` the target renders itself are kept. The write waits like any (id 141) — message "…; it is taken off <target> once it is Ready with every server available, since that restarts every server" — except under the self-repair conditions (ids 152, 155): every down server keeps failing in its neo4j container while running the variable the write changes, the highest ordinals, nothing else unavailable. Then it is written at once with one `PluginSettingsFailing` Warning ("…; taking it off <target> although it is not Ready: …"), the roll replaces those pods first, and a standalone's stuck pod is deleted (`replaceStuckPods`). While the roll runs the message ends "…: waiting for the servers to restart without it", then it is the refusal alone.
  - **A listed name whose JAR the release does not run is refused too** (`entrypointBrokenPlugins`, read 2026-10-25): `n10s` from 5.26.22 — the entrypoint downloads it by neosemantics' versions.json, which maps every 5.26.x to neosemantics 5.26.0, the only release for 5.26; that JAR bundles jackson-annotations 2.18.0 unshaded, Neo4j moved from Jackson 2.18.0 to 2.21 at 5.26.22 (2.22 by 5.26.32; the root `pom.xml` of `neo4j/neo4j` at each tag), and the server stops at startup with `NoSuchMethodError … JsonProperty.isRequired()` — and `n10s` on CalVer 2025.01–2025.04, whose lists still hold it but for which the versions.json names no release (the entrypoint starts the server without it). The error reads `not a plugin the neo4j:<tag> image can run in installMode Managed: <why>; <instead>`, through the same refusal and repair. On 5.26.0–5.26.21 n10s runs and is installed. No neosemantics release starts on 5.26.22 or later; on CalVer the guide's `VerifiedDownload` example installs neosemantics 2025.06.1 (Java 21, no Jackson bundled; verified on 2026.08.1: 55 procedures, 30 functions, `n10s.graphconfig.init()`; on 2026.09.0 it loads only 39 of 55 procedures — four classes fail with `IllegalAccessError` under Neo4j's `ProcedureClassLoader`, and `n10s.graphconfig.init` is missing; the JAR's limit there, which the operator does not judge for a `VerifiedDownload` plugin; v1.22.0 re-walk, 2026-10-25).
  - **An image change the plugin cannot follow is refused at the deployment** (id 187), before any server restarts on it; this rule's refusal and repair are for what reaches the servers all the same — a deployment that already ran such an image, a plugin created on one, an earlier operator's roll.
  - **`fleet-management` from 2026.03 is neither refused nor installed** (id 192): the CalVer list still names it, with no versions URL and no JAR in `products/`, so the entrypoint wgets "null", logs "ERROR: could not query null …" and starts without it — it does not exit, so the name is not refused; Fleet Manager is built in. A Managed `fleet-management` plugin on such an image installs nothing and is `Ready` (`reconcileBuiltIn`); `kubectl neo4j validate --connect` warns (`ManagedPluginBuiltInWarnings`), and offline says when it applies.
  - **The CLI:** `kubectl neo4j validate --connect` resolves the target as the controller does (`PluginTargetImageTag`: cluster first, then standalone) and reports the same error; with no target, or one it cannot read, it says so and applies the offline rule. Offline it cannot know the image, so it warns (`ManagedPluginNameWarnings`) about a name no supported release installs, and about `n10s`, which runs on 5.26.0–5.26.21 only, and its summary says the image check needs `--connect`. The compatibility matrix says nothing of a Managed name no supported image installs (`managedNameUnknownToEveryImage`): it printed "not in the operator's known compatibility matrix" beside the specific warning for `graphql` (v1.22.0 re-walk); the same name in PreBaked or VerifiedDownload mode keeps the matrix note.
- **why:** #563 (confirmed on `neo4j:5.26-enterprise` and `neo4j:2026.08.1-enterprise`, 2026-10-24): `graphql` crash-looped every supported line and `n10s` every CalVer target, while the operator mapped both in `mapPluginName`, kept `neo4j-graphql` in its compatibility matrix, and documented both, with examples. `graphql` left the 5.x list in `docker-neo4j` #508 (2024-08-28), before 5.26.0; `n10s` left CalVer in #556 (2025-05-20, first in 2025.05.0). The same held for any custom name in Managed mode — the guide's "Custom and URL plugins" example was a Managed plugin with `source.url`, which the operator never reads in that mode. Not walked live yet; found on the way: `fleet-management` is not on 5.26.0–5.26.10 or 2025.01–2025.07, so a Managed `fleet-management` plugin is refused there too, and so is `spec.auraFleetManagement` itself (id 191).
- **pinned-by:** `TestEntrypointPluginNames` (per release, floating tags, a CalVer newer than the table, unjudged tags), `TestManagedPluginImageErrors` (graphql on both lines, n10s per line, aliases, a dependency, a custom name, `fleet-management` before 5.26.11, VerifiedDownload/PreBaked/disabled not judged, the message word for word), `TestManagedPluginImageErrors_N10sWhereItsJARDoesNotRun` (5.26.21 installs, 5.26.22, 5.26.32 and a floating 5.26 refused on Jackson, CalVer 2025.01–2025.04 refused, a dependency, VerifiedDownload not judged), `TestManagedPluginNameWarnings`, `TestPluginValidator_AManagedNameNoImageInstallsHasNoMatrixWarning` (`internal/validation/plugin_validator_test.go`), `TestReconcile_N10sIsRefusedWhereItsJARDoesNotRun` (`internal/controller/plugin_refused_test.go`), `TestValidate_Connected_ChecksAPluginAgainstItsTargetsImage` and `TestValidate_Offline_WarnsAboutAPluginNoImageInstalls` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate_plugin_image_test.go`: one offline warning for graphql, one for n10s), `TestPluginTargetImageTag` (`internal/validation/plugin_entrypoint_test.go`); `TestPublishedPluginManifestsRaiseNoWarning` (`internal/validation/examples_test.go`: every published plugin installs on `5.26-enterprise` and `2026.08-enterprise` and raises no offline warning); `TestReconcile_APluginTheTargetsImageCannotInstallIsRefused`, `TestReconcile_APluginTheTargetsImageListsIsInstalled`, `TestReconcile_AVerifiedDownloadOfAnyJARIsNotRefused`, `TestReconcile_ARefusedPluginComesOffTheServersItKeepsDown` (no record, and a record listing a dropped dependency), `TestReconcile_ARefusedPluginsRemovalWaitsWhenSomethingElseIsDown`, `TestReconcile_AnImageChangeRefusesAnInstalledPlugin`, `TestReconcile_ARefusedPluginsStandalonePodIsReplaced`, `TestOtherPlugins_LeavesOutRefusedPlugins`, `TestReconcile_ARefusedPluginCarriesNoHiddenProcedures` (`internal/controller/plugin_refused_test.go`); `TestUnionPluginConfSettings` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_configmap_test.go`); `TestValidate_Connected_ChecksAPluginAgainstItsTargetsImage`, `TestValidate_Offline_WarnsAboutAPluginNoImageInstalls` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate_plugin_image_test.go`). Each part switched off with `go test -overlay` fails its tests: the refusal, the per-line table (`n10s` kept on CalVer), floating tags read as patch 0, the repair, the removal judged as the install, the `otherPlugins` and union filters, the announce-once rule, the allowlist filter, and the CLI's connected and offline checks.
- **enforcement:** unit test; not walked live yet.

### id 173 — A plugin's write adds only its own tokens to a cluster's shared lists; another plugin's arrive with that plugin's write
- **scope:** `setClusterPluginLists`, `pluginListValue`, `splitCSVTokens`, and their callers `applyPlugin` and `withoutPlugin` (`internal/controller/plugin_env_ownership.go`); `targetDrift` and `pluginEnvDrift` (`internal/controller/plugin_controller.go`), which judge a settled pass by what `applyPlugin` makes of the StatefulSet; `pluginListSettings` (`internal/controller/plugin_conf_settings.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **Add only your own:** a plugin's write (its install, an edit, a restore) adds to each shared procedure and extension list of a cluster only its own tokens. A token already in the list stays while this plugin or another enabled, not refused, not deleted plugin of the target asks for it, and goes when none does. A plugin's removal — its deletion's uninstall, `spec.enabled: false` (id 160), a refusal (id 170) — adds nothing at all.
  - **So a settled plugin's drift is its own:** `targetDrift` builds what a settled plugin would write with `applyPlugin`, so a list counts as missing only when the plugin's own tokens are not there. A plugin that has not written yet (one waiting for its target, one just created), and a plugin whose tokens something removed, put their tokens there with their own write — the second with its own `PluginMissing`.
  - **A recreated StatefulSet** (a storage expansion recreates it from the cluster's own template, without what the plugins wrote) gives a disabled or refused plugin nothing to remove, and its pass writes nothing; each enabled plugin restores its own tokens.
  - **Only the plugins' tokens:** the variables hold what the plugins ask for, never the cluster's own entries (its `spec.config` lists, Aura Fleet Management's); the servers join the two at their start (id 179), so a change on either side is that side's one write. The fleet-management plugin asks for no allowlist entry (`pluginListSettings`): the servers add it to an allowlist set elsewhere (id 192).
- **why:** the v1.22.0 combined walk (#561, A5): installing the published GenAI example (`allowedProcedures: genai.*, ai.*`) beside a Ready APOC and GDS took three rolls instead of two. #548's fix built each list over every enabled plugin of the target, GenAI too before GenAI had written anything, so GDS's settled pass — woken by a change of the pod template — found the allowlist missing, announced `PluginMissing` ("StatefulSet c-server does not hold what the plugin sets in NEO4J_dbms_security_procedures_allowlist") and wrote GenAI's tokens; GenAI's own install rolled the servers again. The same walk saw a disabled plugin make a removal write after a storage expansion had recreated the StatefulSet: the removal rebuilt the lists over the other plugins and wrote their tokens back. Reproduced in unit tests on the stack before this fix (the same Warning text, one write each).
- **pinned-by:** `TestReconcile_ASettledPluginDoesNotWriteAPendingPluginsTokens` (GDS and APOC settled beside a GenAI plugin that has not written: no write, no `PluginMissing`; GenAI's install then one write with its tokens and nobody's lost; the settled plugins quiet after it), `TestReconcile_ADisabledPluginWritesNothingToARecreatedStatefulSet`, `TestPluginListValue` (`internal/controller/plugin_shared_lists_test.go`); and #548's `TestReconcile_ClusterProcedureListsFollowThePlugins`, `TestUninstall_LeavesWhatOthersNeed`, which still hold. The two reconcile tests fail on the previous `setClusterPluginLists` (`go test -overlay`).
### id 171 — A StatefulSet a storage expansion deleted is created again as it was, in the same pass, every server keeping the revision it runs; nothing restarts
- **scope:** `deletedStatefulSets` (`remember`, `forget`, `remembered`, `source`), `orphanedRevisions`, `readRevisionTemplate`, `recreateSource.applyTo`, `recreateSource.holdBelowPartition`, `restorePending`, `restoreHeldTemplate`, `stampTemplateHashIfApplied`, `templateApplied`, `storedTemplate`, `appliedTemplate`, `sortTemplateItems`, `statefulSetPartition`, `waitStatefulSetGone`, `templateFromControllerRevision`, `ownedBy`, `recreateRestorePartitionAnnotation`, `recreateRestoreRevisionAnnotation` (`internal/controller/statefulset_recreate.go`); the create branch and the second step at the head of the update branch of `createOrUpdateResourceInternal`, `apiReader`, the `deletedServerStatefulSets` and `APIReader` fields, the forget in `handleDeletion` and the requeue after `reconcileStorageExpansion` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`); `reconcileStorageExpansion`, `orphanDeleteStatefulSet` (`internal/controller/storage_expansion.go`); the restore wait in `stepUpgradeStaging` (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade_statemachine.go`); the restoring case of `serverImageInPlace` (`internal/controller/upgrade_hold.go`); the seed before `CreateOrUpdate` in `reconcileStatefulSet`, `apiReader`, the `deletedStatefulSets` and `APIReader` fields, the forget in `handleDeletion` and the requeue after `reconcileStandaloneStorageExpansion` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`); `reconcileStandaloneStorageExpansion`, `orphanDeleteStandaloneStatefulSet` (`internal/controller/standalone_storage_expansion.go`); the `APIReader` of both reconcilers (`cmd/main.go`); the RBAC `get` on `apps/controllerrevisions` (`config/rbac/role.yaml` and its Helm and bundle copies, generated from the marker in `statefulset_recreate.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **As it was.** A volume claim template cannot change, so a storage expansion patches the claims, orphan-deletes the StatefulSet (its pods keep running) and creates it again — Kubernetes' way to resize a StatefulSet's volumes, which keeps the pod template. The StatefulSet created again takes the deleted one's pod template, replica count, update strategy (a held upgrade's partition, id 149) and annotations (the ownership records of ids 141 and 155, the template hash), with the new claim templates and everything else from the builder. The template is the same, so the revision is the same and no pod restarts. The next pass applies the deployment's spec as it would to any StatefulSet (an image or plugin change made with the expansion waits for that pass, with its usual gates).
  - **In the same pass.** An orphan delete is not immediate: the garbage collector releases the pods and revisions before it removes the object, and until then the cache still holds it. The pass that deleted it waits, at most 15s, until the API server (uncached, `APIReader`) and the cache both have it no more (`waitStatefulSetGone`), and goes on to create it again; when the wait runs out it requeues after 1s. It used to return, and the next pass, started by the delete's watch event, still read the StatefulSet, applied the spec to it and waited for the formation checks: the StatefulSet came back about 10s later (#562 re-walk).
  - **Every server keeps the revision it runs.** A StatefulSet creates a pod at or above its partition from its update revision (its template) and one below it from its current revision, and Kubernetes sets a new StatefulSet's current revision to its update revision. Created again on a held upgrade's template, every server below the partition that restarted would come back on the upgrade's target, ungated. So when the pods below the partition run another revision than the template — the deleted StatefulSet's `status.currentRevision` differs from its `status.updateRevision`, or, from the orphaned pods, the lowest ordinal's revision from the highest's — it is created in two steps (`holdBelowPartition`): first with the template of the revision the servers below the partition run, frozen at the replica count (no pod is updated), the partition and the held template's revision recorded in the annotations `neo4j.com/recreate-restore-partition` and `neo4j.com/recreate-restore-revision`; then, once the StatefulSet controller has recorded its current revision at the generation written (`status.currentRevision`, `observedGeneration`), the held template — from memory, else from that revision — and the partition are written back and the annotations dropped (`restoreHeldTemplate`). A server then comes back on the revision it ran before the expansion if it restarts. Until the second step nothing else is written to the template (the update branch returns after it), the image in place is the servers' (`serverImageInPlace`, id 149), and Staging waits, writing the second step itself when it can — the normal pass does not run while the machine is active. Without the memory and without a readable revision, the annotations are dropped and the servers stay frozen on the template below: the upgrade writes its target again when it goes on (id 162). A partition of zero or none, and a standalone (one replica), need no second step.
  - **Memory, then the revision.** The deleted StatefulSet is kept in memory from the delete until the pass that creates it again, or that writes the held template back (`deletedStatefulSets`, a `sync.Map` keyed `namespace/name`, id 129), and only for the deployment that owned it (owner UID); a deployment deleted meanwhile forgets it. After an operator restart between the delete and the create, the template is read from the ControllerRevision the highest-ordinal orphaned pod runs (its `controller-revision-hash` label; the deleted StatefulSet's revisions are orphaned with it), read uncached through `APIReader` — the cache holds no ControllerRevisions and the operator has only `get` on them — and the replica count is that ordinal plus one. Neither the strategy nor the annotations are known then: the held partition comes from the upgrade record as before (id 149), the cluster records the template items it renders, and the template hash is stamped when the update branch's apply of what the operator renders would leave the template as the API server stores it — its merge of env vars, init containers, volumes and pod annotations, compared by name (`stampTemplateHashIfApplied`, `templateApplied`, `appliedTemplate`), as on any write of the template; a cluster short of a server applies only what the stamp shows changed (`isTemplateChangeSignificant`), and a spec changed with the expansion keeps no stamp, so the next pass writes it; in a two-step create, with the held template, in the second step; the next pass's apply, which keeps every foreign env var, init container and volume (ids 141, 155), leaves the template as it is (pinned for a held cluster and one no upgrade holds). No pods, or a revision that cannot be read: the StatefulSet is created from the spec, as before.
  - **Compared as the API server stores them.** A revision's template is the one the API server stored: defaulted (probe `successThreshold`, `terminationMessagePath`/`terminationMessagePolicy`, `restartPolicy`, `dnsPolicy`, `terminationGracePeriodSeconds`, `schedulerName`, a volume with no source made an `emptyDir`) and converted (`deprecatedServiceAccount`); what the operator renders carries none of it. So both templates — the one about to be written and the apply of what the operator renders — go through the write the operator is about to make, the create of the StatefulSet or the update of the live one, as a dry run (`storedTemplate`, `client.DryRunAll`), and are compared as the API server returns them: its own defaulting, conversion and mutating admission, whatever its version, on both sides, whatever form the template in hand is in. Not a list of defaults to fill in (the defaulters live in `k8s.io/kubernetes`, not a dependency, and conversion fields such as `deprecatedServiceAccount` are not defaults), not a comparison of only the fields the operator sets (it would hide a value someone else set where the operator sets none), and not the field-by-field check of `isTemplateChangeSignificant` (it compares too little: the stamp is what catches a `nodeSelector` or a probe, so stamping on it would hide such a change for good). A dry run the API server refuses (a quota, a webhook) stamps nothing, logged at info; the next write of the template stamps it, as before.
  - **Never from a pod.** A template rebuilt from a running pod is not the template: admission adds volumes (the service-account token), tolerations and defaults to every pod, so it would be a new revision and roll every pod.
- **why:** the #562 walk (2026-10-24): on a 3-server cluster with APOC and GDS installed and an upgrade held at partition 2, a storage expansion created the server StatefulSet again from the cluster's own template, without `NEO4J_PLUGINS`, the plugins' variables, the procedure lists, `plugin-init-*` and `plugin-ca`. The template was a new revision, so the StatefulSet controller replaced the running server-2 while server-1 was held down — quorum lost — the roll ran ungated, and the plugins restored their writes in three more rolls: the plugins were gone for about 17 minutes. The #562 re-walk (2026-10-25), twice, the second time with no plugins: an upgrade paused on `2026.09.9-enterprise` (never published; server-2 in `ImagePullBackOff`, partition 2), a storage expansion — created again as it was, same pods, revisions and partition — then `2026.09.0-enterprise`: server-2 was created again from the new StatefulSet's current revision, which Kubernetes had set to the held 2026.09.9 revision at its creation, and pulled the unpublished image again; with the Staging that ran twice (id 162), the upgrade paused on "timed out waiting for server ordinal 3" and the cluster stayed `Degraded`, every tag change only `UpgradeDeferred`. The same re-walk found the hash not stamped after an operator restart between the delete and the create, and the 10s gap. The re-walk of 2a50fa56 (2026-10-25), an operator restart between the delete and the create forced by holding the orphan-deleted StatefulSet with a finalizer: the hash still absent, every pass logging "still forming", then "Skipping … no significant changes", until Staging wrote the template — the stamp compared the revision's stored template with what the operator renders, which never match on a real cluster, and the tests built the "revision" from the builder, so they matched. Kubernetes documents the orphan delete as keeping the pods (`kubectl delete --cascade=orphan`), and the StatefulSet controller keys a pod's revision on the template's hash (`controller-revision-hash`); a new StatefulSet's current revision is its update revision (`getStatefulSetRevisions`, `pkg/controller/statefulset/stateful_set_control.go` in kubernetes/kubernetes).
- **pinned-by:** `TestCreateOrUpdateResource_AStatefulSetAnExpansionDeletedIsCreatedAgainAsItWas` (the plugins' template, the held partition and the records kept, the new claim size, the memory forgotten), `TestCreateOrUpdateResource_AfterARestartTheTemplateComesFromTheRevision` (no memory: the orphaned pods' revision, three replicas, the partition from the record, the stamp, and the next pass's apply leaves the template, held or not; a first create with no pods takes the spec), `TestTemplateApplied` (a spec changed with the expansion gets no stamp, nor does a template whose dry run is refused), `TestTemplateFromControllerRevision`, `TestReconcileStatefulSet_AStandaloneStatefulSetAnExpansionDeletedIsCreatedAgainAsItWas` (`internal/controller/statefulset_recreate_test.go`); `TestStatefulSetCreatedAgainFromARevisionIsStamped_APIServer` (envtest: revisions holding the templates the API server stored, with the plugins' items; created again from one in one step and in two under a held partition, each stamped; the live StatefulSet's update applies nothing, a `nodeSelector` added to the spec gets no stamp, a template not in stored form compares the same, and the next passes leave the generation; `internal/controller/statefulset_recreate_apiserver_test.go`); `TestHeldUpgrade_AnExpansionThenTheFixedTagWalksToTheEnd` (the re-walk's sequence against `statefulSetEmulator`, which plays the StatefulSet controller's revision rules: after the expansion the current revision is still the one servers 0 and 1 run and nothing restarted, and the fixed tag walks to Stabilizing with each server created once, on it), `TestHeldUpgrade_AnExpansionAcrossAnOperatorRestartKeepsTheRevisions` (from the revisions: first frozen on the servers' template with the annotations, the image in place the servers', no change before the controller has recorded the revision, then the held template, partition 2 and the stamp), `TestStepUpgradeStaging_WritesTheHeldTemplateBackFirst`, `TestWaitStatefulSetGone` (`internal/controller/upgrade_expansion_resume_test.go`); `TestClusterStorageExpansion_TheSamePassCreatesTheStatefulSetAgain` (`internal/controller/walk_noise_test.go`) and the expandable-StorageClass case of `TestReconcileStandaloneStorageExpansion` (`internal/controller/standalone_storage_expansion_test.go`). Each fails with its part reverted under `go test -overlay`: the memory, the revision fallback, the standalone's seed, the two steps, the wait for the recorded revision, the stamp, the stamp's dry run (and its dry run of the template in hand, and the refused dry run), the image in place while restoring, Staging's wait, and the same-pass create on either kind.
- **enforcement:** unit test + envtest (the stamp against the API server's own defaults). The re-walk of 2a50fa56 walked the restart case live (an operator restart between the delete and the create) and found the stamp missing; re-walk it.

### id 177 — A target whose StatefulSet does not exist is waited for quietly; it is not drift and not a failure. A target that does not exist at all is waited for too
- **scope:** `statefulSetAbsent`, `awaitStatefulSet`, `awaitTarget` (`internal/controller/plugin_target_absent.go`); `errTargetNotFound`, `getTargetDeployment` and its callers in `Reconcile` and `uninstallPlugin` (`internal/controller/plugin_controller.go`); the wait in `Reconcile` after the refusal branch, and the NotFound arms of its settled branch, `installChangesTarget` and `writePluginToTarget` calls; `targetDrift`, which returns the NotFound rather than a "does not exist" drift (`internal/controller/plugin_controller.go`); the NotFound arm of `reconcileDisabled` (`internal/controller/plugin_disabled.go`); `effectiveAllowlist` and `reportHiddenProcedures` (`internal/controller/plugin_allowlist.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **The case:** the plugin's target exists (the cluster or standalone CR) but its StatefulSet does not — not created yet, or deleted by a storage expansion that creates it again, as it was, seconds later (id 171). Nothing of the plugin was taken off it and nothing can be put back yet.
  - **No event, no ERROR, no needless status write:** a plugin `Ready` or `Disabled` at its generation keeps its status and is not requeued — the StatefulSet's creation wakes a `Ready` plugin (id 135), whose settled pass finds what it set in a StatefulSet created as it was (nothing written, no `PluginMissing`, no `PluginInstalled` again) and restores, with the one `PluginMissing` that says so, only what a new StatefulSet lacks. A plugin part way through its generation (`Installing`, `Waiting`, `Failed`) keeps its status and looks again after `RequeueAfter` — a disabled plugin's removal in flight included, since a StatefulSet created again may still carry the plugin. Any other plugin is `Waiting` ("Waiting for StatefulSet … of … to be created"). The `ProceduresHidden` condition (id 161) is left as it is: a missing StatefulSet says nothing about the allowlist.
  - **No target at all** (`awaitTarget`): no cluster or standalone named `spec.clusterRef` in the plugin's namespace — the plugin was reconciled before its target, as one `kubectl apply` of a directory or a GitOps sync does. The plugin is phase `Pending` ("Waiting for its target: clusterRef "<ref>" not found (…)"), with one `ClusterNotFound` Warning from the status write that starts the wait (ids 136, 145), and looks again every `RequeueAfter` (nothing watches for the target); once the target is there it goes on as any plugin does — `Waiting` for its StatefulSet, then installed. The same shape as every kind with a `clusterRef`. Only a not-found counts (`errTargetNotFound`): another read error is returned and retried with backoff, and the uninstall of a deleted plugin treats only a not-found as nothing to take away. A disabled plugin whose target does not exist is `Disabled`; a refused plugin (id 170) reports its refusal whatever the StatefulSet.
- **why:** a plugin reconciled before its target went `Failed` ("Target deployment not found") with no requeue and stayed so after the target came, until its CR was edited (#573, from the code; it predates v1.22.0). The v1.22.0 re-walk of the combined build (13 PRs): during a storage expansion the server StatefulSet was orphan-deleted and created again as it was about ten seconds later. In that window each plugin read the missing StatefulSet as drift — a `PluginMissing` and a `PluginInstallFailed` Warning (3 + 3), ERROR logs "Cannot tell whether installing the plugin would change its target" and "Failed to install plugin" — sat in `Installing` for the whole hold (14 minutes), then announced `PluginInstalled` again. There was no extra roll. Reproduced in unit tests on the stack before the fix (the same Warnings and ERRORs).
- **pinned-by:** `TestReconcile_APluginWaitsForATargetNotCreatedYet` (`Pending`, one `ClusterNotFound` Warning, a requeue, no ERROR log, a quiet second pass, then the install once the cluster is there; it fails on the earlier code, which wrote `Failed` with no requeue), `TestReconcile_AMissingStatefulSetIsWaitedForQuietly` (a `Ready` plugin through a recreate: no status write, no event, no ERROR log, and nothing on the StatefulSet's return; a plugin created before its target's StatefulSet `Waiting`, then installed; an install and a disabled plugin's removal in flight keep their status), `TestReconcile_AMissingStatefulSetKeepsProceduresHidden` (`internal/controller/plugin_target_absent_test.go`). Each fails on the earlier code (`go test -overlay`).
- **enforcement:** unit test; not walked live yet.

### id 179 — A plugin's procedure lists add to a cluster's own; the servers join the two at their start
- **scope:** `buildPluginListMergeBlock`, `PluginListMergeMarker` and its place in the startup script, before the entrypoint, and `ServerProcedureList`, its mirror in Go (`internal/resources/cluster.go`); `effectiveAllowlist`, `targetSetsAllowlist`, `hiddenProceduresMessage` (`internal/controller/plugin_allowlist.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **The replacement:** the Neo4j entrypoint writes each `NEO4J_<setting>` variable into neo4j.conf **in place of** the line it finds there (`add_env_setting_to_conf`: `sed` deletes the line, the variable's is appended; only `server.jvm.additional` is appended to). A cluster's plugins set the shared procedure and extension lists (`resources.IsAdditiveConfKey`: `dbms.security.procedures.{allowlist,unrestricted,denylist}`, `dbms.security.http_auth_allowlist`, `server.unmanaged_extension_classes`) as such variables (id 173), while the cluster renders its own entries — its `spec.config` lists and the `fleetManagement.*` entry of `spec.auraFleetManagement` — into its neo4j.conf. So once any plugin set a list, the cluster's own entries were gone from its servers.
  - **Added, never in place of:** the cluster's startup script, after it has assembled `/conf/neo4j.conf` and before it runs the entrypoint, gives each list variable that is set the conf's entries too: the union, the conf's tokens first, each token once, spaces trimmed, nothing glob-expanded or matched as a pattern (`operator_merge_list`). A variable alone, or a conf line alone, is left as it is. The allowlist also gets Aura Fleet Management's entry when `NEO4J_PLUGINS` installs `fleet-management` and the list is set anywhere — the conf or the variable — and stays unset when it is set nowhere (id 192). The entrypoint then writes the union.
  - **Nothing else moves:** the cluster renders its lists into neo4j.conf as before, and each plugin writes only its own tokens (id 173). A change to the cluster's own list changes its ConfigMap, which restarts the servers once (a procedure list is not dynamic, and a set variable blocks the live apply anyway); a plugin's change is its own one write. Deleting a plugin takes its tokens off the variable and leaves the conf's. The alternatives — a plugin writing the cluster's entries into its variable, or the cluster keeping its part of the variable — need one controller to know the other's list and its history, and a change to the cluster's list then rolls the servers twice (the ConfigMap's restart, then the plugin's write).
  - **No restart on upgrade:** the block sits between the restart-neutral markers (id 119): it changes nothing for a running server, so the operator upgrade that adds it restarts no server. A server whose list it changes — a cluster with a list of its own and a plugin that sets the same list — gets the union at its next start, for whatever reason it restarts.
  - **The standalone is not affected:** its plugins put their lists into its neo4j.conf, unioned with its own `spec.config` and fleet entries (`unionPluginConfSettings`, `UpsertNeo4jConfSettings`, `DedupeNeo4jConf`), and their writes take their tokens out of a list variable an earlier operator left on its StatefulSet (`removeStandalonePluginConfEnv`). A list variable of its own `spec.env` joins its conf too (ids 193, 209); one from a Secret or ConfigMap, or patched onto the StatefulSet by hand, is someone else's and still replaces its conf's line. A standalone has no startup script. A cluster's own `spec.env` list is in its neo4j.conf as well, not on its StatefulSet, so the variable is the plugins' (id 209).
  - **What the check reads:** `effectiveAllowlist` (id 161) reads a cluster's allowlist as that union, with `ServerProcedureList`, which says what the block and the entrypoint make of the same inputs (`TestServerProcedureList_AgreesWithTheBlock` runs both).
  - **The servers' effective unrestricted list (`dbms.security.procedures.unrestricted`), in one place** (ids 192, 193):
    - *Cluster:* the union, each token once, in this order, of its neo4j.conf's line — its own `spec.config` list, its own `spec.env` list (id 209) and Aura Fleet Management's `fleetManagement.*` — the neo4j container's `NEO4J_dbms_security_procedures_unrestricted` — its plugins' tokens (automatic: GDS `gds.*,apoc.load.*`, Bloom `bloom.*`, a fleet-management plugin `fleetManagement.*`; `spec.security.allowedProcedures` outside the sandbox; a plugin's `spec.config` list) — and, **unless the cluster sets the list itself** — its `spec.config`, or the variable in its `spec.env` (`OwnsUnrestrictedList`) — the image's entry for each plugin `NEO4J_PLUGINS` installs (`apoc.*`, `gds.*`, `bloom.*`, `genai.*` — `genai.*,ai.*` from 2026.10.0 — `semantics.*` for n10s on 5.x, `fleetManagement.*` where the image installs fleet-management), read by the startup block from the image's own table. Set nowhere (no line, no variable): the image's entries alone, which the entrypoint writes itself, the last plugin's first. With `spec.config` or `spec.env` setting it: the conf's line (theirs, with fleet's entry) and the variable (the plugins' tokens), and no image entries. A `spec.env` variable from a Secret or ConfigMap stays the cluster's variable, read in the pod and joined with the conf's line, and the plugins' tokens are not there (`SpecEnvListWarnings`, id 209).
    - *Standalone:* its neo4j.conf's line — the union of its own `spec.config`, Aura Fleet Management's entry, its plugins' settings and its `spec.env` variable — with the image's entries for what its `NEO4J_PLUGINS` installs added unless the standalone sets the list itself, in its `spec.config` or its `spec.env` (`withImageUnrestrictedDefaults`). A `spec.env` variable from a Secret or ConfigMap replaces the line on the server, and counts as set by the standalone too. Set nowhere: the image's entries, by the entrypoint.
- **why:** #564 (from the code; reported with #553's fix, decided by the release owner: plugins add to the cluster's lists): a cluster's own `spec.config` allowlist or unrestricted list was lost as soon as any plugin set that list, and so was the `fleetManagement.*` entry `spec.auraFleetManagement` adds — fleet management stopped working, without a word, once any plugin restricted procedures.
- **pinned-by:** `TestPluginListMergeBlock` (the cluster's list and a plugin's, fleet's entries on both lists, a shared token kept once, a wildcard token neither expanded nor matched as a pattern, a variable alone, a conf line alone, a key that only starts with the setting's name, an underscore setting), `TestPluginListMergeBlock_InTheStartupScript` (`internal/resources/plugin_list_merge_test.go`); `TestServers_AddAPluginsAllowlistToTheClustersOwn` (the servers' allowlist, and APOC no longer hidden), `TestServers_KeepFleetManagementBesideARestrictingPlugin`, `TestServers_GetALaterChangeToTheClustersOwnList` (one restart, no plugin write), `TestUninstall_LeavesTheClustersOwnListOnTheServers`, `TestOperatorUpgrade_TheListBlockRestartsNoServer`, `TestStandalone_APluginPutsNoListVariableOnTheServer` (`internal/controller/plugin_list_union_test.go`), which start a server as its pod does: the rendered neo4j.conf, the startup script's block run in bash, then the entrypoint's replacement. The first four fail on the earlier code (`go test -overlay`); the upgrade test fails with the block outside the restart-neutral markers; the standalone test pins the code that already held.
- **enforcement:** unit test; not walked live yet.

### id 187 — An image change a Managed plugin of the deployment cannot follow is refused before the roll
- **scope:** `ManagedPluginsImageChangeErrors` (`internal/validation/plugin_image_change.go`) and its calls in `ClusterValidator.ValidateWithWarnings` (`internal/validation/cluster_validator.go`) and `StandaloneValidator.Validate` (`internal/validation/standalone_validator.go`); the refusal path of each reconciler (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`) and `kubectl neo4j validate --connect` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate.go`), which run those validators.
- **rule:**
  - **What is refused:** a `spec.image.tag` other than `status.version` (what the servers run), on a cluster or standalone with a Managed, enabled Neo4jPlugin (not being deleted) whose names the running image runs and the new tag does not — `ManagedPluginImageErrors` (id 170): a name the new image's entrypoint does not list (`n10s` on CalVer from 2025.05, `fleet-management` before 5.26.11 or 2025.08), or one whose JAR that release does not run (`n10s` from 5.26.22 and on CalVer 2025.01–2025.04). The error is on `spec.image.tag`, through the reconciler's usual refusal (phase `Failed`, "Update validation failed: …" on a cluster and "Validation failed: …" on a standalone, one `ValidationFailed` Warning, a look every `RequeueAfter`): it names the plugin, the field and why, says the servers would not start on the new image, and how to go on — change the plugin first (delete it, `spec.enabled: false`, or `installMode: VerifiedDownload` with a JAR built for the new release), and the image change goes ahead. Nothing rolls meanwhile: the servers keep the image they run.
  - **A refusal, not a warning:** a warning would let the roll go on, and every server it restarts with the name in `NEO4J_PLUGINS` does not start until the plugin's own refusal takes the name off (id 170) — an outage of each restarted server, an upgrade held on servers that cannot start (id 162), and a plugin silently gone at the end. Refused, the upgrade does not begin, and the user chooses what replaces the plugin.
  - **Only a change, so nothing is stuck:** a deployment whose running image cannot run the plugin either already carries it — the plugin is refused and taken off by its own reconcile (id 170) — and is not held to it, so no deployment becomes impossible to change because of a plugin. No `status.version` yet (never `Ready`) is not judged; neither is a disabled plugin, a `PreBaked` or `VerifiedDownload` one (the operator does not judge their JARs), a plugin of another target (a standalone that shares its name with a cluster has none: a plugin resolves its `clusterRef` to the cluster first), plugins that cannot be listed, nor anything without a client (`kubectl neo4j validate` offline).
  - **The plugin changed with the new tag set, and how it recovers:** the refusal lifts the moment the plugin stops asking for the name, but a disabled or `VerifiedDownload` plugin's own write waits for its target to be `Ready` (ids 141, 160), and a refused deployment is not; so the upgrade starts first, and the first server it restarts (the highest ordinal) comes up on the new image with the name still in `NEO4J_PLUGINS` and does not start. Nobody has to act: every server down keeps failing on `NEO4J_PLUGINS`, which the plugin's write changes, so the write goes at once although the cluster is upgrading (`selfRepair`, ids 152, 155, one `PluginSettingsFailing` Warning), on the upgrade's template and partition; the StatefulSet replaces that server from it, it starts, and the upgrade walks on (its step waits up to `upgradeTimeout`, 30 minutes by default; the plugin looks again every 30 seconds). Each server restarts once onto the new image without the name. What the user sees: the highest server crash-looping for up to about a minute, one `PluginSettingsFailing` Warning, then the upgrade going on. Enforcing the order instead — keeping the refusal until the plugin's write had landed on the old image — was rejected: a `VerifiedDownload` JAR built for the new release need not run on the old one (neosemantics 2025.06.1 is built against Neo4j 2025.06 with Java 21; the 5.26 images run Java 17 on Debian bullseye and Java 21 on trixie, `neo4j/docker-neo4j` `docker-image-src/5`), so the write would put the servers on the old image at risk; and the message no longer says to set the tag back first, for the same reason. A deleted plugin's uninstall does not wait (id 141), so deleting it with the tag changed restarts nothing that fails.
- **why:** #573 (the v1.22.0 re-walk): moving a cluster with a Managed `n10s` plugin from 5.26 to CalVer, or from 5.26.21 to 5.26.22, was accepted and rolled; each server restarted on an image that cannot run `n10s` and did not start, and the plugin was refused and taken off only after the first of them crash-looped.
- **pinned-by:** `TestImageChange_APluginChangedAfterTheRefusalLiftsRecoversByItself` (`internal/controller/plugin_image_change_order_test.go`: 3 servers on 5.26.21 with a Managed n10s, the upgrade to 2026.08.1 staged with server-2 crash-looping on n10s, the plugin switched to `VerifiedDownload` or disabled; the plugin's write lands on the upgrade's template and partition, server-2 is replaced and starts, the walk reaches `Stabilizing`, each server restarted once onto 2026.08.1 without n10s — the StatefulSet controller played by `statefulSetEmulator`; it fails with `selfRepair` switched off), `TestValidateWithWarnings_RefusesAnImageChangeAManagedPluginCannotFollow` (5.26.21 to 5.26.22, to CalVer, to the floating 5.26 tag refused, naming the plugin, what it runs and the way on; a patch it still runs on, no change, no `status.version`, a deployment already on an image it cannot run, `VerifiedDownload`, disabled, being deleted, another cluster's plugin, and no client not refused), `TestStandaloneValidate_RefusesAnImageChangeAManagedPluginCannotFollow` (and a standalone sharing a cluster's name) (`internal/validation/plugin_image_change_test.go`). Both fail on the earlier validators (`go test -overlay`).
- **enforcement:** unit test; not walked live yet.

### id 192 — Aura Fleet Management's procedures join the allowlist only when something else sets it; they are always unrestricted. From 2026.03 Fleet Manager is built in and nothing is added to NEO4J_PLUGINS
- **scope:** `internal/entrypointsim` (the image entrypoint's own functions, copied verbatim, for the tests); `FleetManagementBuiltIn`, `ManagedPluginBuiltInWarnings` (`internal/validation/plugin_entrypoint.go`); `addFleetManagementPlugin` (`internal/controller/fleet_plugin.go`); `fleetManagementBuiltInFor`, `reconcileBuiltIn` (`internal/controller/plugin_builtin.go`) and the built-in branch of `Reconcile`; the `*` of `desiredStandaloneConfigMap` and the fleet-plugin flag of `collectStandalonePluginConf` (`internal/controller/standalone_conf.go`); the Neo4jPlugin validator of `kubectl neo4j validate` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate.go`); the fleet block of the cluster's neo4j.conf, `AllowlistSetting`, `FleetManagementProcedures`, `FleetManagementPlugin`, the fleet entry of `buildPluginListMergeBlock` and `ServerProcedureList` (`internal/resources/cluster.go`); `renderStandaloneConfigMap`'s fleet block (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`), the order in `desiredStandaloneConfigMap` and the decision in `collectStandalonePluginConf` (`internal/controller/standalone_conf.go`); `pluginListSettings`, `fleetAutomaticAllowlist`, `unionPluginConf` (`internal/controller/plugin_conf_settings.go`); the cluster branch of `applyPluginToTemplate` (`internal/controller/plugin_controller.go`); `fleetOnlyAllowlist` and its removal in `applyPlugin` (`internal/controller/plugin_env_ownership.go`); `targetSetsAllowlist` (`internal/controller/plugin_allowlist.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **What Neo4j asks for:** "add the plugin … to the `dbms.security.procedures.unrestricted` setting", and "if `dbms.security.procedures.allowlist` is set in your configuration, also add the plugin to this setting, otherwise no change is needed. By default, all procedures are loaded" (Operations Manual, `configuration/plugins.adoc`, `5.x` and `dev`, read 2026-10-25). An allowlist of `fleetManagement.*` alone loads fleet management and nothing else.
  - **Cluster:** `spec.auraFleetManagement` renders `dbms.security.procedures.unrestricted=fleetManagement.*` always, and `dbms.security.procedures.allowlist=fleetManagement.*` only when the cluster's own `spec.config` sets the allowlist — the same line as before, so that conf is byte for byte what it was. When a plugin's variable sets the allowlist instead, the startup script adds the entry (id 179), keyed on `NEO4J_PLUGINS` listing `fleet-management` — Aura Fleet Management's and a fleet-management `Neo4jPlugin`'s alike.
  - **Standalone:** decided in its conf union: the fleet allowlist line, and a fleet-management plugin's automatic allowlist entry, only when its own `spec.config`, its `spec.env` (id 193) or another plugin sets the allowlist (`collectStandalonePluginConf` computes the plugins' union without the fleet entry and, if anything else is there, takes the union with it, so the entry keeps its place and order). Where an allowlist is set, the conf is byte for byte what it was.
  - **A fleet-management Neo4jPlugin on a cluster** asks for no allowlist entry (`pluginListSettings`; its unrestricted entry stays). One an earlier operator wrote — the variable holding `fleetManagement.*` alone — is taken off by the plugin's settled pass with one write ("Removing what the plugin no longer sets: NEO4J_dbms_security_procedures_allowlist …", no `PluginMissing`); beside another plugin's entries it waits for the plugin's next write, since the servers' list does not change (the block adds it back). Any other stale list still waits for a write (id 173).
  - **The image narrows it too, and the operator counters it:** the Neo4j image's entrypoint, for each name in `NEO4J_PLUGINS`, applies the plugin's `properties` from its `/startup/neo4j-plugins.json` (`install_neo4j_plugins`, `apply_plugin_default_configuration`): for `fleet-management` it adds `dbms.security.procedures.allowlist=fleetManagement.*` whenever the neo4j.conf it was given has no allowlist line — on every 5.26.12+ and 2025.08–2026.02 server, whatever the operator renders (images 5.26.32, read 2026-10-25; APOC's entry sets only the unrestricted list). Its environment pass (`add_env_setting_to_conf`) runs after and replaces that line with a `NEO4J_dbms_security_procedures_allowlist` variable. So the cluster's startup block exports the variable as `*` when `NEO4J_PLUGINS` lists `fleet-management` and nothing sets the allowlist (no conf line, no variable), and `ServerProcedureList` says the same. `*` is Neo4j's default and a valid value: "The default (`*`) loads all procedures" (`dbms.security.procedures.allowlist`, configuration-settings, `5.x` and `dev`). A conf line that sets the allowlist empty is the user's and stays. A standalone has no startup script: its neo4j.conf gets `dbms.security.procedures.allowlist=*` when Aura Fleet Management or a Managed fleet-management plugin puts the name in `NEO4J_PLUGINS`, nothing else sets the allowlist, and the image is one whose entrypoint installs the plugin (not 2026.03+); the entrypoint then leaves the setting alone.
  - **Built in from 2026.03:** "Starting with Neo4j 2026.03, Fleet Manager is built-in and enabled by default, therefore, the plugin is no longer supported" (`configuration/plugins.adoc`, `dev`); its procedures (`fleetManagement.status`, `fleetManagement.registerToken`, …) are built-in procedures (`procedures/built-in-procedures.adoc`, label new 2026.03). The images (2026.08.1, 2026.09.0) ship `lib/neo4j-fleet-management-<ver>.jar` and no JAR in `products/`; their table names `fleet-management` with no versions URL, so the entrypoint wgets "null", logs an ERROR and starts without loading it or applying its properties. So `addFleetManagementPlugin` adds nothing on such an image (`FleetManagementBuiltIn`: CalVer from 2026.03.0, a tag without a patch its line's newest patch), at the create and in every pass's merge, and a cluster renders nothing into a `NEO4J_PLUGINS` its own `spec.env` sets: a `fleet-management` the user lists there stays theirs (id 223). The merge never removes while Fleet Management is enabled: a deployment that already lists it keeps the entry (the ERROR line at each start) and restarts nothing, and the entry is recorded as Fleet Management's with a metadata patch; turning Fleet Management off takes it out, in the roll the conf change of that disable makes (id 222); Phase 2 is unchanged, since `IsFleetManagementInstalled` asks `SHOW PROCEDURES` for `fleetManagement.status` and registration calls `fleetManagement.registerToken`, both built in. The conf keeps `dbms.security.procedures.unrestricted=fleetManagement.*`: the docs ask nothing of the built-in, and the allowlist and unrestricted lists name extensions in the plugins directory ("Allow listing … from a library", `security/securing-extensions.adoc`), so the line does nothing there and a change of it would restart every such deployment.
  - **A merge that fails says which step failed.** `AuraFleetManagementPluginPatchFailed` said "Failed to add fleet-management to NEO4J_PLUGINS" for every failure of the merge — also on a built-in image, where the merge adds nothing and can only fail before the write: the StatefulSet cannot be read, its template has no `neo4j` container, its `NEO4J_PLUGINS` cannot be parsed. `mergeFleetManagementPlugin` (both reconcilers) marks the write's failure (`fleetPluginWriteError`, which unwraps, so a Conflict is still retried) and `fleetPluginPatchFailure` words the event and its log line: "Failed to add fleet-management to NEO4J_PLUGINS: …" for the write, "Could not check the server StatefulSet's NEO4J_PLUGINS for fleet-management: …" for everything before it. The merge still runs on a built-in image: built-in is judged by the template's own `neo4j` container (a held upgrade's template by the image it rolls out), so skipping the merge by the spec's tag would misjudge that case; on a healthy built-in template a pass reads the StatefulSet from the cache and writes nothing.
  - **A fleet-management Neo4jPlugin on 2026.03+ is a no-op, `Ready`:** `reconcileBuiltIn` writes nothing and says why ("Fleet Manager is built into neo4j:<tag>: the plugin installs nothing …"); the `ProceduresHidden` check does not judge it. Not refused (id 170): the entrypoint does not exit on the name, a refusal would call a working deployment `Failed`, and its repair would restart every server to take off a name the entrypoint skips. What an earlier operator wrote of it stays until the plugin is deleted.
  - **Restarts:** a deployment whose allowlist was fleet's entry alone — no allowlist of its own and none from a plugin — gets a changed neo4j.conf (or variable) and restarts once: that is the fix (fleet-only to Neo4j's default, every procedure). A cluster with Aura Fleet Management and a plugin's allowlist restarts once too (the conf loses the line), and its servers' list goes from the plugin's alone (the replacement of id 179 had dropped fleet's) to fleet's and the plugin's. Every other deployment renders the same conf and restarts nothing.
  - **Not judged:** an allowlist a user writes as `fleetManagement.*` (in `spec.config`, or a plugin's `allowedProcedures`) is theirs and stays.
  - **The unrestricted list is the other way round** (id 193): every plugin's image entry is restored there — the allowlist gets none, since only fleet-management has a default allowlist entry and Neo4j's default allowlist loads everything.
- **why:** found with #564's union (2026-10-25): enabling Aura Fleet Management set the allowlist to `fleetManagement.*`, which hid APOC and every plugin without `allowedProcedures` (`ProceduresHidden` said so); on a standalone the same, and a fleet-management `Neo4jPlugin` did it through its automatic settings. The images, read without starting a JVM (2026-10-25), showed the entrypoint does the same on its own for `fleet-management` in `NEO4J_PLUGINS` — so removing the operator's line alone changed nothing on 5.26.12+ — and that from 2026.03 the plugin is not installable at all.
- **pinned-by:** `TestServerStart_TheEntrypointsFleetDefault` (`internal/resources/entrypoint_fleet_test.go`: the block, then the entrypoint's own `install_neo4j_plugins` and environment pass from `internal/entrypointsim`, on a 5.26.32 image — fleet alone and with a Managed APOC: `*`; with a plugin's allowlist: the union; the cluster's own: kept; a fleet-management plugin's variable: `*`; an empty allowlist of the user's: kept; 2026.08 with an earlier operator's entry: `*`; APOC alone: none — and, as the premise, `fleetManagement.*` without the block; fails with the previous block), `TestFleetManagementBuiltIn` (`internal/validation/plugin_entrypoint_test.go`), `TestAddFleetManagementPlugin_NotOnAnImageWithFleetBuiltIn`, `TestFleetPluginPatchFailed_NamesWhatFailed` (`internal/controller/aura_fleet_followups_test.go`: cluster and standalone on 2026.08.1, a StatefulSet that cannot be read and one without a `neo4j` container, both worded as the check and never as an add; on 5.26.12 a refused write, worded as the add; the built-in cases fail on the earlier wording), `TestReconcile_AuraFleetManagementOnABuiltInImageAddsNoPlugin` (cluster and standalone on 2026.08.1: no `fleet-management`, later passes write nothing), `TestReconcile_AFleetManagementPluginOnABuiltInImageInstallsNothing` (`internal/controller/fleet_builtin_test.go`), `TestClusterReconcile_DisablingFleetManagementOnABuiltInImage` (`internal/controller/fleet_disable_test.go`: an entry an earlier operator wrote is recorded without a roll, and goes with the disable's; id 222), `TestValidate_AFleetManagementPluginOnABuiltInImage` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/validate_plugin_image_test.go`), `TestClusterConf_FleetJoinsTheAllowlistOnlyBesideAnother` (byte for byte and no restart with its own allowlist; no allowlist line and one restart with fleet alone or only an unrestricted list of its own — against the conf an earlier operator rendered), `TestServers_FleetAloneLeavesTheAllowlistUnset` (the servers start without an allowlist, APOC not hidden), `TestReconcile_AFleetManagementPluginTakesItsAllowlistOff` (one write for its entry alone; none beside another plugin's), `TestStandaloneConf_FleetJoinsTheAllowlistOnlyBesideAnother` (six cases, byte for byte where an allowlist is set) (`internal/controller/fleet_allowlist_test.go`); `TestServers_KeepFleetManagementBesideARestrictingPlugin` (`internal/controller/plugin_list_union_test.go`); the fleet cases of `TestPluginListMergeBlock` and `TestServerProcedureList_AgreesWithTheBlock` (`internal/resources/plugin_list_merge_test.go`). The changing cases fail on the earlier code (`go test -overlay`); the byte-for-byte cases pass on both, which is the point.
- **enforcement:** unit test; not walked live yet.

### id 193 — The image's unrestricted entry for each plugin it installs stays on the servers, unless the deployment sets the list itself (spec.config or spec.env); a deployment's spec.env list joins its conf
- **scope:** `imageUnrestrictedDefaultLists`, `ImageUnrestrictedDefaults` (`internal/validation/plugin_entrypoint.go`); `operator_plugin_defaults` and the `plugin-defaults` argument of `operator_merge_list` (`pluginDefaultsArg`) in `buildPluginListMergeBlock`, the defaults of `ServerProcedureList`, `OwnsUnrestrictedList`, `UnrestrictedSetting`, `AdditiveConfKeyForEnvName` (`internal/resources/cluster.go`); `desiredStandaloneConfigMap`, `collectStandalonePluginConf`, `standaloneNeo4jPlugins`, `withImageUnrestrictedDefaults` (`internal/controller/standalone_conf.go`); `SpecEnvListKey`, `SpecEnvLists` (`internal/resources/cluster.go`, shared with the cluster, id 209); the `spec.env` loop of `buildEnvVars` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`); `internal/entrypointsim` (`Releases`, `PluginTable`, the vendored tables of 5.26.0, 5.26.32, 2026.09.0 and 2026.10.0, and the docker-defaults pass).
- **rule:**
  - **What the image does:** for each name in `NEO4J_PLUGINS` it installs — its JAR in the image (`location`) or downloaded (a `versions` URL) — the entrypoint applies the plugin's `properties` from `/startup/neo4j-plugins.json` (`apply_plugin_default_configuration`), but skips each setting the neo4j.conf it was given already has ("Skipping … because it is already set"); its environment pass, after, writes a `NEO4J_<setting>` variable in place of the conf's line. Every plugin's entry sets `dbms.security.procedures.unrestricted`: `apoc` and `apoc-extended` `apoc.*`, `graph-data-science` `gds.*`, `bloom` `bloom.*`, `genai` `genai.*` (`genai.*,ai.*` from 2026.10.0, docker-neo4j #571), `n10s` `semantics.*` (5.x; the image's, faithfully), `fleet-management` `fleetManagement.*` (from 5.26.11 and 2025.08; from 2026.03 the entrypoint cannot install it and applies nothing). Read from the images 5.26.0, 5.26.32 and 2026.09.0 (5.26.0 has jq 1.6, the table and the same entries) and from docker-neo4j at every release tag, 2026-10-25. So once anything else set the list — Aura Fleet Management's conf line, a plugin's list (GDS's `gds.*,apoc.load.*`, Bloom's `bloom.*`, any `spec.security.allowedProcedures` outside the sandbox), a variable of the user's — every other plugin's entry was gone: APOC beside fleet or GDS lost `apoc.*`, GenAI under `spec.security` lost `genai.*`.
  - **Added back, unless the deployment sets the list itself:** the operator adds the image's entry for each installed plugin to an unrestricted list that is set anywhere — except a list the deployment sets itself (`OwnsUnrestrictedList`): the key in its `spec.config`, or `NEO4J_dbms_security_procedures_unrestricted` in its `spec.env` (a value of its own, or one from a Secret or ConfigMap; an empty one, which the entrypoint does not write, does not count). That is Neo4j's own rule, explicit wins: the entrypoint skips its plugin defaults for a conf that sets the list, and writes such a variable in place of the whole line, the defaults included. Such a list is the user's boundary and gets no image entries; Aura Fleet Management's line and the plugins' lists still join it (id 179). Lines the operator renders (Aura Fleet Management's) and the plugins' lists (a cluster's variables, a standalone's conf) are not the deployment's own and do not count. A list set nowhere is left to the entrypoint, which writes the entries itself. Only the unrestricted list: the allowlist keeps id 192. The servers' effective list is spelled out in id 179.
  - **Cluster — from the image:** the startup block's `operator_plugin_defaults` reads the entries from the image's own `/startup/neo4j-plugins.json` with jq, as the entrypoint does, for the `NEO4J_PLUGINS` names whose JAR is in the image or that have a versions URL, in that order, and `operator_merge_list` adds them to id 179's union when the conf or the variable sets the list. Its argument is rendered only when the cluster does not set the list itself (`OwnsUnrestrictedList` over its `spec.config` and `spec.env`). Read at run time rather than from an embedded table, so a server gets what its own release's table says, a CalVer newer than the operator included; the embedded table would also have to be rendered per tag, and be wrong for an image newer than the operator. Run in the images without a JVM (`docker run --rm --entrypoint bash`, 2026-10-25): 5.26.0, 5.26.32 and 2026.09.0 give exactly what the Go table gives (fleet-management only on 5.26.32; `not-json` gives nothing). `ServerProcedureList` takes the entries as an argument (`ImageUnrestrictedDefaults`), including the entrypoint's own order for a list set nowhere (each next plugin's entry in front).
  - **Standalone — from the table:** it has no startup script; its neo4j.conf, which the operator writes, gets the entries from `ImageUnrestrictedDefaults` (per release; pinned against the vendored tables) for `standaloneNeo4jPlugins` — its Managed plugins and their dependencies, fleet-management where the image installs it, its `spec.env`'s `NEO4J_PLUGINS` — when the conf sets the list and the standalone does not set it itself (its `spec.config`, its `spec.env`). A tag that names no version gets none; a `NEO4J_PLUGINS` entry patched onto the StatefulSet by hand is not seen.
  - **A standalone's `spec.env` list joins its conf:** a `NEO4J_<setting>` variable of a shared list (`IsAdditiveConfEnvName`: the procedure allowlist, unrestricted list and denylist, `http_auth_allowlist`, `unmanaged_extension_classes`) in its `spec.env`, not read from a Secret or ConfigMap, is unioned into its neo4j.conf and left off its StatefulSet (`SpecEnvListKey`, `SpecEnvLists`; the owned-env record takes the variable off) — the entrypoint wrote it in place of the conf's line, dropping its `spec.config` and its plugins' entries. A cluster's goes the same way (id 209). An allowlist set this way also takes Aura Fleet Management's entry (id 192), which the variable used to drop. An unrestricted list set this way is the standalone's own: it joins its plugins' lists, and gets no image entries. A variable from a Secret or ConfigMap cannot be read and stays a variable that replaces the line (a `ValidationWarning` says so, id 209); an empty one adds nothing and is left off as well.
  - **Restarts:** a cluster restarts nothing on the operator upgrade (the block is restart-neutral, id 119); each server gets the new list at its next start. Its servers' list gains entries where `NEO4J_PLUGINS` installs a plugin whose entry the set list lacks: Aura Fleet Management (5.26.11+, 2025.08–2026.02) with APOC, GDS, GenAI or Bloom; GDS or Bloom with APOC; a plugin with `spec.security` outside the sandbox beside any other, or its own (GenAI's `genai.*`, and `ai.*` from 2026.10.0); a `spec.env` list with plugins. A standalone restarts once where its conf's list changes — the same shapes, Aura Fleet Management on any release with a Managed plugin (its conf always has fleet's line) — and once where its `spec.env` sets a list (the conf and the env change in the same write). Nothing changes for a deployment that sets the list itself (`spec.config`, or a `spec.env` variable — though a standalone's plain variable moving into its conf is the one restart above), one without plugins, one whose list is set nowhere (APOC alone, GenAI without `spec.security`), or one whose only plugin's list already holds its entry (GDS, Bloom or fleet alone).
  - **Bloom's other image defaults** suffer the same skip-and-replace and are left so: `server.unmanaged_extension_classes=com.neo4j.bloom.server=/browser/bloom` — a Bloom Neo4jPlugin sets the class itself, at `/bloom`, with `/bloom.*` in `http_auth_allowlist` (Bloom's documented mount), and adding the image's mount beside it would serve Bloom twice; `dbms.bloom.license_file=/licenses/bloom.license` — the operator never sets it, so the image's default stands unless the user sets it, and then it is theirs.
- **why:** the v1.22.0 re-walk's follow-ups (round 8, decided by the release owner, 2026-10-25): with Aura Fleet Management or GDS, APOC's procedures that need unrestricted access did not load; with `spec.security`, GenAI's did not. A standalone's hand-set list variable still replaced its conf's line while a cluster's joined.
