- **pinned-by:** `TestServerStart_KeepsThePluginsUnrestrictedDefaults` (`internal/resources/plugin_defaults_test.go`: fleet and APOC, GDS and APOC, GenAI with `spec.security` on 2026.10.0 and 2026.09.0, Bloom and APOC, the cluster's `spec.config` list alone and with a plugin's, its `spec.env` variable with fleet's line and with a plugin's tokens, set nowhere, 2026.09 with fleet-management left in `NEO4J_PLUGINS` — each through the block and the entrypoint's own functions, and, as the premise, what the image alone starts with), `TestServerProcedureList_AgreesWithTheBlock` (both lists on the four vendored releases), `TestPluginListMergeBlock_InTheStartupScript` (the argument only where neither `spec.config` nor `spec.env` sets the list: a value of its own or from a Secret counts, an empty one does not) (`internal/resources/plugin_list_merge_test.go`), `TestServerStart_TheEntrypointsFleetDefault` (`internal/resources/entrypoint_fleet_test.go`); `TestImageUnrestrictedDefaults_MatchTheImages`, `TestImageUnrestrictedDefaults` (`internal/validation/plugin_defaults_test.go`); `TestServers_KeepThePluginsUnrestrictedDefaults` (GDS and APOC installed; `spec.config`'s list untouched), `TestStandaloneConf_KeepsThePluginsUnrestrictedDefaults` (seven shapes, the names `NEO4J_PLUGINS` installs, the server's list and whether the conf changes), `TestStandaloneConf_ItsOwnSpecEnvListGetsNoImageEntries` (a value of its own and one from a Secret), `TestStandalone_ASpecEnvListJoinsTheConf`, `TestStandalone_ASpecEnvAllowlistKeepsFleetManagement` (`internal/controller/plugin_defaults_test.go`); `TestServers_FleetAloneLeavesTheAllowlistUnset` (`internal/controller/fleet_allowlist_test.go`), `TestServers_AddAPluginsAllowlistToTheClustersOwn` (`internal/controller/plugin_list_union_test.go`). Each fails under `go test -overlay` with its part switched off: the block's argument, the standalone's entries, the `spec.env` fold, the table (a wrong 2026.10.0 entry), and `OwnsUnrestrictedList` reading `spec.config` alone (the `spec.env` cases).
- **enforcement:** unit test; the block's function run in the images (no JVM); not walked live yet.

### id 194 — A plugin's ProceduresHidden is judged against the conf its target's controller renders for its spec now, not the ConfigMap as it is
- **scope:** `effectiveAllowlist`, `desiredConf`, `folded` (`internal/controller/plugin_allowlist.go`); `desiredStandaloneConfigMap`, `mergeStandaloneConf` (`internal/controller/standalone_conf.go`), shared with `reconcileConfigMap` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`); `BuildConfigMapForEnterprise` (`internal/resources/cluster.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **What the servers will start with:** the allowlist of id 161 is read from the neo4j.conf the target's controller writes for its spec now — a cluster's as `BuildConfigMapForEnterprise` renders it, a standalone's as `desiredStandaloneConfigMap` renders it with the foreign keys of its live ConfigMap merged back the way its controller merges them (`mergeStandaloneConf`, the same function) — and from the live StatefulSet's variables, which the plugin controller writes itself for the plugins (id 173). A standalone's list variable from its own `spec.env` is not read: its conf holds it (id 193), and the standalone's next write takes the variable off.
  - **Not "skip while it changes":** the other choice, judging nothing while the ConfigMap differs from what the controller renders or a roll is pending, needs a signal for "mid-change", keeps a stale condition through a long roll or a paced upgrade, and then judges the same ConfigMap. Judging the rendered conf is a function of the specs: the answer the servers will have, at once, with no flap. In the seconds before the controller's write, and during the roll, the condition describes the servers' next start rather than the running ones; a list the user has just set that hides a plugin is announced at once.
- **why:** the post-v1.22.0 re-walk (round 7, operator upgrade to the combined build): the ConfigMap an earlier operator wrote still held Aura Fleet Management's `dbms.security.procedures.allowlist=fleetManagement.*` until the target's controller wrote the new one (id 192); the plugin controller's pass in between judged it and fired one transient `PluginProceduresHidden` Warning for APOC, cleared by the next pass.
- **pinned-by:** `TestProceduresHidden_IsJudgedAgainstWhatTheServersWillRun` (`internal/controller/plugin_defaults_test.go`: a cluster's and a standalone's ConfigMap from an earlier operator, no condition and no Warning; a cluster's new list its ConfigMap does not have yet, and a standalone's `spec.env` list whose StatefulSet still has the old value, one Warning each); the existing `TestReconcile_AStandalonePluginHiddenByTheConfAllowlistSaysSo` (a foreign key of the live ConfigMap is still read) (`internal/controller/plugin_allowlist_test.go`). It fails with the live ConfigMap read instead (`go test -overlay`).
- **enforcement:** unit test; not walked live yet.

### id 209 — A deployment's own spec.env list is in its neo4j.conf, joined with its plugins' lists; on a cluster the variable is the plugins', handed over in the write that moves it
- **scope:** `SpecEnvListKey`, `SpecEnvLists`, their place in `buildNeo4jConfigForEnterprise` (with the Aura Fleet Management allowlist line) and the `spec.env` loop of `BuildPodSpecForEnterprise` (`internal/resources/cluster.go`); `handOverSharedLists`, `writtenPluginLists` (`internal/controller/cluster_env_lists.go`) and their call in the template apply of `createOrUpdateResourceInternal` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`); `desiredStandaloneConfigMap` (`internal/controller/standalone_conf.go`) and the `spec.env` loop of `buildEnvVars` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`); `folded` (`internal/controller/plugin_allowlist.go`); `SpecEnvListWarnings` and its calls in both `NoEffectWarnings` (`internal/validation/env_list_warnings.go`, `internal/validation/noeffect_warnings.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **The defect:** a cluster's `spec.env` `NEO4J_<setting>` variable of a shared list (`IsAdditiveConfEnvName`: the procedure allowlist, unrestricted list and denylist, `http_auth_allowlist`, `unmanaged_extension_classes`) went onto the StatefulSet as the cluster's own variable (`neo4j.com/cluster-controller-env-vars`). The plugins leave a variable the target sets alone (`setClusterPluginLists`), and the cluster put its value back over theirs, so the plugins' entries were not on the servers — the startup block joined only the conf's and that variable (id 179). The same list in `spec.config` was joined.
  - **In the conf, as on a standalone:** a shared-list variable of the deployment's `spec.env` that is not read from a Secret or ConfigMap is written into its neo4j.conf, unioned with its `spec.config` list (`SpecEnvLists`; an empty one adds nothing), and left off its StatefulSet — on a cluster too now (`SpecEnvListKey` in `BuildPodSpecForEnterprise`). The cluster's variable is then the plugins', and the servers join the conf's line with it (id 179): the `spec.env` list and the `spec.config` list end the same. It stays the deployment's own for id 193: no image entries are added to an unrestricted list it sets. An allowlist set this way gets Aura Fleet Management's line, as one `spec.config` sets (id 192).
  - **Handed over in the same write:** the cluster write that moves such a variable into the conf stops owning it on the StatefulSet. Dropping it there would leave the plugins' entries off the servers until each plugin noticed and wrote them back — a second roll. So the template apply (`handOverSharedLists`) gives the variable the value the plugins that have written to the StatefulSet ask for in it (`writtenPluginLists`: enabled, not refused by the image, not being deleted, with a record of their own on the StatefulSet; `pluginListSettings`), as their own writes would make it, or drops it when they ask for nothing. A plugin that has not written yet puts its tokens there with its own write (id 173). The plugins' settled passes find their entries and write nothing. When the plugins cannot be listed the variable is dropped, as before, and each plugin writes its own back.
  - **From a Secret or ConfigMap:** such a variable cannot be read, so it stays a variable — the cluster's, which the plugins cannot add to; on a standalone Neo4j's entrypoint writes it in place of the whole list. Each validator says so as a `ValidationWarning` (`SpecEnvListWarnings`), naming the variable and what is lost; it is the user's to keep. `spec.envFrom` is opaque to the operator: a list a Secret supplies that way replaces the line, unseen.
  - **Restarts:** a cluster whose `spec.env` sets such a variable rolls once on the operator upgrade: its neo4j.conf gains the list (a restart-relevant change) and the same pass's template write takes the variable off, or hands it over. A standalone's plain variables already moved in id 193; an empty one is now left off its StatefulSet too (one restart where it was there). Nothing else changes.
- **why:** the v1.23.0 pre-release journey on main (dd429120, 2026-10-26): cluster `fm526` on 5.26.32, upgraded from v1.22.0, with Aura Fleet Management and Managed APOC and GDS. With `dbms.security.procedures.unrestricted: custom.*` in `spec.config` every server listed `custom.*,fleetManagement.*,gds.*,apoc.load.*`; moved to `spec.env`, every server listed `fleetManagement.*,custom.*` alone, the StatefulSet's variable was the cluster's `custom.*` (listed in `neo4j.com/cluster-controller-env-vars`), each server logged 75 "… is unavailable because it is sandboxed" (`gds.util.*`, `apoc.trigger.*`), and GDS stayed `Ready`.
- **pinned-by:** `TestBuildClusterSpecEnvLists` (the conf holds the plain lists with `spec.config`'s and fleet's; the StatefulSet carries none of them, the one from a Secret stays, an empty one adds nothing), `TestServerStart_AClustersSpecEnvListJoinsAPluginsList` (`internal/resources/cluster_env_lists_test.go`: the conf, the block and the entrypoint, `spec.config` and `spec.env` alike), `TestServers_AClustersSpecEnvListJoinsThePlugins` (the cluster controller's own apply beside APOC and GDS installed; both variants end with `custom.*`, `fleetManagement.*`, `gds.*`, `apoc.load.*`; no `PluginMissing`), `TestClusterApply_HandsAListItMovesToTheConfToThePlugins` (the StatefulSet an earlier operator left: one write, the variable GDS's, the plugins write nothing), `TestClusterApply_AListFromASecretStaysTheClusters`, `TestWrittenPluginLists` (`internal/controller/cluster_env_lists_test.go`), `TestStandalone_ASpecEnvListJoinsTheConf` (an empty list left off), `TestStandalone_ASpecEnvAllowlistKeepsFleetManagement` (the order `fleetManagement.*,apoc.*`) (`internal/controller/plugin_defaults_test.go`), `TestSpecEnvListWarnings` (`internal/validation/env_list_warnings_test.go`). Each fails under `go test -overlay` with its part switched off: the fold, the hand-over (two writes, the variable dropped), the warning.
- **enforcement:** unit test; not walked live yet.

### id 184 — Every Aura call spends a budget the credential's CRs share: the provider config revalidates every 10 minutes, and a failure that repeats is the same status
- **scope:** `auraProviderConfigRecheck` and `recheckInterval` (`internal/controller/auraproviderconfig_controller.go`); `newAuraProviderConfigReconciler` (`cmd/main.go`), which both registries build the reconciler with; `MessageWithoutRequestID` (`internal/aura/errors.go`) and every status message built from an Aura error: the `fail()` of `aurainstance_controller.go`, `auracustomermanagedkey_controller.go`, `auraipfilter_controller.go`, `auradatabase_controller.go`, `auradatabasebackup_controller.go`, `auradatabaserestore_controller.go`, `aurainvite_controller.go`, `auraorganizationmember_controller.go`, `auraprojectmember_controller.go` and `aurasnapshot_controller.go`, the `CredentialsInvalid` condition of `auraproviderconfig_controller.go`, and the `note` and `telemetryError` messages of `reconcileAuraFleetProvision` and `syncObserved` (`internal/controller/aura_fleet_provision.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **The revalidation interval is the controller's.** An `AuraProviderConfig` validates its credentials — a token exchange and a `ListInstances` call — every `auraProviderConfigRecheck` (10 minutes). The operator sets no `RequeueAfter` on it; `RequeueAfter` stays an override for tests. Nothing recorded a reason for the 30s it was wired with.
  - **A status message leaves Aura's request ID out.** Aura gives every response its own `X-Request-Id`, which `APIError.Error()` renders (` request-id=<id>`). A status message built from that text changes on every retry, so a refusal that repeats was a status write on every pass — a watch event that starts the next pass at once (ids 122, 156) — and, since the Warning is announced when the message changes (id 145), a Warning on every pass: a loop at the speed of the rate limit, and for a cluster, through `status.auraFleetManagement`, a cluster pass each time. Every such message goes through `MessageWithoutRequestID`. The Warning and the log keep the full error, so the request ID of the first failure is in the event, for Aura support.
- **why:** #566 (2026-10-25, from the code): `cmd/main.go` wired `GetTestRequeueAfter()` (30s) into the provider config as into every controller, overriding its 10 minutes — two Aura calls a minute per config, against a trial credential's 25 a minute that every `AuraInstance`, key, filter, database, member and invite on the credential shares. The request-ID loop was found while making `AuraSnapshot`'s failure announce once: no Aura failure carrying a request ID could be.
- **pinned-by:** `TestAuraProviderConfig_IsWiredWithItsOwnRecheckInterval` (`cmd/aura_provider_config_wiring_test.go`: both registries leave `RequeueAfter` unset; fails with the 30s wiring), `TestAuraProviderConfig_RevalidatesEveryTenMinutesByDefault`, `TestAuraFail_ARefusalThatRepeatsIsOneWriteAndOneWarning` (`internal/controller/aura_fleet_followups_test.go`: an `AuraInstance`, an `AuraProviderConfig` and fleet provisioning refused three times, each time with a new request ID — the status unchanged after the first, one Warning that keeps the first ID; fails on the earlier controllers), `TestMessageWithoutRequestID` (`internal/aura/errors_request_id_test.go`).
- **enforcement:** unit test. No machine check that a NEW status message built from an Aura error goes through `MessageWithoutRequestID` — convention + code review.

### id 191 — Aura Fleet Management needs an image whose entrypoint installs fleet-management; it is refused on an earlier one
- **scope:** `validateAuraFleetManagement`, `fleetManagementImageErrors`, `firstReleasesInstalling` (`internal/validation/fleet_validator.go`), called with `spec.image.tag` by `validateCluster` (`internal/validation/cluster_validator.go`) and `ValidateCreate` (`internal/validation/standalone_validator.go`), so by every reconcile pass and by `kubectl neo4j validate`; `entrypointPluginLists`, `entrypointPluginNames` (`internal/validation/plugin_entrypoint.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **Refused where the entrypoint lacks it:** `spec.auraFleetManagement.enabled: true` adds `fleet-management` to `NEO4J_PLUGINS` (id 175), and the entrypoints of 5.26.0–5.26.10 and 2025.01–2025.07 do not list it (id 170's table). On such a `spec.image.tag` the validator refuses the spec with `spec.auraFleetManagement.enabled: Invalid value: true: Aura Fleet Management needs 5.26.11 or a later 5.26, or 2025.08.0 or later: …`; the first releases are read from the table, not written twice. A floating tag is its line's newest patch, as for a plugin; a tag that names no supported version is left to the image rule.
  - **An existing deployment that already has it** is refused from the first pass of the upgraded operator: phase `Failed`, one `ValidationFailed`. The refusal returns before anything is written, so its StatefulSet stays as it was — the servers that restarted onto `fleet-management` keep failing to start, the others keep running, as before the upgrade — and the operator adds nothing more. Moving `spec.image.tag` to a release that installs the plugin lets the next pass roll the servers onto it. Removing `spec.auraFleetManagement` lifts the refusal but does not take `fleet-management` out of `NEO4J_PLUGINS` (the fleet merge only adds; ids 155, 175); that is a hand edit of the StatefulSet. Neither is worse than before: every such deployment was already failing on the name.
  - **Judged by the tag only**, as a plugin is: a custom image under a supported tag whose `/startup/neo4j-plugins.json` was edited to list `fleet-management` is refused too.
- **why:** #573 (the live walks of the post-v1.22.0 stack, 2026-10-24/25): `fleet_validator.go` had no version check, and #563's table shows `fleet-management` joined the entrypoint's list only at 5.26.11 and 2025.08.0 (`docker-neo4j` dddc2f6, 2025-08-07). The user guide said every supported image bundles it.
- **pinned-by:** `TestFleetManagement_IsRefusedOnAnImageWhoseEntrypointLacksIt` (`internal/validation/fleet_image_test.go`: through the cluster and standalone validators, five tags refused with the field and the first releases named, the same tags accepted without fleet management, six tags accepted with it; fails with the check removed).
- **enforcement:** unit test + the validator.

### id 178 — A deletion that cannot remove what it guards waits, bounded, then names what it left; only a target that took it with its volumes releases at once
- **scope:** `deletionCleanup` (`blocked`, `failed`, `retryTransient`, `settleByTarget`), `fateOfTarget`, `fateOfPresent`, `volumesKeptCause`, `targetDownCause`, `connectFailedCause`, `statementFailedCause`, `deletionDeadline` (`internal/controller/deletion_wait.go`); `finalizerDeletionGracePeriod`, `deletionGracePeriodExceeded` and `classifyFinalizerCleanup` (`internal/controller/finalizer_deletion.go`); `IsHostUnresolvableError` (`internal/neo4j/error_classify.go`); `handleDeletion` of `internal/controller/neo4juser_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jrole_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jrolebinding_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jauthrule_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jcompositedatabase_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jdatabasealias_controller.go` (its `newDeletionClient` seam, `connectForDeletion`) and `internal/controller/neo4jreplicadatabase_controller.go` (`replicaNeo4jClient.DropDatabase`, through `neo4jClientFor`); the client branch of `handleDeletion` in `internal/controller/neo4jdatabase_controller.go`; `handleDeletion`, `deletionTarget` and the order of `Reconcile` in `internal/controller/aurainvite_controller.go`, `internal/controller/auraorganizationmember_controller.go` and `internal/controller/auraprojectmember_controller.go`; `auraDeletionDeadline` (`internal/controller/aura_common.go`); `EventReasonAuraMemberOrphaned` (`internal/controller/events.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **Wait while the cleanup cannot run.** The deletion of a `Neo4jUser`, `Neo4jRole`, `Neo4jRoleBinding`, `Neo4jAuthRule`, `Neo4jCompositeDatabase`, `Neo4jDatabaseAlias` or `Neo4jReplicaDatabase` whose `DROP` / `REVOKE` cannot run — the target CR exists but is not Ready (neither `Ready` nor `Degraded`, id 105), the connection fails (refused, a timeout, a name that does not resolve, an admin Secret that cannot be read), or Neo4j refuses the statement — keeps its finalizer and returns `RequeueAfter` with a nil error (logged at info, id 164). The wait is written to status — the kind's failure phase, its deletion reason, the message `deletion waits to remove <what> on clusterRef "<name>": <cause>; if that still holds at <time>, the finalizer is released and it is left in Neo4j` — and announced with one Warning from the write that changed it (id 145). Not Ready and unreachable share one cause, `… is not Ready or does not accept connections yet`, so a restart that is first one, then the other, is one event. A `Neo4jDatabase` deletion whose client cannot be created waits the same way (reason `DeletionFailed`); its retried `DROP DATABASE` keeps `classifyFinalizerCleanup`, which releases at once only on a database Neo4j reports gone, and otherwise retries until the grace period ends — a name that does not resolve included.
  - **Bounded.** Once `finalizerDeletionGracePeriod` (5 minutes) has passed since `deletionTimestamp`, the finalizer is released with one Warning: `Released the finalizer without removing <what>: <cause>, 5m0s after the deletion was requested. It is still in Neo4j; remove it by hand: <statement>` — ``DROP USER `alice` ``, ``DROP ROLE `analyst` ``, ``REVOKE ROLE `reader` FROM `alice` `` (the roles still granted, from the one whose revoke failed), ``DROP AUTH RULE `rule` ``, ``DROP COMPOSITE DATABASE `sales` CASCADE ALIASES``, ``DROP ALIAS `latest` FOR DATABASE``, ``DROP DATABASE `x` ``. A CR that cannot be deleted is worse than an object left behind and named (id 157).
  - **The target's volumes decide when it is being deleted or gone** (`fateOfTarget`, `settleByTarget`). What the deletion removes lives in the target's `system` database, on its volumes, and `spec.storage.retentionPolicy` says whether they outlive it:
    - **Being deleted with its volumes** (`Delete`, the default, or unset): released at once, no statement, no Warning — the volumes go with the CR (`cleanupPVCs`, the standalone's `cleanupResources`), and the user, grant or rule with them. A namespace teardown no longer waits, one Warning per dependent, until the target CR is gone.
    - **Being deleted, its volumes kept** (`Retain`): the target counts as present. It may still serve — its pods run until its own finalizer completes — so the cleanup runs, or waits as for any target. Releasing at once here skipped a DROP that could succeed, and the volumes keep what it would have removed.
    - **Gone, with volumes of it left** (PersistentVolumeClaims matching `PVCSelectorByInstance(clusterRef)` that are not being deleted): the deletion waits, bounded, for a deployment created again from them — the cluster or standalone's creation enqueues its dependents (`EnqueueDependentsForClusterChange`), so the drop runs once it is Ready — with the cause `clusterRef "<name>" was deleted and its volumes were kept (storage.retentionPolicy: Retain); a deployment created again from them still has it`, and after the grace period releases with the Warning naming what they hold and the statement that removes it. Released at once before, so a cluster deleted with its volumes kept and created again from them brought back every user, grant, rule and database its dependents' deletions were to remove, with no CR and nothing said.
    - **Gone with its volumes** (none left, or every one being deleted, as in a namespace teardown): released at once.
    Whether the deployment is gone is read from its CR and its volumes, never from DNS. Released at once, too: `deletionPolicy: Retain`, a built-in role, a binding that granted nothing (`status.grantedRoles` empty), an auth rule whose target cannot hold one, a promoted replica, a replica the type read reports gone. A connection error is never read as "already gone": an unreadable admin Secret says `secrets "…" not found`, which `IsNotFoundError` matches.
  - **"No such host" is the target restarting, not a target that is gone.** Every deletion client — `ResolvedTarget.NewClient` (user, role, binding, auth rule, composite, alias, replica) and the database's `createNeo4jClient` / `createNeo4jClientForStandalone` — bootstraps on the `{name}-client.{namespace}.svc.cluster.local:7687` ClusterIP Service (`NewClientForEnterprise`, `NewClientForEnterpriseStandalone` in `internal/neo4j/client.go`), which resolves while the Service exists. Under the routing scheme (`neo4j://`) the driver then sends the statement to the server the routing table names. On a cluster that is each server's advertised address, its pod FQDN under the headless Service (`server.default_advertised_address=${HOSTNAME_FQDN}`, `{name}-server-N.{name}-headless.{namespace}.svc.cluster.local`, `internal/resources/cluster.go`): `publishNotReadyAddresses` publishes a pod's record before it is ready, but not before the pod exists, so between a pod's deletion and its replacement the name returns NXDOMAIN while the cluster CR exists. On a standalone the operator sets no advertised address (the Docker entrypoint sets none for Bolt either), so what the routing table names is Neo4j's choice — not walked live. Either way `IsHostUnresolvableError` is `targetUnreachable` for `deletionCleanup` and a retry for `classifyFinalizerCleanup`; it used to release the `Neo4jDatabase` finalizer at once.
  - **A transient is not a wait.** A cleanup statement, or its connection, that Neo4j refuses with an error it asks to be retried (`statementTransient`, id 164) — a deadlock between the DROPs of a bulk delete, a leader switch — is retried after `transientRetry` (5s), logged at info, with nothing on status and no event (`deletionCleanup.failed`, `retryTransient`; the `Neo4jDatabase` DROP checks it before `classifyFinalizerCleanup`). It is the wait's release Warning, naming it, only if it lasts past the grace period. In the round-8 walk a bulk delete's `DROP ROLE` hit `Neo.TransientError.Transaction.DeadlockDetected` and raised a `RoleDeletionFailed` Warning before the retry succeeded.
  - **A replica's type is read before the drop;** a read that fails is a wait, not a "no": nothing is dropped and nothing released.
  - **Aura invites and members** follow id 157: a terminating `AuraInvite`, `AuraOrganizationMember` or `AuraProjectMember` is handled before the credentials are resolved; `Orphan` (or no external ID / user ID) needs none and releases at once, with an `AuraInviteOrphaned` / `AuraMemberOrphaned` Normal event when something is left. A Delete that cannot resolve the credentials, the organization (or, for a project member, the project) records `CredentialsUnavailable` (phase `Error`, announced once) and waits; after the grace period it releases with a Warning `AuraInviteOrphaned` ("it stays pending in Aura and <email> can still accept it") or `AuraMemberOrphaned` ("they keep the role <role> in the organization/project").
  - **Deliberately not changed:** `Neo4jBackup`, `Neo4jRestore` and `Neo4jPlugin` deletions run nothing in Neo4j that leaves access behind (a backup's artifacts and Jobs, a restore's Jobs, a plugin's uninstall is a StatefulSet write, id 155); an `AuraDatabase` whose instance or credentials could not be resolved, and an Aura DELETE the API refuses (other than conflict, transient, not found), were listed here as exceptions; both are bounded now (ids 202, 201).
- **why:** a security review before v1.22.x (2026-10-25, from the code, not walked live): deleting a `Neo4jUser`, `Neo4jRole`, `Neo4jRoleBinding` or `Neo4jAuthRule` while its target restarted, or read Ready before its Bolt port accepted connections (every standalone restart, a `stopCluster` restore, id 172), released the finalizer at once ("cannot connect during … deletion; releasing finalizer", logged at ERROR): the CR was gone and the user, the grant or the rule stayed in Neo4j — access the user had asked to revoke, with nothing on the cluster to say so. A `Neo4jAuthRule` released on a target that was merely not Ready. A `Neo4jDatabase` released on any client error ("assume the database is already gone"), leaving the data. An alias and a replica released when the connection or the drop failed, and waited for ever on a target that was not Ready; a replica whose type could not be read released without dropping. An `AuraInvite`, `AuraOrganizationMember` or `AuraProjectMember` whose credentials could not be resolved released at once and silently: the invite could still be accepted, the member kept their role (#557 had fixed the same shape for the instance, key and filter).
- **pinned-by:** `TestDeletion_DuringARestartWaitsThenNamesWhatIsLeft` (`internal/controller/deletion_during_restart_test.go`: per kind, a target that is not Ready, one that refuses Bolt, and credentials that cannot be read each keep the finalizer, send nothing, and announce one Warning naming the cause and what is left, with the status message; after the grace period, released with the Warning and the statement; a Ready target drops and releases at once; a target CR that is gone releases at once); `TestDeletion_AliasAndReplicaWaitForAStatementNeo4jRefuses` (same file: a refused alias drop, replica drop and replica type read wait); `TestDatabaseDeletion_ANameThatDoesNotResolveDuringARestartWaits` (a `DROP DATABASE` that meets a pod name that does not resolve waits while the cluster CR exists, releases after the grace period naming the database, and releases at once once the CR is gone), `TestDeletion_ANameThatDoesNotResolveIsTheTargetRestarting`, `TestDeletionCauses_ANameThatDoesNotResolveIsTheTargetDown` (`internal/controller/deletion_dns_test.go`) and `TestClassifyFinalizerCleanup` (`internal/controller/finalizer_deletion_test.go`), the first and last failing with the earlier release on "no such host"; `TestAuraDeletion_DeleteWithoutCredentialsWaitsThenOrphans`, `TestAuraDeletion_OrphanNeedsNoCredentials`, `TestAuraDeletion_DeleteWithCredentialsRemovesAtOnce` (`internal/controller/aura_deletion_credentials_test.go`, with the invite and member cases); `TestAuthDeletion_ARetriedDropIsAnnouncedOnce`, `TestCompositeDeletion_ARefusedDropReleasesTheFinalizerAfterTheGracePeriod` (`internal/controller/auth_deletion_retry_test.go`). With each kind's earlier deletion branches put back by `go test -overlay`, its subtests fail: the not-Ready wait (no Warning, or released), the connection waits and the after-grace Warning (released at once, nothing said), the alias and replica statement waits, the Aura credentials wait (released at once). The target's fate: `TestDeletion_ATargetBeingDeletedOrGone` (`internal/controller/deletion_target_fate_test.go`: for each of the eight kinds, a target being deleted with its volumes, Ready or not, releases at once and sends nothing; one being deleted that keeps them takes the drop when Ready and waits when not; a target gone with a volume left waits, naming the kept volumes; gone with its volume being deleted, or none, releases at once) and `TestDeletion_KeptVolumesAreNamedWhenTheWaitEnds` (same file: after the grace period the Warning names the kept volumes and the statement); `TestDeletion_ATransientNeo4jErrorIsRetriedQuietly` (each of the eight kinds, a deadlock on its cleanup statement over three passes: no error, no Warning, nothing on status, the statement retried each pass, released once it succeeds; fails with `retryTransient` switched off) and `TestDeletion_ATransientThatOutlastsTheGraceIsNamed` (`internal/controller/deletion_transient_test.go`) — with `fateOfTarget` put back to the earlier reading (a target that exists holds, one that is gone releases), the being-deleted-with-volumes and the kept-volume cases fail.
- **enforcement:** unit test; not walked live yet. No machine check that a NEW kind's deletion follows the rule — convention + code review: a finalizer that guards something in Neo4j or Aura is released early only when its target took it with its volumes (`settleByTarget`).

### id 189 — An object already gone is released; an outcome of a user's, role's, binding's or rule's own outlasts a target restart
- **scope:** `removeFinalizer`, `removeFinalizerAt`, `finalizerRemoval` and `finalizerListMoved` (`internal/controller/object_patch.go`) and every finalizer release: the `handleDeletion` of `internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jdatabase_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jbackup_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jrestore_controller.go`, `internal/controller/plugin_controller.go` and the kinds of id 178, through `removeFinalizer`; the release of `internal/controller/aurainstance_controller.go`, `internal/controller/auracustomermanagedkey_controller.go`, `internal/controller/auraipfilter_controller.go`, `internal/controller/auradatabase_controller.go`, `internal/controller/aurainvite_controller.go`, `internal/controller/auraorganizationmember_controller.go` and `internal/controller/auraprojectmember_controller.go`, through `removeFinalizerAt`; `recordTargetWait` and `settledAtGeneration` (`internal/controller/target_wait.go`) and the `waitForTarget` that calls it in `internal/controller/neo4juser_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jrole_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jrolebinding_controller.go` and `internal/controller/neo4jauthrule_controller.go`; `ConditionReasonSecretPending` (`internal/controller/events.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **Gone is released.** A finalizer release that finds the object gone — a pass that read it before an earlier pass released the finalizer, or before the deletion that release allowed completed — is success: `removeFinalizer` returns nil on NotFound and logs it at V(1), and the Aura kinds, whose release starts from the request, read the object and release it through `removeFinalizerAt`, which does the same for a read that finds it gone. Never an ERROR, never a returned error (controller-runtime logs a second ERROR, "Reconciler error", and retries). Any other failure of the release is still returned. A new finalizer release goes through one of the two.
  - **A release is not a Conflict.** Removing one finalizer is a JSON patch that tests the finalizer is at the index the copy has it and removes it there (`finalizerRemoval`), without the resourceVersion precondition the other patches carry (id 150). A deletion's copy is often older than the object: the pass wrote status first, or the cache has not seen the last write, and a status write moves the resourceVersion without touching `metadata.finalizers`. A merge patch without the precondition is not the answer: it replaces the whole list with the copy's, and drops a finalizer another writer added since. The tested removal leaves every other entry; when an entry before this one was removed or inserted, the test fails (the API server answers 422, `Invalid`), the object is read again and the removal retried with `retry.DefaultBackoff` (`finalizerListMoved`).
  - **An outcome of the object's own outlasts a target restart.** `recordTargetWait` keeps the phase, message and `Ready` condition of a `Neo4jUser`, `Neo4jRole`, `Neo4jRoleBinding` or `Neo4jAuthRule` whose status records an outcome of its own for its generation (`settledAtGeneration`): `Ready` (id 158), or a `Ready=False` condition stamped at the current generation for a cause of its own — a failure (`UserSyncFailed`, `RoleSyncFailed`, `BindingFailed`, `AuthRuleFailed`, a lasting `ConnectionFailed`) or a wait on something other than the target (`UserNotFound`, `RolesPending`, `OIDCProviderNotConfigured`). `ClusterNotReady=True` says it waits. The pass that finds the target back and meets the same failure writes nothing new and announces nothing; a different failure, or a recovery into `Ready`, is a transition and is announced (id 145). Waits on the target itself (`ClusterNotReady`, `ClusterUnreachable`, `ClusterNotFound`), the password Secret wait that runs before the target is looked at (`SecretPending`), a new object and a spec change not yet applied still wait in `Pending`.
- **why:** the v1.22.x re-walk of 2a50fa56 (2026-10-25) logged "Failed to update cluster after removing finalizer … the object has been modified" and a "Reconciler error" on a cluster deletion, whose retry succeeded the same second, and "Failed to remove finalizer … not found" with a "Reconciler error" in two of six `Neo4jPlugin` deletions; the plugin's release goes through `removeFinalizer`, so the NotFound arm covers it. #572 had made a cluster's finalizer NotFound a success; the live walk (2026-10-25) then logged a NotFound ERROR when the finalizer of a `Neo4jDatabase` already gone was removed (`neo4jdatabase_controller.go`: "Failed to update database after removing finalizer", returned, so controller-runtime's "Reconciler error" too), and the code showed every other kind's release returning it the same way. #573: a `Failed` `Neo4jUser` announced `UserSyncFailed` again after every standalone restart — the restart made it `Pending` (id 158 kept only a `Ready` object), and the pass that found the standalone back met the same failure, a transition back into `Failed`. A binding waiting for its user did the same with `UserNotFound`.
- **pinned-by:** `TestRemoveFinalizer_AnObjectAlreadyGoneIsReleased` (a stale copy of an object the API server no longer has: no error; a conflict is still returned), `TestDeletion_ReleasingAnObjectAlreadyGoneIsNotAnError` (the eight kinds of id 178 through `Reconcile`, the release answered with NotFound), `TestAuraDeletion_ReleasingAnObjectAlreadyGoneIsNotAnError` (the seven Aura kinds) (`internal/controller/finalizer_gone_test.go`); `TestHandleDeletion_AnObjectAlreadyGoneIsNotAnError` (`internal/controller/walk_noise_test.go`, the cluster and the standalone); `TestFinalizerRelease_FromACopyOlderThanTheObject` (every kind released through `Reconcile` from a cache that serves a copy older than the object: the eight of id 178, the plugin, the backup, the restore and the six Aura kinds), `TestHandleDeletion_ReleasesFromACopyOlderThanTheObject` (the cluster and the standalone), `TestRemoveFinalizer_KeepsAnotherWritersFinalizer`, `TestFinalizerRelease_OfAPluginBackupOrRestoreAlreadyGone` and `TestRemoveFinalizer_APIServer` (envtest: a status write since the copy, a finalizer inserted before this one, an object gone) (`internal/controller/finalizer_release_test.go`) — each fails with the locked merge patch put back; `TestRemoveFinalizer_KeepsAnotherWritersFinalizer` also with a merge patch without the lock, and the plugin/backup/restore one with the NotFound arm switched off; `TestUserReconcile_AFailureIsAnnouncedOnceThroughTargetRestarts`, `TestRoleBindingReconcile_AMissingUserIsAnnouncedOnceThroughTargetRestarts`, `TestSettledAtGeneration` (`internal/controller/failure_through_restart_test.go`); `TestRecordTargetWait` (`internal/controller/auth_target_wait_test.go`). Each fails with its part switched off by `go test -overlay`: the NotFound arm of `removeFinalizer` (the first, second and fourth), and of the Aura kinds' release, now `removeFinalizerAt` (the third), and `settledAtGeneration` replaced by `readyAtGeneration` (the restart tests and the kept-failure cases of `TestRecordTargetWait`).
- **enforcement:** unit test; not walked live yet.

### id 180 — A retarget never puts a server the upgrade moved on below the partition; it never comes back on the version before
- **scope:** `retargetPartition`, `serverNotReadyBelow`, `serverPodsUncached` (`internal/controller/upgrade_retarget.go`); their use in `stepUpgradeStaging` (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade_statemachine.go`); `serversDown.pods` (`internal/controller/upgrade_repair.go`); the retarget in `handleRollingUpgrade` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`), which hands the new target to Staging and announces it; `EventReasonUpgradeRetargeted` (`internal/controller/events.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **The hazard.** A StatefulSet creates a pod at or above its partition from its update revision (the template) and a pod below it from its current revision — the version every server ran before the upgrade, until Kubernetes records the rolled-out template as current, which it does only once every pod runs it with the partition at zero. Staging froze a new target at the replica count. On a retarget from A to B — during the walk, or of a `Paused` or `Failed` upgrade — the servers the walk had rolled onto A then sat below the partition, and a pod of theirs recreated before the walk reached it again (a node drain, an eviction, a crash and a delete) came back on the version before the upgrade, over a store A had opened: a downgrade, which Neo4j does not support.
  - **The rule.** Staging never puts a server that runs another revision than the StatefulSet's current one below the partition: it stages the new target at the lowest such server (`retargetPartition`; the pods read uncached, `serverPodsUncached`). Those servers are in the update range: the StatefulSet controller moves them on to B, highest first, each once the one before is Ready, and one recreated meanwhile comes back on B. The walk's own checks — the server available in SHOW SERVERS, its databases caught up (id 127) — apply from the lowest of them down. A server below that partition runs the current revision, and comes back on it.
  - **The trade-off.** Between the servers that ran A, the move to B is checked by Kubernetes readiness only, as for the servers above the lowest one a repair replaces (id 162). That is the price of never going back. That readiness is a server available in the cluster, not a port that answers: a server pod is Ready only once Neo4j reports it available, or after its first 5 minutes (id 181). In the common case — a retarget soon after the walk's first server, or of an upgrade held after it — one server ran A, the one at the partition, and the walk's check covers it fully. The alternatives were weighed: deferring a retarget until the walk to A completes keeps every check but takes every server through A, the target the user is leaving, and never ends for a walk that cannot finish (a server crash-looping on A — started, so its store may be A's); making A the current revision first is only possible once every server runs it, which is the next point.
  - **Every server on A.** When every server runs the StatefulSet's update revision (the walk to A has rolled them all, Stabilizing or Verifying), Kubernetes is about to record it as current: Staging waits for that, then freezes B at the replica count as for any new upgrade, and the walk checks every server. A server recreated meanwhile comes back on A, the version it ran. Not while a repair replaces servers that cannot start (id 162): such a cluster never gets there.
  - **…during a repair (decided in round 8).** With every server on A and one that cannot start on it, Kubernetes never records A as current (`completeRollingUpdate` wants every replica Ready), so there is nothing to wait for, and Staging stages B at the lowest server that moved on — partition 0 when they all did: the StatefulSet replaces the server that cannot start first, then each of the others once the one before is Ready, and the walk checks server 0, Stabilizing and Verifying the whole cluster. Weighed and refused: freezing at the replica count leaves the server that cannot start down, and the walk waits for every server Ready until it stops; freezing at the repair partition (the lowest server that cannot start) walks the others with every check, but leaves them below the partition on A while the current revision is the version before, so one recreated meanwhile comes back on it — the downgrade this rule exists to prevent, which the release owner put above the per-server checks. What the roll is paced by is no longer a port that answers: a server pod is Ready once Neo4j reports it available in the cluster, its databases healthy and its primaries in their Raft groups, for at most 5 minutes after it starts (id 181) — the walk's first gate; what it does not check between those servers is the walk's database catch-up (id 127), and a server still unavailable after the 5 minutes. With `MaxUnavailableStatefulSet` on (Kubernetes 1.35.0–1.35.3, 1.37+) the StatefulSet counts every server that is not available, below the partition too, and restarts none while one is not (id 204).
  - **No restart at a bad moment.** The write that stages a new template at such a partition makes the StatefulSet controller restart servers at once, so it waits until every server below the partition is present and Ready, and every one at or above it is Ready or among the servers a repair replaces — a retarget mid-walk would otherwise restart them while another server is down. A held upgrade's start already waits for every replica Ready (id 149), except in a repair.
  - **Unchanged:** a new upgrade from a completed one (every server on the current revision) freezes at the replica count; a server that never started on A (an image that cannot be pulled) is replaced first by the repair, at the same partition (ids 149, 162); Staging run again keeps the live partition (id 162).
  - **Announced.** A retarget mid-walk is a Normal `UpgradeRetargeted` event ("Rolling upgrade retargeted from A to B during Rolling; the new target is staged"), once per new target, from the status write that records it (id 136): the write's re-read record decides, so a pass whose cached copy still names A writes the same record again and says nothing. It was an info log line only (the walk of be1e0a6f). A held upgrade taken up towards a new tag is announced by `startRollingUpgrade`'s `UpgradeStarted` ("resumed") instead.
- **why:** found in the #562 review (2026-10-25): every path that staged a new target — the mid-walk retarget in `handleRollingUpgrade` and the resume of a held upgrade in `startRollingUpgrade` — froze at the replica count (or at the lowest server a repair replaces), whatever the servers ran. The release owner's decision: a server that ran a newer version must never come back on an older one; prefer safety over saving restarts, and keep the per-server checks wherever possible. Kubernetes: `newVersionedStatefulSetPod` creates a pod below the partition from the current revision, and `completeRollingUpdate` records the update revision as current only when every replica is updated and ready (`pkg/controller/statefulset` in kubernetes/kubernetes).
- **pinned-by:** `TestRetarget_MidWalk_AServerTheWalkRolledNeverGoesBack` (servers 1 and 2 on A, a retarget to B: staged at partition 1, a drain of server-1 brings it back on B, the walk reaches Stabilizing with every server created on B), `TestRetarget_HeldUpgrade_AServerTheWalkRolledNeverGoesBack` (the same from `Paused`), `TestRetarget_MidWalk_WaitsWhileAServerBelowIsDown`, `TestRetarget_AfterEveryServerRolled_WaitsForTheCurrentRevision` (nothing staged until the controller records A as current, then partition 3 and a drained server back on A), `TestRetargetPartition` (`internal/controller/upgrade_retarget_test.go`, against `statefulSetEmulator` in `internal/controller/upgrade_expansion_resume_test.go`, which now records a completed rolling update as current). The B1 path is unchanged: `TestHeldUpgrade_AnExpansionThenTheFixedTagWalksToTheEnd`, `TestStartRollingUpgrade_TheRepairReplacesTheServersThatCannotStartFirst` and the envtest spec "replaces the servers that cannot start on the target when the tag moves on" still pass. Each part reverted under `go test -overlay` fails its tests: the partition at the lowest server that moved on, the wait for the current revision, the wait for the servers below. The event: `TestHandleRollingUpgrade_TargetChangedMidFlightRestages` (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade_statemachine_test.go`: one `UpgradeRetargeted` per phase, none from a stale copy replayed) and the envtest spec "lets a target change at or above the version the servers run re-stage the upgrade" (`internal/controller/cluster_running_version_test.go`: one, from a real `Reconcile`). The round-8 decision: `TestRetarget_RepairWithEveryServerOnTheFirstTarget_NeverGoesBack` (same file as the walk tests: every server on A, server-2 crash-looping on it, a retarget to B stages at partition 0, a drained server-0 comes back on B, and the walk reaches Stabilizing with every server created on B; with the repair partition kept instead, server-0 comes back on the version before and the test fails), and the case "every server moved on, a repair" of `TestRetargetPartition`. `statefulSetEmulator` records the update revision as current once every replica is updated and Ready whatever the partition, as `completeRollingUpdate` does.
- **enforcement:** unit test + envtest. Walked live on be1e0a6f (every retarget scenario passed; the missing event was the only finding).

### id 199 — A finalizer's addition is not a Conflict: a tested JSON patch, every other writer's finalizer kept
- **scope:** `addFinalizer`, `finalizerAddition` and `finalizerListMoved` (`internal/controller/object_patch.go`); the finalizer addition in `Reconcile` of every kind that has one: `internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jdatabase_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jbackup_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jrestore_controller.go`, `internal/controller/plugin_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4juser_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jrole_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jrolebinding_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jauthrule_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jcompositedatabase_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jdatabasealias_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jreplicadatabase_controller.go`, and the seven Aura kinds with a finalizer (`internal/controller/aurainstance_controller.go`, `internal/controller/auracustomermanagedkey_controller.go`, `internal/controller/auraipfilter_controller.go`, `internal/controller/auradatabase_controller.go`, `internal/controller/aurainvite_controller.go`, `internal/controller/auraorganizationmember_controller.go`, `internal/controller/auraprojectmember_controller.go`), which used their own locked patch helpers.
- **rule:** Adding a finalizer is a JSON patch without the resourceVersion precondition the other patches carry (id 150): it tests that `metadata.finalizers` is still the list the copy has and appends the finalizer (`/metadata/finalizers/-`); a copy with none tests the member for `null` — the API server stores an empty list as an absent member, and a test for null passes on an absent one (envtest, Kubernetes 1.34) — and adds the list `[finalizer]`. A status or annotation write since the copy was read is no Conflict. A merge patch without the lock is not the answer: it replaces the whole list with the copy's and drops a finalizer another writer added since. When the list moved, the test fails (422, `Invalid`), the object is read again and the addition retried with `retry.DefaultBackoff`; a finalizer the object already carries is not added again. Every kind adds through `addFinalizer`, the Aura kinds too.
- **why:** the v1.22.x walk (round 8): a new `Neo4jRoleBinding` logged a Conflict and a "Reconciler error" during its creation — its first pass added the finalizer with a merge patch locked to a copy older than the object. #579 had made the removal Conflict-safe (id 189); the addition was the same shape.
- **pinned-by:** `TestFinalizerAdd_FromACopyOlderThanTheObject` (every kind's first pass through `Reconcile`, from a cache that serves a copy older than the object: the eight of id 178, the plugin, the backup, the restore, the six Aura kinds of the deletion tests, the cluster and the standalone; the finalizer is added and the other write kept), `TestAddFinalizer_KeepsAnotherWritersFinalizer` (none in the copy and none, or another writer's, in the object; one in the copy and another writer's added; this one added by another writer), `TestAddFinalizer_APIServer` (envtest: no finalizers and a status write since, no finalizers and another writer's added since, some and a status write since) (`internal/controller/finalizer_add_test.go`). Each fails with the locked merge patch put back.
- **enforcement:** unit test; not walked live yet. No machine check that a NEW finalizer addition goes through `addFinalizer` — convention + code review.

### id 203 — An upgrade step decides from the record and the StatefulSet as stored; the cache only where a stale read delays
- **scope:** `upgradeRecordMovedOn`, `serverStatefulSetUncached` (`internal/controller/upgrade_uncached.go`); the wait before Rolling, Stabilizing and Verifying in `handleRollingUpgrade` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`); the StatefulSet read of `stepUpgradeRolling` and the pod read before `serversDownIn` in `stepUpgradeStaging` (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade_statemachine.go`); `serversDownOnTarget`, `serversDownIn` (`internal/controller/upgrade_repair.go`); `serverPodsUncached` (`internal/controller/upgrade_retarget.go`); `storedUpgradeStatus` (Staging's own, id 162).
- **rule:**
  - **The record.** Before Rolling, Stabilizing or Verifying acts, the pass reads the cluster's upgrade record from the API server (`APIReader`) and waits (5s) while its phase, target or `currentPartition` differs from the cached copy's (`upgradeRecordMovedOn`); a record that cannot be read waits too. Staging already did (`storedUpgradeStatus`, id 162). A stale cached copy is consistently old — spec and status from the same moment — so it looks like a state the cluster was in: a pass started by something other than the write's own watch event (a pod event, a requeue, a lost watch) acted on the record a write had replaced, and its own write put the old step back over the new one: a Verifying pass could complete an upgrade the user had just retargeted, ending the hold, and the normal pass then let the StatefulSet roll every server, ungated; a Rolling pass lowered the partition under a retarget recorded meanwhile.
  - **The StatefulSet.** The Rolling step reads the server StatefulSet from the API server: its generation, observed generation, updated and ready replicas decide whether the step is settled. A cached copy from before Staging wrote a new target counts the servers on the old target as updated, every one ready: the step found the step settled, checked the server at the partition (available, on the old target) and lowered the partition onto the new template, so the next server restarted with no check. A read that fails other than NotFound waits (15s) instead of stopping the upgrade.
  - **The pods.** Staging judges the servers that cannot start (`serversDownIn`) from the pods as stored, read once with the ones it stages by (`serverPodsUncached`, id 180): judged from a cache from before a server failed, it froze every server and the walk waited for one that stays down until it is replaced, then stopped. Pods the walk replaces are read the same way (id 204).
  - **Where the cache stays.** Where a stale read only delays: `isUpgradeRequired` and `startRollingUpgrade` (`heldUpgradeRepair`, `validateStatefulSetsReady`, the replica count), whose start Staging judges again from the API server; the cluster's own Ready checks; the partition write itself (`updateServerStatefulSet`, which a stale read turns into a conflict and a retry).
- **why:** round 8 (2026-10-25), from the code: Staging read the record and the StatefulSet from the API server since #562's re-walk, the other steps the cache. The release owner: read uncached where a step moves the partition or declares a server done; keep the cache where a stale read only delays.
- **pinned-by:** `TestRolling_DecidesFromTheStatefulSetAsStored` (a cache holding the StatefulSet from before Staging wrote the new target: no partition is written), `TestUpgradeSteps_DecideFromTheRecordAsStored` (a pass with the cluster from before a retarget was recorded: the partition and the record stay), `TestStaging_JudgesTheServersDownFromThePodsAsStored` (a cache from before server-2 failed: staged at partition 2, above it, not frozen) (`internal/controller/upgrade_uncached_test.go`). Each fails with its read put back on the cache under `go test -overlay`.
- **enforcement:** unit test; not walked live yet.

### id 204 — `MaxUnavailableStatefulSet`: one server at a time, said explicitly; a server that cannot start and is due for the update is replaced by the operator where the StatefulSet does not
- **scope:** the server StatefulSet's `updateStrategy.rollingUpdate.maxUnavailable` (`buildStatefulSetForEnterprise`, `internal/resources/cluster.go`); `stuckServerPods`, `replaceStuckServerPods`, `replaceStuckServerPodsUncached`, `reconcileStuckServerPods` (`internal/controller/stuck_server_pods.go`); their calls after the StatefulSet apply of the normal pass (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`) and in `stepUpgradeRolling` (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade_statemachine.go`); `EventReasonServerPodReplaced` (`internal/controller/events.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **What the gate changes.** `MaxUnavailableStatefulSet` is beta and on by default in Kubernetes 1.35.0–1.35.3, turned off in 1.35.4 and in 1.36, and on again from 1.37 (`pkg/features/kube_features.go` at those tags). With it on, the API server defaults an unset `rollingUpdate.maxUnavailable` to 1 (`pkg/apis/apps/v1/defaults.go`) and the StatefulSet controller takes the update through `updateStatefulSetAfterInvariantEstablished` (`pkg/controller/statefulset/stateful_set_control.go`); with it off, the API server drops the field (`pkg/registry/apps/statefulset/strategy.go`) and the controller deletes the highest pod in the partition's range that is not on the update revision, available or not, then waits for it. Unset, `maxUnavailable` is 1 either way (`getStatefulSetMaxUnavailable`). In 1.35.0–1.35.3 the controller counts every pod that is not available, at any ordinal, and deletes nothing while the count reaches `maxUnavailable`. In 1.37, for `Parallel` pod management (the server StatefulSet's), it first deletes every pod at or above the partition that is on an older revision and not available, then spends what is left of `maxUnavailable` on available ones.
  - **Can more than one server go down?** Not through the update with the gate on: every unavailable pod counts, below the partition too, so a pod is deleted only while every other is available (1.35.0–1.35.3), or only pods already down are, beyond the budget (1.37). With the gate off the controller can: it deletes the next pod in the range without counting one down below the partition. The operator's own gates keep that from happening on its writes — the upgrade walk lowers the partition only with every replica Ready and the server it checks available (id 127), a plugin writes only to a target with every server available (id 141) except to repair servers that are down (ids 152, 155), and an image change waits for a cluster with every server available (id 105).
  - **What it broke, and the fix.** Every repair the operator makes on a cluster short of a server — a plugin's own init container or settings (ids 152, 155), a held upgrade's new tag (id 162), a corrected spec — writes the template and relies on the StatefulSet to replace the pod that cannot start: on an older revision, at or above the partition. In 1.35.0–1.35.3 that pod is the one unavailable pod, so nothing is replaced and the repair waits for ever — Kubernetes' "forced rollback", which a standalone's OrderedReady StatefulSet already needed (`replaceStuckPods`). So the cluster replaces such a pod itself: one at or above the partition, below the replica count, on another revision than the `status.updateRevision` of a spec the StatefulSet controller has seen (`observedGeneration`), not being deleted, not Ready, and failing to start (`podCannotStart`: an image it cannot pull, a container that keeps exiting, an init container that failed), read from the API server, deleted with its UID as a precondition, with one Normal `ServerPodReplaced` event naming the pod, the revisions and why it cannot start. It is down, so nothing it served is lost; it comes back from the update revision. 1.37's controller, and the one without the gate, delete it too: one of the two finds it gone, which is passed over. A pod below the partition is never touched (it would come back on the current revision), nor one still starting. The normal pass looks after the StatefulSet apply, only while the cached StatefulSet shows an update that has not reached every pod with one not Ready (`reconcileStuckServerPods`); the walk on each Rolling pass with a replica not Ready.
  - **`maxUnavailable: 1`, explicit.** The server StatefulSet says it: one server at a time whatever a Kubernetes version defaults. It is what the API server defaults with the gate on and what the controller assumes unset; with the gate off the API server drops it. It is not part of the pod template, so the template, its revision and every server stay as they are when an operator upgrade writes it (restart-neutral). A value above 1 would let the controller delete a second available server while one restarts, which Neo4j's rolling procedure does not allow. The standalone's StatefulSet (one replica, OrderedReady) is left as it is.
- **why:** round 8 (2026-10-25): the release owner asked what the gate changes for the partitioned rolling updates and the plugin self-repair, read from the Kubernetes source at v1.35.0, v1.35.3, v1.35.4, v1.36.0 and v1.37.0 and the `apps/v1` API documentation ("Defaults to 1"). The controller of 1.35.0–1.35.3 never replaces an unavailable pod on an older revision while it is the one unavailable pod, which every repair of a cluster relied on.
- **pinned-by:** `TestStuckServerPods` (the pod a plugin's repair is for and one that cannot pull its image are stuck; one on the update revision, one still starting, one below the partition, and any while the controller has not seen the spec are not), `TestReconcileStuckServerPods_ReplacesThePodTheRepairIsFor` (deleted, announced, the servers that are up untouched; nothing once the update reached every pod), `TestReconcile_ReplacesAServerThatCannotStartOnTheRevisionBeingLeft` (through `Reconcile`), `TestRepair_OnKubernetes135_TheWalkReplacesTheServerThatCannotStart` (the repair of an upgrade held on a tag never published, against `statefulSetEmulator` playing 1.35.0–1.35.3: server-2 replaced by the walk, every server restarted once onto the fixed tag, one `ServerPodReplaced`), `TestServerStatefulSet_MaxUnavailableRestartsNothing` (`internal/controller/stuck_server_pods_test.go`); `TestBuildServerStatefulSetForEnterprise_AlwaysRollingUpdate` (`internal/resources/cluster_test.go`: `maxUnavailable` 1). Each fails with its part reverted under `go test -overlay`: the walk's call, the normal pass's call, the partition, the update revision and the observed generation in `stuckServerPods`, the field.
- **enforcement:** unit test; to walk live on a Kind node image `kindest/node:v1.35.1` (`docs/developer_guide/release_verification.md`).

### id 195 — Of two Neo4jPlugins with one target and spec.name, an enabled one installs; a disabled one never holds it back, and neither leaving the tuple takes the plugin off the servers
- **scope:** `checkForDuplicatePlugin`, `sameTuplePlugins`, `winsTuple`, `pluginSuccessor`, the duplicate branch of `Reconcile`, `SetupWithManager`, `pluginsSharingTuple`, `tupleOwnershipChanged` (`internal/controller/plugin_controller.go`); `otherPlugins`, `withoutPlugin`, `successorDownloads` (`internal/controller/plugin_env_ownership.go`); `disabledMessage` (`internal/controller/plugin_disabled.go`); `EventReasonPluginDuplicate` (`internal/controller/events.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **Who installs:** of the live `Neo4jPlugin`s with the same `clusterRef` and `spec.name`, an enabled one wins over a disabled one, then the oldest `creationTimestamp`, then the lower UID (`winsTuple`). A CR being deleted takes no part.
  - **An enabled loser** is `Failed` with one `PluginDuplicate` Warning naming the winner (`duplicate Neo4jPlugin: "<winner>" (older) … delete one CR, or set spec.enabled: false on one, …`) and writes nothing.
  - **A disabled CR is never a duplicate:** the duplicate check runs for enabled CRs only. A disabled CR goes to its removal (id 160), which leaves what the CR that installs the plugin in its place installs (`pluginSuccessor`), and is `Disabled` with one `PluginDisabled` naming that CR (`Plugin disabled (spec.enabled: false): Neo4jPlugin "<name>" installs "<plugin>" on <target> in its place`).
  - **Leaving the tuple keeps the plugin on the servers:** when the CR whose removal runs is disabled or being deleted, `otherPlugins` lists its successor — the winner of the rest, when that is enabled and its target's image takes it — so the removal keeps the successor's `NEO4J_PLUGINS` names, variables and list tokens, and the init container and volumes a `VerifiedDownload` successor writes under the same names (`successorDownloads`). Deleting the winner hands the plugin to the duplicate without a restart when the two ask for the same plugin; deleting a loser, enabled or disabled, writes nothing to the pod template. Each used to take the plugin off the servers, and the winner put it back: two restarts.
  - **The others of a tuple are reconciled when one of them changes:** a watch on `Neo4jPlugin` maps a CR to the others with its `clusterRef` and `spec.name` (`pluginsSharingTuple`; the old object too, so a CR that moves to another tuple wakes the ones it leaves), on create, delete, a spec change (generation) and the start of a deletion — not on a status write (`tupleOwnershipChanged`). A loser returns no requeue, so before this a duplicate held back by a CR since deleted or disabled stayed `Failed` until the operator restarted; the comment that said a watch fired for it was wrong.
- **why:** #566 walk follow-up (round 8, from the code): a disabled `Neo4jPlugin` won the duplicate check whenever it was older, so a newer enabled CR with the same `spec.name` stayed `Failed` until the disabled one was deleted — and, with no watch on the tuple, until the operator restarted after that. The deletion of either duplicate ran the uninstall with the other left out of `otherPlugins` (same `spec.name`), so it took the shared plugin off the servers.
- **pinned-by:** `TestCheckForDuplicatePlugin_AnEnabledCRWinsOverADisabledOne` (two enabled CRs and a disabled one, the disabled one oldest, between and newest, seen from each), `TestReconcile_ADisabledDuplicateDoesNotHoldBackAnEnabledOne` (cluster and standalone: the enabled CR installs, the disabled one is `Disabled` naming it, neither its pass nor its deletion writes the pod template), `TestReconcile_TheWinnersDeletionHandsThePluginToItsDuplicate`, `TestReconcile_DeletingALosingDuplicateLeavesTheWinnersInstall`, `TestReconcile_TheWinnersDeletionKeepsTheDuplicatesInitContainer`, `TestPluginsSharingTuple`, `TestTupleOwnershipChanged`, `TestPluginController_ADuplicateFollowsTheCRThatHoldsItBack` (envtest: the held-back CR follows the winner's disabling, re-enabling and deletion) (`internal/controller/plugin_duplicate_test.go`). Each fails on the code before the fix (`go test -overlay`); the envtest also with only the watch removed.
- **enforcement:** unit test + envtest; not walked live yet.

### id 196 — A Disabled plugin reports nothing installed
- **scope:** `writePluginStatus` (`internal/controller/plugin_controller.go`); the `InstalledVersion` and `InstallationTime` fields (`api/v1beta1/neo4jplugin_types.go`); their rows in `docs/api_reference/neo4jplugin.md`.
- **rule:** the status write that makes a plugin `Disabled` clears `status.installedVersion` and `status.installationTime`, and so does the next pass of a plugin already `Disabled` with them set (an operator before this kept them); that pass announces nothing. While the removal rolls out the plugin is `Installing` and keeps both: the servers still run it. Enabled again, its next `Ready` records a new installation (version and time). A disabled duplicate (id 195) clears them too: the plugin on the servers is the other CR's.
- **why:** #566 walk follow-up (round 8): a disabled plugin kept the version and time of its last install, so its status read as installed while nothing of it was on the servers.
- **pinned-by:** `TestReconcile_ADisabledPluginReportsNothingInstalled` (cluster and standalone: kept while `Installing`, cleared at `Disabled`, recorded again when enabled), `TestUpdatePluginStatus_ClearsAnAlreadyDisabledPlugin` (`internal/controller/plugin_status_test.go`). Both fail on the code before the fix (`go test -overlay`).
- **enforcement:** unit test; not walked live yet.

### id 208 — The image's own neo4j.conf never reaches a server; on a cluster the user's server.jvm.additional follows the operator's list and keeps it, a standalone has no operator list
- **scope:** `buildJVMSettings`, `userJVMVariableFromSource`, `JVMAdditionalSetting`, `JVMAdditionalEnv`, the `NEO4J_server_jvm_additional` variable of `BuildPodSpecForEnterprise`, the `server.jvm.additional` key of `buildNeo4jConfigForEnterprise`, and the `/operator-conf` → `/conf` copy of `buildStartupScriptForEnterprise` (`internal/resources/cluster.go`); `withoutMovedJVMLines` and its use in `ReconcileConfigMap` (`internal/controller/configmap_manager.go`); `renderStandaloneConfigMap`, `buildEnvVars`, `buildVolumeMounts` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`); the docs that say what a server gets: `docs/api_reference/neo4jenterprisecluster.md` (JVM arguments), `docs/api_reference/neo4jenterprisestandalone.md` (the `spec.env` example and JVM arguments), `docs/user_guide/performance.md`, `docs/user_guide/guides/resource_sizing.md`.
- **rule:**
  - **The image's file is replaced.** The entrypoint, finding `/conf`, empties `${NEO4J_HOME}/conf` and copies `/conf` into it (`rm -rf "${NEO4J_HOME}"/conf/*`, lines 479–483 of `docker-entrypoint.sh` on 5.26.32 and 2026.09.0), so on both kinds the server reads the operator's rendered conf plus what variables add — none of the image's settings. Its `server.jvm.additional` lines (20 on 5.26 and 2025.01, 23 from 2026.03, 24 from 2026.08) are put back by the operator since #594 (id 212); until then neither kind ran them.
  - **A cluster** gets the operator's list as `NEO4J_server_jvm_additional` (`buildJVMSettings`): G1 with a 200 ms pause target and sizing flags, compressed oops and class pointers, string deduplication, `-XX:+ExitOnOutOfMemoryError`, and the truststore flags when trusted CAs are set.
  - **On a cluster the user's own `server.jvm.additional` follows that list, in the same variable, and the list stays** (since v1.23.0, #594): `spec.config["server.jvm.additional"]`, then a plain `spec.env` `NEO4J_server_jvm_additional` (its entry is joined into the operator's, not added as a second variable). The JVM takes the last of two conflicting flags, so a user turns an operator flag off with its `-XX:-` form (`-XX:-UseStringDeduplication`) or gives another value (`-XX:MaxGCPauseMillis=100`). `spec.config`'s line is left out of the cluster's `neo4j.conf`: there it was given a second time, and before the operator's list, which then won a conflicting flag. The one exception is a `spec.env` value from a Secret or ConfigMap (`valueFrom`), which cannot be joined: as before, the variable is the user's, the operator renders none, and `spec.config`'s line stays in the conf. Until v1.23.0 either user setting replaced the operator's list, a `spec.config` line also took the truststore flags with it, and that line was given twice. Neither replaces the image's lines (id 212).
  - **Restart:** the ConfigMap manager's restart decision compares the desired ConfigMap with the existing one less its `server.jvm.additional` lines when the desired `neo4j.conf` has none (`withoutMovedJVMLines`): the line leaving the conf is not a roll of its own, and the variable's change on the pod template is the cluster's one roll. At the upgrade to v1.23.0 a cluster that sets `spec.config["server.jvm.additional"]` or a plain `spec.env` `NEO4J_server_jvm_additional` rolls once (its variable gains the operator's list); one that sets neither keeps a byte-identical variable and a `valueFrom` one renders as before, so neither rolls. A conf that keeps the line (`valueFrom`) is compared as it is, so a change of that line restarts the servers, as before.
  - **A standalone** gets no list of the operator's: besides the image's lines (id 212), its rendered conf (`renderStandaloneConfigMap`) has no `server.jvm.additional` line except the two truststore flags when trusted CAs are set, and its pod no such variable unless `spec.env` sets one. The entrypoint appends `server.jvm.additional` from a variable rather than replacing it (its one append-not-replace setting, line 273 of `docker-entrypoint.sh`).
  - **The docs say so:** the cluster API reference lists the default flags `buildJVMSettings` emits and says a user's line adds to them, after them; neither reference says a variable adds to arguments the conf already sets, beyond the image's lines the operator renders.
- **why:** round 8 live walk: the standalone API reference's new `spec.env` example said `NEO4J_server_jvm_additional` was added to "the JVM arguments neo4j.conf already sets"; on a standalone the JVM ran with that one option. The image's 24 lines were absent from the standalone's conf, and from a cluster's too, which ran the operator's list instead. The release owner then chose to put the image's lines back before v1.23.0 (#594, id 212).
- **why (the user's line):** #594, round 10 (the release owner's ask): a cluster's `spec.config` `server.jvm.additional` also became the whole of `NEO4J_server_jvm_additional`, so one user flag took the operator's G1 pause target, string deduplication, `ExitOnOutOfMemoryError` and truststore flags with it, and the flag was given twice (conf and variable). The model chosen: the user's line is added after the operator's list, which stays; to remove an operator flag, the user negates it.
- **pinned-by:** `TestClusterJVMDefaultsAreDocumented` (`internal/resources/cluster_jvm_docs_test.go`: every flag `buildJVMSettings` emits is in the cluster API reference's JVM arguments section, which says the image's lines come first and a user's line adds to the list), `TestBuildJVMSettings_UserFlagsComeAfterTheOperatorsList` (nothing set: the list unchanged; `spec.config`, `spec.env` and both after it, once, with no conf line; trusted CAs keep their flags; `valueFrom` keeps the user's variable and the conf line), `TestClusterJVMArguments_TheUsersFlagWinsAndIsGivenOnce` (the pod's conf and variable run through the entrypoint: the user's `-XX:-UseStringDeduplication` once, after the operator's `+`) (`internal/resources/cluster_jvm_user_test.go`); `TestUpgrade_UserJVMLineLeavingTheConfRestartsNothingMore`, `TestUpgrade_AJVMLineKeptInTheConfStillRestartsWhenItChanges` (`internal/controller/image_jvm_upgrade_test.go`); `TestStandaloneRendersTheImagesJVMArgumentsFirst` (`internal/controller/standalone_jvm_args_test.go`). The user-line tests fail on the replace model (`go test -overlay`), the upgrade test without `withoutMovedJVMLines`, the kept-line test with the JVM lines dropped from both sides of the decision.
- **enforcement:** unit test (docs pinned to the rendered output); the user-line model not walked live yet.

### id 211 — A Neo4jUser for the operator's own login that leaves out admin draws a warning
- **scope:** `Validate`, `operatorAdminUsername`, `grantsAdmin`, `validateClusterRef` (`internal/validation/user_validator.go`); the `ValidationWarning` the user controller raises from it (`advisoryWarnings`, `internal/controller/neo4juser_controller.go`); `docs/api_reference/neo4juser.md`, `docs/user_guide/user_role_management.md`, the "drop Neo4j refuses" recipe in `docs/developer_guide/release_verification.md`.
- **rule:** a `Neo4jUser` whose username is the one the operator signs in to its target as — the username in the target's admin Secret (`resolveAdminSecretName`), read as the operator's client reads it (`NEO4J_AUTH` as `user/password` first, then `username`), else `neo4j` — and whose `spec.roles` do not grant `admin` (by name, or through a `Neo4jRole` CR of the same target whose Neo4j role name is `admin`) draws one `ValidationWarning` (rule 102: advisory, never a refusal): `spec.roles` is authoritative, so the operator would revoke `admin` from its own login and every CR on that target would then fail with `Forbidden`. A target that does not exist yet draws none (its Secret cannot be told). The bootstrap-admin password warning (`riskyUsernames`) is separate and unchanged.
- **why:** pre-release journey on `dd429120`: the release-verification recipe for a drop Neo4j refuses created a `Neo4jUser` for `neo4j` with no roles; the operator revoked `admin` from the login it uses, and every CR went `Forbidden`. The recipe now lists `roles: [admin]`.
- **pinned-by:** `TestUserValidator_OperatorAdminWithoutAdminRoleWarns` (`internal/validation/user_validator_test.go`: `neo4j` with no roles and with other roles, the admin Secret's `username` and `NEO4J_AUTH` user, a standalone target, `admin` by name and through a `Neo4jRole` CR, another user, `neo4j` when the Secret names another admin, a target not there yet). Fails on the validator before the change (`go test -overlay`).
- **enforcement:** validator warning (inline) + unit test; not walked live yet.

### id 212 — Both kinds run the Neo4j image's own JVM arguments first, less -XX:+AlwaysPreTouch, restart-neutral: a cluster reads them from the image at each start, a standalone from a per-release table
- **scope:** `buildImageJVMArgumentsBlock` in `buildStartupScriptForEnterprise` (`internal/resources/cluster.go`, `internal/resources/image_jvm.go`); `imageJVMArgumentLists`, `leftOutImageJVMArgument`, `ImageJVMArguments`, `StandaloneImageJVMArgumentsBlock` (`internal/resources/image_jvm.go`); `renderStandaloneConfigMap`, `withoutDeferredConf` and its use in `standaloneConfSemanticHash`, `renderedConfStamp` and `reconcileConfigMap` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`); `JVMReleases`, `ImageJVMArguments`, `Image.Conf` and `apply_mounted_conf` (`internal/entrypointsim/`, test support); the API references' JVM arguments sections, `docs/user_guide/performance.md`, `docs/user_guide/guides/resource_sizing.md`.
- **rule:**
  - **What is rendered:** the `server.jvm.additional` lines of the image's own `neo4j.conf` — 20 on 5.26.0 to 5.26.32 and 2025.01.0; 23 from 2026.03.1 (adding the netty leak-detection property, `java.util.concurrent` to the `--add-opens`, and the Lucene vectorization property); 24 from 2026.08.1 (adding `--add-modules=jdk.incubator.vector`) — first in the server's `neo4j.conf`, always: the `--add-opens`, `--enable-native-access` and vector-module flags are what Neo4j's runtime needs, not tuning, so neither `spec.config` nor `spec.env` removes them. What follows wins a conflicting flag (the JVM takes the last): a user turns an image flag off with its `-XX:-` form or overrides a property with its own `-D`.
  - **Less one line, on both kinds: `-XX:+AlwaysPreTouch`** (`leftOutImageJVMArgument`; the cluster's block drops it with `grep -v -x -F`, `ImageJVMArguments` leaves it out of the standalone's list, and the table itself stays the images' verbatim). Pre-touch writes every page of the heap at start, and the operator sets a cluster's initial heap to its maximum, so a server holds its whole heap from the moment it starts. A user who wants it adds it in `spec.config` (it then appears once, after the image's lines).
  - **A cluster** reads them from the image itself: a restart-neutral block of the startup script, after the ConfigMap's files are copied to `/conf`, puts `grep '^server.jvm.additional=' "${NEO4J_HOME}/conf/neo4j.conf"` before `/conf/neo4j.conf`'s content. The script runs before the entrypoint, so the file is still the image's (a container starts from a fresh image layer each time).
