Then, appended last by the entrypoint's environment pass, comes `NEO4J_server_jvm_additional` (`buildJVMSettings`, id 208): the operator's list, then the user's own `server.jvm.additional` (`spec.config`'s, then `spec.env`'s), so the order is image, operator, user. The operator's list repeats `-XX:+UseG1GC` and the two `Unlock…VMOptions` of the image's, and contradicts none of its flags; it is unchanged.
  - **A standalone** has no startup script, so its conf carries the image's lines from `imageJVMArgumentLists`, keyed by the first release known to ship each list; a release between known ones gets the earlier list (each list holds the one before it), a CalVer newer than the newest known the newest list (best effort), a tag that is no supported Neo4j none. The block opens the conf, between the restart-neutral markers, before the user's `spec.config` line and the truststore flags; `spec.env`'s variable is appended after everything by the entrypoint.
  - **Restart-neutral on both kinds.** The cluster's block sits between `RestartNeutralBegin` and `RestartNeutralEnd`, which `normalizeStartupScript` drops from the restart decision. The standalone's block is dropped, markers included, by `withoutDeferredConf` from the semantic hash, from the live-apply comparison, and from the stamp check of a StatefulSet without a semantic hash; it ends without a blank line, so the conf without it is byte for byte what an earlier operator rendered. The operator upgrade that adds the block restarts no server and no standalone; each gets the lines at its next start. The `RestartPending` condition does not name the servers still waiting for them: it would then appear on every cluster.
  - **Verified against the images** (read-only, 2026-10-26): every local image's own list is vendored (`internal/entrypointsim/testdata/neo4j-conf-jvm-<release>.txt`, 5.26.0 to 2026.09.0); `java <image list> <operator list> -version` exits 0 on 5.26.0, 5.26.32, 2025.01.0, 2026.06, 2026.08.1 and 2026.09.0, with a repeated `--add-modules=jdk.incubator.vector` too (only the incubator warning), and `-XX:-AlwaysPreTouch` after the image's `-XX:+AlwaysPreTouch` leaves it false.
- **why (pre-touch):** the live walk of #598 (`81fb2b2f`), which rendered it: a 3-server 2026.08.1 cluster at the documented minimum (`requests`/`limits` 1536Mi, CPU 1; heap 768M, page cache 384M) under a write load from a separate pod (3 rounds × 12 batches of 50k-node transactions). v1.22.0: idle 529–589Mi, 36/36 batches. #598: idle 1136–1167Mi (the heap pre-touched), the writer OOMKilled with 5 restarts, crash-looping (an earlier run OOMKilled about 8 s after each start). #598 with `-XX:-AlwaysPreTouch`: idle 511–559Mi, 36/36. Startup time the same either way. Standalones barely moved: no heap is set for them. Removing the line is itself restart-neutral (the block's lines are out of the restart decision on both kinds), and nothing released renders the block, so no running standalone or cluster restarts for it.
- **why:** #594: the round 8 live walk found that neither kind ran the image's JVM arguments (id 208), so the servers started without the image's `--add-opens` and `--enable-native-access` flags and, on CalVer from 2026.08, without the vector incubator module Neo4j's vector search uses. The release owner chose to restore them before v1.23.0, restart-neutral.
- **pinned-by:** `TestImageJVMArguments_AreTheImagesOwn` (the table against every vendored image, floating, between-release, newer and digest tags, and each list holding the one before), `TestStandaloneImageJVMArgumentsBlock`, `TestImageJVMArgumentsBlock_PutsTheImagesLinesFirst` (the cluster block run in bash with the image's entrypoint functions over a fake image conf: image less pre-touch, then the operator's list with the user's flag — opting into pre-touch, once — after it; the image's other settings left out), `TestBuildStartupScript_CarriesTheImageJVMArgumentsBlock` (`internal/resources/image_jvm_test.go`); `TestUpgrade_ImageJVMBlockRestartsNothing` (`internal/controller/image_jvm_upgrade_test.go`); `TestStandaloneRendersTheImagesJVMArgumentsFirst`, `TestStandalone_ImageJVMBlockRestartsNothing` (`internal/controller/standalone_jvm_args_test.go`: also a block rendered with pre-touch, as #598 did, losing the line without a restart). Each fails without the change (`go test -overlay`), and those that check pre-touch fail with it kept, in the block or in the table.
- **enforcement:** unit test; walked live on #598 (order, flags in the running JVM, the vector module, a user override, an upgrade from v1.22.0 restarting nothing); the pre-touch removal not walked yet.

### id 213 — A scale-down drains every server it removes, one that is down too; it never releases the hold while it cannot tell which servers those are
- **scope:** `reconcileScaleDownDrain`, `initialRemovedServerIDs`, `serverOrdinal`, `unidentifiedRemovedServerIDs`, `scaleDownClient`, `scaleDownClientFor`, `ScaleDownHeldPodsAnnotation`, `holdPods`, `setDrainServers`, `scaleDownDrainInProgress` and the reconciler's `drainClient` seam (`internal/controller/scale_down.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`); `withKnownAddresses`, `serverAddressBook` (`internal/controller/cluster_availability.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **The ordinal of a server that is down.** The drain decides which servers a scale-down removes by the pod ordinal each `SHOW SERVERS` row names — its address, else its name. A server that is down is listed with no address (NULL) and its id for a name, so its row names no ordinal. The drain fills in the address it last reported (`withKnownAddresses`: this process's address book, then `status.diagnostics.servers`, which survives an operator restart), as the shortfall assessment and the diagnostics do (id 105).
  - **Else by elimination.** A row, not dropped, that still names no ordinal (the operator restarted, with `spec.monitoring.enabled: false`, while the server was down) is a removed server when every server the scale-down keeps (ordinals below `spec.topology.servers`) is named by another row: it is none of those, and is drained with the removed ones (`unidentifiedRemovedServerIDs`). An old identity of a kept ordinal that is still listed is drained the same way: nothing runs it. When a kept server is not named either, the drain cannot tell whether such a row is it: it drains the servers it can name, then keeps holding replicas (the pod names as the hold, in `neo4j.com/scale-down-held-pods`: the drain set holds server ids only) with `ServersPendingDrain` `True` naming the rows and the kept servers, until a server reports its address — never cordoning or deallocating a server it cannot name.
  - **Held by name only until the servers are named.** The drain set (`neo4j.com/scale-down-draining-servers`) holds server ids only. The replicas are held by the removed pods' names in `neo4j.com/scale-down-held-pods` before Neo4j is first asked (so the pass's StatefulSet apply cannot lower them before any drain ran) and while the drain cannot tell which servers go. The patch that names the servers in the drain set removes it, and so do the drain's end and a set-back; a set-back with nothing named or cordoned removes it without asking Neo4j. Both annotations hold the replicas (`scaleDownDrainInProgress`). The pod names used to go into the drain set, read back as ids that matched no server; a drain set an earlier operator left with them still holds, and the next pass replaces them with ids.
  - **A server the spec keeps again leaves the set.** A server of the drain set whose ordinal is below `spec.topology.servers` once more, and that no other row reports as that pod's server, is given back, not drained (a partial set-back, id 224); an earlier identity of a kept pod stays in the set.
  - **Drained like the others.** The server that is down goes through the same steps: cordon, the settle before the deallocation (id 231), the `DRYRUN` deallocation of the whole set (Neo4j refuses it for a database that is offline or has one primary there, and the scale-down is blocked as before, id 123), deallocate, drop once it hosts only `system`. Neo4j documents removing a server whose unavailability proves permanent (Operations Manual, "Managing servers in a cluster", error handling, `dev` and `5.x`). While the drain runs, the replicas stay held, so the StatefulSet creates a pod being replaced again and the server usually comes back before its turn.
- **why:** #594, on the v1.23.0 journey (2026-10-26): a 5→3 scale-down issued while `c3-server-3`'s pod was being replaced drained and dropped only server-4. `SHOW SERVERS` listed server-3 `Enabled`/`Unavailable` with a NULL address, so `initialRemovedServerIDs` could not derive its ordinal; the drain released the replica hold once server-4 was dropped, the StatefulSet removed server-3's pod, and server-3 stayed `Enabled`/`Unavailable` until the walker dropped it by hand. The cluster read `Ready` with `Degraded=False` while `ServersHealthy=False` for 4.5 minutes and more.
- **pinned-by:** `TestScaleDownDrain_AServerDownWhenItStartsIsDrainedToo` (`internal/controller/scale_down_unavailable_test.go`, the drain run pass by pass against a fake system database that lists a server that is down with no address: server-3 named by its last address while kept server-1 is down too; by elimination with no address known; and, with kept server-1 down and unknown, server-4 drained, server-1 and server-3 untouched and the replicas held with the reason, then server-3 drained once server-1 is back), `TestUnidentifiedRemovedServerIDs` (same file); `TestScaleDownDrain_TheDrainSetHoldsServerIDsOnly`, `TestScaleDownDrainInProgress_EitherRecordHolds` (`internal/controller/scale_down_held_pods_test.go`: every value a patch gives the drain set, before Neo4j is asked, for a stuck cluster lowered — the walk of the #619 fixes saw "c3-server-3,c3-server-4" there — and while the drain cannot tell, holds no pod name; a set-back with nothing named or cordoned lets the held pods go without asking Neo4j; with the pods written to the drain set again, or the set-back leaving them, under `go test -overlay`, they fail). Each fails with its part reverted under `go test -overlay`: the known addresses, the elimination, the hold while it cannot tell.
- **enforcement:** unit test; not walked live yet.

### id 214 — During an upgrade the Degraded condition follows SHOW SERVERS; the phase and the step gates do not
- **scope:** `collectDiagnosticsDuringUpgrade`, `recordShortfallDuringUpgrade` (`internal/controller/upgrade_diagnostics.go`); the `servers` seam of `upgradeGates` (`internal/controller/upgrade_database_gate.go`).
- **rule:** While an upgrade is active, the pass that collects the diagnostics (at most once per `--ready-poll-interval`, once the cluster has formed, id 131) writes the `Degraded` condition from the same `SHOW SERVERS`, as the normal pass writes it for a formed cluster short of a server (id 105): `True`, reason `ServersUnavailable`, naming each `Enabled` server that is not `Available` ("2 of 3 servers available; unavailable: c-server-2 (Unavailable)", the address it last reported filling in the one a server that is down does not), or `False`, `AllServersAvailable`, once every one of the StatefulSet's servers is available; a server not yet `Enabled` leaves it as it is. Only that condition, only when it changes. With `spec.monitoring.enabled: false` the diagnostics are not collected, but this `SHOW SERVERS` is. A server the walk restarts on purpose is unavailable, and the condition says so, as for a routine restart outside an upgrade (id 105). The phase stays as the upgrade keeps it, and no step gate reads the condition: when the upgrade stops, the normal pass takes over with the condition's `lastTransitionTime` as the grace clock, so a server down for longer than the grace turns the phase `Degraded` at once. What reads `Degraded=True` while the upgrade runs: `noteUpgradeInClusterMessage`, which on a retarget writes the message of a walk on a cluster short of servers (`clusterShortUpgradeMessage`) rather than the Ready one, and on a stop leaves the message for the next normal pass to write, the shortfall's; and the plugin and live-config gates, which wait during an upgrade anyway.
- **why:** #594, on the v1.23.0 journey (2026-10-26): a Rolling step with server-2 in `ImagePullBackOff` for 30 minutes read `Degraded=False`, "All 3 servers are available", while `ServersHealthy=False`. Only the normal pass wrote the condition, and it does not run during an upgrade (id 131); #580 made a `Failed` upgrade's retry let it run (id 149), not a walk in progress.
- **pinned-by:** `TestReconcile_DegradedFollowsTheServersDuringAnUpgrade` (`internal/controller/upgrade_diagnostics_test.go`: through `Reconcile` mid-Rolling, server-2 down — `Degraded=True` naming it, phase `Ready`, the upgrade `Rolling`, the partition still 2 — then back, `Degraded=False`). It fails with the write reverted under `go test -overlay`.
- **enforcement:** unit test; not walked live yet.

### id 216 — A role is announced Ready once its privileges are applied; learn mode names what it cannot tell; a dependent whose target went away warns only if it outlasts it
- **scope:** `reportReady`, `writeReady` (`internal/controller/neo4jrole_controller.go`) and `ConditionReasonPrivilegesWaitForDatabase` (`internal/controller/events.go`); `unattributedMessage`, `aliasesNamedBy` and the `aliases` passed to `reconcileLearned` (`internal/controller/neo4jrole_learn.go`); `targetGoneNotices`, `hadFoundTarget`, `targetGoneNoticeDelay` (`internal/controller/target_gone.go`) and the missing-target branch of `Reconcile` in `internal/controller/neo4juser_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jrole_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jrolebinding_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jauthrule_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jcompositedatabase_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jdatabasealias_controller.go`, `internal/controller/neo4jreplicadatabase_controller.go` and `internal/controller/neo4jdatabase_controller.go`; the `PrivilegesSynced` and `PrivilegesResolve` guidance of `kubectl neo4j explain` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/explain.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **RoleReady follows the privileges.** A role whose spec statements wait for their database (skipped, `PrivilegesResolve=False`) and that has not been announced Ready is phase `Ready` with `Ready=True` reason `PrivilegesWaitForDatabase` and announces nothing; `RoleReady` comes from the write that records reason `RoleReady` — the pass that applies them, after its `PrivilegesApplied`, with their count. A role announced Ready keeps reason `RoleReady` when a database it grants on goes away and comes back (`PrivilegesResolve`, `PrivilegeTargetDropped` say so): not a second `RoleReady`. A spec change does not announce again, as before.
  - **Learn mode names its cause.** `PrivilegesSynced=Unknown` (`UnattributedPrivileges`) counts and lists the rows learn mode keeps only when there are any. A statement whose own rows it cannot tell (ambiguous: granting it added no row) is named, with each alias it grants through and that alias's target database (`` `current` is an alias for database `other` ``, from the alias layout the pass read), and what clears it: grant on the target database instead of the alias, or remove the statement another one already covers — or run with `--privilege-normalisation=probe`.
  - **A target that went away warns only if the dependent outlasts it.** A dependent not being deleted whose target CR does not exist waits at once (`Pending`, reason `ClusterNotFound`, rule 182). Its `ClusterNotFound` Warning: when the dependent never found its target (no `Ready` condition, or reason `ClusterNotFound` — applied before its cluster), at once, as before; when it had (a `Ready` condition for another reason), only once the wait has lasted `targetGoneNoticeDelay` (one minute) and the dependent is still there. A teardown — a namespace deletion, a `kubectl delete -f` of a directory that lists the cluster first — deletes the dependent within seconds, its deletion takes the path of rule 178 with no `ClusterNotFound`, and nothing is announced. A cluster deleted on its own leaves users, roles and databases that warn a minute later. Memory, per reconciler: an operator restart during the minute forgets it, and the wait already on status is not announced (rule 136). The operator reads no `Namespace` (it has no RBAC for them, and a namespace-scoped install could not get them).
  - **Not changed:** `Neo4jPlugin`, `Neo4jBackup`, `Neo4jRestore`, `Neo4jShardedDatabase` and `Neo4jReplicaPromotion` still announce a missing target when their wait starts.
- **why:** #594, from the v1.23.0 pre-release journey (2026-10-26). A role applied with the database it grants on, in one directory, announced "Role "c3_reader" is ready (0 privileges in sync)" a second before "applied 2 added / 0 revoked privileges" — its first pass found the database missing and skipped both statements. A role whose only learn-mode problem was `GRANT … ON DATABASE current` with `current` an alias for `other`, which another statement already granted, read "cannot attribute 0 privilege row(s) on this role to a spec statement". And the Phase 1 teardown, which deleted the standalone first, raised a `ClusterNotFound` Warning on every dependent.
- **pinned-by:** `TestRoleReady_IsAnnouncedOnceItsPrivilegesAreApplied` (`internal/controller/neo4jrole_ready_order_test.go`: no `RoleReady` while both statements wait, one with "(2 privileges in sync)" when they are applied, none when a database goes and comes back; fails with the waiting reason switched off); `TestUnattributedMessage_NamesTheAmbiguousStatementAndItsAlias` (`internal/controller/neo4jrole_unattributed_message_test.go`; fails with the earlier message); `TestDependents_ATargetThatWentAwayIsAnnouncedOnlyIfTheDependentOutlastsIt` (`internal/controller/target_gone_test.go`: the eight kinds, a dependent with a `Ready` condition and no target: `Pending` at once, no Warning, one a minute later; deleted meanwhile, released with none; fails with the Warning announced when the wait starts); `TestReconcile_AMissingClusterIsAWaitThatEnds` (rule 182: an object applied before its cluster still warns at once).
- **enforcement:** unit test; not walked live yet.

### id 217 — Of two database, sharded database, composite or alias CRs whose names in Neo4j differ only by case, the oldest owns the name; the other is Failed and acts on nothing, and a deletion leaves the name to a CR that still gives it
- **scope:** `nameClaim`, `nameOwner`, `otherLiveClaim`, `nameClaimKind` (`duplicate`, `keptFor`, `othersOfTheName`, `onClaimChange`), `nameClaimChanged`, `databaseNames`, `shardedDatabaseNames`, `compositeDatabaseNames`, `aliasNames` (`internal/controller/duplicate_names.go`); the duplicate branch of `Reconcile`, `handleDeletion` and `SetupWithManager` of `internal/controller/neo4jdatabase_controller.go`, `neo4jcompositedatabase_controller.go` and `neo4jdatabasealias_controller.go`, and of `Reconcile` and `SetupWithManager` of `neo4jshardeddatabase_controller.go`; `EventReasonDuplicateName`, `EventReasonDuplicateNameKept` (`internal/controller/events.go`); the API references of the four kinds; `docs/user_guide/kubernetes-events.md`.
- **rule:**
  - **One name:** Neo4j reads database, composite and alias names ignoring case (it lower-cases them; a sharded family's names are lower-cased too, rules 48, 53, id 146), so two CRs of one kind in one namespace, on the same `clusterRef`, whose name in Neo4j (`spec.name`; a composite's `compositeName`, an alias's `effectiveAliasName`) is equal ignoring case claim one object in Neo4j. Of the live ones, the oldest `creationTimestamp` owns it, then the lower UID, as for `Neo4jPlugin` (id 195). A CR being deleted takes no part.
  - **The other is `Failed`:** with reason `DuplicateName` (a `Neo4jDatabase`'s `Ready` condition; `databasePhaseFor` maps it to `Failed`, id 190) and one `DuplicateName` Warning, announced from the status write that enters it (`duplicate <Kind>: "<owner>" (older) already gives <what> "<name>" on clusterRef "<target>", and Neo4j's names ignore case; delete or rename one of them before this one can reconcile`). Its pass sends Neo4j nothing (the check runs before the target is resolved) and returns no requeue.
  - **A deletion leaves the name to a CR that still gives it:** the deletion of a `Neo4jDatabase`, `Neo4jCompositeDatabase` or `Neo4jDatabaseAlias` (after `deletionPolicy: Retain`, which keeps it anyway) drops nothing while another live CR of its kind gives the name: it releases its finalizer with one Normal `DuplicateNameKept` (`<what> "<name>" kept, not dropped: <Kind> "<other>" still gives it`). Deleting the owner hands the name to the other CR, which then reconciles it; deleting the other drops nothing the owner gives. A `Neo4jShardedDatabase` has no finalizer and drops nothing on deletion.
  - **The others of a name are reconciled when one of them changes:** a watch on the kind maps a CR to the others with the same target and name ignoring case (`othersOfTheName`; on an update the old object too, so a rename wakes the CRs it leaves), on create, delete, a spec change (generation) and the start of a deletion — not on a status write (`nameClaimChanged`).
  - **Not covered:** two CRs of different kinds that give one name (database, composite and alias names share one namespace in Neo4j), and two `Neo4jReplicaDatabase` CRs (a replica's database is a copy its upstream seeds again); both are left for a later release.
- **why:** #594 follow-up (round 10, the release owner's ask, from the code): two `Neo4jShardedDatabase` CRs whose `spec.name` differed only by case adopted the same family, with no duplicate detection. The same gap held for `Neo4jDatabase` (both CRs reported `Ready` on one database, and deleting either dropped the database the other still named), `Neo4jCompositeDatabase` (one composite, two owners) and `Neo4jDatabaseAlias` (each pass re-pointed the one alias between the two targets).
- **pinned-by:** `TestNameOwner_TheOldestLiveClaimOfANameAsNeo4jReadsItOwnsIt`, `TestNameClaims_TheWatchWakesTheOthersOfTheName`, `TestDatabaseReconcile_ACaseOnlyDuplicateIsFailedAndActsOnNothing` (also the owner's deletion handing the database over), `TestShardedReconcile_ACaseOnlyDuplicateIsFailedAndAdoptsNothing`, `TestCompositeReconcile_ACaseOnlyDuplicateIsFailed`, `TestAliasReconcile_ACaseOnlyDuplicateIsFailed`, `TestDeletion_LeavesANameAnotherCRStillGives` (database, composite and alias; a twin on another target contests nothing) (`internal/controller/duplicate_names_test.go`). Each reconcile and deletion test fails on the controllers before the fix (`go test -overlay`).
- **enforcement:** unit test; not walked live yet.

### id 218 — A replica's lag column and a promotion's lag taken show 0 at zero lag; blank means not read yet
- **scope:** `ReplicationLag` (`api/v1beta1/neo4jreplicadatabase_types.go`) and `ObservedLagTxIds` (`api/v1beta1/neo4jreplicapromotion_types.go`), both `*int64`, and their writers in `internal/controller/neo4jreplicadatabase_controller.go` and `internal/controller/neo4jreplicapromotion_controller.go`; the generated CRDs (`config/crd/bases/`, `charts/neo4j-operator/crds/`, `bundle/manifests/`); `docs/api_reference/neo4jreplicadatabase.md`, `docs/api_reference/neo4jreplicapromotion.md`, `docs/user_guide/cross_cluster_replication.md`.
- **rule:** `status.replicationLag` and `status.observedLagTxIds` are pointers with `omitempty`, so a reading of 0 is written and shown (`kubectl get neo4jreplicadatabase` LAG `0`; the promotion's `LAG TAKEN` `0`). Absent means not read yet: a replica being created or seeded before the operator first reads its lag, a promotion that has not completed. While the operator cannot read the lag (`ClusterNotReady`), the replica keeps its last reading on status — the condition says it is stale — and the lag metric is withdrawn (id 183's wait; the metric's "withdrawn" meaning is unchanged). Stored objects from an earlier operator read the same: a missing field was 0 or not read, and becomes 0 at the next reading.
- **why:** #594 follow-up (round 10): the fields were `int64` with `omitempty`, so a replica caught up with its upstream — the healthy state — showed an empty LAG column, the same as one never read.
- **pinned-by:** `TestReplicaStatus_ZeroLagIsShown` (absent before the first reading, `0` written at zero lag, kept while the cluster is not ready with the metric withdrawn), `TestPromotionStatus_ZeroLagTakenIsShown` (`internal/controller/replica_lag_column_test.go`). Both fail on the code before the fix (`go test -overlay`).
- **enforcement:** unit test; not walked live yet.

### id 219 — A scale-down drain that ends says so, and keeps saying so; ServersPendingDrain is set False from the condition as stored
- **scope:** the branch of `reconcileScaleDownDrain` that finds nothing left to drain, `setBackScaleDown` (was `clearScaleDownState`), `settleScaleDownCondition`, `scaleDownDrainCompleteMessage`, `scaleDownNotInProgressMessage` (`internal/controller/scale_down.go`); the `ServersPendingDrain` row of `docs/api_reference/neo4jenterprisecluster.md`; the scale-down section of `docs/user_guide/clustering.md`.
- **rule:** The pass of a scale-down that finds nothing left to drain — every server it removes dropped or gone, none it cannot tell (id 213) — releases the replica hold and sets `ServersPendingDrain` `False`, reason `NoServersPendingDrain`, message "Scale-down drain complete". A scale-down set back before its drain ended (`spec.topology.servers` raised to the replicas the StatefulSet keeps) sets it `False` with "Scale-down not in progress", once it has given back the servers the drain cordoned; a set-back that comes after the drain took a server past undoing is refused with `ScaleDownBlocked` instead, and the replicas stay held (id 224). Either is written only over a condition that is `True` as stored, decided in the write from the object it re-reads (`settleScaleDownCondition`), never from the pass's own copy; a condition already `False`, or absent, is left as it is. So the passes after a completed drain — one that reads the StatefulSet at its old replica count from the cache, those after the replicas are lowered, one on a copy from before the completion's write — leave "Scale-down drain complete" and its `lastTransitionTime` as they are, and a scale-down with nothing to drain writes no condition. The completion drops the drain's records, the time of its cordon too (id 231). A raise right after it waits until the removed servers' pods and volumes are gone (id 229).
- **why:** #606, the walk of the #594 fixes (d35c5aa9, 2026-10-27): after a completed drain the condition read "Scale-down not in progress", where the docs and the verification row (#601) said "Scale-down drain complete". The completion's message had been in the code since the initial commit, on a branch no pass could reach: every id in the drain set is a live server not dropped, so no active server meant an empty set, which the check above it had already sent to `clearScaleDownState`, the reset of an aborted scale-down. That reset was judged from the pass's copy, so the message restored alone would have been turned into "not in progress" by a pass on a copy the cache handed over from before the completion's write.
- **pinned-by:** `TestScaleDownDrain_ACompletedDrainSaysSoOnEveryPassAfter` (a 5→3 drain to its end: "Scale-down drain complete"; then a pass with the StatefulSet at 5 replicas, three at 3, and one on the copy the releasing pass read: status, reason, message and `lastTransitionTime` unchanged, the hold released), `TestScaleDownDrain_AScaleDownSetBackIsNotInProgress`, `TestScaleDownDrain_NothingToDrainWritesNoCondition` (`internal/controller/scale_down_complete_test.go`). The first fails under `go test -overlay` on the code before the fix ("Scale-down not in progress" at the end of the drain), and on the fix with the reset judged from the copy (its last pass).
- **enforcement:** unit test; walked live on 2026-10-28 (#606 walk, `5043c68f`): after a 5 → 3 drain the condition read "Scale-down drain complete" with the same `lastTransitionTime` four minutes later, and a scale-down set back read "Scale-down not in progress".

### id 222 — Turning Aura Fleet Management off takes out the NEO4J_PLUGINS entry its own path put there, in the disable's one roll
- **scope:** `fleetEntriesAnnotation`, `fleetEntryRecorded`, `setFleetEntryRecord`, `fleetEntryListed`, `neo4jPluginsOwnedByTarget`, `fleetEntryClaimed`, `fleetEntryClaimedOn`, `removeFleetEntry`, `dropFleetEntry`, `fleetDisableEdit`, `fleetRecordUpkeep`, `keepFleetRecord`, `reconcileFleetEntry`, `removeFleetManagementPlugin`, `standaloneFleetEntryDue` (`internal/controller/fleet_disable.go`); `triggerRollingRestartForConfigChange`, `confRollPending` (`internal/controller/configmap_manager.go`); the create branch of `createOrUpdateResourceInternal`, `mergeFleetManagementPlugin` and the call of `reconcileFleetEntry` in `Reconcile` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`); `reconcileStatefulSet` and `mergeFleetManagementPlugin` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **The record:** the path that adds `fleet-management` to the neo4j container's `NEO4J_PLUGINS` — the write that creates the StatefulSet (id 175), the enable's conf roll (id 226) and every pass's merge — records it in the same write, under `neo4j.com/aura-fleet-management-entries` on the server StatefulSet (a cluster's `{name}-server`, a standalone's `{name}`). It is StatefulSet metadata: it restarts nothing. Nothing is recorded when `NEO4J_PLUGINS` is the deployment's own (its `spec.env` sets it, and the cluster's or the standalone's owned-env record lists it): the deployment carries the name in that list itself, and the merge never writes it (id 223).
  - **Adopted while enabled:** a pass writes the record alone, as a merge patch of the StatefulSet's metadata, when `NEO4J_PLUGINS` lists the name, the variable is not the deployment's own and no `Neo4jPlugin` claims the name; it drops a record whose entry is gone, or whose variable has become the deployment's own (`fleetRecordUpkeep`, `keepFleetRecord`; the template write that takes the variable back drops it itself, id 227). The cached StatefulSet decides whether a patch is due, and the patch is made against a read from the API server; on a cluster it runs after the pass's template apply (`reconcileFleetEntry`), so it never makes that apply conflict with a cache that has not seen it, on a standalone in the merge, which runs after its StatefulSet write. A deployment an earlier operator gave the name, and one on 2026.03+ where the merge keeps it (id 192), is recorded on the first enabled pass after the upgrade, without a restart.
  - **A plugin's claim:** a `Neo4jPlugin` claims the name when a `neo4j.com/plugin-entries.<plugin>` record on the StatefulSet lists it (a disabled plugin's too, until its own removal takes it, id 160), or when a plugin of the target that is enabled, not refused by its image, not being deleted and `Managed` adds it as its `spec.name` or a dependency, as the entrypoint knows the name (`fleetEntryClaimed`). A plugin's own removal keeps the name the same way while `spec.auraFleetManagement` installs it (`withoutPluginEntries`, id 155): neither takes what the other still needs.
  - **The removal:** once the spec no longer installs it (`enabled: false`, or the block removed), an entry the record lists leaves `NEO4J_PLUGINS` (a last entry leaves `[]`) and the record goes. A claimed entry, an entry in a `NEO4J_PLUGINS` that is the deployment's own, and a `NEO4J_PLUGINS` that is not a JSON list (the entrypoint cannot read it either) stay, and only the record goes. **An entry nobody recorded stays:** on a deployment whose Fleet Management was already off when the operator was upgraded it cannot be told from one a user put there, and is taken out by hand. On a standalone on 5.26.12+ or 2025.08–2026.02 such a leftover also narrows the allowlist: the conf no longer sets it to `*`, and the entrypoint sets `fleetManagement.*` for the name (id 192).
  - **One roll:** turning it off also changes neo4j.conf — Fleet Management's `dbms.security.procedures.unrestricted` line, and the allowlist line or a standalone's `*`, are rendered from the spec — and that change is not dynamic: it rolls the servers. The removal goes in that roll's write: on a cluster the ConfigMap's restart stamp (`triggerRollingRestartForConfigChange`, `fleetDisableEdit`); on a standalone the StatefulSet write that carries the new conf hash — only when the hash this pass stamps is the conf the standalone renders now (`standaloneFleetEntryDue`): a ConfigMap read from a cache that has not seen this pass's write stamps the old conf, and the removal then waits for the pass that stamps the new one. A removal no conf change carries is written alone: on a standalone by the same StatefulSet write, on a cluster by `removeFleetManagementPlugin` after the template apply (`reconcileFleetEntry`), only while no conf roll is due (`confRollPending`), with the StatefulSet read from the API server before the write, so the pass after the stamp finds the record gone and writes nothing. A `NEO4J_PLUGINS` the deployment's own `spec.env` sets goes back to the user's list in the same roll — on a cluster the restart stamp carries the list as the cluster renders it (`carryOwnNeo4jPlugins`), on a standalone the StatefulSet write with the new conf hash applies its template — and the two paths do not overlap: that list has no record, so the removal here only drops a record left from before it was the deployment's (id 223).
  - **What it waits for:** nothing a plugin waits for (id 141): it is the deployment's own spec change, like the conf change it rides and the merge that adds the name. On a target that is down it rolls with that conf change, which waits for nothing either, and it writes nothing a repair writes (a plugin's init container or settings, id 155; a stuck server's replacement, id 204). A `Paused` or `Failed` upgrade's partition holds it as it holds the conf stamp (id 149), and it is not written into the first step of a two-step recreate (`restorePending`, id 171). A plugin's write after it does not put the name back — a plugin adds only its own names — and it never takes a plugin's.
  - **2026.03 and later:** the entry the merge keeps there while enabled (id 192) is adopted and goes with the disable's roll, so its `ERROR: could not query null` line stops; Fleet Manager itself is built into the server, and the operator does not turn it off. `status.auraFleetManagement` is left as it was.
  - **Back on:** the name is added again and recorded, in the write that rolls the servers for the conf's new lines — one roll, as for any enable on a running deployment (ids 175, 226).
- **why:** #606 (moved from #582, from the code): both controllers ran the merge only while Fleet Management was enabled, and it only adds; `addFleetManagementPlugin` also lists the name in the write that creates a StatefulSet, outside the owned-env record (id 175). Turning the feature off therefore left the JAR on the servers for good, and on a standalone the leftover name narrowed the allowlist to `fleetManagement.*` once the conf stopped setting `*`.
- **pinned-by:** `TestClusterReconcile_DisablingFleetManagementTakesItsEntryOut` (`enabled: false` and the block removed: one roll, the conf change's restart stamp, which takes the entry and the record; later passes write nothing; on again, added and recorded again), `TestClusterReconcile_DisablingFleetManagementKeepsAPluginsEntry` (a plugin's record; a `Managed` plugin not written yet), `TestClusterReconcile_AnEntryWithoutARecord` (adopted while enabled by a metadata patch, then taken out; not adopted while a plugin claims it; left when already off), `TestClusterReconcile_DisablingFleetManagementLeavesTheUsersOwnList`, `TestClusterReconcile_DisablingFleetManagementOnABuiltInImage` (2026.08.1: adopted without a roll, gone with the disable's), `TestRemoveFleetManagementPlugin_WaitsForTheConfRoll`, `TestFleetRemoval_AndThePluginControllerLeaveEachOthersEntries` (an APOC install after the removal; a fleet-management plugin's entry through the disable, then its own removal), `TestStandaloneReconcile_DisablingFleetManagementTakesItsEntryOut`, `TestStandaloneReconcile_DisablingFleetManagementKeepsWhatIsNotItsOwn`, `TestStandaloneReconcile_TheRemovalWaitsForTheNewConf` (a stale ConfigMap: nothing written; then the new conf hash and the removal in one write), `TestFleetEntryClaimed` (`internal/controller/fleet_disable_test.go`). Each part switched off under `go test -overlay` fails its tests: the removal, the record, the adoption, the claim, the ride on the conf stamp (two rolls), the standalone's conf check and the cluster's wait for a due conf roll.
- **enforcement:** unit test; walked live on 2026-10-28 (#606 walk, `5043c68f`). On a 5.26.32 cluster and standalone, and a 2026.09.0 cluster with a hand-added entry: each disable was one roll and took the entry and the record out; a re-enable added both back; a `Neo4jPlugin`'s entry was kept; the record was adopted after an upgrade from v1.23.0 with no restart; a `spec.env` `NEO4J_PLUGINS` was left alone.

### id 223 — A NEO4J_PLUGINS the deployment's own spec.env sets carries fleet-management as the deployment renders it; the fleet merge never writes it
- **scope:** `ownNeo4jPluginsWithFleet`, `clusterNeo4jPlugins`, `buildServerStatefulSet`, `carryOwnNeo4jPlugins`, `addFleetEntryInWrite`, `errNeo4jPluginsNotAList` (`internal/controller/fleet_owned_plugins.go`); the `valueFrom` skip of `addFleetManagementPlugin` (`internal/controller/fleet_plugin.go`); the call of `buildServerStatefulSet` in `Reconcile`, the create branch and the template apply of `createOrUpdateResourceInternal`, and the owned-variable check of `mergeFleetManagementPlugin` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`); `buildDesiredServerStatefulSet` (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade_statemachine.go`); `triggerRollingRestartForConfigChange` (`internal/controller/configmap_manager.go`); the template write of `reconcileStatefulSet` and the owned-variable check of `mergeFleetManagementPlugin` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`); `FleetPluginsEnvWarnings`, `isPluginList` (`internal/validation/fleet_validator.go`) and their calls in both `NoEffectWarnings` (`internal/validation/noeffect_warnings.go`); `docs/user_guide/aura_fleet_management.md` ("Your own `NEO4J_PLUGINS` in `spec.env`").
- **rule:**
  - **The defect:** a cluster whose `spec.env` sets `NEO4J_PLUGINS` owns that variable (its `neo4j.com/cluster-controller-env-vars` record lists it), and its template apply puts back a live value that differs (`envVarsEqual`). The plugin controller refuses to write such a variable (id 135); the fleet merge had no such guard. With Aura Fleet Management on it added `fleet-management`, the template apply of the same pass put the user's value back, and the servers restarted without end.
  - **The cluster renders the name:** `spec.env` and `spec.auraFleetManagement` are both the cluster's own spec, so the server StatefulSet the cluster builds (`buildServerStatefulSet`, for the normal pass and the upgrade's Staging) carries its own `NEO4J_PLUGINS` with `fleet-management` appended (`MergeNeo4jPluginList`: the user's names and order kept, a list that names it already byte for byte) while the spec installs Aura Fleet Management on an image whose entrypoint installs the plugin (not `FleetManagementBuiltIn`, judged on `spec.image.tag`, the image the builder renders). The template hash and the owned-env record take that value, so the template apply puts it back like any of the cluster's variables, and the merge finds the name listed. Only where the spec already puts the variable in the template: the static template still never carries `NEO4J_PLUGINS` otherwise. Nothing is recorded as Fleet Management's (id 222): the list is the deployment's.
  - **The merge never writes a variable the deployment owns:** `mergeFleetManagementPlugin` (both kinds) leaves a `NEO4J_PLUGINS` the target's record lists alone, as `pluginEnvOwnedError` does for a plugin. The cluster's create and template apply still add the name where it is missing (`addFleetEntryInWrite`, as id 175): to the cluster's own variable that adds nothing, its rendering carries the name.
  - **One roll each way:** turning Aura Fleet Management on or off changes `neo4j.conf` (id 192), and the ConfigMap's restart stamp — the write that rolls the servers for it — carries the cluster's own `NEO4J_PLUGINS` as it renders it now (`carryOwnNeo4jPlugins`): a plain value the live record lists as the cluster's, and the spec still sets. The template apply later in the pass finds the variable as it wants it and writes the template hash alone (no template change). Off, the list is what the user wrote: the name the cluster appended goes, a `fleet-management` the user wrote stays; nothing is recorded, so the disable's removal of id 222 has nothing of its own to take, and only drops a record left from before the variable was the cluster's. A cluster without `spec.env` `NEO4J_PLUGINS` wrote two template changes on an enable — the stamp, then the merge (#608 item 3); its stamp now carries the merge's addition, one roll too (id 226).
  - **The variable leaves or joins `spec.env` while Fleet Management is on:** the template apply that stops owning it keeps `fleet-management` in it and records it as Fleet Management's in the same write (`addFleetEntryInWrite` after `writeOwnedTemplateItems`), one roll, where the next pass's merge put the name back after the apply dropped it; the apply that starts owning it writes the user's list with the name, one roll, and drops the record the variable had as Fleet Management's in the same write (id 227).
  - **Cannot be added to:** a `NEO4J_PLUGINS` read from a Secret or ConfigMap (`valueFrom`), or a value that is not a JSON list of names, is left as the spec gives it. Each validator raises one `ValidationWarning` (`FleetPluginsEnvWarnings`: "… Aura Fleet Management needs fleet-management in that list, and the operator cannot add it"; announced when it starts to apply, id 145), and the merge reports nothing — it writes nothing. Before, the cluster's create and every template write of the standalone failed, and a running cluster's merge raised `AuraFleetManagementPluginPatchFailed`: `addFleetManagementPlugin` put a value beside `valueFrom`, which the API server refuses ("may not be specified when `value` is not empty"), and a list that did not parse was an error. It now skips a `valueFrom` variable, and a list that does not parse no longer fails a deployment's own write (`errNeo4jPluginsNotAList`). Not a refusal: the list may name the plugin already, and the user's Secret is theirs to keep.
  - **2026.03 and later:** nothing is appended (Fleet Manager is built in, id 192), no warning is raised, and a `fleet-management` the user lists stays theirs on and off. An upgrade's Staging onto such an image renders the list without the name the cluster appended, in the write that changes the image.
  - **Standalone:** its controller applies its template when the hash of what it renders moves, and never puts a live value back (id 135), so it never looped, lost the name, or rolled twice for it: its template write appends the name to its own list (`addFleetEntryInWrite`), and that write is the one the conf hash of the same toggle moves (the disable's of id 222). Its merge now leaves its own variable alone too, so an enable on a cache that has not seen the new ConfigMap rolls the pod once, with the new conf hash, not first for the merge. Its `valueFrom` and not-a-list cases were the same failed write as the cluster's.
  - **Not changed:** a `Neo4jPlugin` on a deployment whose `spec.env` sets `NEO4J_PLUGINS` is still `Failed` (`pluginEnvOwnedError`, id 135), and a list the user keeps in a Secret is still the user's.
  - **Restarts:** on the operator upgrade, a cluster in the loop (5.26.11+, 2025.08–2026.02, with a JSON list in `spec.env` and Aura Fleet Management on) rolls at most once more — to the list with the name, none if its last write was the merge's — and stops. With a list from a Secret, or not a JSON list: a cluster created that way was never created and is now; one that turned Fleet Management on later stops raising `AuraFleetManagementPluginPatchFailed` and restarts nothing; a standalone whose template writes failed applies its template once (one roll). Nothing else changes.
- **why:** #608 item 1, the #606 walk (2026-10-28): a 3 × 5.26.32-enterprise cluster with `spec.env` `NEO4J_PLUGINS='["apoc"]'` and `spec.auraFleetManagement.enabled: true` (no token). The server template alternated between `["apoc"]` and `["apoc","fleet-management"]`, `NEO4J_PLUGINS` the only difference: revision 9 to 32 in six minutes, the generation up 4 a minute, servers restarting once or twice a minute, phase `Forming`, no ERROR. v1.23.0 the same (revision 35 to 52).
- **pinned-by:** `TestClusterReconcile_ItsOwnNeo4jPluginsWithFleetManagementSettles` (forming and with every server Ready, six passes: no write; a hand-edited value put back in one roll; a StatefulSet an earlier operator's loop left at either value: at most one roll, then nothing — 11 rolls in six passes and `["apoc"]` on the earlier code), `TestClusterReconcile_TogglingFleetManagementWithItsOwnNeo4jPlugins` (off and on: one roll each, the conf's restart stamp; a `fleet-management` the user wrote kept), `TestClusterReconcile_EnablingFleetManagementWithItsOwnNeo4jPlugins` (one roll; a cluster without the variable: two), `TestClusterReconcile_ItsOwnNeo4jPluginsTheOperatorCannotAddTo` (from a Secret, not a JSON list, a list of numbers: nothing written, one `ValidationWarning`, no `AuraFleetManagementPluginPatchFailed`), `TestClusterReconcile_ItsOwnNeo4jPluginsOnABuiltInImage`, `TestClusterReconcile_SpecEnvNeo4jPluginsComesAndGoesWhileFleetIsOn`, `TestBuildDesiredServerStatefulSet_RendersItsOwnNeo4jPlugins`, `TestClusterReconcile_APluginIsStillRefusedTheClustersOwnNeo4jPlugins`, `TestReconcile_ANeo4jPluginsFromASecretWithFleetManagement_APIServer` (envtest: both kinds created; the API server refused the earlier write), `TestStandaloneReconcile_ItsOwnNeo4jPluginsWithFleetManagement`, `TestStandaloneReconcile_EnablingFleetManagementWithItsOwnNeo4jPluginsIsOneRoll`, `TestStandaloneReconcile_ItsOwnNeo4jPluginsTheOperatorCannotAddTo` (`internal/controller/fleet_owned_plugins_test.go`); `TestFleetPluginsEnvWarnings` (`internal/validation/fleet_env_warnings_test.go`). Each part switched off under `go test -overlay` fails its tests: the rendering (the hand edit stays, Staging drops the name), the stamp's carry (two rolls each way), the cluster's and the standalone's merge guard (a Warning for a list that does not parse; two writes on a stale cache), the `valueFrom` skip and the not-a-list tolerance (the writes fail), the apply's hand-over (a second roll), the warning. The built-in, plugin and standalone-settle cases pass on the earlier code too, which is their point.
- **enforcement:** unit test + envtest; walked live on 2026-10-28 (`09ebae00`): a 5.26.32 cluster looping under v1.23.0 (generation 1 → 21 in about 3½ minutes) settled with no further roll once this build took over and stayed so for six minutes; each Fleet Management toggle and each `spec.env` change was one roll on the cluster and on a standalone; a user-listed `fleet-management` stayed through off and on; a Secret-sourced list raised one `ValidationWarning` and no write, and the standalone's pod was created with it; a `Neo4jPlugin` whose name the cluster's list lacks was refused (rule 135).

### id 224 — A scale-down set back gives back the servers its drain cordoned and refuses what Neo4j cannot undo; a cordoned server is a cluster member
- **scope:** `ScaleDownCordonedServersAnnotation`, `cordonedByDrain`, `setBackScaleDown`, `giveBackCordoned`, `serverPastUndoing`, `serversPastUndoing`, `drainTouched`, `reportedOrdinals`, `runsInOrdinal`, `keptByTheSpec`, `setBackRefusal`, `serverLabel`, `clearDrainRecords`, `setScaleDownIDs`, and the cordon step and drain-set loop of `reconcileScaleDownDrain` (`internal/controller/scale_down.go`); `EnableServer`, `buildEnableServerStatement` (`internal/neo4j/server_management.go`); `EventReasonScaleDownSetBack` (`internal/controller/events.go`); `serverIsMember`, `serverServes` (`internal/controller/cluster_availability.go`) and the counts that use them: `assessServerShortfall`, `countAvailableServers` (`internal/controller/reconcile_cadence.go`, also the largest view's and the legacy count in `verifyNeo4jClusterFormation` and `legacyClusterFormationCheck`), `countUniqueServersInGroup` and `getVisibleServerAddresses` (`internal/controller/splitbrain_detector.go`), `recordShortfallDuringUpgrade` (`internal/controller/upgrade_diagnostics.go`), `podServerAvailable` (`internal/controller/rolling_upgrade_statemachine.go`); the `ServersPendingDrain` guidance of `kubectl neo4j explain` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/explain.go`); the scale-down section of `docs/user_guide/clustering.md`.
- **rule:**
  - **The record.** The drain's cordon step records the servers it cordons in `neo4j.com/scale-down-cordoned-servers` on the cluster CR (a merge patch of that annotation alone, id 150), before it runs `CORDON`: an operator restart between the two leaves no cordon unrecorded, and a recorded server that is not cordoned is left as it is. `SHOW SERVERS` does not say who cordoned a server, and the drain set is not enough on its own: a removed server cordoned by hand before the scale-down is in the drain set, which deallocates it without cordoning it. The record goes when the drain ends and when a set-back is resolved.
  - **A set-back gives back what the drain took.** When the StatefulSet runs no more servers than `spec.topology.servers` and a drain is on record (its drain set or its cordon record), the pass reads `SHOW SERVERS` from the leader and runs `ENABLE SERVER '<id>'` for each recorded server that is still `Cordoned`, never one the record does not list. It then drops both records, releases the hold and settles `ServersPendingDrain` with "Scale-down not in progress" (id 219), with one Normal `ScaleDownSetBack` naming the servers it enabled ("Scale-down set back: enabled 2 server(s) the drain had cordoned: c-server-3 (<id>), c-server-4 (<id>)"). `ENABLE SERVER` is the documented way back from `Cordoned` on both lines (Operations Manual, "Managing servers in a cluster", `5.x` and `current`: "A server in the cordoned state can be transitioned to deallocating state or back to enabled. To re-enable a server, run the `ENABLE SERVER` command"); `dbms.cluster.uncordonServer` is deprecated since 5.23 and Cypher 5 only on CalVer. A recorded server that is no longer `Cordoned` is left alone, so the passes after a set-back, and one on a copy of the cluster from before its writes, enable nothing again. While Neo4j cannot be asked, the records and the hold stay and the next pass asks again. A cluster with no drain on record asks Neo4j nothing on this path, as before. The drain waits 20 s after its cordon before it deallocates, so a set-back in that time is given back (id 231); before, the DEALLOCATE followed the cordon within about a second, and a set-back by hand was nearly always refused.
  - **A partial set-back.** A scale-down set back part of the way (5 → 3, then 4) keeps a server of the drain set whose pod ordinal is below the new spec, read from the address `SHOW SERVERS` reports or the one it last reported, when no other row reports that pod's address as its own (an earlier identity of the pod is drained as before, id 213). It leaves the drain set and is given back as above, and the drain goes on with the rest. Before, the union of the drain set kept it, so the drain dropped a server whose pod the StatefulSet then kept.
  - **Past undoing: refused.** A server the drain took that is `Deallocating`, `Deallocated` or `Dropped`, in a pod the StatefulSet keeps, cannot be given back. Such a pod is one below the new spec on a partial set-back, or any pod on a full one (also when no ordinal is known). The manual says of deallocation "This state is irreversible", and of a dropped server that it "cannot rejoin a cluster". The set-back is then refused: nothing is enabled, nothing more is drained and the replicas stay held. `ServersPendingDrain` is `True` with reason `ScaleDownBlocked` and one `ScaleDownBlocked` Warning (id 145): "Scale-down set back too late for c-server-3 (<id>), …: the drain has begun deallocating them, and Neo4j cannot enable a server again once it deallocates, nor can a dropped server rejoin. Set spec.topology.servers to 3 so the drain can finish them, then raise it to add new servers in their place. Replicas are held at 5." Lowering the spec lets the drain finish, and the replicas then go down with the removed servers' PVCs (`WhenScaled: Delete`). Raising it afterwards adds new servers with new identities, once those pods and PVCs are gone: the raise waits for them (id 229). The operator does not run that sequence itself: it would lower the replicas below what the spec asks for and delete volumes the spec keeps, and it would have to wait out the garbage collection of those PVCs before scaling up, or a new pod would start on the old volume with the dropped identity. A server the drain never took (one deallocated by hand) is not the set-back's to refuse. While refused, the cluster reads `Forming`, as for any spec that asks for more servers than are members.
  - **A cordoned server is a member.** A cordoned server "cannot be assigned to host any newly created databases; however, the server retains the databases it already hosts" (same section). The formation check, the split-brain check's views and its pacing (id 125), the shortfall assessment (id 105) and the upgrade's `Degraded` write (id 214) count a server that is `Enabled` or `Cordoned` and `Available` (`serverServes`). Before, they counted only `Enabled`, so a cluster with servers cordoned (by hand, as the manual's error handling does, or by a set-back drain) read as a scale-up in progress: `Forming` with "Cluster formation verification incomplete", which paused every dependent. A cluster with a server cordoned by hand is now `Ready`, and `ServersHealthy` still names it (`state=Cordoned`). A cordoned server that is down is a server short, not one joining. Free, deallocating, deallocated and dropped servers are not members.
  - **The upgrade's wait follows.** A cluster with a server cordoned by hand is `Ready`, so an upgrade can start on it. The rolling upgrade's wait for each restarted server (`podServerAvailable`, from `isServerAvailable`) takes a member that is `Available` at the pod's address: a cordoned server keeps its state across the restart. It used to wait for `Enabled`, so such an upgrade would have waited at that server until it failed. **Not changed:** the final version check (`versionMismatchesFromServers`) still skips a server that is not `Enabled`, so a stale row cannot wedge it, and `WaitForServerAvailable` in `internal/neo4j/client.go` still waits for `Enabled`.
- **why:** #608 item 2, from the walk of the #606 fixes (3 × 2026.08.1, 2026-10-28): scale 3 → 5 and wait for `Ready`, then 5 → 3 and at once back to 5. `ServersPendingDrain` read `False` "Scale-down not in progress" and the replicas stayed 5, but server-3 and server-4, which the drain's first pass had cordoned, stayed `Cordoned`. The cluster sat in `Forming`, Ready `Unknown` "Cluster formation verification incomplete", for 4+ minutes, until a later 5 → 3 dropped them. v1.23.0 did the same. The set-back released the hold and reset the condition without asking Neo4j, and the formation check, the legacy count and the shortfall assessment counted only `Enabled` servers.
- **pinned-by:** `TestScaleDownDrain_ASetBackEnablesWhatTheDrainCordoned` (3 → 5 → 3 → 5: both enabled, the records gone, "Scale-down not in progress", one `ScaleDownSetBack`; three more passes and one on the copy from before the set-back enable nothing), `TestScaleDownDrain_ASetBackLeavesAServerCordonedByHand` (a removed and a kept server cordoned by hand stay `Cordoned`; the formation count, the split-brain analysis and the shortfall assessment find the cluster whole), `TestScaleDownDrain_ASetBackTooLateIsRefused` (deallocating with no address in `SHOW SERVERS`; dropped and still running; a server deallocated by hand that the drain never took; lowered again, the drain finishes), `TestScaleDownDrain_APartialSetBack`, `TestScaleDownDrain_ASetBackWaitsUntilNeo4jCanBeAsked`, `TestScaleDownDrain_NoDrainOnRecordAsksNeo4jNothing`, `TestServersPastUndoing` (`internal/controller/scale_down_set_back_test.go`); `TestAssessServerShortfall_NoneShortAndCordonedMembers`, `TestRecordShortfallDuringUpgrade_CountsACordonedServer`, `TestPodServerAvailable_ACordonedServerThatIsBack` (`internal/controller/degraded_none_short_test.go`); `TestSplitBrainCheckSkippable` (`internal/controller/reconcile_cadence_test.go`); `TestBuildEnableServerStatement` (`internal/neo4j/server_management_test.go`). Each part switched off under `go test -overlay` fails its tests: the set-back as before, the give-back without the record (a server cordoned by hand enabled), the partial set-back, the refusal, the refusal of a server the drain never took, the give-back's state check (each server enabled twice), the member count, the upgrade's wait.
- **enforcement:** unit test; walked live on 2026-10-28 (trial `f2083ad2`, 3 × 2026.08.1): 5 → 3 → 5 gave back both cordoned servers with one `ScaleDownSetBack`, the cluster `Ready` throughout; a server cordoned by hand stayed cordoned through a set-back and an upgrade 2026.08.1 → 2026.09.0 that walked past it; a set-back 0.11 s after deallocation began was refused, and lowering the spec finished the drain. The walk saw two `ScaleDownBlocked` Warnings for that one refusal — the message named each server's state, which moved on from `Deallocating` to `Deallocated` — so the message names the servers alone (`setBackRefusal`; `TestScaleDownDrain_ASetBackTooLateIsRefused` moves the states on).

### id 225 — The Degraded condition says a server is short only when one is; a formed cluster short of nothing is left as it is
- **scope:** `verdictNoneShort`, the `NotReadyPods` input and the check after the member count in `assessServerShortfall` (`internal/controller/cluster_availability.go`); `reconcileServerShortfall` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`), which reads `notReadyServerPods` before the assessment and hands it to `outageDetail`.
- **rule:** The normal pass assesses a formed cluster that the formation check or the rollout check did not pass (id 105). When `SHOW SERVERS` lists every server `spec.topology.servers` asks for as a member (id 224) and `Available`, and Kubernetes reports every one of their pods (`{cluster}-server-0` to N-1) ready, nothing is short. The verdict is `NoneShort` and nothing is written: the phase, the message and the `Degraded` condition stay as they are, and the pass that finds both checks passing writes `Ready` with `Degraded=False`. A server that is not `Available`, or a pod of one that is not ready, missing or terminating, is still a shortfall, and its message names it: "2 of 3 servers available; unavailable: c-server-1 (Unavailable)", or "Neo4j still lists 3 of 3 servers available; server pods not ready: c-server-2 (terminating)". `Degraded=True` (`ServersUnavailable`) never reads "3 of 3 servers available". The upgrade's `Degraded` write already wrote `True` only for a server it names (id 214).
- **why:** #608 item 4, from the walk of the #606 fixes (2026-10-28): for about 10 s after a completed 5 → 3 drain, `Degraded` read `True` "3 of 3 servers available". The drain had released its hold and the apply had lowered the replicas. The removed servers' pods were still terminating and `SHOW SERVERS` still listed them `Dropped`, and the formation check did not pass, most likely because the split-brain check asks every server pod there is, the removed ones too (from the code; the walk has not confirmed which check it was). The assessment found every server the spec keeps available and each of their pods ready, and still took the shortfall branch, whose message is the plain count.
- **pinned-by:** `TestReconcileServerShortfall_AfterADrainNothingIsShort` (Ready, the removed pods terminating and listed `Dropped`, three formation and rollout details: phase, message and `Degraded=False` unchanged, its `lastTransitionTime` too, no event; a kept server down is still named), `TestAssessServerShortfall_NoneShortAndCordonedMembers` (`internal/controller/degraded_none_short_test.go`); the terminating and missing pod rows of `TestAssessServerShortfall` (`internal/controller/cluster_availability_test.go`). They fail with the `NoneShort` branch switched off under `go test -overlay`: `Degraded=True` with the plain count of three of three, as the walk saw, and the status message moved to the shortfall's.
- **enforcement:** unit test; walked live on 2026-10-28 (trial `f2083ad2`): 96 one-second samples across a 5 → 3 drain, 91 after "Scale-down drain complete" while the removed pods terminated, all `Degraded` `False` / `AllServersAvailable`; none read "3 of 3 servers available".

### id 226 — Turning Aura Fleet Management on for a running deployment rolls its servers once: the conf change's write carries the name
- **scope:** the Aura Fleet Management block of `triggerRollingRestartForConfigChange` and `confRollPending` (`internal/controller/configmap_manager.go`); `addFleetEntryInWrite` (`internal/controller/fleet_owned_plugins.go`); `mergeFleetManagementPlugin` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go` and `internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`); `standaloneConfRollDue` (`internal/controller/fleet_enable.go`); `renderedConfSemantic` (`internal/controller/fleet_disable.go`).
- **rule:**
  - **What the enable changes:** turning Aura Fleet Management on changes `neo4j.conf` (Fleet Management's procedure lines, id 192), which is not dynamic and rolls the servers, and `NEO4J_PLUGINS` (the `fleet-management` name, id 175). Both go in one write, as the disable's do (id 222).
  - **Cluster:** the ConfigMap's restart stamp — the write that rolls the servers for the conf change (`triggerRollingRestartForConfigChange`) — adds `fleet-management` to a `NEO4J_PLUGINS` the cluster does not own and records it as Fleet Management's (`addFleetEntryInWrite`; id 222), in that write. A variable the cluster owns carries the name as it renders it (`carryOwnNeo4jPlugins`, id 223); one that cannot take it, and an image with Fleet Manager built in, get nothing. Any conf roll while the spec installs the plugin carries a missing name. The merge later in the pass finds it listed and writes nothing.
  - **The cluster's merge waits for a due conf roll and writes against the API server:** it decides from the cached StatefulSet; when the name is due it waits while a conf roll is due (`confRollPending`: the ConfigMap as read differs from what the cluster renders — the manager's one-second debounce put the conf write off, or the cache has not seen it), and otherwise re-reads the StatefulSet from the API server (`apiReader`) and writes against that. A cache that has not seen the stamp neither writes the name a second time nor runs out of Conflict retries, which was an `AuraFleetManagementPluginPatchFailed`. The name with no conf change to carry it (a stamp that failed, a conf that was already right) is written alone, as before.
  - **Standalone:** the StatefulSet write that carries its new conf hash adds the name already (`addFleetEntryInWrite`, id 175), so a pass that read the new ConfigMap was one write. One that read it from a cache that had not seen the pass's ConfigMap write stamped the old conf, and the merge then added the name alone: two rolls. The merge now waits while the conf the StatefulSet records (`neo4j.com/config-semantic-hash`) is not the one the standalone renders now (`standaloneConfRollDue`, `renderedConfSemantic`), as the disable's removal waits (`standaloneFleetEntryDue`, id 222). A StatefulSet without that record, or a conf that cannot be rendered, is not waited for.
  - **Not changed:** a new deployment's StatefulSet is created with the name (id 175); on 2026.03 and later nothing is added (id 192); the disable is one roll (id 222); a cluster whose own `spec.env` sets `NEO4J_PLUGINS` was one roll already (id 223).
- **why:** #608 item 3, the #606 walk (2026-10-28): enabling Fleet Management on a running 3 × 5.26.32 cluster without `spec.env` `NEO4J_PLUGINS` wrote two controllerrevisions in the same second — the conf-restart stamp, then the `NEO4J_PLUGINS` merge. The pods went straight to the second, so they restarted once, but a slower StatefulSet controller could restart a pod twice. #613's `TestClusterReconcile_EnablingFleetManagementWithItsOwnNeo4jPlugins` measured "1 with, 2 without".
- **pinned-by:** `TestClusterReconcile_EnablingFleetManagementIsOneRoll` (every server Ready, and forming: one write, the restart stamp, with the name and its record; then nothing), `TestClusterReconcile_TheMergeWaitsForTheConfRoll` (the conf write put off by the debounce: the pass writes nothing; the next one rolls once, with the name), `TestMergeFleetManagementPlugin_ACacheThatHasNotSeenTheStamp` (no write, no error), `TestStandaloneReconcile_EnablingFleetManagementIsOneRoll` (a whole pass; a ConfigMap read before the pass's write: nothing, then one write with the new conf hash and the name) (`internal/controller/fleet_enable_test.go`); `TestClusterReconcile_EnablingFleetManagementWithItsOwnNeo4jPlugins` (`internal/controller/fleet_owned_plugins_test.go`: now one template change with `spec.env` `NEO4J_PLUGINS` and one without). Each part switched off under `go test -overlay` fails its tests: the stamp's addition (two rolls in the cluster tests, two without `spec.env` `NEO4J_PLUGINS`), the merge's wait (the put-off pass writes the name), the API-server read (the merge's write runs out of Conflict retries), the standalone's wait (the merge writes first).
- **enforcement:** unit test; walked live on 2026-10-28 (trial `f2083ad2`): enabling Fleet Management on a running 3 × 5.26.32 cluster without `spec.env` `NEO4J_PLUGINS` was one StatefulSet write carrying `["fleet-management"]` and its record, one new revision, each pod replaced once; on a standalone, one write and one pod replacement; the plugin-only line was logged once at info (once more after an off and on).

### id 227 — Fleet Management's record goes when NEO4J_PLUGINS becomes the deployment's own, in the write that takes it back
- **scope:** `addFleetEntryInWrite` (`internal/controller/fleet_owned_plugins.go`); `fleetRecordUpkeep` (`internal/controller/fleet_disable.go`); the template apply of `createOrUpdateResourceInternal` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`) and the template write of `reconcileStatefulSet` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisestandalone_controller.go`).
- **rule:** a `NEO4J_PLUGINS` the deployment's own `spec.env` sets carries no record (id 222). When `spec.env` sets the variable again while Fleet Management is on, the template write that takes it back — the write that rolls the servers for the user's list, with `fleet-management` appended (id 223) — also drops `neo4j.com/aura-fleet-management-entries`: no write and no roll is added. A record left on such a variable before this change (the record exists since #611, after v1.23.0, so only a build from main wrote one) is dropped by the enabled pass's upkeep (`fleetRecordUpkeep`: after a cluster's template apply, `reconcileFleetEntry`; in a standalone's merge) with a patch of the StatefulSet's metadata, which restarts nothing. With Fleet Management off the disable's removal already only dropped such a record (id 222). The record is not involved in what the servers run: the disable never took a name out of a variable the deployment owns.
- **why:** #608, found by the walk of #613 (2026-10-28): Fleet Management on, `spec.env` emptied (the entry recorded as Fleet Management's), then `spec.env` `NEO4J_PLUGINS` set again (the cluster owns the variable once more): the record stayed until the next disable, which dropped it in its one roll. Cosmetic, but the annotation said the entry was Fleet Management's when it was the user's list.
- **pinned-by:** `TestClusterReconcile_TheRecordGoesWhenSpecEnvTakesNeo4jPluginsBack`, `TestStandaloneReconcile_TheRecordGoesWhenSpecEnvTakesNeo4jPluginsBack` (`internal/controller/fleet_enable_test.go`: the sequence — one write, one roll, the record gone; then a record left on the user's list: one metadata patch, no roll). With the drop in the write switched off under `go test -overlay` the take-back is two writes (the next pass's patch); with the upkeep's case switched off the left record stays.
- **enforcement:** unit test; walked live on 2026-10-28 (trial `f2083ad2`): on the cluster and the standalone, `spec.env` `NEO4J_PLUGINS` set, emptied and set again — the record appeared with the emptying write and went in the write that took the variable back, with no metadata-only write in the two minutes after.

### id 228 — A Neo4jPlugin refused for something the user must fix is a status logged once at info, never an ERROR; a real failure keeps its ERROR
- **scope:** `pluginInstallRefusal`, `pluginListUnreadableError`, `ownedEnvRefusal`, `refuseInstall` (`internal/controller/plugin_install_refusal.go`); in `internal/controller/plugin_controller.go` the validation and duplicate refusals of `Reconcile`, its refusal checks after `installChangesTarget` and `writePluginToTarget`, the settled branch's read error, `installChangesTarget`, the owned-variable check of `writePluginToTarget`, the `NEO4J_PLUGINS` parse of `applyPluginEnv`; `reportRefused` (`internal/controller/plugin_refused.go`); `logRefusal`, `logAnnounced` (`internal/controller/refusal_log.go`); `pluginEnvOwnedError`.
- **rule:**
  - **The refusals** (id 136's "…and so is its log line", for the plugin): a spec the validator refuses (phase `Invalid`, `ValidationFailed`), a newer duplicate of a `(clusterRef, spec.name)` tuple (`Failed`, `PluginDuplicate`, id 195), a name the target's image cannot install (`Failed`, `PluginUnknownToImage`, id 170), a write that would change a variable the cluster renders from its own spec (`Failed`, `PluginInstallFailed`, id 135), and a `NEO4J_PLUGINS` on the target that is not a JSON list of names (`Failed`, `PluginInstallFailed`). Each is logged by `logRefusal` from the status write that records it — at info when the write changed the status (`PluginUnknownToImage`: when it announces the refusal), at V(1) on the passes and steps in between — never as an ERROR and never as a returned error; its event comes from the same write.
  - **An install refusal is decided first:** from the StatefulSet, in `installChangesTarget` (which returns `pluginEnvOwnedError` for an install that would change the cluster's own variables) — before the gate that waits for a Ready target and the Bolt check that it answers, which are for a write there is none of. `writePluginToTarget` keeps its check for a record that changed in between. The refusal is the error itself, unwrapped, so its message is the same whichever step met it: status "Plugin installation refused: <reason>", event "Plugin <name> installation refused: <reason>".
  - **How it is woken:** every `RequeueAfter` (30 seconds), as before: the fix is in the target's spec, which changes without an edit of the plugin, and the StatefulSet watch wakes only `Ready` plugins (id 135). Those passes write nothing and log at V(1). An edit of the plugin wakes it too.
  - **Real failures stay errors:** a StatefulSet write the API server refuses (Forbidden, Invalid, a quota), a read that fails, anything not listed above — `ERROR "Failed to install plugin"`, `Failed`, one `PluginInstallFailed` per failure and a retry every `RequeueAfter` (`installFailed`, id 135); a Conflict or a server that cannot be reached is still `Installing` (`retryablePluginError`).
- **why:** #608, found by the walk of #613 (2026-10-28): a Managed GDS plugin on a cluster whose own `spec.env` `NEO4J_PLUGINS` lacked its name logged `ERROR "Failed to install plugin"` with a stack trace on every retry, about every 27 seconds, while the cluster, standalone, database and restore refusals log once at info (ids 136, 197, 215). Each retry also ran the Bolt functional check for a write it was going to refuse. The `Invalid`, duplicate and image refusals logged nothing at info at all, and a `NEO4J_PLUGINS` that is not a JSON list logged two ERRORs per pass.
- **pinned-by:** `TestReconcile_ARefusedPluginIsLoggedOnceNotAsAnError` (three passes through `Reconcile` into a level-capturing logger for each of the five refusals: the phase, one event, no ERROR, one info line, two at V(1), no StatefulSet write; the install refusals ask the target nothing), `TestReconcile_AnInstallRefusalIsNotAWaitForTheTarget` (a forming cluster: refused, not `Waiting`; the cluster's list fixed, installed on the next look), `TestReconcile_ARealInstallFailureIsStillLoggedAsAnError` (a Forbidden write: `Failed`, ERROR "Failed to install plugin"), `TestPluginInstallRefusal` (`internal/controller/plugin_install_refusal_test.go`). With `plugin_controller.go` and `plugin_refused.go` as on `59e765eb` (`go test -overlay`) the first two fail: an ERROR on each of the three passes (two a pass for the list that does not parse), no info line for any refusal, the Bolt check asked three times, and the forming cluster's plugin `Waiting`.
- **enforcement:** unit test; walked live on 2026-10-28 (trial `f2083ad2`): a Managed GDS plugin on a cluster whose own list lacked its name was `Failed` within 8 s with "Plugin installation refused: …", one `PluginInstallFailed`, one info line and no ERROR over three minutes, and no template write; adding its name to `spec.env` installed it. No machine check that a NEW plugin refusal goes through `refuseInstall` or `logRefusal` — convention + code review.

### id 229 — A raise waits until the pods and volumes of the servers a scale-down removed are gone; a kept pod that runs a dropped server is named
- **scope:** `holdRaiseForRemovedServers`, `removedServerLeftovers`, `ownedByPod`, `dataVolumeName`, `raiseHoldMessage`, `leftoverFixes`, `raiseHoldPoll`, `removedServersGrace`, `droppedServersInKeptPods`, `droppedServersMessage` (`internal/controller/raise_hold.go`); `holdTopologyChange`, `writeTopologyDeferral` (`internal/controller/upgrade_topology.go`); its call in `Reconcile`, the wakes `Reconcile` applies to its result, and the Forming branch of `reconcileServerShortfall` (`internal/controller/neo4jenterprisecluster_controller.go`); `passWakes` (`internal/controller/pass_wake.go`); `ConditionReasonWaitsForRemovedServers` (`internal/controller/conditions.go`); `EventReasonScaleUpDeferred`, `EventReasonScaleUpBlocked`, `EventReasonDroppedServersReturned` (`internal/controller/events.go`); the `TopologyChangeDeferred` guidance of `kubectl neo4j explain` (`cmd/kubectl-neo4j/explain.go`); the scale-up and scale-down sections of `docs/user_guide/clustering.md`.
- **rule:**
  - **Held.** On every normal pass `holdTopologyChange` reads the server StatefulSet from the API server (not the cache: one that has not seen the drain lower the replicas reads no raise, and a hold below the replicas there are would lower them with no drain). When `spec.topology.servers` is above its replicas, it reads, from the API server, the pod and the data volume (`data-<cluster>-server-<n>`) of each ordinal from the replicas up. A pod that is still there, or a volume being reclaimed — being deleted, or owned by its pod, as `persistentVolumeClaimRetentionPolicy.whenScaled: Delete` leaves a condemned pod's — holds the raise below that ordinal: the pass's copy of `spec.topology.servers` is set to the first such ordinal, as the held-upgrade hold does (id 163), so the ConfigMap, the drain and the formation check work on the servers there are and the cluster stays `Ready`. `TopologyChangeDeferred` is `True`, reason `WaitsForRemovedServers`: "spec.topology.servers is 5; the server StatefulSet keeps 3 servers until the pods and volumes of the servers a scale-down removed are gone (c-server-3, c-server-4): a server added on a removed server's volume would start as the server Neo4j dropped, and could not rejoin", with one Normal `ScaleUpDeferred` ("Adding servers 3-4 waits until …") from the write that sets that reason. The pass asks to run again within 5 s (`raiseHoldPoll`, through `passWakes`): nothing the operator watches changes when the collector deletes a volume. As ordinals clear, the raise goes on up to the next one; once none is left, the change is applied and the condition goes `False` with "No change of spec.topology.servers waits for the pods and volumes of removed servers" (`noTopologyChangeWaits` names the wait that ended; after an upgrade's it reads "… waits for an upgrade", and a `False` condition keeps its message).
  - **Not held.** A scale-up with nothing left at those ordinals reads two objects per ordinal, once, and goes on. A volume nothing reclaims — no pod owns it, it is not being deleted — is not held for: on Kubernetes 1.32+ (the chart's floor; `StatefulSetAutoDeletePVC` is GA) a removed pod's volume is always handed to the collector, so such a volume was made some other way, perhaps ready for a new server.
  - **Past the grace.** What is still there `removedServersGrace` (2 minutes) after the condition went `True` — a pod terminating on a node that is gone, a volume a foreign finalizer keeps — is still held for: a server added on that volume would start the dropped identity. The message then names each — a pod "is being deleted, due to stop by <time>": a pod's `deletionTimestamp` is the end of its grace period, not when its deletion began (the walk of the #619 fixes read "terminating since" a time 30 s ahead) — and how to remove it (`kubectl delete pod … --grace-period=0 --force` if its node is gone, `kubectl delete pvc …`, or the finalizer that keeps it), or to set `spec.topology.servers` back; one `ScaleUpBlocked` Warning from the write that first says so. The operator deletes nothing: it cannot read which identity a volume holds, and the user may keep volumes on purpose.
  - **Already stuck.** A formed cluster whose `SHOW SERVERS` lists a server `Dropped` at the address of a ready pod below `spec.topology.servers` that no member is the server of (the address it reports or last reported, `withKnownAddresses`; a pod that is not ready may be a new server starting while the row of the one it replaces lingers), or, with no address known for any dropped row, a ready kept pod whose server is no member (`droppedServersInKeptPods`), reads `Forming` with "c-server-3, c-server-4 run servers Neo4j has dropped (…): they started on the volumes of servers a scale-down removed, before those were deleted, and a dropped server cannot rejoin. Set spec.topology.servers to 3; once ServersPendingDrain reads "Scale-down drain complete", raise it again: …", in `status.message` and the `Ready` condition, with one `DroppedServersReturned` Warning from the write that puts it there. Lowering the spec drains nothing (a dropped server is not drained), releases the replicas, and the raise after it is held as above.
